# What was built

The project's history, moved out of `CLAUDE.md` on 09.11.2026 so it is no longer loaded in every session. New finished work goes at the end, one paragraph each; the decisions are in `docs/decisions-pending.md`.

M1–M7 and the roofs are done as a PoC (2026-10-24): layered walls with joins, zones and lintels; floors; structure; roofs with walls under them; doors and windows with their configurator; materials and drafting standards; the 2D drawings (plans, sections, elevations, annotations); sheets with PDF and DXF; joinery and lintel schedules; phases; old projects (format 2) open; the macOS build. A second round added CAD drafting (command line, snaps, ortho, tracking, layers), the `studio` module (solids from sketches, live booleans), the `civil` module (terrain from survey points and LandXML, platforms), steel profiles, slab and roof openings, VV/graphics sets, a Romanian UI and an MCP bridge (`docs/mcp.md`). The old Lapin's issue list is checked in `docs/issues-old-lapin.md`. A third round (25.10.2026) added the IFC export, and the UX items #115–118: 2D zoom commands, panes (split) and detached view windows, Lapin's own tool icons (`ui/src/icons`), the key plan over the views. Then `docs/plan-direct-editing.md` (#119): grips on axes, outlines and sketches, live length and angle input, the spreadsheet-style properties panel with user parameters, CAD names and roles, closed/morphing/two-rail sweeps and lofts, seven new dimension kinds, the cut order in Manage, Studio classes and types, walls attached to roofs. Then (#121): curved walls and beams (an arc axis, joined by its tangent), grips in 3D, the cut order by material, type parameters inherited by elements, curtain walls with their configurator, flip arrows in plan, move to storey. Then (25.10.2026) the visual seams: where one material goes on in the same plane from one element into another (a facade over the storey above), no line is drawn between them, in 3D, elevations and sections (`Seams`, `SeamTests`); the elements stay apart. Then `docs/plan-views-and-joinery.md` (#125–127): the browser's sets and duplicating views, sheets, levels and 3D views; the joinery configurator's drawings from the symbol engine, used by plans and elevations; reveals, sills, surrounds and thresholds as parts of doors and windows.

Then the engine in the browser (#124, `docs/plan-browser-engine.md`, stages 1–4 done on 25.10.2026): the same engine AOT-compiled in a Web Worker, 6.8 MB, 2.4× native; the desktop unchanged. Live at https://lapin.ciuntucbimstudio.ro/web/ (`deploy/web/deploy.sh`).

Then (#129): foreign IFC recognised into native elements on import (`docs/spec-ifc.md`, Recunoașterea): `IIfcRecognizer` in the platform, bodies read as prisms, walls with axes, layers, openings, doors and windows, floors, columns, beams, pad footings; the rest as references drawn with real faces.

Then (26.10.2026, #130): native DXF import (`docs/dxf-import.md`): layers, shapes, hatches with the file's patterns, texts, linear dimensions, blocks exploded; into sections and elevations on the view's plane, mirrored when the view looks towards −X or −Y; `JOIN` welds lines into polylines.

Then (#131) the testers' reports of 21–23.10 resolved: the material picker, separation pens, line caps, textures per face, project units (metric and imperial), hatch origins; the reports script is `deploy/feedback/reports.sh`.

Then (#132) links: IFC and DXF files linked read-only (`docs/links.md`), Manage → Links (show, halftone, reload, convert, open separately, unlink), View → Fog.

Then (#134) versions in the `.lapin` file (`docs/versions.md`): each a manifest in `versions/index.json`, the parts the live project no longer has kept once under `versions/blobs/`; Manage → Versions (save, rename, update to current with revisions, restore as one undo step, compare element by element, export, open separately, delete).

Then (#136) a new project starts with Foundations, Ground floor and First floor; a plan draws only its own storey's elements unless its view says otherwise (View → Storeys shown, or the plan view's properties: some or all storeys, halftone), in the engine's drawing so sheets print it.

Then (#137) the visual joins are complete by default (a cut side another area's corner stands on is split there, so a core running through a butt corner no longer draws the boundary), and view styles (`docs/view-styles.md`): Architectural, Structural (cores only), MEP and the project's own, each with its layers (all, without finishes, core), joins and overhead; a view takes its discipline's style and may override any field (View → View settings, Manage → View styles); finishes by the material's category.

Then (#138) a view's or sheet's discipline is chosen in its properties and the browser files it under that discipline's set; the properties panel shows the view in front when nothing is selected. Then (#139) a drawing's settings live in the properties panel while it is selected in the browser: discipline, style, view range, reference level (kept on the plan view), graphics, storeys shown.

Then (#141) the 3D mesh exports (`docs/plan-mesh-export.md`): glTF/GLB, OpenUSD (USDA, USDZ) and COLLADA 1.4.1 (#142) written by `engine/src/Platform/Lapin.Mesh` after their public specifications, no dependencies; File → Export 3D…; textures and OBJ next, imports later if needed. Then (#143) door and window markers in plan (a line across the wall, the mark outside, width/height and sill inside), configured as tag types in Drafting standards → Tags; Annotate → Door & window tags.

Then (#147) doors and windows say how they open in IFC by IFC's own rule (+Y the way the panels open, left and right seen looking that way): OperationType/PartitioningType, panel and lining properties, an oriented placement and a mirrored type for elements flipped on one axis; the import reads them back (`docs/spec-ifc.md`, Deschiderea ușilor și ferestrelor). (#148) An IFC file's own 2D (footprints, plan symbols, fills, texts) is drawn in plans instead of the cut, switchable per view style (IFC 2D symbols). (#149) A dimension string is one object, like a polyline (`pts`, grips on points and segments, per-segment values; old chains upgraded on open by `IProjectUpgrade`). (#150) Rooms (`docs/rooms.md`): IfcSpace placed by a click inside walls (its outline follows them) or drawn, numbered, measured, scheduled; colour schemes (fill and hatch by function, department…) chosen per plan and drawn under the walls, the quick configurator View → Room colours, a colour legend; IfcSpace in IFC both ways.

Then (#151) site works (`docs/plan-site-works.md`): the `sitework` module, tab Site works — facade scaffolding along a line or round the walls' outer faces, formwork on floors (soffit, edges) and beams (bottom, sides, chamfers) from configurable system types, temporary in the execution phase, with schedules, a formwork plan and IFC assemblies; named element faces (`IElementFaces`, `core.faceAt`) and the 3D face pick. Then (#152) plans seen from above or from below (a reflected ceiling plan), curved beams and walls formed and scaffolded, formwork for walls, columns, ring beams and footings, formwork sets and reuse, deviz articles on the types.

Then (#153) working together (`docs/plan-multiplayer.md`): the desktop shares the open project as a listen server (File → Share session…), guests join through the relay (`engine/src/Relay/Lapin.Relay`, `deploy/relay`) from Lapin (Join session…) or from the browser by the link, as thin clients, end-to-end encrypted (AES-256-GCM, a key from the invitation's secret); each undoes only their own edits (`History`); a guest is limited to `EngineDispatcher.AllowedRemote`; presence (participants, cursors and selections in plan). A cloud server is for the premium edition.

Then (#155) the barrel roof (`shape: barrel`, `rise`; `RoofSolver.SolveBarrel`, planar strips on a circle) and `WallTops`: a wall attached to any body (a Studio solid, a slab, an imported element, a foreign roof) rises to its underside, read by vertical rays along its axis.

Then (#157) sections in 3D read as solids: each cut layer closed by its caps in its own material (`SectionCaps` in the core, bubble-core's `BubbleBim.Geometry` and Lapin.Geometry's copy; `view.caps`), the section's drawing (Hatches) on request; View → Section box (BX) with face handles, Fit and Cut at plan; the same caps in BubbleBIM Pro.

Then (#163, `docs/plan-urban.md`) real coordinates and the site's imports in the free Site tab — `GeoRef` (Stereo 70, UTM, Web Mercator, WGS 84, tested against PROJ), the project's system in Manage → Location and `IfcProjectedCRS`, survey points from DXF, GeoJSON parcels, CityJSON neighbours, an orthophoto with its world file — and the premium `urban` module in the private sibling repo `../lapin-urban` (`Ciuntuc-BIM-Studio/lapin-urban`; `products/lapin-urban.json`): planning rules (POT, CUT, heights, setbacks, H/2, road axis), the buildable volume, the indicators, the sun study (3D sun and shadows through the open `ui/src/platform/sunlight.ts`, shadow plans). The guide runner takes another edition and its scenes: `scripts/user-docs/run.mjs --product lapin-urban --scenes ../lapin-urban/docs/scenes.mjs`.

Then (#166, `docs/plan-instancing.md`, 29.10.2026) large models: references that share an IFC body (a type's map, one product definition shape) are instances of one `LapinSharedBody` (`bodyOf`, `xf`), sent to the interface once (`Diff.Meshes`) and drawn by `InstancedMesh` (`ui/src/viewport/instancing.ts`); a big diff goes in parts (`Session.Parts`, 20,000 a frame); IFC export writes them as mapped items. 1,000,000 cubes import in 61 s and turn at 60 fps; `LAPIN_IFC_BENCH=<file>` runs the optional benchmark (`IfcBenchmarkTests`).

Then (#169) the browser's **Site** (`docs/site-plans.md`): site plans (1:500) and location plans (1:2000) as plan views with `site`, the whole model from above on every storey, a north arrow, the map's coordinate grid and a scale bar (`SitePlans`); the parcel's corners numbered and its coordinate inventory (`ParcelCoordinates`); a new project starts with one of each. Then (#172) the map under them: the orthophoto drawn by the engine (on sheets and in the PDF: `PenImage`, `Raster`) and a DXF placed by the map's coordinates (`importDxf {map: true}`); OpenStreetMap later as a separate plugin. Then (#175) 3D edges only where the surface folds by more than `Tessellator.CreaseDeg` (20°) or ends, and corners (L) of walls, beams and strip footings mitred by default, T joins butt. Then (#176, `docs/sections.md`) developed sections: jogged (a polyline), curved (an arc) and along an element (a road, a wall, a drafted line, with stations), each piece a planar cut seen between its ends (`ViewSpec.Span`), laid side by side (`SectionPaths`, `Views.Pieces`, `frames` in the drawing). Then (#179, `docs/fragments.md`) That Open Fragments both ways, no dependency: `Lapin.Mesh/Fragments` writes `.frag` from the mesh scene (File → Export 3D…) and reads one into references with stored bodies, identical bodies shared (File → Import Fragments…); checked against the official library with `scripts/fragments-check.mjs`. Then (#195) lasso selection: Space during a selection drag turns the box into a lasso (its mode fixed then), the same test in every 2D view (`selectBox.ts`, `selectDrag.tsx`); WP and CP on the command line's select step. Then (#196–202, `docs/plan-studio-editor.md`) Studio as a geometry editor: sketches on elevations and on reference planes, push/pull, extrusions symmetric, tapered, up to a face or made to cut, profiles from lines and arcs, primitives, split, arrays, and a solid's steps (`Features`: chamfer, fillet, shell, slice, 3D move/rotate/mirror; found again by point and normal, redone when the sketch changes), sweeps that twist, scale and rise into a helix, smooth and guided lofts, polyline sides typed as lengths, any element made a Studio solid, and File → Import mesh (OBJ, STL, glTF: `Lapin.Mesh/MeshImport`). Then (#203, `docs/plan-display-precision.md`) precision against display speed, from the engine: one rule for every curve (`Lapin.Geometry/Chords`: a chord at most 1 mm off its arc, steps of 0.5–15°), coarser copies of heavy parts for the screen (`MeshSimplify`, quadric edge collapse; `RenderPart.Lods`), drawn by distance (`ui/src/viewport/lod.ts`, instances too) within the project's View → Display quality (fast 3 px, balanced 1 px, precise); the model, drawings, exports, quantities and picking use the parts themselves. Then (#204, `docs/groups.md`) groups (IfcGroup, `Lapin.Platform/Groups.cs`; an imported IFC, Fragments or mesh file comes in as one), opened by a double-click (the platform's edit scope), selecting a region or a lasso in 3D (View → Select region, `ui/src/viewport/select3d.ts`), and hiding for now (HH, HI, HR). Then (#205, `docs/fragments.md`, Exchange) IFC and Fragments as the main exchange: one File menu section, the same survey panel and filters (kinds by discipline, storeys) for importing and exporting both (`FragSurvey`, `ModelSurvey`, `SurveyChoice`), and a Fragments import of 20,000 items from 128 s to 5 s (`RelateAll`, `RelatedSets`).

Then (#171, `docs/plan-assistant.md`, 29.10.2026) the model assistant, desktop only: a local LLM through Ollama (127.0.0.1) in a tab beside Properties, the loop in the interface (`ui/src/assistant`), eight curated tools (lists, one element, aggregates computed by Lapin, standard Psets, schedules, `propose_properties`), proposals applied by the user as one undo step; parameters take a `pset` (`StandardPsets`: a `Pset_…` only with its own names; the user's never `Pset_`); Windows reaches Ollama through the shell (`ollama_request`), its webview's origin being refused; the web build has none of it.

Then (#214, `docs/spec-ifc.md`, Totul editabil) an import's third way, everything editable: IFC (How → Everything editable), Fragments and meshes, what recognition leaves a reference made a Studio solid of its triangles, the same element (`EditableReferences`, `IReferenceEditor`); and recognition from triangles (`Lapin.Ifc/MeshRecognition`: a Fragments file's walls with their doors and windows, slabs and columns, an IFC's leftovers, Modify → Convert to native). Then (#230) stage B: finishes exported as walls of their own made a layer of the wall they cover (`FinishLayers`; 95 of Bloc-P-4's 140), B-rep columns cut at the top by a slope read by their bottom face (`MeshRecognition.StandingOutline`), and Manage → Map imported types (`TypeMappings`, `ITypeAssigner` from each module: an imported type replaced by the project's, the choice remembered for later imports). Then (#255) layered walls meeting in a T or crossing in an X read as one build-up: the ending wall's core runs through the other's finishes to its core and its finishes stop at the other's face (`Walls.LayerCorner`, `LayerCrossings`); a crossing is a join of its own (`JoinKind.X`, both ATPATH, found by `Joins.Auto`), the wall already there through, neither split. Then (#264) a wall layer changes material up its height (`MaterialLayer.Parts`: tiles to 2.10 m, the layer's own above), and (#265) room finishes (`RoomFinishes.cs`, Finishes → Finish room): a `LapinRoomFinishType` laid in rooms as `LapinRoomFinish` floor, walls and ceiling following the room's outline (openings, floor openings and thresholds), a skirting and an optional structural slab made with it (`finishOf`), each measured gross and net by material, the Room finishes schedule.

The railway (#213, 31.10.2026) is the private module `rail` in the sibling repo `../lapin-rail` (`Ciuntuc-BIM-Studio/lapin-rail`, `products/lapin-rail.json` with `"external"`, its plan `lapin-rail/docs/plan-rail.md`): tracks, turnouts, crossings, platforms, the norms and the realignment, over the open `civil` (roads on IFC 4.3 alignments, `engine/src/Geometry/Lapin.Alignments`) through `ICorridorKind`, `LineworkElements.Makers`, `LandXml.AlignmentSources` and the platform's `IIfcAlignments`; the BubbleBIM editions (Civile, MEP) are kept in the private repos' `products/`, never here. A product says its storeys, disciplines, tab order and brand (`scripts/product.mjs`).

MEP (`docs/plan-mep.md`) is two modules since 28.10.2026 (#160): `mep`, open, in this repo and in the `lapin` edition — the network core (systems, nodes with ports, runs, connections, the layout workflow, IFC network, lists); and `mep-pro`, premium, in the private sibling repo `../lapin-mep` (`requires: mep`, commands `mep-pro.…`), built through `products/lapin-mep.json` (`"external"`) — the engineering (sizing, checks, assistants, laying rules by material, resolve), hooked into the open one through `MepHooks` (engine) and `mepHooks` (UI).

Then (#180, `docs/plan-cde.md`, 30.10.2026) the CDE functions, stage 1, in the platform beside Sheets and Versions (`Lapin.Platform/Cde`): Manage → Project folder (a local, shared or synced folder kept in the project, the ISO 19650 containers `WIP / Shared / Published / Archived`, the naming after ISO 19650-2 `Project-Originator-Volume-Level-Type-Role-Number` with the status and revision after an underscore), Manage → Check (`ICdeRule`, blocking or warning, a click goes to the sheet, view or element; `CdeHooks` for a company's rules), Manage → Issue… (`cde.issue` in the dispatcher: the checks, each sheet's PDF, the IFC, DXF on request, SHA-256 per file, the transmittal as PDF and CSV, the manifest, the issue recorded as `LapinIssue` with each sheet's drawing hash, then a version named after the issue); the title block shows the revision, status and who checked, with a revision table from the issues. Stages 2–3 (BCF topics, PDF annotations read back) are open; the direct SharePoint link and a company's rule sets are premium, later. Stage 2 (#182, 30.10.2026): remarks on the model as BCF, `LapinRemark` entities whose id is the topic GUID as a GlobalId (`Lapin.Platform/Cde/Remarks`: reader for 2.1 and 3.0, writer for 2.1 by default and 3.0 on request, no dependency; `project.importBcf` / `project.exportBcf`), Manage → Remarks… (`ui/src/modules/core/remarks/`: list with filters, comments, a remark from the 3D view with its snapshot, Show in 3D with the camera, selection and section box; `bcfCamera.ts` converts the camera both ways). Stage 2 (BCF topics) is open; the direct SharePoint link and a company's rule sets are premium, later. Stage 3 (30.10.2026, #183): the reviewers' PDF annotations read back as remarks on the sheet — `Lapin.Platform/Cde/PdfRemarks`, a PDF reader of Lapin's own (xref tables and streams, object streams, incremental updates, Flate; the markup annotations with their /IRT threads, mapped onto the paper through /Rotate), `LapinSheetRemark` on the sheet with its source so a re-read updates, `cde.readPdf` and `cde.scanRemarks` in the host, Manage → Remarks and the overlay in the sheet view (screen only, never printed).

Then (#189, `docs/plan-studio-thick.md`, 31.10.2026) Studio's thick solids and work planes: Ring, Box (an optional bottom and top as slabs, grouped in an IfcElementAssembly) and Wall strip give a drafted shape a thickness and a height (`Thick`, `Boxes`); on a plan they lay native walls of the current type by default (`architecture.wallsAlong`), elsewhere Studio solids classed IfcWall; they stand on any view's plane (`Views.PlaneOf`: a developed section's piece, an elevation's own plane once fixed, `planeDepth`) or on a reference plane (a view of kind `plane`, `RefPlanes`: three points, a face, a view's plane offset), and follow it when it moves. Then (#192) the planes are seen and picked in 3D (translucent panes, a handle along the normal, View → Reference planes) and in plans and sections (dashed lines, `Views.RefPlaneMarks`, never printed), and every export carries Studio solids in world space with their class, type and assembly (`StudioExportTests`; re-imported as references).

Then (#209–211, `docs/plan-rebar.md`, 31.10.2026) Rebar 2D: the free `rebar` module after the user's Configurator Armare — bars on any view as parametric shapes bent at the Eurocode radius (`Lapin.Rebar/RebarShapes`), hooks, marks, distributions with zones, the bar bending schedule (`IScheduleSource` kind `rebar`); sections across an element at a station (`AcrossSections`: `view.create {across, station}`, the window through `ViewSpec.Span`/`Rise`) and views inset in another (`Insets`, `view.inset`), joined by Rebar 2D → Section here; stirrups configured on the beam and column types and drawn in 2D as the view lies to the axis (#215, `TypeStirrups`), bars drawn by hand standing in space, laid across their host by the cover and seen as dots in cross-sections (#216, `Rebar3D`), a column's stirrup patterns (diamond, two inside) and its longitudinal bars with starter bars on the type (#218, `ColumnBars`); the premium `rebar-pro` (3D bars, optimisers, the section configurator) is planned (#212), in a sibling repo.

Then (#229, `docs/plan-roof-framing.md`, 01.11.2026; Rebar paused) the roof's timber framing: rafters on purlins, couples with collar ties or prefabricated trusses (Fink, mono) along the main ridge (`RoofParts.Framing`, `Stations`), Nordic sections in mm and the timber grade, rafters cut to their profile (bird's mouth on the plate, plumb or square tails), every member a part of its own (`Lapin_Timber`), trusses as IFC element assemblies of typed IfcMembers, the **Roof timber** cutting list (`RoofTimberSchedule`), the Swedish truss and Nordic collar-tie styles. Then (#231) the roof configurator (`ui/src/modules/architecture/RoofDialog.tsx`): the styles, then shape and edges, covering and drainage, framing and openings, over the engine's preview of the draft (`architecture.roofPreview`: the plan, a section through the framing, a summary), applied as one step. Then (#233) the project's own roof styles (`ProjectRoofStyles`, `roofStyles` on the project): a configured roof saved under a name, offered beside the catalogue's, saved again (its roofs follow), renamed, deleted. Then (#236) sheets: Print (the composed paper as vector SVG at its paper size, Tauri's webview print on macOS), the frame and title block never picked, pinned viewports, crop handles and Shift-drag to slide on the sheet (`sheet.cropViewport`, `sheet.panViewport`), and named view boundaries (`LapinViewBoundary`, Annotate → View boundary) a viewport is bound to, one view on several sheets by its boundaries. Then (#235) gutters and downpipes made parametric (`Roofs/Drainage.cs`): the gutter's size, offset, drop, fall and caps, on or off per eave edge; downpipes as `LapinDownpipe` elements on a gutter's station with a route of segments (`architecture.makeDownpipes`, `addDownpipe`, `updateDownpipe`), IfcPipeSegment GUTTER and RIGIDSEGMENT "Downpipe". Then (#237, #238) the testers' requests: no gutter on a shed's high edge, walls drawn on a face of the core or a finish face (`WallReference`, the tool's option in the command line's bar: `ToolDef.options`), and railings of their own (`LapinRailing`, `Stairs/Railings.cs`, shared with the stairs'). Then (#239, `docs/plan-wood.md`, 02.11.2026) the independent `wood` module, stage 1: timber-frame walls from the configurator (`FrameSpec` on the wall type, `Framing.Layout`: plates, studs on a module, door and window openings framed by width-band rules with an opening's own rule, shared headers, noggins, insulation; an `IGeometryFilter` puts the members in place of the core), the Timber schedule (walls and roofs), IFC; stages 2–5 free, stage 6 (EC5, connectors, BTLx) premium. Then (#240) corners and T junctions by rule, boards laid as sheets (the Boards schedule) and the frame elevation (`wood.frameView`), (#241) CLT walls and floors in panels (`Clt.cs`, the CLT panels schedule); then, free after all, (#244) joisted floors (`Joists.cs`: rim, trimmers and headers round floor openings, blocking, IfcBeam JOIST), (#245) prefabricated wall panels (`Framing.Panelize`: joints clear of openings, on the module, each panel framed alone, marks `<wall>-P<n>`, the Wall panels schedule, an IfcElementAssembly per panel) and (#246) CLT details: half-lap and spline joints modelled, opening corners as the router leaves them, the CLT panel elevation, CLT roofs (`woodClt` on the roof).

Then (#268, `docs/plan-steel.md`, stage 1 of #266) the open `steel` module: `LapinSteelMember` (written as IfcMember; Studio owns IfcMember) with configurable ends (`SteelEnd`: extension, a cut by a plane, a mitre or a fit on another member by id, copes), the same ends on straight steel beams and columns through a geometry filter, IFC as extrusions clipped by half-spaces with copes as openings (`IIfcSweptBodies`), Convert to member from a drafted outline (`SketchBands`), RHS, CHS and generic cold-formed C, Z, Σ in `Section`. Then (#270, stage 2) the portal frame hall configurator (Steel → Portal frame hall…): a `LapinSteelHall` keeps its spec and aggregates its members by role (`hallRole`), rebuilt over the same ids, with its own IfcGrid; frames of structure columns and steel rafters fitted and mitred, haunches (`Haunch`: cut from the section or welded from plates, per hall and per haunch); gable posts or a regular frame; the engine's preview (`steel.hallPreview`); hall styles; IFC as an IfcBuildingSystem (`IIfcSystems`), a rafter with haunches an IfcElementAssembly (`IfcSweptBody.Parts`). Then (#273, stage 3) the connections (`LapinSteelNode`, `SteelNodes`): column bases (pinned or fixed, anchors straight, hooked or with an anchor plate, into a structure footing under the column), eaves end plates with column stiffeners, apexes and splices, gable posts' fin plates; plates, bolts and anchors derived from `NodeParams` (defaults by profile), the members set back through `SteelEnd.Extension`; found from the topology (Steel → Find connections) or made by the hall (its Nodes tab, `hallNode`); IFC as an IfcElementAssembly of IfcPlate and IfcMechanicalFastener realizing IfcRelConnectsWithRealizingElements (`IIfcConnections`); the Steel parts schedule; deleting a hall takes its members, connections and grid. Then (#275, stage 4) the hall's secondary steel (`HallSecondary`, tab Secondary steel): purlins (a bay or continuous), side and gable rails, an eaves beam, X bracing in the roof and the long walls, fly braces, all hall members by role; their cleats, gussets and fly brace bolts as connections (`cleat`, `gusset`, `fly`); IfcMember PURLIN, BRACE, USERDEFINED. Then (#276, stage 5) the Steel members list (`steelMembers`: marks by kind, profile, length and end cuts, weights, paint area, totals by hall), a hall's anchor plan (`steel.anchorPlan`: bases, anchors dimensioned to the grid, base types B1… and their table) and frame elevation (`steel.frameView`), views placed on sheets like any other.

Then (#300–301, `docs/plan-4d5d.md`, 04.11.2026) 4D and 5D planned and stage 1 done: the open `planning` module (`engine/src/Modules/Construction/Lapin.Planning`, `ui/src/modules/planning`, tab Planning): IfcWorkSchedule, IfcTask in a WBS with milestones, IfcRelSequence FS/SS/FF/SF with lags, IfcWorkCalendar with Romania's legal holidays (Orthodox Easter computed) and a winter break for wet tasks; the critical path worked out by the engine on every change (`Cpm`, never stored); the Gantt as a pane view (`view: 'gantt'`, a module's `views`) under the 3D, edited by dragging bars, ends and links, the keyboard and Properties; IFC processes both ways (`IIfcProcesses`), MS Project XML both ways (`MsProject`), XLSX. Then (#302, stage 2) the model's elements in the tasks: each task's rule (classes, types, storeys, materials, groups, phases) and exceptions (`add`, `remove`), the elements derived on every ask (`TaskElements`) so new ones join by themselves; parts of layered elements (`part`: core, finishes, a material, a category) in tasks of their own; the selection dragged onto a row (the module contract's `overlays`), `TA`, the selection one between the Gantt and the views (`expandSelection`); the CDE check `planningTasks`; IFC IfcRelAssignsToProduct / IfcRelAssignsToProcess with the rule in `Lapin_Task`, MS Project's Text1, XLSX. Then (#303, stage 3) the generator, Planning → Generate schedule… (`Generator`, `GenerateDialog.tsx`): templates (concrete frame, load-bearing masonry, steel hall, timber frame; chosen by the model) and the project's styles (`scheduleStyles`), a task a step × storey made with a rule so its elements stay linked, linked storey after storey (`cycle` steps: walls wait for the floor below), durations = quantity (takeoff, by the task's part) × the user's norm ÷ (crew × the calendar's hours) — norms imported only (CSV/XLSX, `LapinNormTable`, `XlsxReader`), plus Lapin's few rows marked indicative; the engine's preview on a draft model (`ScheduleDraft`) with the counter of linked elements; regeneration over the same ids (`gen`) keeping tasks edited by hand (`own`), hand-made tasks and links and exceptions; demolitions first (rule `demolished`); formwork and scaffolds living by their hosts' tasks (`ITaskLife` in the platform, `stripAfter` on the formwork type, `TaskLives`). Then (#304, stage 4) the 4D simulation: Planning → Simulate shows the model on a day of the schedule (`planning.timeline` once a change, `Timeline.cs`; the states worked out in the interface, `simulation.ts`, as shared looks so the 3D restyles only what changed, meshes and instances kept): not started hidden or ghosts, under way and demolishing coloured, demolished gone, formwork and scaffolds only while they stand, a layered wall's layers apart (`RenderPart.Key`, a look's `parts`); the player at the Gantt's top (▶, speed, a day or a week a step, the date line dragged on the time scale), a card with the legend over the model, the colours on the schedule (`planning.setSimulation`); State at date on plans, sections, elevations and 3D views (`stateDate`, `planning.viewDate`; `StateAtDate` is an `IViewElementFilter` and the platform's new `IViewPartFilter`, so sheets and the PDF print it; the phases step aside); the Gantt on sheets (`GanttSheet`, an `ISheetContent` of the IfcWorkSchedule, paged) and the stages sheet (`LapinStages`: a drawing view drawn by the engine at each date, or the 3D's pictures); Export video… (MediaRecorder: WebM, MP4 where only that; PNG frames in a ZIP otherwise). Then (#305, stage 5) progress: the status date (the work left starts the working day after it; `Cpm.Solve` puts finished tasks on their actual dates off the critical path, started ones do what is left after it), each task's % complete, actual start and finish typed (`planning.setProgress`) or from its elements marked on site (Planning → Mark done / Mark started, MD / MS: `marks` on the task, the quantity done in the task's unit or a count; `TaskProgress`, `PlanningModule.ReadResolved`), summaries weighted by duration; the marking commands open to every guest of a shared session, one who joined to look too (`EngineDispatcher.SiteCommands`); one baseline (Save baseline: `baseline` on each task, a thin bar under the bars, the % and Variance columns, late rows), late and ahead in the simulation and on views at a date; `IVersionCompare` (`ScheduleCompare`) in Manage → Versions → Compare; IFC (IfcTaskTime actual fields, a BASELINE IfcWorkSchedule), MS Project (StatusDate, PercentComplete, Actual*, Baseline 0) and the workbook read back (Import progress…). Then (#306, stage 6) the estimate in the open `cost` module (`engine/src/Modules/Construction/Lapin.Cost`, `requires: core, architecture`; the Quantities tab renamed Cost): the user's price lists only (CSV/XLSX, columns guessed from Romanian or English headings and matched in a dialog, articles split into material, labour, equipment, transport, maybe man-hours, trade and resources; `LapinPriceList` in the project, the app's library in `localStorage`; Lapin's template marked indicative), articles on types (`articles`: code, measure, part core/finishes/layer/material, factor, waste, chapter; the site works types' `spec.article` of #152 read as one) and article rules by material or category (`LapinArticleRule`; the type's article first), Cost → Map articles… (suggested by code or words, the core proposed, remembered on the computer), the estimate (`IfcCostSchedule`, `Costing.Compute`, derived on every ask: chapters by kind of work or storey, lines of the user's kept, CAM, overheads, profit, VAT, RON or EUR at the project's rate with the other's equivalent) as a pane view (`view: 'estimate'`), the forms F3, F2, C6–C9 (`CostForms`, Romanian headings, assumptions in plan-4d5d.md, Răspunsuri 2) as schedules (`estimate-*`), one workbook and a PDF; IFC IfcCostSchedule/IfcCostItem/IfcCostValue/IfcRelAssignsToControl and IfcMonetaryUnit both ways (`IIfcControls`). Then (#307, stage 7) cost over time, with no linking of its own: the platform's `IWorkSchedules` (planning) and `IScheduleCosts` (cost), each line shared by its elements (`EstimateLine.Shares`) and each element's part by the tasks building it, spread over their working days (`CostOverTime`); the Gantt's Cost column, budget and cost to date; Cost → Cost over time (`view: 'costTime'`: the S-curve planned, baseline and done to the status date, the months' bars, the month table), the curve on sheets (`CostCurveSheet`), the form `monthly`, XLSX and PDF; versions compared by cost (`CostCompare`, `IVersionCompare.Measures`); cost items assigned to their tasks in IFC; existing elements not priced, demolished ones only by a `demolition` article. Monthly billing, earned value and several baselines are premium stage 8.

Then (#308, 05.11.2026) stage 8 of `docs/plan-4d5d.md`: the premium `planning-pro` module in the private sibling repo `../lapin-planning` (`Ciuntuc-BIM-Studio/lapin-planning`, `products/lapin-planning.json` with `"external"`, `requires: planning, cost`, commands `planning-pro.…`): resources (crews, equipment, formwork sets held after their task) and serial levelling with the histogram, Primavera P6 XER and XML both ways, earned value, the monthly work statements from progress, named baselines and scenarios compared, the site diary (photos, daily reports, a guest may add them). Hooks in the open repo: a task's `levelStart` kept by `Cpm.Solve` (`levelled: false` without it), `PlanningModule.Solve` public, `addModuleViews` for any module's view kind, the two guest site commands, and the guide runner's `--docs` (an edition's own pages): `run.mjs --product lapin-planning --scenes ../lapin-planning/docs/scenes.mjs --docs ../lapin-planning/docs/user`.

Then (#311, `docs/plan-documents.md`, 05.11.2026) written documents, stages 1 and 3: `LapinDocument` (`Lapin.Platform/Documents`) made from a template of blocks (headings, paragraphs, lists, key–value fields, tables from any schedule, view pictures, repeats over storeys, rooms, wall types, sheets…, conditions, the designer's inputs, contents) and filled from the project through `IDocumentFields` (platform and architecture: project information, areas built/gross/usable/basement, the height regime, rooms per storey, build-ups) and `IDocumentTables`; written by Lapin as DOCX, ODT and PDF (`Lapin.Drawing/Documents`, no library; `scripts/documents-check.mjs` opens them with LibreOffice); Manage → Documents… and Project information… (`project.setInfo`, read by title blocks too), documents issued with Manage → Issue…; the Romanian templates memoriu tehnic de arhitectură, caiet de sarcini (tâmplărie și finisaje) and borderou (`scripts/documents-templates-check.mjs`). Then (#321) stages 2, 4 and 5, free: the visual editor (TipTap; Edit in the Documents dialog, field chips with values, Insert, block settings; `document.source`, `document.block`), templates saved in the project or this computer's library (Manage → Document templates…, export/import), the company style (`page.style`: fonts, Times or Helvetica in the PDF, heading colour, logo), the MCP `lapin_document_export`, pictures in the issued DOCX and pictures cropped to a view boundary.

Then (#318, `docs/ifc-optimize.md`, 05.11.2026) File → Optimize IFC…: a file-to-file pass over an IFC's own STEP (`IfcOptimize`) writes a lighter copy beside it with an ending (`.optimized`), everything but geometry byte for byte (header, every IfcRoot and GlobalId, properties, quantities; no `Lapin_*`), meshes cleaned and simplified within a measured tolerance (`MeshSimplify.Reduce`), identical bodies shared as mapped items, orphans removed; the original never written.

Then (#326, `docs/plan-buildup-tags.md`, 05.11.2026, a tester's idea) materials carry their data (Manage → Materials → Data: description, parameters with IFC's material psets, how they are measured, catalogue thicknesses; a layer's note), the quantity takeoff by material splits area materials by thickness and flags off-catalogue layers (Manage → Check too), and the build-up tag (`buildup` tag type: header, a line per layer from a `{field}` template with optional `[ … ]` parts, footer, order, zero layers, one leader or one per layer; `BuildUps.cs`, `BuildupTags.cs`).

Then (#330, `docs/plan-furniture.md`, 05.11.2026) the open `furniture` module (tab Furniture): `IfcFurniture` of an `IfcFurnitureType` whose `spec` holds the kind, size, the kind's parameters and materials by role — sofas (seats, arms, a chaise longue), corner sofas, armchairs, chairs, beds, bedside tables, wardrobes (hinged or sliding), chests, bookcases, sideboards, tables (rectangular, round, oval), coffee tables, desks and straight kitchens (units, worktop, sink, hob, wall units, tall unit) — bodies and plan symbols built by Lapin (`FurnitureBodies`, symbolic in plan); a click backs a piece onto the nearest wall's face (`furniture.place`), chairs round a table, a kitchen along a wall, Flip/mirror to the other hand; Furniture types with the engine's preview; the Furniture and Furniture by room schedules; IFC both ways.

Then (#331, `docs/plan-buildup-tags.md`, 06.11.2026, a tester's idea) tag leaders as ArchiCAD's labels: `leaderShape` elbow (default) or straight on the tag type and the tag, an oblique to the knee (the tag's `at`) then a shoulder under the text (`underline` first, each, last, none; `extension`), no box; the knee grip snapped to 30/45/60/90° (Shift free), the text flipped to the knee's side, per-layer build-up leaders with their knees in a column (`TagLeaders.cs`, `drafting.tagPreview`); older projects' tags kept straight by `TagLeaderUpgrade` (`leaderRev`).

Then (#342, a tester's report #96) balconies: `LapinBalcony` on a wall's face (Architecture → Balcony, BY), by outline or along a gallery's edge, in ten built-in styles (Juliet, cantilevered, stacked on posts, brackets, tie rods, rounded, curved, loggia, corner, mezzanine) or the project's own (`LapinBalconyType`), the configurator with the engine's preview (`architecture.balconyPreview`), a loggia cutting its recess (`balconyId`) and standing its back wall, Stack on storeys above through Copy to storeys, the Balconies schedule, IFC as an IfcElementAssembly "Balcony" of its parts (`engine/src/Modules/Architecture/Lapin.Architecture/Balconies`).

Then (#343, testers' reports #54 and #97, `docs/plan-elevators.md`) the open `transport` module: elevators as `IfcTransportElement` ELEVATOR of an `IfcTransportElementType`, from a configurator of twelve kinds (EN 81-20 load/area/persons, EN 81-70 accessible cars) and project styles (`elevatorStyles`), over the storeys they serve (`topStoreyId`, `unserved`) with pit, headroom, machine room, car and landing doors; the shaft opens the floors it passes (`elevatorId` openings, kept in step); plans of every storey it reaches (`IStoreySpans`, `GeometryPart.CutInPlan`); the Elevator schedule; IFC with `Pset_TransportElementCommon` (`IIfcElementSets`).

Then (#344, `docs/overrides.md`, 06.11.2026) override points for editions, the first step towards the separate Lapin Lite-STEP edition (a playground for AI-inclined developers, after the Lite-STEP spec Rev 2): engine commands and queries wrapped by `r.Override` / `r.OverrideQuery` (`next`, one undo step, the original's name and rules), results a module computes in the background delivered later (`IDeliveries`: stamp, deliver, stale when changed), the UI's `replaces` for tools, dialogs, views and File actions (`fileActions.ts`), Help → About listing them; the `lapin` edition replaces nothing (tests).

Then (#345) other tools' parts kept in `.lapin` (`PackageForeign.cs`: entries outside Lapin's names written back byte for byte, listed in the manifest's optional `foreign`, never in versions) and the multi-cycle IFC round-trip (`IfcCycleTests`, `LAPIN_IFC_CYCLES`): zero drift over 1,000 cycles; room numbers kept as labels and material relations keeping their GlobalIds; the spatial structure's GlobalIds are still the receiving project's (a skipped test).

Then (testers' reports #60 and #56, decisions 347 and 348, 06.11.2026) bay, bow and box windows: a window type that projects (`JoinerySpec.Bay`, `BayWindows.cs`: the grid's columns its faces, each a flat window of the type, corner posts, a seat, a sill, a base, a hipped, lean-to or flat roof with its flashing; still one IfcWindow in one opening, scheduled with its faces unfolded; Lapin's bay styles and the project's own, `ProjectStyles`), Architecture → Bay window; and sunshades (`Sunshades.cs`: IfcShadingDevice of an IfcShadingDeviceType, canopies, fins, louvres, egg-crates, perforated screens, awnings, pergolas and external blinds, on a door or window, along a wall or on a facade line; the noon helper for 21 June from `Sun.At`; styles, schedule, quantities, IFC).

Then (#349, #350, testers' reports #55, #57) exhaust fans in the open `mep` module (`Lapin.Mep/Fans`: `LapinExhaustFan` as IfcFan of an IfcFanType with Pset_FanTypeCommon, six kinds, built-in types, a wall fan's round provision, `mep.fanDuct` to a wall or roof outlet, the fan schedule and the rooms' extract check) and gates on the Site tab (`Lapin.Architecture/Gates`: `LapinGate` as IfcDoor GATE of an IfcDoorType, seven kinds with piers, built-in styles, on a wall or parcel line, a sliding gate's parking zone, the gate schedule); both built with `Lapin.Platform/LocalBuilder` and configured in `ui/src/shell/typeConfigurator.tsx`.

Then (#351, `docs/spec-ifc.md`, Poarta de fidelitate) the recognition fidelity gate, report mode by default (`RecognitionFidelity.cs`: a recognised element's solid against the file's body, volume, box, base outline at 1 mm / 0.5 %; `enforce` leaves failures references): on Bloc-P-4 every slab passes and 322 of 410 walls fail, mostly through automatic joins overlapping neighbours and walls rising to imported roofs; BURKWIL's recognised columns ignore the file's base. Imported references lose their openings on export (a skipped test).

Then (#352, `docs/user/groups.md`) View → Drill into assemblies, off by default (`drillSelect` in `lapin.display`): a click selects the outermost whole (group, then `IfcRelAggregates` up to the spatial structure), further clicks step in, Esc steps out, a breadcrumb over the view, Ctrl/Cmd+click the element itself (`ui/src/platform/assemblies.ts`, `DrillCrumbs.tsx`, `core.assemblyPath`).

Then (#353) an IFC opened as a project (`project.importIfc {asProject}`, `IfcImport.SeedOf`, `Session.New(SpatialSeed)`) is made with the file's project, site, building and storeys, their GlobalIds and the spatial relations' kept; importing into an existing project merges as before, new storeys taking the file's ids.

Then (#354) imported references keep their uncut body and the file's openings with voids and fills as attributes (`bodyUncut`, `ifcOpenings` in `StoredBodies`), written back by the IFC export under the file's GlobalIds; views and quantities stay on the cut body.

Then (#355–356) the gate's findings fixed: recognised columns keep their base and height, non-rectangular or multi-body beams and sloping roof slabs stay references; recognised walls joined only where the file's footprints meet that way (`FoundJoins`, replacing `Joins.Auto` for imports), openings kept in place, flat tops not raised to imported roofs; Bloc-P-4 322 → 95 of 410 walls failing, wall volume within 0.24 %.

Then (#359–361) the rest of the gate's findings: polygon column sections (`kind: polygon`), mixed sections of one file type typed apart, faceted tops left references; recognised windows keep the file's opening (reveal none) and walls an end cut at an angle (`startAngle`, `endAngle`); walls made of `IfcBuildingElementPart`s read layer by layer (`PartProfiles`, `ForeignWallParts`: `topProfile` for gables, `layerEnds` for stepped ends), the file's `IfcRelConnectsPathElements` tried first. Bloc-P-4: 0 of 511 failing.

Then (#364) the fidelity gate enforces by default (the user's principle: an IFC import reproduces the file, never enriched with Lapin's native features): what differs by more than 1 mm or 0.5 % comes in as the reference it was, in imports, Fragments and Convert to native; Bloc-P-4 0 of 511 reverted.

Then (#365) recognised elements carry only what the file has (no lintels, window boards, thresholds or reveals of Lapin's own on their types; U only from the file) and openings take the `IfcOpeningElement`'s size (`holeWidth`, `holeHeight`, `fillOffset`); BURKWIL 135 → 97 reverted, Bloc-P-4 equal to the file.

Then (#374, `docs/plan-studio-sketch.md`, 07.11.2026) sketching on faces in 3D, two ways over one core (`ui/src/viewport/sketch/`): SketchUp's (Studio → Sketch: Line, Rectangle, Circle on the face under the cursor, inference with the axes' colours and arrow/Shift locks, the typed value, a closed loop pushed or pulled at once) and CAD's (Sketch on face makes the face's plane the active work plane, the command line's commands draw on it, Finish sketch, Look at plane); `studio.onFace` gives the plane alone, keeps open lines, pushes a drafted `sketchId`. Stage A done.

Then (#375) stage B: a line across a face splits it into regions (`SketchRegions`; `studio.faceAt` lights the region, Push/Pull takes it alone), the direct Arc and Offset, TRIM/EXTEND/FILLET on the work plane, grips on sketch lines in 3D (`SketchGrips3D.tsx`), snapping to other bodies' edges in the plane (`sketch/edges.ts`).

Then (#376) stage C, the plan complete: the tape measure and guides (`LapinGuide`, never exported; `Guides3D.tsx`, snapped to), Follow Me (`studio.followMe`, Studio operation `followMe`: a face swept where it stands along a drafted path or a face's edges, mitred), SketchUp's one-letter shortcuts in 3D as a switch, off by default (`SKETCHUP_KEYS`).

Then (#377) the user's answers: a loop drawn inside a face is a hole of the face round it, as SketchUp's (`FaceRegion`), and two arc buttons each doing one thing (2-point arc, the direct tool; 3-point arc, ARC); Follow Me as its own operation and the shortcut switch in Studio → Sketch confirmed.

Then (#378) a 3D tool waiting for a value owns the value's keys (`ui/src/app/valueOwner.ts`, asked first by `keys.ts`): digits no longer start quick tools mid-shape; a face's plane reads as seen from outside (x left→right, level; y up), as the engine's.

Then (#379) Ask Lapin finds its most asked entries in the interface's other languages (keywords per language, their small words ignored, Chinese keywords by containment; `ui/src/help/faq.ts`).

Then (#380, report #108) the Doors and windows configurator's 3D preview: the draft in a piece of wall of the project's first wall type, built in a model of its own by the same code (`architecture.joineryPreview3d`, `JoineryPreview3D.cs`/`.tsx`).

Then (#381, report #107) a wall's Thickness typed on the wall: the type with the same build-up and that thickness, found or made (`architecture.setWallThickness`, `WallThickness.cs`); a straight wall's Length editable in Properties.

Then (#382, tester's report #106) an imported DXF in 3D: its flat drawing (shapes and hatches marked `dxf`) on its view's plane (`Views.PlaneOf`), batched by the engine (`drafting.underlays3d`, `Cad/Underlays3d.cs`: one batch a view plane, import and layer) and drawn as one LineSegments and a faint fill each (`ui/src/viewport/underlays3d.ts`), asked again only when its sources change; View → DXF in 3D, each import's In 3D (`dxfImports` on the project, `dxfImport` on its entities, `drafting.setUnderlay3d`), a link's 3D in Manage → Links; snapped to by the 3D sketch (`sceneSegments`).

Then (#383, testers' reports #104, #105, `docs/plan-3d-modelling.md`) modelling in 3D as in plan, stage A: MOVE and COPY in the 3D view snapped to the bodies' endpoints, midpoints, edges and faces and the red, green and blue axes (`snap3d.ts`, `scene3d.ts`, `Move3D.tsx`), a selected element dragged by its body (View → Drag to move in 3D), `element.transform`'s `up` raising what has a height of its own (`ElementRise`, `IElementRise`).

Then (#384, report #105) stage B: every tool that takes points works in the 3D view through `Place3D.tsx` (`gesture3d.ts`: the plan's clicks), the cursor snapped in 3D and brought onto the active level, the outline drawn there, lengths typed; doors and windows at the face point clicked.

Then (#385) stage C: grips in 3D snapped to the other elements, catalog cards and ribbon tools dropped on the 3D view (`Drop3D.tsx`), a one-click element on the storey of the floor it lands on (`storeyAt`).

Then (#386, a tester's report #113) an imported DXF found again on reopening: nothing was lost on save, but the interface opened the level's empty default plan while the DXF lay on the level's own plan view; now a level's first floor plan view is its plan everywhere (`StoreyPlans`, `ui/src/viewport/storeyPlan.ts`), what was drawn on the default plan moving onto it (`DefaultPlanUpgrade` for older projects).

Then (#387–388, testers' reports #110, #111) previews are drawings on paper in both themes (`.paper-card` in `theme.css`, the theme's tokens put back to paper inside; `run.mjs --theme graphite` for a look), and a configurator's style select remembers the style applied, "(modified)" once a field differs (`ui/src/shell/StyleSelect.tsx`; `style` on `JoinerySpec` and `JoineryBay`).

Then (#389, tester's report #112) an arched casement: `JoineryShape.Transom` false drops the spring-line transom, the top row of panes following the head (`JoineryShapes.PaneOutline`: frame, sash, glass, beads, mullions and glazing bars to the arch, the opening symbol meeting it); the Arched casement style.

Then (#390, tester's report #109) a U stair turned by winders sets each corner: `Winders`, `WindersEnd` and `Between` straight treads along the short side (each corner its own fan, the well as wide as those treads), on the type and on the stair (`winders`, `windersEnd`, `treadsBetween`); the library's U stair with winders.

Then (#391, `docs/plan-visibility-graphics.md`, 07.11.2026) Visibility / Graphics as in Revit, stage A: one resolver (`VisibilityGraphics`) for drawings, sheets, 3D (`view.looks3d`) and mesh exports; new categories (rebar, coverings, formwork, scaffolds, mep, steel connections, transport, shading); subcategories by parts (core, finishes, insulation, membranes, other layers, reveals); links per view (by host view or custom with their own categories and classes, shown and halftone here); a view style's graphics set as Revit's view template.

Then (#392, a tester's report #114) wall openings edited like doors and windows (`BareOpenings.cs`, `OpeningDialog.tsx`): a reveal finish (plaster or a lining) as a part of the wall, the lintel by the wall's rule or none, several at once, a small configurator, and Change to door/window… (`architecture.openingToFill`, a fitted type) and back (`architecture.fillToOpening`), the opening's GlobalId kept.

Then (#393) stage B: annotation categories by a mark's owner (dimensions, texts, tags, room tags, grids, levels, section and elevation marks, callouts, reference planes, view boundaries, legends), imported categories (each DXF import by file and layer, halftone; traced PDFs and images), rule-based view filters (`ViewFilters.cs`: the project's `viewFilters`, a view's ordered `graphics.filters`; Manage → View filters).

Then (#394) stage C: graphic overrides — a filter's colour, halftone, pattern, pen and 3D transparency (the first filter in the view's list giving each), a category's halftone, transparency and line pattern, an annotation category's colour and halftone, the view's detail level; in drawings, sheets and 3D (`view.looks3d`). Point clouds (stage D) are planned with an estimate, not built.

Then (#395, `docs/plan-visibility-graphics.md`, stage D1, 08.11.2026) point clouds as links, open formats only: LAS, LAZ (a C# port of laz-rs, Apache 2.0), E57, PLY, XYZ/PTS/CSV read by Lapin (`engine/src/Platform/Lapin.PointClouds`), indexed in the background into an octree outside the project (the user folder's `PointClouds/`), streamed to the 3D view by a point budget (`ui/src/viewport/pointClouds3d.ts`, `pointcloud.tiles`), placed by the map, origin or centred with a unit check, File → Link point cloud…, Manage → Links.

Then (#396, stage D2) point clouds in views: Visibility / Graphics → Point clouds (shown, halftone, colours, ASPRS classes, slice), plans and sections drawing the slice their cut takes as dots under the drawing (a raster carried by its `PenImage`, printed on sheets and in the PDF, its points snaps: `PointCloudSlices`), 3D snaps to cloud points (`cloudPointsNear`), Export 3D… carrying a sample as GLB/glTF points when ticked.

Then (#397, `docs/bos.md`, 08.11.2026) BIM Open Schema (`.bos`, Ara 3D) both ways, in the Exchange section beside IFC and Fragments: Lapin's own Parquet (`Lapin.Mesh/Bos`: Thrift compact, pages v1/v2, dictionaries, delta, Snappy, GZIP, Brotli; no dependency), File → Import BIM Open Schema… (`project.bosSurvey`, `project.importBos`: references with their bodies as a Fragments file comes in, Revit categories and levels read too) and File → Export IFC, Fragments or BOS… (`project.exportMesh {format: bos}`, written as Ara 3D's IFC converter writes); `LAPIN_BOS_SAMPLE` for a real file.

Then (#398, 08.11.2026) the web deploy split and fast: the app live first, the guide after (`deploy.sh --app-only/--docs-only/--dry-run`), its scenes in parallel workers (`run.mjs --jobs`) and only those whose inputs changed (`incremental.mjs`, a full run weekly or with `--full`).

Then (#399, 08.11.2026) four issues from filming: a new plan framed on its own model (never the previous view's zoom), a picture in a 2D view drawn only where the screen sees it (`ui/src/viewport/imageCrop.ts`), a point cloud's slice read down to its picture's pixel and up to 100 m thick; M, CO, RO, MI typed in 3D with nothing selected pick bodies by click; 3D grips snap as Move does (axes, faces, the snap named, values typed); what stands on its level keeps it in a 3D move unless the blue axis or a dz says otherwise.

Then (#400) a linked or imported IFC's layered walls, slabs and roofs split by their file's layer sets while they stay references (`ReferenceLayers`, `layerAxis`, `StoredBodies.ViewGeometry`, nothing stored): Finishes, Insulation and Core hide as a native wall's ("engineers don't want to see wall and floor finishings"); a reference of one finish (a plaster part, `partOf`) goes with Finishes.

Then (#401) the detail level as in Revit: coarse draws a layered element as one outline hatched as its core, medium its layers, fine the doors' and windows' details and a link's bars (`PlanDetail.OfBuilding`: fine to 1:50, medium to 1:100, coarse from 1:200; `PlanDetail.IsDetailPart`, `MaterialDrawing.Apply(mergeOwn)`).

Then (#402) rebar in 3D in the open edition: the Rebar category is hidden by default in an architectural 3D view and shown in a structural one (`GraphicsSets.HiddenByDefault3d`); a linked structural IFC's IfcReinforcingBar come as shared, instanced references (11,000 bars linked in 1.5 s), drawn in plans and sections only at a fine detail level; the rebar module's bars in space follow the same category (`rebar.bodies {view}`, View → Bars in 3D switches it); `rebar-pro` keeps bars of their own in the model, the optimisers and the configurators ("architects don't want to see rebars").

Then (#403) rebar hidden by default in every 3D view, whatever its discipline (`GraphicsSets.HiddenIn3d`), shown only by the Rebar category or View → Bars in 3D.

Then (#404) a view's level of development (LOD 100 massing, 200 layers, 300 details, 350 connections, 400 fabrication; `PlanDetail.Lods`, `ViewDetail.Of`), never by its scale: the view's own, else its view style's (350, structural 400); older projects upgraded once to keep how their views drew (`ViewDetail.LevelUpgrade`, `detailRev`).

Then (#405) at LOD 100 steel members draw as one line on their axis (`IStickAxes`, `DrawSymbol.Points3`) and doors and windows as their opening and swing; their plan symbols per level (`JoinerySymbols.Plan(level)`: 200 simplified, 300 whole), their parts by `PlanDetail.Shows`.

Then (#406, 08.11.2026) an import's storeys are the file's: at its levels in its unit (`StoreyUnits`, a scale per source document; DigitalHub's E01 at +3.75 no longer +0.00), and they replace the project's for IFC, Fragments and BOS imports, never links (`ImportStoreys.Replace`, one undo step: a project storey at a file level within 5 cm merged into it with its elements kept in place and its views moved, an empty one removed, one holding something kept and reported); recognition from triangles no longer cubic (`PrismsOfFaces`), reading only what a recogniser `Takes`, in parallel: `digitalhub-federated.bos` from never to 12 s in Release (`LAPIN_BOS_BENCH`); the 3D view frames the model after an import (`fitAfterImport`).

Then (#407, `docs/plan-profile-libraries.md`, stage A) profile libraries: `SectionProfile` and its worked-out properties (`Lapin.Platform/ProfileLibraries`, checked against the published tables), the built-in EN set (standard dimensions only), CSV/XLSX tables with columns and units guessed (`ProfileTables`), this computer's libraries in IndexedDB, the project's copies (`LapinSectionProfile`, `section.profileId`), Manage → Profile libraries and the searchable picker in the steel and structure types.

Then (#408, stage B) profile libraries as IFC both ways (`Lapin.Ifc/IfcProfiles.cs`): every `IfcProfileDef` of any file, with `Pset_ProfileMechanical` and IFC2x3's profile properties, units and Y/Z settled by the geometry; a library or the project's profiles exported as an IFC4 `IfcProjectLibrary`; Bonsai's EU, US and AU steel libraries read locally.

Then (#410, `docs/components.md`, 09.11.2026, a tester's wish) components as in SketchUp, for what stands on its own (Studio solids, floors, rooms; never native walls): `LapinComponent`, an instance is a group with `component` and `componentXf`, its members real elements; `Components.Settle` after each command carries what changed inside one instance to the others, in the same undo step, their GlobalIds kept.

Then (#411, stage C) profile libraries finished: a firm's folders of `.lapinprofiles` files (desktop), Update from the library with the types and halls following, closed outlines from Drafting or a DXF as profiles, the picker in halls, sweeps, footings, curtain walls and joinery frames (sizes only), Draw its outline for Studio, the guide's Profile libraries page.

Then (#412, `docs/links.md`, Hotlink-uri) another Lapin project linked read-only as a placed module, File → Link Lapin project… (`Lapin.Protocol/HotLinks.cs`): the source read into a session of its own, placed, written as IFC with Lapin's intent and brought in by `IfcImport.Link`, its GlobalIds derived from the link's `seed`, so one project is placed many times; Manage → Links says when the source changed.

Then (#413, `docs/plan-browser-engine.md`, Lazy parts, 09.11.2026) the web build's first load back under its 15 MB budget (13.7, 14.6 with everything; 17 MB budget for the total): `LAZY` in `scripts/build-web.mjs` lists assemblies left to the interpreter and fetched by the worker when the runtime first needs them (`Lapin.Browser`'s `Resolving`), so everyday code must not mention their types (`EngineDispatcher.Mesh.cs`); dialogs and pane views are chunks through `ui/src/platform/lazyPart.tsx`; `check-web` proves nothing lazy is fetched to open or draw.

Then (#414) mirrored component instances (`componentXf`'s fifth number), and hotlinks placed anew under the same ids (`project.reloadLink {x, y, rotation, data}`, read again from a picked file in the browser), with what the source links itself and its references moved along (`HotLinks.OwnAll`, `Carry`), the source opened in another window and a notice when it changes (`LinkedProjectsWatch`).

Then (#415, `docs/plan-profile-libraries.md`) steel members draw their real section at LOD 400 only: root fillets, toe radii and sloping flanges by `Chords` (`Section.Real`, `ElementGeometry.FineShape`, `GeometryOptions.Fine`, `Session.FineGeometry`), in drawings (`DrawPart.Smooth`: no line per flat) and in a 3D view (`view.fine`, `ui/src/viewport/fineBodies.ts`, asked only then); quantities, exports and every lower level keep the sharp body; a hall of 272 members goes from 11,326 to 18,816 triangles.

Then (#416, `docs/plan-profile-libraries.md`) the IFC export writes beams, columns, steel members and halls by their parametric profile (`IfcSweep.Parametric`, `ProfileIfc.Parametric`, `FrameIfcBodies`, `Section.Sweep`) with `ProfileName` and `Pset_ProfileMechanical`, one definition for all that share it; outlines, Σ and lipped Z stay arbitrary, a cut or mitred element stays a B-rep; L, U, C, Z keep their place (centred on the box as IFC's; flipped, swept the other way), read back with the same body.

Then (#417, `docs/plan-profile-libraries.md`) curtain wall mullions and transoms and door and window frames and sashes take a library profile's real outline, holes included (`MemberOutline`, `CurtainSection.Profile`, `JoinerySpec.FrameSection`, `SashSection`, `JoineryBox.Member`), x across the member towards the glass, y out of the building, butted at corners as before, in 3D, sections and plans; a profile system stays, its stop, lip and drips giving way where the profile is the real shape, beads kept.

Then (#418, a tester's report #125) timber-frame walls no longer build each other again: a frame reads only the walls near it (`NearReads`: cells of a grid in plan, touched from where a wall was and is, the second round of `Session.DiffOf`) and a plan's separation lines are found on a grid (`Pens.EdgeGrid`); adding a wall among 40 from ~150 ms and 3 MB sent to ~8 ms and 43 KB, the plan from ~700 to ~120 ms.

Then (#419, `docs/plan-profile-libraries.md`) quantities follow the profile's real area: `Section.Area` includes root fillets, toe radii and sloping flanges (an IPE 300 is 53.81 cm², `SharpArea` the drawn body's 51.88, `RealRatio` their quotient); Qto, the takeoff, the Steel members list, hall weights, estimates and planning durations read it, `NetVolume` as the sharp body times the ratio; schedule totals of rolled steel rise by 2–4 %.

Then (#420, `docs/plan-units.md`) hotlinks mirrored (`HotLinks.Placing.Mirrored`); apartment types, fixed in size and built on components (`kind: "unit"`), are planned as the free module's next step.

Then (#421, `docs/rooms.md`) a room's doors and windows chosen in its properties by type and side, off by default, beside placing them on walls (`RoomOpenings.cs`): ordinary doors and windows in the walls on those sides, found again by key, each entry keeping a point on its side so it follows copies, turns and mirrors.

Then (#422, `docs/plan-units.md`) apartment types, step A: a component of `kind: "unit"` takes walls with their doors and windows (Modify → Make apartment type), instances copied, turned and mirrored, an edit carrying only the members that changed, under the same ids.

Then (#423, `docs/experiment-editing-speed.md`) who may cut a wall is worked out once per model revision and class (`ModuleCatalog.CandidatesOf`), not once per wall: a type change on 4,440 walls 4.4 s → 2.8 s, opening 3.9 s → 2.6 s.

Then (#424) the recovery journal's `fsync` leaves the session's thread: a line is with the system when written and on the disk within 100 ms or at close (up to ~100 ms of work exposed to a power cut, accepted); 3–5 ms off every edit.

Then (#425) undo and redo take the builds elements had in models of 5,000 entities and up (`Session.Builds.cs`: `Entity.Version`, a 128-bit `ModelStore.ReadStamp` of everything a build read, at most 3 builds per element, 384 MB estimated, oldest first): on 18,000 entities undoing a wall height ×0.13, a type change ×0.58.

Then (#426) memory on very large models: the seam face tables kept only near the last edit, and the geometry of storeys nobody works on let go above 640 MB of builds (browser 160 MB) and built again when asked for (`SessionTuning`); the estimate is measured against the heap.

Then (#427, a tester's report #123) the side panels on either side: View → Panels puts the project browser and the properties left or right, or both on one side stacked with a divider (`panels.ts`: `browserSide`, `propsSide`, `stackRatio`, `LAYOUTS`; `SideColumn`, `StackSep` in `App.tsx`), kept on this computer; the default is unchanged.

Then (#428, a tester's report #120) images to trace and a PDF's pictures were drawn upside down (read as mirrored): the corners reached `PenImage` from the bottom left instead of the top left; fixed in `ViewImages.Marks`, tested in plans, sections and elevations.

Then (#429, a tester's report #122) a SOLID hatch can be selected again: its outline and its area are sent to the interface to click, a pattern hatch too (inside, where nothing else lies).

Then (#430, a tester's report #121) EXPLODE (X, Draft → Edit → Explode): a polyline or a rectangle split into its lines on the same layer, pen and group, in one undo step (`drafting.explode`); circles, arcs, hatches and dimension strings stay.

Then (#431, a tester's question #119) Measure in the 3D view gives the true length in space between points at different heights (`ToolInput.heights`, `measured3d`), and Ask Lapin answers "is there a tape tool" in every language.

Then (#432, testers' report #76) doors and windows carry data as free text (U-value, g-value, safety glass, fire rating, fittings, and their own; on the type with the element's own over it, as the user's parameters, standard names in IFC, the rest in `Lapin_Joinery`), the frame's position in the wall as a value from the exterior finish, the core or the centre (`JoineryPosition`), and a sloped exterior sill cuts the wedge over it out of the wall. Then (#433, report #124) the kitchen's layouts: galley, L-shaped, U-shaped, island and peninsula besides the straight run (`KitchenLayouts`), with their presets, schedule and tucking into corners.

Then (#434, a tester's report #118) a DXF's survey points in the browser answered `Unknown command "civil.dxfPointLayers"`: the interface asked a query as a command (also on the desktop); `scripts/check-commands.mjs` (in `check-web.mjs` and the UI tests) checks that every command and query the interface names exists as the kind it is called as; Lambert-93 (EPSG:2154) in `GeoRef`, tested against PROJ, and numbers of an unknown map system are kept and said so.

Then (#435, a tester's report #115) footings under a plan's range (pad and strip, `Generator.HiddenBelowInPlan`) are drawn dashed in the plan of their storey; a foundation plan cuts and sees them.

Then (#436, a tester's report #116) a strip footing is the concrete alone by default, the wall (its stem) optional: `addStripFooting {stem}`, the tool's option in the command line's bar (`stripOptions.tsx`), `StripFootings.FootingAloneType`.

Then (#437, a tester's report #117) what is drawn in a foundation plan goes on the level under it, which the plan now shows; Properties → Placement gives an element's Offset from level and its absolute Elevation, both editable (`elevation.ts`), and footings an `offset` of their own.
Then (testers' reports #126-#130, decisions 438-442) a dimension held on a grid axis or a wall's centre line stays where it measured when the line is stretched (`DimEndRef.S2`, `Len`); tags turned or aligned to their element, always readable (`Turn`, `rotation`, `align`, a turning grip in 15° steps), the tagged element highlighted (`tagTarget.ts`); a curtain wall's boundary drawn in elevation (`setCurtainWallProfile`, `CurtainWalls.Layout(…, top)`); chosen curtain members with profiles of their own, place and offset (`MemberPlace`, `memberStyles`, Members…) and a section editor (`profiles.fromRings`).
Then (#443, `docs/plan-units.md`, Limita) apartment types, step B: an inner wall's free end stops in the building's wall at each instance, cutting only itself and never carried (`Joins.Stop`, `stopsOn`, `Components.StopInnerWalls`, `FollowsLimit`, `Leaving`), and a room's doors and windows stand in the building's wall at every instance (`IInstanceMembers`, `RoomOpenings.Settle`, `Edited`), of the other hand when mirrored; Copy to storeys and IFC checked (`UnitBoundaryTests`), with `Lapin_Unit` on the group and instances restored on import without "As one group".
Then (#444, a tester's question 7) a hall's bays typed in the project's unit (a bare number under a metre still metres) and its Length field (`hall.ts`), and frameless glass doors and windows (`Frameless.cs`, `JoinerySpec.Frameless`): toughened glass on pivots with patch fittings or on glass hinges, sliding leaves on a track in front of fixed panes in channels, a pull bar, the leaf drawn open as its glass (`JoineryBox.Leaf`), no lining in IFC; the Frameless glass style.
Then (#445, a tester's report: "0,0 origin with a UCS, a static grid, multi-window, dark mode keeps resetting, a preferences dialog, snap types", and the user's answer: Revit's origin points, seen in plans and 3D with coordinates and rotation, a more intuitive way to float views) File → Preferences… (Ctrl+,; `app/prefs.ts`, `PreferencesDialog.tsx`): the theme kept (light, dark, as the system), the 2D grid adaptive or fixed with its step and heavier lines (`gridStepAt`, `gridSnapStep`; the 3D ground grid takes the step), each object snap on or off and polar tracking's angle (`findSnap` with the modes chosen, `resolveAim`'s `polarStep`; a right click on SNAP), and the coordinates read at the cursor in the status line (`CursorCoords.tsx`: internal origin, project base point, survey point's map coordinates as `GeoRef.ToMap`, UCS); a UCS kept in the project (`project.location` `ucs`, `Location.Ucs`; the UCS command: origin and X axis, World, Base point, Survey, Rotate; `app/frame.ts`) in which typed coordinates, ortho, polar, tracking and the grid follow its axes (`resolveAim`'s `frame`, `parseCoordinateInput`'s `frame`), its icon in plans (`UcsIcon.tsx`); the internal origin, the base point's map coordinates and a true-north arrow in plans (`Location.Marks`) and the three points over the 3D view (`Location3D.tsx`), View → Coordinate points; view tabs dragged out of the window into a window of their own where dropped, onto a view's right or bottom quarter to split, onto the other pane, with a tab menu (`app/tabDrag.ts`, `openViewWindow`'s `at`).
Then (#446, the user's question: how a plan of a georeferenced model is framed so nobody has to look for it) Fit frames the model's main body (`zoom.ts` `coreBounds`: each element's, annotation's and slice's box, those whose middle lies within 8× the median distance from the median middle, never under 60 m, kept): the Fit button, the middle button twice, a plan opened and the 3D view's Frame everything; what is left out is counted in a notice; ZE frames everything.
Then (#447, the three proposals of #446, approved by the user) plans of a georeferenced project on the map: a plan's DXF in the plan's own coordinates, from the project base point (mm) or in map coordinates (`Views.Dxf`'s `coords`, `export.dxf`'s; eastings and northings in metres through `GeoRef.ToMap`, texts turned to true north, `$INSUNITS` 6; the plan bar's DXF button); the spot coordinate (`dimKind` `coordinate`, DIMCOORD, Annotate → Dimension: E and N on the map, else X and Y from the base point or the plan's origin, worked out on every drawing); true north up on screen (the plan bar's compass, per plan on this computer, `useTrueNorth`; `toScreen`/`toWorld` turned so drawing, snaps, grid, UCS, pan and the zoom window turn together; sheets and the PDF keep project north up).

Then (#448, the user's request after a community member could not move an OBJ brought in as IFC: imported furniture and decoration should be usable) references move, turn, mirror, copy, rise and array like any element (`ReferencePlacement`, an `IElementPlacement` and `IElementRise` of core's): the body stays the file's; the first transformation hands an element's own body to a `LapinSharedBody` and makes the element an instance placed by its 12 numbers, so copies share the triangles and IFC writes them as mapped items; a mirror bakes a body of its own again, its triangles turned back; the uncut body, the file's openings, its 2D and its layer axis go with it; a copy leaves the file's openings behind. Links stay read-only; a reference is editable whatever module owns its class (`CommandContext.EnsureEditable`). `element.array` copies anything in a row (a step in x, y, z) or round a centre in one undo step, from Modify → Array and from Studio's Array, Polar array and Move 3D, which take non-solids as elements.

Then (#449, the user's request: references, and anything, pinned from their properties so they cannot be moved) any element is pinned (`Pins`, the `pinned` attribute; `element.pin`): element.transform, element.delete, element.moveToStorey, drafting.transform and Studio's move, rotate and mirror steps refuse it until it is unpinned, and element.update refuses only the keys that place it (its ends, point, rotation, outline), so its type, height and material still change; copies and arrays are not pinned; a moved wall leaves a pinned neighbour's ends; a group pins its members and does not move with one pinned. Modify → Pin and Unpin (UP), the Pinned box under Placement, no grips and Delete disabled while pinned. References now show as editable whatever module owns their class, as the engine has treated them since #448.

The portable Windows build looks for updates again (report #132, decision 450): its exe is the app as cargo built it, not marked by the NSIS bundler, so Tauri's updater asked for windows-x86_64-unknown.json, which was never published, and said it could not fetch a valid release JSON. publish-update.mjs now uploads the installer's manifest under that name too, so the portable app downloads the installer and installs Lapin beside it.

Quick views and a steel column (a tester's requests, lot D, decision 458): three buttons before the view tabs (3D, Section, Measure, with their shortcuts in the tooltips, lit while their tool runs), and a Column tool on the Steel tab that reuses structure.column with a column type of an HEB 200 from the built-in EN catalogue (made the first time, then reused). The tabs stay separate. Scene `steel` checks both.
Editing model elements in sections and elevations (a tester's requests, decisions 453 and 454). The bug ("the transform tools in sections and elevations make items disappear or do nothing") was element.transform taking a section's or an elevation's points as plan points: MOVE there sent the view's offset as `by`, and the engine moved the elements by it in plan, so 0.5 m up in an elevation was 0.5 m back in plan, out of the view's depth, and along a view that does not look north it went the wrong way; ROTATE and MIRROR turned them about the view's points read as plan points, far off. Only a wall sweep in its wall used the move in the model (`world`, decision 298). Now the view sends its frame (`frame: {origin, right, up}`, `ui/src/viewport/viewFrame.ts`, a developed section's piece under the point) and element.transform carries what lies on the view (annotations, a bar drawn on it: `ElementTransform.OnView`) by the view's points and the model's elements in the model: along the view in plan, and up or down through `ElementRise` (a wall's or column's base offset, a beam's top offset, a floor's offset, a footing's offset), a door or window sliding in its wall as before. MIRROR about a vertical line mirrors the elements across the plane through it square to the view; any other line, and ROTATE, are refused for elements with what to do instead (drafted shapes still turn and mirror any way). Pinned elements are refused as everywhere (decision 449). The selection is dragged by its body in a section or an elevation as in a plan (the trace, the readout, typed length and angle, Ctrl/Cmd/Alt to copy, Esc to leave it), one undo step, and while it is dragged the Properties panel's Offset from level and elevation follow it (`useLiveRise`, `raisedBy`). Tests: `SectionTransformTests` (8), `viewFrame.test.ts` (3), `elevation.test.ts` (+1); the guide's Drafting page in 9 languages and the scene `section-move`.

Curtain wall cells take panels and doors of their own (report #133, decision 451). A curtain panel type (`IfcPlateType` CURTAIN_PANEL with its `panel` spec, `CurtainPanelSpec`: glass, solid, a framed infill set back in its frame, or open; thickness, offset out of the axis, materials) is saved once and given to chosen cells of any curtain wall (`panelTypes` on the wall, by cell key or `panel` for all), so it stays with its cell when the grid changes, as #128's member styles do. A cell can be replaced by a curtain wall door (`CurtainCells`): an IfcDoor of a door type, part of its wall (IfcRelAggregates) and on its storey, with its `cell`, `fit` (the cell's size, following the grid) or its type's size aligned in the cell with an offset; a transom over it and a mullion beside it where glass is left, glazed with the cell's panel, and in the bottom row on the floor with the sill transom cut away under it (`CurtainCells.Built`). Its body and plan symbol are the joinery's own, in the bay's frame; it flips, changes type (refused where it no longer fits), moves or copies to another cell (an `IHostedPlacement` with a host of its own), goes with its wall when moved, copied or deleted, and keeps its id and settings when its cell goes and comes back. Door schedules list it at the size it stands, the IFC export writes it as an IfcDoor in its IfcCurtainWall's decomposition with its own overall size. `createCurtainDoorType` makes a glazed aluminium door (single or double, swinging or sliding, a frame profile from the libraries); the Panels and doors… dialog draws the engine's layout with the cells to pick. CurtainCellTests, curtainCells.test.ts, the curtain-cells scene and the Walls page in every language.

Profile standards (a tester's request, decision 457): every profile is shown under a standard, `ProfileStandards.Group` reading its standard's name (EN 10365, DIN EN, SR EN → EN; ASTM, AISC → AISC / ASTM; BS, DIN, JIS, GB, AS/NZS, CSA, IS, GOST), a library's own `standard` (Lapin's is EN; one on this computer is set with Shown under) before it, the library's name when neither says one, so a firm's library is a group of its own. The pickers list the project's copies first, then each standard under a heading, with chips at the top that hide or show one for the user (`profileStandardsHidden` in the preferences, File → Preferences → Profiles too). A project keeps which standards its pickers offer (`profileStandards` on IfcProject, profiles.setStandards, one undo step; none: all), ticked under Standards in this project in Manage → Profile libraries, and a new project starts with the user's choice (`newProjectProfileStandards`, sent with project.new, no undo step).
