A project already held only the profiles its types use (`LapinSectionProfile`), never a library, so the choice is a list of names and the file stays as light as it was; a copy now also says the standard it is shown under. The AISC Shapes Database is not shipped: AISC allows developers to include it only under its own licence agreement with flow-down terms, which an MIT program cannot pass on; users import their own copy with Table… as before.

Then (#455 and #456, a tester's requests) the section and elevation markers take a size, an elevation reaches as far as its line, and cuts take section symbols. **Manage → Drafting standards → Project** has **Marker size** (paper mm: an elevation's circle across, a section's arrows 0.7 of it; 10 mm, today's size, unless set) and **Marker text** (their names' height; empty: the Standard text's), `StyleBook.MarkSize`/`MarkText` through `standards.options`; a section or an elevation overrides them with `markSize` and `markTextSize` in its properties (`SectionMarkers.Sizes`), in plans and in the traces in the views a section crosses, so sheets, the PDF and the DXF follow. Every plan now marks each elevation (`ElevationMarkers`): a chain line across its look in front of the building, a circle with its black arrow at its middle and the name behind, hidden by the Elevation marks category; its line's ends are grips (`elevationGrips` in sections.tsx, over the mark's `data`) that set `extentMin`/`extentMax` along the elevation's right through `view.setExtent` (one undo step), and the elevation draws only between them (`ViewSpec.Span`). A material's cut hatch from its name follows the section symbols (ISO 128-50's 45° lines, DIN 1356 / STAS): masonry and brick LINES45 (BRICK's courses stay the elevation's), concrete CONCRETE, reinforced concrete the new RCONCRETE (speckle over 45° lines), insulation BATT, timber WOOD, steel STEEL, earth, gravel; plaster, glass, air and membranes none; any other name LINES45. `MaterialHatchUpgrade` (`hatchRev` on the project) gives a project saved before the old suggestion as each material's own where the new one differs (an unknown name an explicit none), so it keeps the look it had; materials named only, not kept (a track's ballast), cannot be upgraded and take the new table. Tests: `ViewMarkerSizeTests`, `SectionHatchDefaultTests`, `elevationGrips.test.ts`; DraftingResourcesTests' brick and unknown-name cases now expect LINES45. The guide's views.md and materials.md in every language, the views-elevation-marker scene.

Curtain wall doors under a drawn boundary and in elevations (report #133, the user's answers, decision 459). A door now goes into a cell trimmed by the curtain wall's boundary (#127, `panel:i:top`) wherever its rectangle fits under the boundary (`DoorSeat.Trimmed`, the cell's convex outline along the bay's chord; its head checked at its sides): the mullions beside it rise to the boundary, the panes beside them fill up to it, a transom spans its head and the glass over it follows the slope (`CurtainCells.Cut`, plain pieces with outlines). Filling such a cell it is as wide as the cell and stops a transom under the boundary's lower side (none where the boundary is level). A door that does not fit is refused, or after a profile change says, how many millimetres are left under the boundary and what to do. A framed panel in a trimmed cell is framed along its outline (`CurtainWalls.InsetConvex`: each side moved in by the frame's width), its infill inside; a cell too small or too sharp for the frame keeps the infill alone. The elevation symbols (`ElevationSymbols`) draw a curtain wall door's opening marks in its cell's frame as for any wall door; sections draw none, for wall doors either. CurtainCellBoundaryTests (5), the curtain-cells-boundary scene, and the Walls page in 9 languages.

Then (#460, the user's answers to #455 and #456) cut hatches follow Revit's default materials, each marker kind has its own size, and markers move. A material's cut pattern from its name is now what Revit's default materials carry, drawn with Lapin's own patterns: brick, masonry and stone 45° lines 3.2 mm apart (MASONRY, Revit's "Masonry - Brick"), concrete blocks and aerated concrete a diagonal crosshatch (BLOCK, Revit's CMU "Diagonal crosshatch"), concrete cast in place or reinforced the speckle (CONCRETE), rigid insulation a 2.4 mm square grid (RIGID, Revit's "Insulation - Rigid"), wool BATT, gypsum and plaster a stipple (GYPSUM), sand a dense one (SAND), plywood, OSB and CLT WOODCROSS, tiles TILES; glass, air, paint, membranes and unknown names none, as Revit's Default material. `MaterialHatchUpgrade` is at `hatchRev` 2: a project with no mark keeps decision 325's suggestions, one at 1 decision 456's (`MaterialGraphics.LegacyFromName(name, rev)`). Drafting standards → Project has Section marker size and text, Elevation marker size and text (`StyleBook.SectionMarkSize`… read from decision 455's single `markSize`/`markText` when that is what a project kept). An elevation's marker stands a symbol and a half and 25 mm on paper out from the building (clear of section heads) and moves by its circle's grip or Move (`markerShift`, `ElevationMarkers.Moved`), never changing what the elevation sees: unlike Revit, whose marker line is the view's near clip, a Lapin elevation looks at the whole building from outside, its reach set by its extent grips. A section moves, turns and mirrors with its cut as in Revit (`ViewMarkerPlacement`, an `IElementPlacement`; across and along sections follow their element), and each head moves off its line's end by its grip (`view.moveHead`, `headA`/`headB`, a fine leader back), the cut unchanged; pinned views refuse both. Tests: `ViewMarkerMoveTests`, `SectionHatchDefaultTests` (both earlier revisions), `ViewMarkerSizeTests` (the kinds apart, the old single size), `elevationGrips.test.ts`; the guide's views.md and materials.md in every language, the views-elevation-marker scene (the size typed, the extent, the marker dragged out, the head moved).
Then (#461, the user's request: a forum for users and fans, reached from the app) Lapin links to its forum, Discourse at forum.lapinbim.com (category `lapin`), hosted on the Hetzner server apart from the app (deploy/forum/README.md, docs/plan-forum.md); Skool stays for courses. Help → Forum and Ask on the forum… (a new topic with the app's version and desktop or web below the text), Ask on the forum in Ask Me when it has no answer, Forum on the start screen. The engine opens only the forum's address (`Support.LinkUrl`: `forum`, `forumTopic` with the title and text escaped into Discourse's /new-topic query), the page makes the same address in the browser (`forumTopicUrl`). The forum is in English, posts in any language, with no translation by the forum; its accounts are its own, Lapin has none.
The dimension tool's behaviour (decision 464, a tester's report). Aligned and linear dimensions are single by default and the command asks for the next one after each (Esc leaves); a Continuous box in the command bar (C or S on the command line; remembered) makes them a string, previewed with each segment's value in the project's unit. A string is finished by Enter, a right-click (now the Enter of any command step that has one: `finishStep`), the bar's Finish string button, or a click on the last point again within 6 px (so a double-click too); Esc, another tool or another command keeps the string and notifies "Dimension string kept (N points) · Undo removes it". A linear dimension's direction holds with hysteresis (30°/60°) and Tab flips it, shown in the bar. The segment-middle grip of a string now moves the dimension line like its ends do; Ctrl/⌘-drag (`Grip.insertAt`) inserts a point where dropped, Ctrl/⌘-click still one halfway. UI only: `drafting/dimensionTool.tsx`, `Step.labels`, `CadHost.pxPerMetre`.
Then (#450, for modules that drive elements from their own specification, e.g. premium generators) two generic hooks. `IDrivenElements` (a service, as `IInstanceMembers`): before core's generic commands act on elements a module drives, or on a group it owns (delete, transform with its copies and arrays, update, rename, parameters, the group and component commands, Move and Copy to storeys, Duplicate storey), the module is asked with the ids that are its own; it lets the command go on, refuses it with a message as a pinned element is refused, or does the edit in its own terms and names the ids it took care of, which the command leaves out, all in the one undo step. After every command it is told what changed (`Session.Execute`, before components settle), so an edit made through any command is written back into its specification and a rebuild keeps it. `IIfcExportFilter` keeps a module's own groups and attribute keys out of the IFC file. No module of the repository uses them; tests with a small test generator (`DrivenElementsTests`); docs/overrides.md, Driven elements.

**Plan overlays and grip snap (10.11.2026, decision 462).** Two generic interface hooks for modules that drive elements from their own specification. `planOverlays` on a module: components drawn inside every plan view's SVG with the plan's own transform (`toScreen`, `toWorld`, scale, true-north turn, the view and its storey), taking no pointer unless an element carries `data-overlay-hit`, so a module can draw a ghost of what a drag would do and clickable labels in plan coordinates without touching selection, grips or tools. `gripSnap(element, grip, to, free)`: asked while a grip is dragged, after the owner's `constrain`, so a module can keep the grips of elements it does not own (a grid axis it drives) to a step. Test of `snapGrip` in `grips.test.ts`; docs/overrides.md, Interface: plan overlays and grip snap.

**Door and Window checked before the click, for hosts a module drives (decision 463, 10.11.2026).** `LapinModule.placementCheck(req)` is asked, throttled, as the cursor moves over a wall while the Door or Window tool is on (`req`: kind, host, `along` the host's axis in metres, width, type); a module answers `{ok, message?, snapAlong?}`. A refusal draws the ghost in the error colour with its reason by the cursor, and a `snapAlong` within a metre of the cursor moves ghost and click to the place offered, on the same side of the wall; the engine still has the last word. The `pick` gesture's `placing` says what the click would put there. Pure parts and the store in `platform/placement.ts`, tested in `placement.test.ts`; docs/overrides.md, Interface: placement checks.
Then (#450, the user's request: a double door with no mullion between its leaves, keeping the mullions by its side panels, and two leaves meeting as a joiner makes them) a joinery spec takes a width per mullion and per transom (`mullions`, `transoms`: one entry per boundary, null for the frame's, 0 for none), which the grid, bars, crossings, the head's carried mullions, `Problem`, bay faces, mirroring and the IFC lining follow, and a `meeting` for two swinging leaves whose free edges touch with no mullion: butt, rebated (each leaf stepped on half its thickness, 14 mm overlap, the active leaf's lip on the room side) or an astragal (45 × 12 mm on the active leaf's room face), the active leaf (right by default) with the handle and the passive one with flush bolts at its top and bottom. Without a meeting a pair meets butt with both handles, as before. IFC keeps the double door's OperationType, writes a panel per leaf, leaves out the mullion that is not there; the catalog has a Double door preset (rebated, right active) and the preview's grid the per-boundary widths and where leaves meet.

Long roads on a terrain (decision 467, a parallel session's work of 10.11, committed at the user's request): a path cuts the ground with one tube per course and stretch (Paths.GroundCutters, SiteGeometry.LoftMany) instead of a solid per pair of sections, which took the terrain's boolean minutes and gigabytes on a long road; pavements, the ground and earthworks no longer have their seams worked out again at every edit nearby (Session.Unseamed; over 20 s on two 1.4 km roads); ManifoldBooleans checks each cutter going in and the result once, so manifold runs the chain as one batch.

Entourage kinds from pictures (decision 468, PR #21 by mikage-ao, finished with follow-up fixes): Site → Entourage → Entourage kinds… makes kinds of the project's own (`LapinEntourageKind`, a resource) from a PNG or JPEG, the picture and its kind in one undo step (`civil.addEntourageKind` takes an image of the project or the file's data), each with its name, height, width (in the project's units) and group (person, car, other: a car kind's rows are spaced as cars). A figure of one names the kind's id as its `kind` (element.update checks it is a built-in kind or the project's, the class's field now text) and stands as a thin card, the picture on its faces in 3D as a cut-out (alphaTest, no blending, its shadow cut out too); in plan the card's outline in every Entourage in 2D mode but none, in elevations and sections a flat rectangle (outline and abstract silhouettes, the body's edges when detailed). Deleting follows the types' rule: a kind its figures use stays, the message naming how many use it, and the picture a kind shows is not deleted while the kind stays, whatever the order asked (a new `IDeletion.Check` runs for every id before element.delete removes anything); a kind deleted takes its picture when nothing else uses it, and `Images.DropIfUnused` now keeps an image an orthophoto or an entourage kind uses (it looked at materials only). The IFC export writes the figure as the other entourage (IfcBuildingElementProxy "Entourage") and never the kind or its picture; the .lapin keeps both. PDF import's PNG writer (`PdfImport.Png`) writes grey with alpha and RGBA too, so a picture's see-through pixels are kept (`A_png_keeps_its_see_through_pixels`). UI: `EntourageKindsDialog.tsx`, the kinds under Pictures in the kind lists, `pictureOf`/`pictureUv` in `textures.ts` (tested), its strings in every language; the guide's Your own pictures under People and cars (both languages) and the entourage scene's `entourage-kinds` picture. Tests `EntourageTests` (21, 13 new).

**Side pieces on both sides of a path (report #136, decision 469).** A path or road with gutters, kerbs or sidewalks on both sides showed them on one side only, with thin blades across the carriageway at every station: `Corridors.Sweep` lofted each part of a section to the first part of the same key at the next station, the left one, so the right side's pieces became lofts reaching across the road. A part now goes on to the part of the same key and rank (`Paths.GroundCutters`, decision 467, already matched them so; the blades were older). A road's inspector showed the alley's `width` (1.5 m) it ignores while its quantities said 6 m, and the old single kerb switch unchecked: for `template: road` it now shows **Lane width** (1–6 m, `laneWidth`) and hides `width`, `kerb`, `kerbHeight` and `kerbWidth` (`ShowIf`); an alley keeps them. Tests `Side_pieces_asked_on_both_sides_stand_on_both_sides_and_nothing_reaches_across` (straight and around a 12 m curve: each piece on one side between the carriageway's edge and the outline, as many left as right, mirrored volumes) and `A_road_shows_its_lane_width_and_an_alley_its_width_in_the_inspector` in `CorridorTests`.

Project templates and settings that survive a restart (decision 470, GitHub issue #20 and the user's issue #9). The reporter on Linux Mint found every new project back at Lapin's defaults: wall, door and window types and units belong to the project, so they never carried over, and the interface's preferences lived only in the webview's localStorage, which WebKitGTK writes to disk late and does not flush when the app quits. File → Save as template… keeps a project's set-up and no model (`ProjectTemplates.Strip`): the project, site, building and storeys, every type, the set-up resources (materials, profiles, title blocks, images, price lists, norms, calendars, type mappings, the cut order), the views, sheets and schedules, with what they use, and the project's own attributes (units, drafting standards, view styles, graphics, filters, location, profile standards) and the office's part of its information; elements, annotations, groups and the resources that are the project's content (`ClassDescriptor.Template: false`: issues, remarks, links, components, documents, clash reports, IFC records, shared bodies, estimates, schedules and tasks, halls, guides, stages) are left out unless something kept uses them. A new project from a template (`Session.NewFromTemplate`, `project.newFromTemplate` by `id`, `path` or `data`) gets new GlobalIds everywhere (`ProjectTemplates.Renew` follows ids in values, keys and texts such as `plan:<storey>`), has no file and nothing to undo, and is upgraded as an opened file is; any `.lapin` file serves as a template. The desktop keeps templates as `.lapin` files in `<user-dir>/templates` (`TemplateLibrary`, `templates.list/save/import/export/rename/delete`), the browser in the page's IndexedDB (`template.make`). File → New from template… and the start screen list them after Lapin's default; the default chosen in Preferences → General → New projects is what File → New project starts from. The desktop also keeps every `lapin.*` key in `settings.json` in the app's data folder (the shell's `user_settings_read` and `user_settings_write`, written beside and moved over) a moment after each change and when the window closes, and puts it back into localStorage before the interface's modules load (`main.tsx` → `restoreUserSettings` → `boot.tsx`), so preferences no longer depend on the webview's storage. No Linux machine was run: the WebKitGTK cause is read from the code, not reproduced. Tests: `ProjectTemplateTests` (six), the shell's settings test, `templates.test.ts`, `userSettings.test.ts`; the guide's Files page and Getting started in English and Romanian, scene `project-templates`.

**Area rounding (report #76, decision 471).** A tester in Finland asked for room areas rounded to x,0 or x,5 m², as the Nordic countries write them. Manage → Drafting standards → Project → Area rounding: none (as measured, to 0.01 m²), 0.1, 0.5 or 1 m² (ft² in an imperial project), to the nearest step, half up, kept in the project's standards (`areaRounding`, `standards.options`, one undo step). One rule, `Lapin.Drawing/AreaRounding` (the area first to 0.0001 m², so a corner's coordinate noise never takes 12.25 below the half; one decimal at 0.1 and 0.5, none at 1), with its copy for the interface (`areaText`, `toStep` in `unitsStore.ts`). It applies to every space area shown: rooms' stamps (`{area}`, `{grossArea}`) in plans and sheets, the colour legend and the Room colours dialog, the room schedule, the documents' area fields (`DocFormat.AreaStep`) and the floor areas in the properties' quantities. Each room is rounded on its own and a total is the sum of the rows shown (SFS 5139 rounds each room), so a column adds up: 12.27 and 12.25 m² show 12.5 + 12.5 = 25.0 at 0.5, 12 + 12 = 24 at 1. Presentation only: the model, quantities, IFC Qto, costs, planning and volumes keep the exact area; material quantities (wall faces, boards, formwork, roof coverings) and user-typed area parameters are not space areas and are left as they were; an exported schedule is what the table shows. Tests: `AreaRoundingTests` (7), `unitsStore.test.ts` (3 new); the guide's Rooms → Area rounding and Getting started → Units in both languages, the `standards` scene checking the row.

Kitchen units and appliances (decision 472, a tester's report #76): a kitchen type can lay its main run from units of its own (`units` in its spec, from the left as seen from the front, after a blind corner) and its wall line from its own (`upper`), each checked by the engine (`KitchenUnits.Normalise`). Base units have a drawer over a door, doors (one or two, hinged left or right), drawers (1–4), pan drawers or open shelves; there are sinks (single, 1½, double, corner) with a standard, pull-out or no tap, a hob over an oven (600/800/900, induction or gas), freestanding cookers (500/600/900, the worktop stopping beside them), integrated dishwashers of 450 or 600, fridges (under-counter, tall integrated, freestanding), oven tall units, tall units, fillers and gaps. Wall units have doors, glazed doors or shelves, their own height and mounting height, and chimney or integrated hoods; they step past tall units, and without a listed wall line each base unit gets one (a hood over a hob). Cabinets take any width from 100 to 800 mm, appliances only their sizes. Whatever the units leave of the run is split evenly as before; units that do not fit are left out with a warning on the element and in the preview, and Fit to units (`furniture.fitKitchen`) sets a kitchen's length to what they need. New material roles `plinth` and `handles`; a kitchen type saved before them builds its plinth in its body's material and its handles in metal, so old kitchens open unchanged (the spec only gains optional keys, no upgrade). Plan symbols: sink bowls and tap, hob zones or gas burners, tall units crossed, DW/F/OV in stroke letters, base doors' swings dashed at fine detail only, wall units and hoods overhead. The types dialog lists the base and wall lines under a kitchen's parameters (`KitchenUnitsEditor`, from the engine's catalog `furniture.kitchenUnits`), the schedule Kitchen units lists every kitchen's units, and the units travel with the type through IFC. Tests: `KitchenUnitTests` (26), `kitchenUnits.test.ts`; the guide's Kitchen units and appliances (both languages) and the scene `kitchen-units`. Left: units of their own on the other runs (leg, opposite run, island), corner carousels, door swings on elevations, per-unit prices.

Reports #140 and #142 (decision 462): every dialog moves by its title bar, and the 3D view gets an X-ray style. `shell/dialogDrag.ts` is one listener for the whole window (installed in `boot.tsx`): a press on a `.dialog-head` (not on a button, field or link in it) drags its `.dialog` by the CSS `translate` property, with pointer capture so mouse, pen and touch work alike (`touch-action: none` on the bar), the dialog kept wholly inside the window (`clampOffset`, centred on an axis where it is larger), its offset remembered for the session under its `data-dialog` or its classes and title and applied again by a MutationObserver when it opens, a double click on the title recentring it, a window resize clamping the open ones; the dialogs' layout, focus (`dialogFocus.ts`) and backdrops are untouched, so every existing and future dialog is covered with no call of its own. X-ray is a fourth `visualStyle` (`xray`: shaded → edges → X-ray → wireframe in View → Visual style, and View → X-ray to switch it directly): `viewport/xray.ts` lays a see-through look (opacity 0.25, parts included) over every element's own look except the selection's, so plain meshes and instanced ones, picking, the selection's highlight and the 4D looks go through the paths they had; edges stay as in the edges style. Per-category transparency stays in Visibility / Graphics, which the notice, the guide and the Ask Lapin entry `xray-view` (and `dialog-move`) point to. Tests: `dialogDrag.test.ts`, `visualStyle.test.ts`, `faq.test.ts`; the guide's Views page (X-ray) and Interface page (Moving a dialog) in every language, and the scene `views-xray`.

Then (#460 continued, the user's answer to its open item) an elevation's marker moved into the building clips the elevation, as Revit's: its line is where the elevation starts seeing (`ElevationMarkers.Clip`: the marker's `markerDepth` past the nearest the model comes along the look; `Views.Spec` moves the elevation's frame to that plane and cuts there), what lies between the line and the viewer is not drawn and what it cuts is drawn cut with the materials' cut hatches (the drawing resolves an elevation that cuts as a section). A marker left outside, where a new one stands, sees the whole building as before, so no project changes. The marker keeps where it stands (`markerDepth`, `markerAlong`; decision 460's `markerShift` read as it was): its circle's grip puts it through `view.moveMarker` {depth, along}, the Move tool from where it stands (`ElevationMarkers.Moved`, the elevation's own front and scale for one never moved); pinned, refused. Opening symbols on an elevation show only where it sees (`ElevationMarkers.Sees`: within its reach and beyond its line), which also fixes symbols drawn past a shortened elevation's reach. Tests: `ElevationClipTests`, `ViewMarkerMoveTests`, `elevationGrips.test.ts`; views.md in every language, the views-elevation-marker scene's elevation-clipped picture. Decision 459 records the user's choice: no opening symbols in sections, for wall doors and curtain wall doors alike.

Keyboard workflow (decision 473, an idea from a user): the letter shortcuts (WA) can be turned off (Preferences → General → Keyboard, and the Keyboard shortcuts dialog), the ` key (by `e.code`, itself a rebindable binding) gives the command line all typing until Enter has run it, Esc or the same key (it stays while a command asks for input), and users keep command aliases of their own (the dialog's Command aliases page; kept and exported with the bindings, resolved before the built-in names; a shortcut that is an alias, or starts one, waits so on + Enter reaches it; clashes shown). `keyIntent` got `letters` and `takeAll`, `resolveKeymap` the aliases, `useCad.takeAll` the mode; tests in `keys.test.ts` and `shortcuts.test.ts`; the Interface page of the guide (en, ro), the scene `interface`, and every language.

**SDNF export and import (decision 474, report #141; first numbered 463, which was taken).** A tester asked for the Steel Detailing Neutral File, "about the only format that allows round-tripping through supported applications". The steel module now writes SDNF 3.0 and reads 3.0 and 2.0 (`engine/src/Modules/Steel/Lapin.Steel/Sdnf/`, docs/sdnf.md), after Intergraph's format as AVEVA's Structural Design guide reproduces its records and CATIA's documentation its general rules and version 2.0. File → Export IFC, Fragments or BOS… has an SDNF choice (where the product has the steel module): packet 00, the structure's columns and beams of any material and the steel members as packet 10's linear members (each line the axis Lapin builds the body on, with its cardinal point; a beam's clear span with the columns' centres as its nodes' offsets; the section's up as the orientation vector; steel ends as cutbacks), the steel connections' plates as packet 20's two loops, the grids as packet 50, in millimetres. Each member's SDNF number is the one it came with or one from its GlobalId, and comments map numbers to GlobalIds. File → Import SDNF… brings members back as native columns, beams and steel members (by the file's type and the member's attitude), profiles resolved against the project's profiles, the catalogue and the built-in EN library or generic names, an unknown profile kept by name on a placeholder; plates as IfcPlate bodies, grids as IfcGrid; one undo step; a file Lapin wrote, or one imported before from the same source, updates the same elements by id. Tests: `SdnfTests` (the packet layout, a round trip to 0.1 mm, a re-import updating by id, a foreign 3.0 file in feet and a 2.0 file, a hall's plates). Guide: Import and export, SDNF, in every language; scene `sdnf`. Left: end releases, holes, curved members, a name mapping between programs' profile tables.
Casework from boards (decision 475, stage 1 of the flexible furniture configurator in `docs/plan-furniture.md`): a new furniture kind `casework` built by `Casework.cs`. A piece is carcasses side by side (`carcasses` in the type's spec: a given width or a share of what is left, a `gap` knee space with a gable under the top, a height of its own), each a box of boards (sides, bottom and top between or over the sides, a back set in or laid on) holding zones stacked from the bottom with dividers between: open, doors (1–4, hand, glazed), drawers (1–6 by ratio, each with its box sized to 50 mm runner steps), a flap; adjustable or fixed shelves, a hanging rail, inner drawers. The base is a recessed plinth, metal, tapered or sled legs, nothing, or the piece hangs at `mount`; the top is none, overhanging or a worktop, optionally with a basin cut-out; fronts are slab, framed (stiles, rails, a panel set back, glass when glazed) or fluted (real grooves); handles none, a milled J-profile, bar, knob or edge pull; sliding doors run across the carcasses on two tracks; a bed is its own form (rails, headboard, base board, mattress, side drawers). Eleven presets (`Casework.Presets`, applied like the joinery styles) and seven modern styles as one-step patches. Every board is a solid of its role (new roles `back` and `legs`) and a `BoardRow` for the cut list; the `cutList` schedule lists each type's boards then its fronts in whole mm, times its pieces. IFC: the preset gives the predefined type (SHELF, DESK, BED, else USERDEFINED with its name), changed with it on the type and its pieces; the carcasses travel with the type. The types dialog shows `CaseworkEditor` (preset, styles, construction sections, carcasses with their zones top first, add/split/move/remove, Fit width) beside the engine's preview with the cut list. The old wardrobe, chest, sideboard and bookcase kinds are untouched, so projects open as before. Joinery ideas from FreeCAD's Woodworking workbench (dprojects, MIT), rewritten. Tests: `CaseworkTests` (19), `casework.test.ts`; the guide's Casework and modern furniture in every language with the scene `casework`. Left: stages 2 (CC0 soft furniture stretched by zones, with a licence record) and 3 (saved configurations in the user's library and templates).

My furniture library (decision 476, stage 3 of the flexible furniture configurator in `docs/plan-furniture.md`): any furniture type (casework, a kitchen, a sofa; later a mesh-based one, whose spec the library keeps as it is) is saved from Furniture types → Save to my library… with a name, a category, tags, an article code, an indicative price of the user's own and a thumbnail drawn from the engine's preview. The furniture module gives a type as a library item (`furniture.libraryItem`: its class, name, PredefinedType, spec and the materials it names by value, with their attributes and texture image) and takes one back (`furniture.insertFromLibrary`, one undo step: materials matched by name or made, an identical type — same canonical spec and IFC type — reused, otherwise a new IfcFurnitureType with a fresh GlobalId under a free name; a kind the edition lacks is refused by name). The library is kept like project templates: on the desktop by the engine as one JSON file per entry in `<user-dir>/furniture/` (`FurnitureLibrary` in Lapin.Protocol, `furnitureLibrary.*`), in the browser in IndexedDB (`lapin-furniture`, through the generalised `indexedRows` of `ui/src/engine/templates.ts`) with the engine checking, packing and unpacking entries. Interior → My furniture… (also in Furniture types) searches and filters the thumbnails by category and tag, inserts or places an item (its kind's tool started with it), renames, retags, duplicates, deletes, exports one item or the whole library as a `.lapinlib` file (UTF-8 JSON `{format, version, items}`) and imports one (an unchanged entry skipped, a changed one with the same id added under a new id), and adds an item to a project template (`templates.addFurniture`). Save as template… can add every type of the library (`furniture` on `templates.save` / `template.make`, inserted in a scratch session so the open project is untouched); a template already keeps the types its project has. Tests: `FurnitureLibraryTests` and `ui/src/modules/furniture/library.test.ts`; the guide's furniture page has a "My furniture library" section in every language and the scene `furniture-library`.
**11.11.2026 — Furniture from CC0 models stretched by zones (decision 477).** Stage 2 of the flexible furniture configurator (docs/plan-furniture.md). A new kind **`model`** (Interior → From a model): a type names one of 39 curated models from Kenney's Furniture Kit 2.0 (CC0, verified on kenney.nl and in the kit's License.txt; Poly Haven's CC0 verified too and allowed, none shipped: its photo-scanned, textured models are megabytes each) — sofas, a sofa with chaise, corner sections, an ottoman, armchairs, a recliner, chairs, an office chair, bar stools, benches, dining, round and coffee tables, a side table, floor, table, pendant and wall lamps, plants, rugs. The GLBs (396 KB) and the kit's isometric previews (200 KB, `ui/public/furniture-models`) ship unchanged; `scripts/furniture-assets.mjs` picks them from the unpacked kit (read as data) and writes the register `furniture-assets.json` (source, licence, verification, archive and file hashes, and per model its extent at its own proportions — the kit's units × 2 —, start size, least and most, stretch zones, slots → roles, hanging height, whether it backs onto walls). `pnpm check:licenses` checks it: every model and preview listed with its hash, licence in CC0-1.0/MIT-0, no file shipped unlisted. The stretch is a 3D nine-slice (`FurnitureModels.Stretch`): each axis's zone is the widest band with no vertex in the middle 70 % of the extent (none under a fifth: the axis scales whole; lamps and the round table zone in height only, plants and the round rug scale whole); what lies before stays, after moves, the band stretches — arms and legs keep their size, a table's top its thickness. Sizes are clamped to the model's range (a rug may be 10 mm thick). Slots map to roles (new roles accent, shade, foliage, pot); a type's materials are its model's roles only; a new model brings its size and roles and retypes the type and pieces (SOFA, CHAIR, TABLE, USERDEFINED with Floor lamp, Plant, Rug…). Plan: the stretched body seen from above by the engine's `HiddenLines`, chained and straightened, plus a sofa's seats at 1:50; hung above 1.2 m it is overhead. The meshes are read with Lapin.Mesh's glTF reader (its pieces now carry their material's name) from a new assembly `Lapin.Furniture.Models` holding only the GLBs as resources, a lazy part of the browser engine (with Lapin.Mesh), reached only through `ModelMeshes` when a model's piece is first built; stretched bodies are cached by model and size. The types dialog shows `ModelEditor`: a gallery by group, sizes in the model's range, the hanging height, four looks and a swatch row per role; the engine's preview adds the front as shaded triangles. The web build: 13.8 MB at the first load (unchanged by the models), 15.4 MB with every lazy part; `Lapin.Furniture.Models` is 60 KB brotli, fetched when the first piece of a model is built. On the way, `KitchenUnits.Typical` (decision 472) built its JsonArray with a collection expression, which the browser's trimmed build refused (IL2026): now `new JsonArray(…)`. Tests: `FurnitureModelTests` (12), `models.test.ts`; the guide's Furniture from models in every language with the scene `furniture-models`.


**11.11.2026 — A tester's notes on the planning version (decision 478).** Four points from one tester, fixed together. (1) **The Gantt under the simulation:** in the pane under the 3D view (about 240 px at 1280×720) the toolbar and the simulation's player, both wrapping, left the table 33 px, so the last rows and the scrollbar were out of reach. They now sit in `.gantt-top`, which scrolls within 45 % of the pane, and the table keeps at least 96 px; the settings popover is placed and scrolls in the pane. (2) **Ventilation left out of a generated schedule:** the Installations step took a fixed list of 24 classes, without IfcUnitaryEquipment, IfcAirToAirHeatRecovery, IfcDamper, IfcDuctSilencer, coils, control elements, IFC2X3's generic flow classes or Lapin's exhaust fan. `Lapin.Model/IfcSchema` now holds the IfcDistributionElement tree (IFC2X3, IFC4, IFC4X3; modules register their own classes, `LapinExhaustFan` as an IfcFan); a task rule naming a service supertype takes its subtypes (`TaskRule.TakesClass`; other classes still match exactly), the step names IfcDistributionElement, and a spec stored with the old list reads as the new one. The estimate's Installations chapter (`Costing.ChapterOf`) uses the same tree. Left as they were: the clash search set `SearchSets.ServicesClasses` (its own hand list), and elements with no storey in a per-storey step (counted as without a task). (3) **Hide and isolate from the right-click:** the 3D menu selects what is under the cursor when it is not selected, then offers Hide (HH), Isolate (HI) and, while something is hidden, Show all (HR), with the keys shown (`MenuItem.shortcut`). (4) **Orbiting over the model:** a press on a selected element no longer takes the drag from the orbit. A drag that starts at once orbits; a press held still for 350 ms turns the cursor to a move cursor, ends the orbit begun with the press (a `pointercancel` the press's own listener ignores) and the drag moves the selection. View → Drag to move in 3D still turns it off. Tests: `PlanningGeneratorTests` (2), `CostTests` (1), `selectionMenu.test.ts`; the guide's views and groups pages in every language.
**11.11.2026 — My tools: a ribbon tab of the user's own (decision 479).** UI only. A tab **My tools**, first in the tab row of every workspace, holds the tools a user takes from the whole application, in named groups (created, renamed, deleted), each tool large or small. Put in by right-click on any ribbon tool (Add to My tools / in a group / in a new group), by dragging a tool from another tab onto the tab's header (held over it for 600 ms the tab opens, so the tool is dropped in its place), or in **Customize My tools…** (View → Interface, the gear at the tab's end, Commands…). Ordered by dragging in the ribbon itself (a tool within a group or across groups, a group by its name; a drop line shows where it lands, the others slide by a FLIP animation, `shell/flip.ts`; a drop elsewhere changes nothing) and by keys (Alt+←/→ in the band; in the dialog Alt+↑/↓ across group ends, Alt+←/→ to the group before or after, Delete, Ctrl+Z). The dialog shows the whole catalogue (`choices()` of the quick tools dialog, searched, grouped by tab) beside the tab, drag both ways (back to the left removes), ↑ ↓ buttons, a move-to-group list, size toggle, Undo (a 50-step stack in the store, shared with the ribbon's menu), Reset, Export… and Import… (a small JSON file, `lapin-my-tools`, new group ids on import). `app/myTools.ts`: pure functions on a tab (add/move/nudge/step/rename/delete, `visibleTab` hides a tool the edition lacks without losing it, `readMyTools` repairs anything unreadable) and a zustand store kept in `localStorage` (`lapin.myTools`, per user, desktop and browser apart) holding a list of tabs, so several personal tabs can follow; one ships. Tests: `myTools.test.ts` (21); the guide's My tools section in every language with the scene `my-tools` (right-click, drag onto the header, reorder in the ribbon, rename, the dialog with search and Alt+↑, Ctrl+Z); the 40 new phrases in all ten languages.


**11.11.2026 — SDNF: curved members, holes and the bolts' holes (decision 480).** The SDNF exchange of decision 474 now carries packets 60 and 22, after their record tables on AVEVA's SDNF page (pictures beside it) and the packet lists of Tekla, SDS2 and ProStructures (docs/sdnf.md). **Curved members (packet 60):** eleven records, a linear member's ten with the arc between its second and third (centre, start angle, sweep, the plane's normal). Lapin's curved members are the structure's curved beams (an arc in plan, `PathCurve`; steel members stay straight), so the export writes each curved beam as a straight one is written (its clear span along the arc at its top, cardinal point 8, the columns' centres as the offsets to its nodes) with the normal about which the sweep turns counter-clockwise and the start angle from the plane's x (Lapin's reading: the descriptions say neither); the import makes a level curved member with its section upright a curved beam (the cardinal point's line moved to the top centre, across the arc a smaller or larger radius; the rise from the centre, the ends and the turn), and leaves out and says one arching in another plane. **Holes (packet 22):** number and parent plate, periphery, depth, vertices per face, the two loops, two records more. Imported, a through hole is cut in its plate's body (`Prism` with holes, laid onto the face along the thickness, kept when inside), the plate keeping its SDNF definition and holes (`sdnf.def`, `sdnf.holes`) so the export writes it back under its own number (imported plates were not exported before); a hole whose parent is a member (as Lapin writes a member's holes) is kept on the member in its frame (`MemberHoles`, attribute `holes`: section x, y and the station from the member's own start, so it moves with it), taken off the body by manifold in the steel module's geometry and written to IFC as an opening of its swept body. Recesses, holes off their plate and holes naming nothing are said. A file read again puts its holes in place of an element's: never doubled, and gone when the file no longer has them. **Bolts:** no SDNF packet carries them, so the import says the holes came without them; the export writes the holes the connections' bolts and anchors make in their plates (each fastener line crossing both faces within its grip, inside the outline, round at `NodeSpec.Hole`: 2 mm over the bolt, 3 mm past M24), from the lines `SteelNodes.Build` now records (`BoltLines`). Not done: arched (non-level) curved members, recesses, slots by length (Lapin's fin plates' slotted holes go out round), bolt holes in members' flanges and webs. Tests: `SdnfTests` (+5: curved beams out and back to 0.1 mm, a foreign level and a vertical curved member, plate and web holes out and back, a re-import replacing holes, a hall's bolt holes); the guide's SDNF paragraphs in every language and the `sdnf` scene's packet 22 check.

**Arrays that keep their arrangement, and along a path (decision 481, 11.11.2026).** Modify → Array no longer leaves just N copies: `element.array` makes a `LapinArray` beside them (`Lapin.Platform/Arrays.cs`), a Lapin resource the IFC export never writes, holding the rule and its items (the original first, then each copy with the ids it is and the pose the rule gave it). Besides the row (a step, or `fit` an extent shared out) and the polar array, there is now **along a path**: `{kind: "path", along | points, count | spacing, start, end, offset, orient: "tangent" | "fixed", flip, base}`. What an array can follow is asked of the modules (`IArrayPaths`): Drafting's lines, polylines, arcs and circles in a plan (arcs exactly), Civil's roads, paths, ditches and retaining walls along their alignment (`Chain`, curves and clothoids exactly) and site feature lines; without one, a path element's axis (`PathCurve`, arched walls exactly) or the line a section would follow. `array.update` places the items again by the difference between where the rule put each and where it puts it now, so the copies keep their GlobalIds, one moved by hand keeps its offset, one deleted stays a gap, new ones are copied from the original and the surplus deleted, in one undo step; a path element edited by any command moves the copies along it in that command's step (`IDrivenElements.After`), and a path deleted leaves its last line. Each copy is made, and each move done, through `ElementTransform.Run`, so a module driving its elements (`IDrivenElements.Before`, `Copy`) makes its copies its own way. `array.explode` leaves the copies plain; `array.of` and `array.preview` (the rule's places, for the tool's ghost) are queries. The interface: *Along a path* in the Array dialog, then a click on the path with the engine's preview and the base point; an Array section in the properties of any copy (count, step, angle, spacing, ends, offset, orientation; Select the array, Explode). Tests: `ArrayArrangementTests` (11: ids kept through count changes in a row, a fitted row, a polar array; a hand-moved copy and a gap; a curved wall path with tangent and offset, edited and undone; by spacing with held-back ends; a drafted arc and a road with a 15 m curve; a deleted path; a group arrayed and exploded; nothing left and nothing in IFC; a driving module asked with Copy). The guide's groups page (en, ro) and the scene `array-path`.

**Lapin for the browser (decision 482, 11.11.2026).** File → Lapin for the browser (.html)… writes the project as one web page that any browser opens by double-click, from `file://` with no network, and that is the project too: after the page, behind a `<plaintext hidden>` tag the browser reads as hidden text, the `.lapin` package as Save writes it (versions, issues' files, other tools' entries); a zip is read from its end and `ZipArchive` counts offsets from the stream's start, so the whole `.html` is a zip that Lapin, unzip and Python read as it stands (`HtmlPackage`, Lapin.Package). The engine writes it all (`HtmlExport`, Lapin.Protocol; `project.exportHtml`, delivered as every export; `project.htmlPlan` for the dialog): the 3D model as GLB in base64 (`MeshScene` + `GltfWriter`, as the 3D view shows it, each node's GlobalId in its extras), the plans, sections, elevations and sheets as SVG by the new `SvgWriter` (Lapin.Drawing.Export) from the pens `PdfWriter` prints (fills even-odd, lines with colour, width and dashes, texts at the PDF's cap height, pictures by their corners), each element's pick mark an invisible path with `data-gid`; every shown element's properties (class, type, storey, materials, the class's fields, a file's property sets, the quantity sets); and the modules' tables through a new platform hook, `IPageTables`, which the cost module fills with the estimate (`CostPageTable`: chapters and lines with their elements, the totals, marked indicative when it prices by Lapin's own values), so an edition without cost has no table. Scripts are kept closed (`<\/script`, `<\!--`, JSON's `<` as `<`). The viewer is Lapin's own code (ui/src/standalone: three.js, GLTFLoader, OrbitControls; tabs 3D with storeys, isolate, hide; Drawings with pan, zoom and print; a tab per table, sortable and filtered; one selection across them and its properties; paper and graphite themes; the exporting user's phrase book), built on its own as one IIFE by the Vite plugin `virtual:lapin-viewer` and handed to the engine by the interface (`ui/src/engine/htmlExport.ts`), so the desktop, dev and web builds write the same page and the engine embeds nothing. File → Open (desktop and browser) takes such a page: `ProjectFiles` refuses a page not of Lapin's or one exported without its project, and opens the package unsaved with no file of its own, its versions and other tools' entries kept for the first save, which asks where (as `<page>.lapin`) and never writes over the page. The guide's `run.mjs` takes a picture of another page of the browser (`shot(name, { page })`). Tests: `HtmlExportTests` (13: a package after a page read as the package alone, page and package told apart, SVG from the PDF's pens, a picture by its corners, a plan on paper with its picks, scripts kept closed, the page without a viewer, export then open on the desktop and from bytes, versions kept for the first save, a foreign page and a viewer-only page refused, the options, the estimate only with the cost module), `ui/src/standalone/data.test.ts` (10). The guide's import-export page (all nine languages) and the scene `html-export`; docs/html-export.md. Later the same day (the user's decision), a drawing that shows no model element (told by its pick marks, so by what the view draws through its Visibility / Graphics, not by storey membership; a sheet by its viewports or a schedule) is listed in the dialog unticked with *(no element)* and left out of a page written without a list (`HtmlExport.TakenByDefault`, `default`/`empty` in `project.htmlPlan`; test `A_drawing_that_shows_no_element_is_listed_but_not_taken_by_default`).
**Rebar partitions (decision 483, 11.11.2026, a tester's idea).** A bar or distribution has a partition (`partition`, a text in Properties; the drawing tools' bar sets the one new bars go in), as in Revit: marks are numbered apart in each (`Rebars.NextMark(m, partition)`), a type's stirrups and column bars have one each (`stirrupsPartition`, `columnBarsPartition`), the bar bending schedule gains a Partition column once any bar has one, its marks grouped by partition with each one's mass, and lists one partition when asked (`partitions` on the schedule, `rebar.partitions`); view filters read it as a parameter (`RebarPartitionTests`).

**Content of an absent module (decision 484, 11.11.2026).** A project holding what a module this edition lacks wrote opens, is edited, saved and exported as any, that content kept as it came. A module's own classes were already kept by the package under their module and written back; now an entity of a platform class a module owns (a group it drives) carries `ownerModule` with the module's id (`ModuleOwned`), and the platform's own `AbsentModules` service (an `IDrivenElements` and an `IIfcExportFilter`, registered after every module) keeps it as it is when its module is absent: its members are ordinary elements (moved, changed, deleted, copied as a plain group, given doors and windows), the group itself is not renamed, changed, ungrouped, filled or emptied by hand (each refused, naming the module), never dropped as empty (`Groups.DropEmpty`, `element.delete`), and left out of an IFC export (its members written as any). `Session.AbsentModules()` names the modules the project needs and the edition lacks; `project.open` and `project.openBytes` return them as `absent`, and the interface says once, as the project opens, that its content is kept and saved back unchanged (all languages). With the module again, the module compares what it drives with what was done meanwhile. docs/overrides.md. Tests: `AbsentModuleTests` (3: a test module owning a group and a class, edited without it, saved, reopened with it; its class written back under its module; its group out of IFC without it).

**Cut parts and needle triangles (decision 485, 11.11.2026).** Cut priorities now take out of each part of an element only the cutters whose box meets that part's own, no longer every cutter that reaches the element's whole box. A roof's downpipes take its box down to the ground, so a floor anywhere under the roof cut its covering, which it never touches, and the covering came back non-manifold. A roof over floors builds about a third faster, since fewer pairs are cut. A triangle mesh read back from the boolean library no longer loses its needle triangles (three corners on one line): such a triangle joined no face and its three edges were left open; its middle corner is now put on the triangle across its long edge, which is split in two, so the solid stays closed. Seen on a wall cut for a window with an outside sill and a reveal in its own material, with or without finish layers. Tests: `CutPartsTests`, `RevealSillShellTests`, `SolidFromMeshTests`. Left: the lintel is not cut by the reveal's head, which reaches 20 mm above the opening.

**An edition's guide of its own (decision 486, 11.11.2026).** The guide's runner (`scripts/user-docs/run.mjs`) takes two more arguments for an edition whose pages live in another repository: `--out <folder>` writes the pages and pictures there instead of `build/user-docs` (the incremental state beside it, `<folder>-cache`), so nothing of a private edition is ever left in the folder `deploy/web/deploy.sh` publishes; `--alone` (with `--scenes` and `--docs`) makes a guide of the edition's own pages and scenes only, none of the public guide's merged in. Without them nothing changes.

**A refusal's reason in the interface's language (decision 487, 12.11.2026).** When a module refuses a door or window where the cursor is (`placementCheck`), the plan writes its reason by the cursor, wrapped into lines. Wrapped, the lines escaped the translation every other text gets, so a module's sentence stayed in English. The reason now goes through the language's phrases and patterns first, then is wrapped (`reasonLines`, `platform/placement.ts`), and is written again when the language changes. Test: `placement.test.ts`.

**A frameless glass door on a floor spring (decision 488, 12.11.2026).** A forum user (topic #28) wanted a floor hinge for a frameless glass door; its **Fixing** offered only pivots with patch fittings (a floor spring named, never drawn) and glass hinges. A third fixing, `floor-spring`, hangs a swinging leaf on a floor spring let into the floor under its pivot: the box below the floor finish with its cover plate flush (`JoineryBox.Underfloor`), the pivot `Pivot` in from the leaf's hinge edge (55 mm, 30 to 150 mm, within a third of the leaf), a top pivot in the gap over the leaf, a top patch fitting, a bottom patch fitting or a heel strap (`Bottom`), a lock patch opposite, a hold-open angle (`HoldOpen`: 0 or 60–180°). Single or double action is the leaf's operation, which the dialog's **Opening action** switches. Four indicative models (`Frameless.Springs`: 60, 100, 150, 300 kg with their widest leaf, EN 1154 force and box); `auto` takes the lightest that carries the heaviest and widest leaf (toughened glass 2500 kg/m³), and a model too light is refused with the leaf's weight. The plan swings the leaf about its pivot (its short end past it, a double-acting leaf's other swing dashed) and draws the cover plate; elevations draw the box dashed and the pivot's axis; the schedule's new **Hanging** row (`ScheduleLayout.HangingText`, shown when a frameless door is listed) names the fixing; IFC keeps `DOUBLE_SWING_*`/`SINGLE_SWING_*`/`SWING_FIXED_*` and writes `Lapin_FloorSpring` on the door (model, carried and actual leaf mass, closing force, action, pivot offset, fittings, box, `HoldOpenAngle` in radians). The fittings stay parts of the door's body, as the patch fittings were (no `IfcDiscreteAccessory`). The preset **New frameless door on floor spring** (`doorPresets["floor-spring"]`, 1000 × 2100, double acting, hold-open 90°); the catalog lists `floorSprings`. Tests: `FloorSpringTests`; scene `door-floor-spring`. The same day, the plan for doors and windows combined into one object with per-pane glazing data and Uw after EN ISO 10077-1 (topic #34): `docs/plan-door-window-assemblies.md`, not built.
**Several elements at once, and Match properties (decision 490, 12.11.2026; forum topic #29).** A forum user selected 23 beams, chose a phase in the properties panel and found only one beam had it. The engine was not at fault: `element.update`, `phases.assign`, the type commands and `moveOpening` all take `ids`. Several property sections sent the primary element alone: the phases (`phases.assign {id}`), the type of steel members, furniture, sunshades, curtain walls, gates and fans, furniture's, sunshades' and entourage's own fields, a drafted shape's layer, colour, role and hatch, a text's and a dimension's style, a room's fields, a view's discipline, and a typed absolute elevation. They now go to every selected element that takes the change (`selectionWith`, `sharedValue` in `shell/params.ts`): the phases to every selected element on a storey whatever its class, showing *varies* where they differ; the absolute elevation by each element's own offset from its own level, one `edit.batch`. What is each element's own stays single (a room's number, a text's words, a shape's points). Then **Match properties** (Modify, <kbd>MA</kbd>, `MATCHPROP`, `MATCHTYPE` kept as an alias; AutoCAD's MATCHPROP, Revit's Match Type) replaces Match type: the selected element (or the first clicked) is the source, each element clicked takes what it carries in one `edit.batch`, so one undo step; **Apply to the selection** gives it to every other selected element; <kbd>Enter</kbd> or <kbd>Esc</kbd> ends it. The groups carried, chosen in a settings popover on the command line's bar and remembered on the computer: the type (to the same class, through the module's own `setType`, its command recorded by `stepsOf` in `engine/session.ts`), the fields both classes declare with the same kind (heights, offsets, materials, cut priority, a text's style) and a door's or window's frame position and sill, the user's parameters (a standard set's only to the same class), the phases, a drafted shape's layer, colour and hatch, and the name (off by default). Placement, shape and identity are never carried (start, end, at, contour, rotation, a room's number, a mark; `NEVER` in `modules/core/matchProperties.ts`). Pinned elements take them (decision 449). The element under the cursor is lit while the command waits for one, in plans and drawings (the TAG command's hover, now any step with `hover`) and in 3D (Draft3D raycasts and feeds `element` to the step; Viewport3D lights the hovered object). Tests: `MultiEditTests` (engine), `matchProperties.test.ts`, `params.test.ts`, `tagTarget.test.ts`; scene `match-properties`; guide: Interface → Several elements at once, Match properties, in every language. Left: no per-element graphic overrides or worksets exist to carry; the 3D hover does not light instanced elements.
**Snaps take only what the view shows (forum #31, decision 491, 12.11.2026).** A plan snapped to every element of its storey: the modelling lines and snap points the interface keeps for each element were offered whether the view drew the element or not, so an element hidden by the view's Visibility / Graphics (its category, a filter, a link, a phase) or lying outside its view range still caught the cursor. The drawing the engine makes for the view comes from the one resolver every drawing, sheet and export uses, and carries a pick mark for every element it draws and a mark for every annotation; the plan now snaps to the modelling lines and points of those elements only (`viewport/cad/shown.ts`), and to everything on screen while the modelling lines are shown instead of the drawing. Sections, elevations and the 3D drafting already snapped to the engine's drawing alone, and the 3D view's snaps to the bodies it shows (a look's hidden element is not drawn). Tests: `shown.test.ts`, `VisibilityGraphicsTests.A_hidden_element_leaves_nothing_in_the_drawing_to_snap_to`.

**A view's scale picked in its corner (forum #30, decision 492, 12.11.2026).** A view's scale was set only in its properties. At the top of a plan, a section or an elevation, beside the zoom chip (now its size on screen against paper alone), a scale picker shows the view's scale and changes it as the properties do (`element.update`, one undo step): the project's standard scales, **Custom…** for one typed (1:75, 75 or 1/75), and **Default scales…**, which opens Drafting standards on Project. A level's default plan, held by no view, becomes the level's floor plan view at the scale picked (`view.create`, one step too). On a sheet, the picked viewport's view shows its scale there (not a schedule's or a legend's). Drafting standards → Project gains **Standard scales** (the list the picker offers; empty: 1:1 to 1:2000, the usual ones) and **New plans / sections / elevations at 1:** (the scale `view.create` gives a view of that kind when none is asked for, and a level's default plan's; empty: 1:100), kept in the standards (`scales`, `planScale`, `sectionScale`, `elevationScale`) and read back by `standards.get` with the list in force (`scaleChoices`). A module's own views (a stair's, a steel hall's, a section across an element) keep their own scales. Tests: `ViewScaleTests`, `viewScale.test.ts`; scenes `views` (picks 1:20 on section A-A, undoes it), `standards`, `sheets`.

**A layer's material hatches at hand (forum #30, decision 493, 12.11.2026).** A material's hatches were set only in Manage → Materials, away from the layer table where the material is chosen. In Wall types (each layer, and each piece up a layer's height) and Floor types, a swatch beside the material shows its cut hatch; a click opens, beside it, the material's 2D graphics: its hatch where a section cuts it, where a plan cuts it and where an elevation sees it, each with its scale, angle, alignment, fill and pen. It is the Materials dialog's own table, taken out of it (`MaterialGraphicsTable`) so both edit with the same command (`material.update`, one undo step each change); what is set is the material's, so every type and element made of it follows. The rows read the materials once per model version for every row (`materialHatch.ts`); the editor opens under the swatch or over it, inside the window, and Esc or a press outside closes it, not the dialog under it. A roof has no layer table (its covering and structure are its properties), so it has no swatch. Tests: `materialHatch.test.ts`; scene `walls` (picks CROSS for the first layer's material there and finds it the material's own).

On 12.11.2026 (decisions 494 and 495), two IFC exchange pieces. (494) A reference's kept openings (decision 354: the uncut body and the file's IfcOpeningElement, IfcRelVoidsElement and IfcRelFillsElement as attributes, `bodyUncut` and `ifcOpenings`, written back by the export; the task as asked had them lost, but that was fixed on 06.11) now go with the reference the way a native wall's doors go with it (`ReferenceFills`, an `IHostedPlacement` and an `IDeletion` in core, and `ReferencePlacement`): moved, turned, mirrored or raised, the doors and windows filling its openings are carried too (one selected with its wall is carried once, its host being the reference); copied, the copy keeps its uncut body and openings under new GlobalIds (it used to be its cut body alone) and the doors are copied into them; deleted, the doors in it go too, with any shared body left without instances. A door deleted, moved or copied on its own leaves the file's opening empty (a reference's hole does not slide or close), which IFC allows. Adding a new opening to a reference with the opening tool is left for later (it needs the booleans at edit time). Older projects (a cut body only) need no upgrade: they have no openings to carry. Tests: the move, rotation and copy with the export's ids never doubled, the deletion and its undo, 100 IFC cycles of a referenced wall with a door (`LAPIN_IFC_CYCLES`) with the same opening, voids, fills, GlobalIds and volumes each time, and `LAPIN_IFC_REFERENCES` for a real file: on Bloc-P-4, 1,459 references, 515 with 525 kept openings and 175 fills, imported as references in 0.70–0.89 s (the import unchanged), every reference moved in 2.2 s, exported with all 525 openings, voids and 175 fills. (495) **Bonsai drawing styles** in File → Export IFC… (desktop): `BonsaiStyles` writes `name.css` and `name-patterns.svg` beside the IFC and `BBIM_Documentation` (`StylesheetPath`, `PatternsPath`) on IfcProject, as Bonsai's public source names them (read for the naming only): base rules for `cut`, `projection`, `surface`, Bonsai's edge classes, annotation and text, each IFC class's cut and projection lines from the object styles, each material's cut hatch, fill and elevation hatch under `material-<name>` and `layer-material-<name>` (names stripped to ASCII letters and digits, as Bonsai does), a layer set's core's; the hatches made from Lapin's .pat line families as SVG patterns (a sheared, turned line per family, laid in one tile over the background). Tests: `BonsaiStylesTests`; `LAPIN_BONSAI_DUMP` writes a page of every hatch (checked in Inkscape). What to verify in Bonsai by hand is listed in docs/spec-ifc.md (Stilurile de desen pentru Bonsai).
**Finish layers by the kind of view (decision 496, 12.11.2026; the forum's topic #30, Poland).** Polish drawings print plans without any finishes and sections with the floors' finishes but not the walls'. A type's layer now has a category of its own (`MaterialLayer.Category`, IFC4's `IfcMaterialLayer.Category`: Structure, Finish, Insulation, Membrane, Other; empty, its material's), set in the new **Category** column of Wall types and Floor types and read by one rule, `LayerSets.SubOf`, for the view styles, Visibility / Graphics and 3D. A view style has four more choices, each on by default and for a style saved before them: `wallFinishesPlan`, `floorFinishesPlan`, `wallFinishesSection`, `floorFinishesSection` (sections and elevations), in Manage → View styles and, per view, in View settings (**Wall finishes**, **Floor finishes**). Visibility / Graphics resolves them (`VisibilityGraphics.FinishesHidden`, from the view's style and kind), so plans, sections, sheets, PDF and DXF agree. They cover walls' and slabs' finish layers, references that are all finish, and rooms' wall and floor finishes; 3D views keep their own layers. Each layer is a closed solid, so what is left draws its cut outline at the faces it keeps, joins and corners as before. The drawing says which categories lost their finishes (`hiddenFinishes`) and a wall's plan pieces carry their layer's role (`PlanItem.Sub`), so the plan no longer snaps to faces it does not draw: decision 491's filter of what a plan snaps to (`viewport/cad/shown.ts`) leaves out the hidden finish pieces of the elements it keeps, so a wall without its plaster snaps to its core's outline (`shown.test.ts`). IFC writes the layer's own category as its Category (Core stays the core's) and the import reads it back. Quantities and bodies are unchanged. Tests: `FinishLayerViewTests` (6), `finishSnaps.test.ts`; scene `finishes-by-view`.

**Walls through the floors' layers by priority (decision 497, 12.11.2026; the forum's topic #30).** Walls stood on the floor's top, on its finish and screed, and **Join** joins walls only. A wall type's layer can now go on down (`MaterialLayer.ExtendDown`, ↓ in the new **Into floors** column of Wall types) and up (`ExtendUp`, ↑). The rule is the walls' joins' (report #81): a layer passes the other element's layers of a lower priority and stops at the first as high or higher, with the same ranks (the layer's own, else its material's in the cut order, else 50 for a core and 0 for the rest). Floor types gained the **Priority** column (saving a floor type used to drop it). Down: from the base of a wall standing on (or in) a floor, to the top of the first floor layer ranked as high, so by default the brick core goes through the finish and the screed onto the concrete. Up: from the head of a wall that meets a floor above (its underside, where the floor outranks the wall and cuts its head), through the floor's lower layers (a ceiling plaster) to the underside of the first ranked as high; a gap is not filled. `Walls.Extensions` takes each such layer's footprint as the joins leave it (`Walls.LayoutOf`, so corners and T's are right) between the two heights, kept to the floor's outline. The piece is added after the cut priorities and united with the layer's part it continues, so the section draws no line at the floor's face. The floor takes the same pieces out of its layers (`Slabs.WallExtensions`, read only when a wall type has such a layer), and the quantities of both follow. A zoned core goes down with its first zone and up with its last; curved walls are left for later. IFC keeps the bodies as built and the layer sets (`IfcMaterialLayerSetUsage`), and marks each such layer with an `IfcMaterialProperties` named `Lapin_LayerJoins` (ExtendToFloorBelow, ExtendToFloorAbove) that the import reads back. Unticked is null in the file, so a project saved before opens unchanged with no upgrade. A new type made in the dialog has its core ticked down; types made by command (the default one too) do not. Tests: `WallFloorLayerJoinTests` (6), `layerFlags.test.ts`; scene `wall-into-floor`.

**A click selects only what the view shows (decision 498, 12.11.2026).** Decision 491 kept a plan's snaps to what its drawing draws; selection had no such rule. A plan's click, window, crossing box and lasso, the element lit under the cursor (tags, Match properties) and a drafting command's pick took their candidates from every element of the storey (`PlanView`: `pickAt`, `nearestOf`, `regionIds` over all its render items), so a wall hidden by the view's Visibility / Graphics, a filter, a phase or its range was still taken by a click on the place it would be. In 3D the ray was cast at every element whose own group was visible (`Viewport3D`'s `pickAt`), but three.js meets hidden objects too, so a hidden layer, a wireframe's faces and anything a section box or a section shown in 3D cuts away still took the click (only the cut above the storeys shown was checked). Sections, elevations and sheets already picked from the engine's drawing alone (`drawingPick.ts`, its marks). Now the plan picks from the same items its snaps take (`viewport/cad/shown.ts`: `shownItems`, keeping a part whose whole the drawing names, so drilling into assemblies goes on as before), and 3D takes a hit only on an object shown all the way up and on the kept side of every clipping plane (`viewport/seen3d.ts`: `seenHit`; a level of detail's finest copy still counts while a coarser one is drawn); a region in 3D also leaves out what a clipping plane takes away wholly (`clippedAway`). The engine's drawing now also marks an element it draws only with overhead or drawn hidden lines (a beam above a plan's cut), with no pick lines, so it stays selectable by its own modelling lines (`Views.PickMarks`). Modelling lines shown instead of the drawing still pick everything on screen. The project browser, schedules, a link in Properties and the dependencies still select a hidden element by name: the view in front publishes what it hides (`useShownHere`; plans and drawings by their marks, 3D by its objects and clipping) and Properties says *Not shown in this view*. There is no select-all or Tab-cycling in the views to change. Tests: `shownSelect.test.ts`, `seen3d.test.ts`, `VisibilityGraphicsTests.An_element_drawn_only_overhead_is_marked_as_shown_without_pick_lines`; scene `select-shown`; the guide's Interface page (What a click selects) in every language.

**Python scripting, stage 1: the `lapinbim` client (decision 489, 12.11.2026).** The user answered the twelve questions of `docs/plan-python-scripting.md` §11 with its recommendations, and stage 1 is built: `python/lapinbim`, a pure-Python, standard-library-only (3.11+) MIT package that drives the open Lapin from outside, as the MCP bridge does. Its own WebSocket client over `socket` (handshake, masked frames, fragments, ping/pong, close), the LPF1 frame reader (arrays as `memoryview`s on the frame, numpy-ready without copying), JSON-RPC calls one at a time from any thread, the engine found by `LAPIN_PORT`, else the first live `host.json`, else `pnpm dev`'s 47810, on 127.0.0.1 only. Layer 1 is raw (`execute`, `query`, `undo`, `redo`, `save`, `info`, `snapshot`, `state`, `call`); layer 2 is typed from the **command catalogue**: `lapin.architecture.add_wall(storey_id=…, x1=0, …)`, the namespace the command's prefix and both names snake_case, metres and degrees, refused commands raising `LapinError` with the engine's message. The catalogue is made from the engine's source by `scripts/command-catalogue.mjs` (the MCP bridge's `argsFromSource` moved there and widened: the keys of the helpers a handler hands its `a` to, two levels deep, a loop over literal names, and a type guessed per key from how it is read; 551 commands, 182 queries, about 2 200 arguments), written as `engine/src/Platform/Lapin.Protocol/catalogue.json`, regenerated by the Lapin.Protocol build before it compiles (an MSBuild target, incremental on the engine's .cs files; skipped without node), embedded, and served as `session.catalogue` (`CommandCatalogue.cs`: the product's commands with label and module, its queries, each with its arguments; read-only, open to guests like `session.info`). The MCP bridge now reads `lapin_info`'s arguments from it, so an installed app has them too (the repo's file for an engine from before). `.pyi` stubs are generated from the catalogue (`python -m lapinbim stubs`; builtins aliased since `component.list` would hide `list`), checked with mypy: arguments and their types complete, a wrong type or a missing command is flagged. `lapin.batch("Facades")` gathers the commands into one `edit.batch`, which now takes a `label` naming its undo step (`Session.LabelOf`, trimmed, at most 120 characters; not a text: refused); inside, commands return `Deferred` results filled at the end, a nested batch joins the outer one, an exception sends nothing. `lapin.model.walls` (a class by the plural of its label: `floors` for IfcSlab, `rooms` for IfcSpace), `model.of(…)`, `model[id]`: `Element`s from `doc.snapshot` (its parts joined), kept until a diff arrives, with `quantities()`, `quantity("NetVolume")`, `update`, `rename`, `set_param`, `delete`. `lapin.on("diff", f)` (and any event) on a thread of its own. Packaging: `pyproject.toml` with the package's own PEP 517/660 backend (`python/build_backend/lapin_build.py`, standard library: wheel, sdist, editable; `pip install ./python` works offline), version `0.1.<commit count>` like the app's, and `scripts/publish-python.mjs`, which stages the package, writes the catalogue and stubs of an engine of its own into it, builds and checks the wheel, and with `--upload` runs twine with the user's own token (nothing uploaded by us; `twine check --strict` passes). Tests: `CommandCatalogueTests` (every registered command and query is in the file, `session.catalogue`'s arguments, label and module, the batch label as the undo step), `python/tests` with `unittest` against an engine of its own on a free port (`pnpm test:python`, part of `pnpm test`, on the oldest Python from 3.11 and the newest; skipped with a message without Python, dotnet, node or a built engine), and `lapin-mcp-check.mjs` checks the bridge's arguments. Developer page `docs/python.md`; `docs/mcp.md` says where the arguments come from. Left for stage 2 onwards: the console, `doc.begin/commit`, the recorder, project scripts, script tools; a premium module built in its own repo has its commands listed without arguments (its catalogue is not in the open repo's file).
**Lapin Extensions, stage 0 (decision 499, 12.11.2026).** The user decided an extensions marketplace, **Lapin Extensions** (store.lapinbim.com later): content packs first (furniture, project templates, profiles, hatch patterns, title blocks, price lists), then Python scripts (after the Python plan's stage 4), JS UI plug-ins and signed C# modules for partners; 0 % commission at first, paid extensions and Lapin's premium modules later through a merchant of record; the plan, the manifest, the stages, security and the legal to-do are in `docs/plan-extensions.md`. Stage 0 needs no new server: a public GitHub repository `lapin-extensions` holds one manifest per extension (prepared locally, not yet on GitHub), its CI checks pull requests (manifest schema, the package downloaded and its size, SHA-256 and kind's data checked, a changed version raised, only its author changing it; Node, no dependencies) and builds the index; the package lives in its author's GitHub release. In Lapin, **File → Extensions…** (`file.extensions`): **Browse** (search, the kinds as categories, an extension's author, licence — SPDX, required, marked permissive or "read its terms" —, the Lapin versions it works with, size, SHA-256, what it holds, a script's permissions and that it runs only once the Python console exists) and **Installed** (open where it went, Update when the catalogue has a newer version, Uninstall). Install checks the Lapin version range, the kind's module in the edition and code on the desktop only, downloads the file, checks its size and SHA-256 against the manifest, reads it as its kind's file (`packs.ts`) and hands it to the importer Lapin already has: My furniture (`furnitureLibrary.import`), the project templates (`templates.import`), the computer's profile libraries, My patterns, title blocks and price lists (a new `.lapinprices` JSON, marked indicative); a script is kept as a file and never run. Each kind's installer is registered by the module owning its library (`addContentInstaller`). What is installed, with a receipt of what it added, is kept with the `lapin.*` settings; Uninstall takes away only what is on the receipt; Update downloads and checks first, then replaces. The catalogue's address: Catalogue… in the dialog, else `LAPIN_EXTENSIONS_INDEX` given to the desktop engine (a local index while developing), else `https://lapinbim.com/extensions/index.json` (not live yet). The desktop reads through its host (`extensions.fetch`, past the call queue: https, http on this computer only, local files, 64 MB at most, never a guest's; `extensions.source`); the browser uses fetch and lapinbim.com's copies of the packages (`package.mirror`), since GitHub's release files answer no browser. An extension's homepage opens through `support.open` (`extensionPage`, https only). The three examples (a living-room furniture pack, a Romanian project template, a Python script) are built by `tools/make-examples.mjs` driving a Lapin engine. Tests: `manifest.test.ts`, `install.test.ts` (a fake fetch: SHA-256 refused, compatibility, update, uninstall), `packs.test.ts`, `ExtensionFetchTests`; scene `extensions`; the guide's Extensions page in every language.
**The partial structure display (decision 500, 12.11.2026; the forum's topic #30).** The user chose ArchiCAD's way over a country template: one choice per view, the partial structure display, with four presets: **Entire model** (`all`), **Without finishes** (`noFinishes`: the finish layers left out, plasters, renders, floor finishes and screeds of category Finish; insulation, membranes and structure drawn), **Core only** (`core`) and **Core of load-bearing elements only** (`coreLoadBearing`: the cores of the load-bearing elements alone, anything bearing no load not drawn at all). It is the view styles' `layers` mode grown a fourth value and said by kind of view: a style has `layersPlan` (its plans), `layersSection` (its sections and elevations) and `layers` (its 3D and other views, and what the first two take when unset; `Style.LayersFor`), a view its own `layers` over them, set in View settings, in Manage → View styles (Structure in plans / sections / 3D) and from the view's corner beside the scale picker of decision 492 (`StructurePicker`, `viewStructure.ts`: `view.setStyle`, one undo step, the style's value clearing the view's own; on a sheet the selected placed drawing's view; a level's default plan becomes its plan view through `view.create {layers}`). `ViewStyles.BearsLoad` reads `LoadBearing` as the analytical model does (`Provisions.LoadBearingOrNull`: Pset_…Common on the element or its type, or `loadBearing`) for walls, floors, roofs, columns, beams, members, plates, footings and piles; unset, columns, beams, footings, piles, floors and roofs bear load and walls do not; a covering or a room's finish never; other elements are kept. Visibility / Graphics resolves it (`VisibilityGraphics.Structure`: `HidesPart` the layers, `Hides` the elements bearing no load), so drawings, sheets, PDF, DXF and picking follow; the 3D gets `coreLoadBearing` in `hiddenBy`. A plan applies it, and decision 496's finish choices, to what its cut plane cuts: a part seen wholly under or over the cut shows as it is (`HidesPart(…, cut: false)`), else a plan without finishes lost its floors (their finish on top, the rest under the range).
