The drawing tells the interface its `structure` and the layer roles left out (`hiddenSubs`: finishes; for the cores also insulation and membranes), which the snaps skip (`finishSnaps.ts`). Decision 496's four switches stay, as the advanced detail by element kind. A new project's Architectural style draws its plans Without finishes and the rest the Entire model; a project made before draws as it did (`ViewStyles.StructureUpgrade`, `structureRev`: without styles of its own it gets the ones it read then, `LegacyDefaults`, written on it; its own styles and decision 496's choices are kept). The default wall type (render, brick, plaster) has its core ticked ↓ into floors, so it stands on the structural slab; it is made at a project's first wall, so walls and types already made stay. Quantities and IFC are unchanged. Tests: `PartialStructureTests` (9), tests drawing plans with every layer call `Fixture.EveryLayerInPlans`; `viewStructure.test.ts`; scenes `finishes-by-view` (rewritten), `views-3d-layers`.

**Lapin Extensions: the user's answers to the plan's open questions (decision 499, 12.11.2026).** The user took every recommendation of `docs/plan-extensions.md` §9, now written there as answers. Any SPDX licence is accepted; one that can be taken only under a non-commercial licence (an SPDX id with `-NC`, `PolyForm-Noncommercial-1.0.0`, `NCGL-UK-2.0`, a `LicenseRef-…NonCommercial`; the expression evaluated, so `MIT OR CC-BY-NC-4.0` is not) is marked **Non-commercial** in Browse and in the details, and the catalogue's `validate.mjs` prints it so (the same `isNonCommercial` in `manifest.ts` and `tools/lib.mjs`). Types became a kind of their own, `types`: a `.lapintypes` JSON pack (`lapin-type-pack` v1, chosen over a small `.lapin` so the catalogue's CI reads it without an engine) holding the chosen types with their attributes and what they need (layer sets, the materials they name by id or under a material key, profile copies, any other resource their attributes point at), tied by `ref`s. The engine's `types.export`, `types.readPack`, `types.list` and `types.importFrom` (core, `TypePacks`) work for every type class the edition describes: brought in as one undo step, a material is the project's of that name or made as the pack has it, an identical resource is reused, an identical type (class, name, attributes, layers) is reused, one of the same name that differs is added beside it as „Name (2)” and the project's is never changed, and only the chosen types come with what they need; a bad pack is refused whole. **Manage → Type packs…** exports ticked types and imports a pack's chosen ones; the kind's installer keeps the pack on the computer and brings its types into the open project (Installed → Add its types to this project for another). **Price lists…** exports a list of the computer's library as `.lapinprices`, always indicative. The index will be on Hetzner beside the forum, every package mirrored on lapinbim.com, a new signing key for stage 1 separate from the updater's, the 64 MB limit kept, download statistics from stage 1 (documented only; the hosting is done separately). Tests: `TypePackTests`, `typesKind.test.ts`, `manifest.test.ts`, the catalogue's `tools/lib.test.mjs`; scene `extensions-types`; the guide's Extensions page in every language.

**Python scripting, stage 2: the Script panel (decision 489, 12.11.2026).** The open module `scripting` joins the free edition through the modules' hooks only. Its engine half is the host's (`engine/src/Host/Lapin.Host/ScriptHost.cs`): `script.run`, `script.stop`, `script.restart`, `script.status` and the user's own scripts (`script.files`, `script.load`, `script.save`, `script.delete`, `script.reveal`: plain `.py` files in `<user-dir>/scripts`, opened in the external editor named in Preferences or the system's text editor, never its action for `.py`), answered past the call queue, refused to guests and to an edition without the module, with the kernel's lines sent to the computer's windows as `script.output`. Python is the user's own, 3.11 or newer: the one named in Preferences, else `LAPIN_PYTHON`, `py -3`, `python3`, `python` and the usual places (python.org, Homebrew, pyenv), each asked its version, a clear message when none fits. The kernel, `python -I -B -u` running `lapinbim.kernel` (the package copied by `build:product` to `resources/python` where the product has the module; a checkout's `python/` in `pnpm dev`), binds `lapin`, keeps its names between runs, shows a console line's value, refuses `input()`, reports errors with their line and only the script's frames, and connects back over the protocol; Stop interrupts it (SIGINT, `interrupt_main` on Windows), then kills it after 2 s. One Run is one undo step: the engine now holds a step open (`doc.begin {label}`, `doc.commit`, `doc.rollback`; `Session.Step.cs` over `History.Begin/Commit/Rollback`), tied to the connection that opened it: its commands fold into one transaction named after the script and journalled once, their diffs still go out as they are made, other connections' edits, undo, redo, saves and project changes are refused (*A script is editing the model*), reads go on, delayed deliveries wait, a refused command takes back only itself, and a lost connection rolls the step back for every window; guests cannot open one. The interface half (`ui/src/modules/scripting`) is the Script panel behind Preferences → Developer extras (off by default; with it, a Developer group on View through the new `RibbonGroupDef.shown`): Mine, an editor in CodeMirror 6 (MIT, fetched only with the panel) with Python highlighting, the last error's line marked and completion after `lapin.` from `session.catalogue`, Run (Ctrl+Enter), Stop, Restart, Save and Open in external editor (the file followed), and a console with its history; in the browser it says Python runs in the desktop app. The user decided the same day that `lapinbim` goes to PyPI only with an app release, that premium repositories make their own catalogue (`command-catalogue.mjs --root … --out …`, embedded as `Lapin.catalogue.json` and read by `session.catalogue`), and that a lighter model read comes later. Tests: `ScriptStepTests`, `ScriptHostTests` (with this computer's Python, passing with a message without one), `CommandCatalogueTests`, `python/tests/test_kernel.py`, `logic.test.ts`, `prefs.test.ts`; scene `python-console`; the guide's Python scripts page in every language.
**Structural analysis, stage S0 (12.11.2026, decision 501; docs/plan-fem.md).** The study of an FEM engine compared the open solvers. OpenSees allows only non-commercial or internal use, CalculiX and Code_Aster are GPL, BriefFiniteElement.NET and anaStruct are LGPL, and xara and Kratos are permissive but C++ and hard to bring to wasm. The user chose Lapin's own C# solver, MIT, in stages S0–S5. S0 adds the module `analysis` (`engine/src/Modules/Structure/Lapin.Analysis`, `ui/src/modules/analysis`). It brings load cases as `IfcStructuralLoadCase` (the standard set: G1 self weight, G2 finishes and partitions, Q-A imposed, S snow, W wind) and the user's loads as `IfcStructuralPlanarAction`, `IfcStructuralCurveAction` and `IfcStructuralPointAction` (annotation classes on a storey, drawn on its plans and in 3D, kN-based). It derives loads on every ask: self weight from the analytical model, floor finishes from the layers outside the core, non-load-bearing walls as line loads, and rooms by their imposed-load category. The category is the room's `loadCategory`, else derived from its function; its qk comes from EN 1991-1-1 Table 6.2/6.10 recommended values, overridable on the project. The EN 1990 Annex A1 combinations are derived on every ask too: ULS 6.10 per leading action, permanent only, and wind uplift; SLS characteristic, frequent and quasi-permanent; ψ from Table A1.1. All values are marked indicative, and the user kept the EN values for now (the Romanian national annex and CR 0-2012 later). The IFC export can write the Structural Analysis View (`structural: true`, IFC4 and IFC4X3). It carries an `IfcStructuralAnalysisModel` with point connections, curve and surface members on shared topology, and supports as boundary conditions. Each member is assigned to its physical element. The cases are written with their self-weight coefficients, the actions in N, N/m and N/m² connected to the member under them, and the combinations as load groups with factors. GlobalIds are stable. An import ignores the SAV. The platform contract `IStructuralLoads` feeds the export. Tests: `LoadCaseTests`, `DerivedLoadTests`, `CombinationTests`, `StructuralIfcTests`. The guide's page and scene come with it.

**12.11.2026: the partial structure display's follow-ups (decision 502).** The user approved three follow-ups to decision 500. First, a wall that says nothing of its load now counts as load-bearing for **Core of load-bearing elements only**, so the preset is useful before every wall is marked. The exceptions are a wall whose own or type `PredefinedType` is IFC's `PARTITIONING`, and a wall or type with `LoadBearing` unticked (`ViewStyles.BearsLoad`); a curtain wall still bears none. Lapin ships no partition wall types: only the default type, which bears load. So nothing needed marking, and a partition type is marked by unticking `Pset_WallCommon.LoadBearing`. The analytical model keeps its own rule on purpose: there a wall bears load only when it says so (`Provisions.LoadBearing`), and the others are partitions whose weight the derived loads put on the floors. Second, in that preset a wall left out takes its doors, windows and openings with it (`VisibilityGraphics.HostOf`: the opening a door fills and the wall that opening voids, or a reference's kept openings). The rule covers plans, sections, elevations, sheets, PDF and DXF, and with them the marks, so snaps and picking too. The 3D still shows those doors, because a render part's `hiddenBy` cannot depend on another element. Third, separate finish elements now go with the layers of their role (`ViewStyles.FinishElementSub`, `LeavesOut`). Room finishes (floor, walls and ceiling) and skirtings count as finishes. A reference made of one material goes by that material. A covering goes by its IFC kind: flooring, cladding, ceiling, moulding and skirting board are finishes; insulation and membrane go with their roles; roofing stays with its roof. **Without finishes** leaves out the finishes; the core presets leave out insulation and membranes as well. A plan applies decision 500's cut-plane rule: wall finishes that the plane cuts disappear, while a floor finish or skirting seen below the plane stays. Sections and elevations leave them out everywhere, and the 3D and its exports hide them through `hiddenBy`. A style says it through `finishElements` (on by default; the **Finish elements** column in Manage → View styles). `StructureUpgrade` now goes to `structureRev` 2. A project marked below 2 gets `finishElements: false` on its styles, or on defaults written for it, so its drawings stay identical. Its 3D is not frozen. The two `coreLoadBearing` changes were not frozen either: that preset is from the same day. Tests: `PartialStructureTests` (16). The `finishes-by-view` scene checks the room's finishes, the skirting and a partition's door. The guide and the interface are updated in every language.

**Python scripting, stage 3: the recorder, the project's scripts, trust (decision 489, 12.11.2026).** The Script panel's Recorder tab hears every call the window makes (`EngineClient.onCall`) and writes each command as one line of Python from the catalogue: what a command made is bound to a variable, the storey is found by its name and elevation (`lapin.model.storey`), a type by its class and name (`lapin.model.find`), another element by its id, the selection as `lapin.selection()` when asked; a batch is a `with lapin.batch()` block, and an Undo takes the last step back out of the script (a Redo puts it back), so a recording runs on a new project as one undo step. Record, Stop, Copy and Save as script. A project's scripts are `LapinScript` entities of the new engine module `scripting` (`Lapin.Scripting`): `scripting.save`, `rename` and `delete` are one undo step each, refused to guests alone or in a batch; each script is also written as `scripts/<name>.py` in the .lapin (a module text part, `IPackageTexts`), other tools' files in `scripts/` stay foreign parts, and an edition without the module writes them back unchanged. A project's script never runs as the project opens; its first run, and its first after a change, asks Run once / Trust / Cancel, and trust is kept on the computer by the code's SHA-256 (`scripting-trust.json`). Approved with stage 2's open questions: the kernel's own connections (`lapinbim.Lapin()` in a script) join its run's step by a per-process token (`script.join`), others are still refused; a step inside a step joins it; `lapin.step()` in the package; the engine's and the console's messages translated in every language. Also `lapin.selection()` (the window tells the host its selection) and element proxies (`wall.height = 3`, `refresh`, `fields`). Tests: `ProjectScriptTests`, `ScriptStepTests`, `python/tests/test_steps.py`, `recorder.test.ts`, `trust.test.ts`; scene `python-recorder`; the guide's Python page in every language.

**Structural analysis, stage S1 (12.11.2026, decision 503; docs/plan-fem.md §9).** Linear static analysis of bars, Lapin's own C# solver (`Lapin.Analysis/Solver`). It uses 3D bar elements with 12 degrees of freedom, Timoshenko by default (shear areas from the sections' geometry, after EN 1993-1-1 6.2.6), rigid arms for eccentric ends and for unsymmetric sections' centroids, and releases by static condensation. Releases are the element's own `analysisReleases` (`analysis.setReleases`) or the defaults of `FrameReleases`: a connection's cleat, gusset or fly brace pins; timber roof members are pinned about the strong axis where they bear; the rest is fixed. The model is assembled with Reverse Cuthill-McKee ordering and factored once by a skyline LDLᵀ for every case. A turning that moves no node is held by a spring, and a real mechanism is reported with its node, degree of freedom and the parts that move. Reactions are R = K·U − F, and the balance is checked on every case. Load-bearing walls are line supports until S2 (shells). Loads come from S0. Slabs carry theirs to beams and walls by the 45° rule, and framed roofs to their rafters by tributary width on each real roof plane (snow per horizontal projection). Point and line loads on a slab with no bar under them go to its corners by statically consistent weights. Combinations are built by superposition, with ULS and SLS envelopes. Results are derived and cached in memory by revision: `analysis.run`, `analysis.results` (displacements, reactions, N/Vy/Vz/T/My/Mz at 11 stations with exact extremes, deflection) and `analysis.diagram` (world polylines). The structural IFC view writes releases as `AppliedCondition` and eccentric ends as `IfcRelConnectsWithEccentricity`. In the interface, Run analysis opens the results dialog (balance per case, refused bars, the mechanism with the elements to select, the maxima). A 3D card draws the diagrams, the deformed shape and the reactions, its labels kept apart. A bar's Analysis section takes its releases and shows its extremes. A new `overlays3d` hook on UI modules lets a module draw in 3D. The solver was checked against closed forms (cantilever, simply supported and continuous beams, portal, torsion, releases, offsets, shear) and, at development only, against PyNite (1e-15). Timings: 26,460 DOFs in 14 s. Tests: `FrameSolverTests`, `LoadMappingTests`, `AnalysisRunTests`, `FramedRoofLoadTests`, `StructuralIfcTests`. The user ruled out any edition or module under another licence (no LGPL solver), so the solver stays Lapin's own code. Scene `analysis`; the guide's Analysis page in every language.

**Lapin Extensions, stage 1: store.lapinbim.com, signing, the legal drafts (decision 504, 12.11.2026).** The catalogue gets its web face and its author workflow; nothing is deployed yet, and the steps are in `deploy/store/README.md`. The store's pages are generated from the catalogue by `lapin-extensions/site/build.mjs` (Node, no dependencies): the list with search and kind filters (it works without JavaScript), a page per extension at `/extension/<id>/` (description, screenshots, versions read from the manifest's git history, the licence marked permissive / read its terms / non-commercial, the Lapin versions, size, SHA-256, author, homepage, forum topic, how to install it, the store's download count), author pages, Publish and the legal drafts, in Lapin's paper and graphite looks, English first with its texts in one strings file. A `lapin://extensions/<id>` deep link was not made: it needs `tauri-plugin-deep-link` (and `single-instance`), a new Rust dependency and the scheme registered in every platform's package, so the page says File → Extensions…, search the name, and copies the id. The store service, `deploy/store/` (built like `deploy/feedback`: Node 22, no dependencies, JSON files under a lock, the `lapin-store` container on 127.0.0.1:3465), serves the pages, the catalogue (lapinbim.com/extensions/* is routed to it), and the files authors upload. Sign-in is DiscourseConnect with forum.lapinbim.com as the identity provider: the forum id, user name and display name are kept, the e-mail never, and sessions live in memory for 14 days. The author dashboard submits an extension or a new version: the manifest's fields, the file checked by the catalogue's own `checkPackage` (a copy of `tools/lib.mjs`, compared by the test), up to six screenshots checked by their bytes, and the author terms accepted. Send then opens a pull request in `lapin-extensions` with the manifest and the screenshots, and the dashboard follows its state and checks. The packages are hosted by the store (`store.lapinbim.com/packages/…` is the manifest's `package.url`, served only as a sandboxed download) rather than in GitHub releases: the author needs no GitHub account, and the file is exactly the one checked. An id belongs to the first author whose pull request is merged. Downloads go through `/api/download/<id>/<version>`, which counts them per extension, version and month and keeps nothing about who, then redirects to the copy. The installer now tries that address first, then the author's file, then lapinbim.com's copy (`packageSources`), and takes the first that gives the right bytes. Signing: the store has its own Ed25519 key, separate from the updater's (`scripts/store-key.mjs`: an encrypted `~/.lapin/store-key.pem`, its password in the keychain item `lapin-store-key`; not made yet). `tools/publish.mjs` signs each package (`package.signature`, over its id, version, SHA-256 and size) and the index (`index.json.sig`) on the Mac, copies the packages after checking them, builds the pages and uploads with rsync. Lapin verifies both (`signature.ts`: WebCrypto's Ed25519, else its own RFC 8032 verifier in BigInt, which passes the RFC's vectors) against `STORE_KEYS` in `keys.ts`. While that list is empty signing is not live. Once a key is listed, Lapin's own catalogue is refused unsigned or wrongly signed, and so are its unsigned packages. A catalogue of one's own may be unsigned, and Browse warns above the list, but never wrongly signed. The manifest gains `screenshots`, `author.forum`, `package.download` and `package.signature` in Lapin, the catalogue's rules and its schema. The legal drafts are in `lapin-extensions/legal/`, in English with Romanian copies, marked as drafts for a lawyer: terms of use, author terms, a privacy notice (GDPR) and notice and action (DSA). The user's decision on stage 2 is recorded in `plan-extensions.md` §11. Paid extensions are sold by their authors through their own seller, at 0 % commission, with a `purchase` field proposed. Lapin's premium modules become a separate local-plus-server platform rather than store items. Tests: `signature.test.ts`, `pnpm test:store` (16), the catalogue's `npm test` (12); the `extensions` scene checks the unsigned warning; the guide's Extensions page is updated in every language.

**Python scripting, stage 4: tools and dialogs (decision 489, 12.11.2026).** Scripts now reach users who never open the Script panel. `lapin.ui.ask(title, fields)` shows a dialog of the window's own, built from a field list (a number with its unit, a text, a choice, a checkbox, an element picked in the model or taken from the selection, a storey, a type of a class), and returns a dict, or None on Cancel; nothing of the script is drawn or run in the window. It is the host's `script.ask` / `script.answer`: the first window to answer wins and the others close their dialog, the asking connection is not held meanwhile, a Stop takes the question away and stops the script (lapinbim raises KeyboardInterrupt on `{stopped}`, so it never goes on as if cancelled), and a lost connection takes its questions with it. A script becomes a tool by its plug-in header (`# lapin: name=…, version=…, tool=…, icon=…, tooltip=…, run=…, requires=scripting, lapin>=0.1.N`, read without running anything) or by `lapin.ui.tool(...)` (also a decorator, registered when the panel runs the script and said back by the kernel). Its tool goes once into My tools' group Scripts, and is offered in Customize My tools…, the quick tools and Commands… (`platform/extraTools.ts` feeds `allTools`). A click is one Run and one undo step named after the tool: the script runs as the module `__lapin_tool__`, then its function is called (without `run`, the whole script runs as `__main__`). Tools come from Mine, from the project's scripts only when trusted on this computer (an untrusted project never adds buttons; Forget trust is `script.untrust`), and from scripts installed from Lapin Extensions, whose first click asks trust by SHA-256 (Installed → Run script runs them too). Tools work with Developer extras off. Icons are Lapin's own, a lucide set or a letter. As the user answered on stage 3, a guest of a shared session can no longer delete, rename, group, copy, move or transform a project's script through the generic commands, alone or in a batch (`ScriptingModule.Before` refuses when `HostFiles.GuestCall`). The lapin-extensions example `ro.ciuntucbimstudio.pereti-perimetrali` 1.1.0 uses `lapin.ui.ask` and a tool header. Tests: `ScriptToolTests`, `python/tests/test_ui.py`, `header.test.ts`, `ask.test.ts`, `tools.test.ts`; scene `python-tools`; the guide's Python page in every language, docs/python.md.
**Structural analysis checked against ifc_structural_mechanics and PyNite (12.11.2026, decision 505).** With the user's approval, ifc_structural_mechanics (GPL-3.0; IfcOpenShell, Gmsh, CalculiX 2.23 built locally) was run as a separate program, on the developer's machine only, on Lapin's IFC Structural Analysis View, and PyNite (MIT) on the problem Lapin's solver takes. Nothing of either is in the repository: `scripts/fem-check` holds Lapin's own harness (README with the installs and the licence note, `fem_check.py`, `pynite_run.py`), and the optional test `SavDumpTests` (`LAPIN_SAV_OUT=<folder>`) writes the models (cantilever, simple beam, portal, a space frame with releases and a UPN column and the same all rectangular, the guide's floor, the steel hall with UPN purlins and the default one) with Lapin's results. It found five faults in the export and one in the solver's defaults, all fixed: profiles carried only their dimensions, which readers draw differently for sloped flanges (a UPN 160 came out 2.6 % lighter; now Pset_ProfileMechanical holds Lapin's area, second moments and torsion constant); materials did not carry the E, ν and density the analysis takes from their class (now written in Pset_MaterialMechanical / Pset_MaterialCommon, each with its source); line loads were connected to no bar (now one curve action for each bar's stretch, on that bar); releases and eccentricities were written in Lapin's local axes instead of IFC's (z on `Axis`, y = z × x), so a beam pinned about its strong axis came out pinned about its weak one; an unsymmetric section's centroid was written as an eccentricity although the profile already places it (now only joint arms are eccentricities, the bar's edge then ending at its own vertices); and a cantilever's free tip was released, which with an unsymmetric section's arm made the hall with channel purlins a mechanism (`FrameReleases`: neither end of a cantilever is released). After the fixes, on solid rectangles the reactions' sum agrees to 0.034 % (9.81 against 9.80665), each support to 0.4 %, displacements to 0.8 %, forces to 2 %; with UPN sections the same except torsion (their equivalent rectangles); on the floor the 45° rule against their plate differs by 10–17 %, as expected until S2. PyNite agrees to 1e-9 without arms and 2e-5 with them. Tests: `StructuralIfcTests` (line loads on bars, materials' values, release axes, the unsymmetric bar), `AnalysisRunTests` (the hall with UPN purlins). docs/plan-fem.md §8.7, §9.7, §9.8.

Type libraries without silent loss, instance defaults (decision 506, a Finnish tester building wall libraries for RAVA 3.5 Pro, 12.11.2026). The wall and floor types' JSON (version 2) now carries each type's parameters and the full definition of every material its layers use (hatches, fills, pens, colour, parameters), and the layers' category and extend flags; old files still import, the CSV is unchanged. Before an import, `architecture.readTypes` and `types.readPack` report each material and type of the same name that differs and what differs (`LibraryMerge`), and the dialogs (`LibraryChoices`) let the user keep, update in place (same id and GlobalId) or keep both („Name (2)”) a material, and update, keep both or skip a type, with Apply to all; updating a layered type rewrites its layer set in place and re-lays its elements through `ITypeAssigner.Updated`. Without choices both commands behave as before. The Wall and Floor types dialogs gain **Type library (.lapintypes)…** and a note on what each format carries. The empty-list report: the engine imported into a project with no wall or floor types (now tested); in the UI a failure only showed in the dialog's last line, below the fold, and `pickFile` treated a refused native picker as a cancel — now failures are notified, the picker falls back to an attached file input, the first imported type opens in the editor and a deleted drawing type no longer leaves the editor on nothing. A type's parameter can be an instance default (`instance: true`): `TypeDefaults.Settle`, run after every command, copies it onto each element that took the type in that command (one undo step), the IFC export leaves it off the type's sets, and `types.applyInstanceDefaults` fills elements placed before. The IFC export now writes parameter angles as `IfcPlaneAngleMeasure` in radians in every set, and `Pset_*Common.Status` from the phases. Tests: `TypeExchangeTests`, `TypePackTests`, `TypeDefaultsTests`, `LibraryChoices.test.ts`; scene `type-import-choices`.

**Pad footings rise to their columns (decision 507, 12.11.2026).** The user's report: pad footings floated under the columns, a pad on the Ground floor 0.9 m under its column, one drawn on the Foundations level (−1.50) as the guide said dropped to −3.30. A pad footing now has a level worked out on every ask, never stored (`PadFootings.Level`): its own top when it has one (`top`, an absolute elevation); else, following a column (`followColumn`, true unless the pad came from an IFC file), its top on the base of the column it is under (storey elevation + `baseOffset`), its bottom the steps' heights under that; else, as before, its storey's elevation (and `offset`) with its type's bottom under it. Under a column: the pad's point within the column's section outline, placed and turned, or within 1 mm of it; of the columns of any storey that hold it (their points within 3 m), the one whose base is lowest, then, as low within 1 µm, the one whose point is nearest, then the first by id. The body, the cut envelope, the analytical support (`StructureAnalytical`), the steel base node's anchors, plans, sections and the IFC export all read the envelope or the geometry, so they follow; the geometry's tracked reads (the columns' class, their storeys and types) rebuild the pad when a column moves, changes storey or base offset. A recognised pad is written with `followColumn: false` and an older import's pads (with `imported`, no flag) default to it, so an import keeps reproducing the file (test `A_recognised_pad_footing_under_a_column_stays_where_the_file_puts_it`). No `IProjectUpgrade`: the only pads that move are those under a column whose top did not meet its base, which is the reported fault; a project had no way to set a pad's level other than its type's bottom or its `offset`, both of which left the gap. Commands: `structure.setPadLevel` (`top`, or `follow: true`; one undo step), `structure.padsUnderColumns` (the given columns, else the storey's, else the storey above's when it has none; one pad under the lowest column at each point that has none, at its point and rotation, of the project's first pad type or `typeId`, in the highest storey at or under the pad's bottom within 1 mm, else the lowest; one undo step), query `structure.padLevel`. Interface: the Structure tab's Foundation group has **Pad footings under columns**; the Pad footing tool snaps to the columns of the plan's storey and of the one above (`ToolDef.snapPoints`); Properties shows a pad's level, its top and bottom, the column it follows, an own top to type and **At the column's base**. Tests `PadFootingLevelTests`, `IfcRecognitionTests.PadLevel`, `SteelNodeTests` (the anchors into a following pad, then into one with its own top), `padLevel.test.ts`; scene `structure` (the pads made by the command, `structure-pad-level`); the guide in every language.

**Jogged sections projected, as Revit draws them (decision 508, 12.11.2026; a tester's report #146: "the section cuts along the break as well… grid 4 appears offset in section").** A jogged section is now projected by default: only the pieces running the way of the first (within 1°) cut the model, each on a plane of the first piece's direction and seeing only its stretch; the jogs cut nothing and add no width, the cut passing from one parallel piece to the next at the middle of the jogs between them; everything is drawn in one frame along the first piece, so a grid stands where it crosses in plan. A line that turns back over itself is drawn developed. The view's setting **Jogged: Projected | Developed** (`view.setLine {id, jogged}`, also on `view.create`) keeps the developed drawing; curved sections and sections along or across an element stay developed. Grids take the cut whose stretch holds their crossing (`Views.CrossingX`), in both modes, which also corrects grids on a developed section's later pieces; an axis crossing only a jog is not drawn; the dashed jog lines only in developed mode; section markers in other views show the cuts only. No upgrade: projected stores nothing, so jogged sections saved before open projected and narrower by their jogs (a sheet viewport may want re-centring); Developed gives back the old drawing. `SectionPaths`, `Views`, `SectionMarkers`, `Grids`, `Underlays3d`, `sections.tsx`; tests in `SectionPathTests` (existing jogged tests set `jogged: "developed"`); docs/sections.md; scene `views` (`section-jogged-setting`, `section-jogged-developed`); the guide in English and Romanian.

**An export asks for its file name (decision 509, 12.11.2026; a tester's report #145: "the project was named 'project' even though I had saved it").** In the browser, saving a project called the engine's `project.saveBytes` with a fixed `project.lapin` before asking anything, so the engine held the project under that name and IFC and SDNF exports came out as `project.ifc`. The shared helpers in `ui/src/engine/files.ts` now ask first: `chooseTarget` uses `showSaveFilePicker` with the suggested name and extension, falling back to a small Save as… box (prefilled, the extension put back) where the picker is missing or refused (Safari, Firefox, a click gone stale), and `writeTarget` writes. `saveFile`, `exportFile` (asking before the engine works, so the click is still fresh), versions and issues, and `saveProject` go through them; the engine holds the project under the chosen name, so every export suggests it; a cancelled save no longer marks the project saved. The template and furniture library exports use `saveFile`. Desktop keeps Tauri's save dialog. Optimize IFC's batch and the CDE's file download still download directly. Tests `saveFile.test.ts`; the guide's Files page in English and Romanian.

**Stairs cut at the plan's cut plane (decision 510, 12.11.2026; a tester's report #143: "the whole stair is visible; the tread numbers overlap the walking line").** A plan now draws a stair as the convention has it: what is under the view's cut seen, a break line across the flight where the nosing line reaches the cut (30° off square; square, or radial on a spiral, where the cut falls in winders, on a landing or at a flight's first riser), the part above dashed in the overhead style, the walking line seen to the cut and dashed on to the arrow at the top; landings and winders classed edge by edge by their height; a stair wholly under the cut as before, one wholly above all dashed. The symbol is drawn by `StairMarks` from the view's own cut (`Views.Range(m, view).Cut`), so the screen, sheets, the PDF and the configurator's preview agree; `ElementGeometry.Plan` keeps the whole stair as `outline` lines for picking and snapping only. The stair resolves its pens like the drawing (overrides, object styles, the view's category, filters and phases: `StairPens`, a copy of the drawing's order; a hook in `Views` handing the cut to an element's plan symbols would share it). The type chooses, under Shape → In plan, **Over the cut plane** (dashed, not drawn, or the whole stair with no cut, the old drawing) and the **break line** (zigzag or two parallel lines). The tread numbers sit beside the walking line, a 1 mm paper gap off it (right going up, the outside of a turn, outwards on a spiral, away from the middle on a split stair), their circle no bigger than half the flight. `Stairs/StairPlan.cs`; tests `StairPlanCutTests` (the break's station, every shape at five cut heights, numbers clear of the line at 1:50 and 1:100, the drawing's layers); docs/spec-stairs.md; the guide in English and Romanian. Left: the storey above draws no "down" part, ramps are still drawn whole.

**Temporary dimensions for doors and windows (decision 511, 12.11.2026; a tester's report: "place this window's corner 1500 mm from this wall face", "its axis 2500 mm from the other window's axis").** With one door, window or bare wall opening selected in a plan, the architecture module's plan overlay (`TempDimensions.tsx`, the first user of `LapinModule.planOverlays`) draws Revit-like temporary dimensions along its host wall, on the face more meeting walls come in to (the inside of an outer wall): from each edge to the nearest face of a wall that meets or crosses the host (else the host's own end as its joins leave it), plus a dashed second row to the nearest opening of the same wall when that is nearer. The references come from a new engine query, `architecture.openingReferences {id | wallId}` (`OpeningReferences.cs`): for every straight wall of the storey whose joined footprint touches a face of the host, where its faces, its core's faces and its axis cross each host face line, metres from the host's start; the host's ends on each face (`RoomBounds.JoinedFootprint`); the other openings; a curved host answers `curved` and shows none. The pure logic (`openingDims.ts`): targets per side nearest first, a wall end that coincides with a wall face dropped, a wall the opening stands across ignored; a click on the reference end cycles near face → far face → core faces → axis (another opening: near edge → centre → far edge), on the opening's end edge ↔ centre; a value clicked becomes an input (the project's unit, sums), Enter moves the opening with `architecture.moveOpening` (one undo step, refused with the engine's notice when it would leave the wall or overlap), Escape leaves it. The padlock makes it a permanent aligned dimension (`drafting.addDimension`, ends held by the associator on what they lie on; no distance lock). The dimensions follow a grip being dragged (a new `viewport/gripDrag.ts` store set by the grip layer, the middle and a bare opening's jambs) and show, read-only, while a door or window is being placed (`usePlacing` in `platform/placement.ts`, set by the plan as it asks the placement checks). Plans only; curved walls, sections and elevations have none. Tests: `OpeningReferenceTests` (5), `openingDims.test.ts` (9); scene `temp-dimensions` (the guide's doors and windows page, both languages).

**Temporary dimensions on curved walls and in sections and elevations (decision 511, continued, 12.11.2026).** `architecture.openingReferences` now answers for a curved host: positions are arc lengths along its axis; each face's line is the arc at its offset (`PathCurve.Line`), the host's ends on it come from `PathCurve.Ends` with its joins; a straight wall meeting it is measured where its faces, core faces and axis cross each face's circle (the crossing nearest the wall, nearly tangent walls left out); `scale` gives each face's length per metre of axis (its radius over the axis's), with `centre`, `radius`, `end` and `rise`. It also gives, for any host, the storey's level and the wall's base (`levels`) and the opening's `bottom`, `top` (absolute) and the `sill` `architecture.moveOpening` takes for the element selected. `openingDims.ts` places points round the arc (past its ends too), reads a value on the drawn face (`faceScale`) and moves the opening by the axis length it makes; the plan draws the dimensions as arcs concentric with the wall, and their padlock makes an arc-length dimension (`drafting.addDimension`, dimKind arc; static, it does not follow the wall). A new module hook, `LapinModule.drawingOverlays` (`viewport/DrawingOverlays.tsx`, mounted by `DrawingView`), draws in sections and elevations: with one door, window or wall opening selected that the view draws, its wall straight and running along the view within 10° (`faceOn`: the face that looks back at the viewer), its along-the-wall dimensions over its head (`toView` through the drawing's frame) and two up its side, storey level to bottom and to top (`verticalDims`), typed to `architecture.moveOpening`'s sill (`sillFor`: moved by the difference, so the head moves the window too); their padlock makes an aligned dimension of that view. Jogged or curved sections, walls cut obliquely or seen edge-on, and curved walls in sections show none; the floor finish is not a reference (the storey's level is). Tests: `OpeningReferenceTests` (6), `openingDims.test.ts` (13); the `temp-dimensions` scene adds a curved wall and the south elevation.
**The structural analysis's gaps closed (decision 512, 12.11.2026).** The user's „mergem pe recomandarea ta - continua - cu lipsurile și cu S2”: the gaps of the structural analysis' S0/S1 closed (docs/plan-fem.md §9.9). Snow and wind from the norms: the site's values on the IfcProject (`climate`, `analysis.setClimate`, `analysis.climate`; s0,k and qb the user's, read from CR 1-1-3 and CR 1-1-4's maps, which Lapin does not ship; Ce, Ct, γIs; terrain category 0–IV, γIw), the cases they go in made in the same undo step (S undrifted and S±X, S±Y drifted; W±X, W±Y each with the roofs' suction /s and pressure /p). Snow on every roof plane by μ1(α) per m² of plan (framed roofs, roof decks, a steel hall's planes carried by its purlins through `AnalyticalRoof.Members`, uncovered floors at 0.8), drifted with 0.5 μ1 on the planes facing the wind; wind on the façades of the walls' and columns' hull (zones A–E by h/d, the lack of correlation, Figure 7.4's bands) and on flat, duo- and mono-pitch roofs (Tables 7.2, 7.3, 7.4), qp from EN's ce(z); a façade's wind carried by the beams, rails and floor edges in its plane by vertical strips, its foot's strip to the ground (`toGround`); snow arrangements and wind directions alternatives in the combinations (one of each). Rods and ties axial (`FrameReleases.Axial`, `analysis.setAxial`, Properties' Axial member): pinned at their ends, their weight at them, PIN_JOINED_MEMBER in the SAV — the hall's Ø20 bracing no longer sags 645 mm. Level beams of different depths at a column keep their levels and meet its one joint by a rigid arm (`JoinAtColumns`; no 25 mm column pieces, no tilted beams). The IFC Structural Analysis View follows the export's Exclude and Phase (`AnalyticalOptions.Exclude`). The analysis' notes and the cases' default names translated in every language (phrases and patterns, `t(name)`). Tests `ClimateLoadTests`, `AxialMemberTests`, `BeamLevelTests`, `StructuralIfcTests.The_view_leaves_out…`, `notes.test.ts`, `loads.test.ts`; scene `loads` (`loads-climate`); the guide in every language. Corrected before release, after fem-check found `space-frame-rect` beyond its tolerances (PyNite up to 99 % on end forces, ifc_structural_mechanics 6–54 % on supports): the joint's node was the column's end, which sits at the shallow diagonal's axis, so the deep beams hung 10 cm under it with their own ends on the column's axis, where Gmsh joined them to the column (losing a beam's strong-axis hinge) and PyNite cut the column (its end forces then the 10 cm piece's); the solver itself was right. Now the node is at the deepest beam's level, the column's end (and a column meeting it there) moves to it and the shallower beams rise on arms above the column's head; `pynite_run.py` keeps each bar whole. space-frame-rect: supports under 0.34 %, nodes under 1.4 %, PyNite under 1.3e-6. A guard on framed roofs: the load given to the members against q × the strips' area, said in the notes beyond 1 % (every language); `BeamLevelTests`, `FramedRoofLoadTests` (an L roof whose rafters miss each other by millimetres) (docs/plan-fem.md §9.9, 4).
**Structural analysis, stage S2a: shells and the sparse solver (12.11.2026, decision 513; docs/plan-fem.md §10).** The library behind S2, Lapin's own MIT code written from the published literature, with no code or dependency from any FE or sparse library. It goes in `Lapin.Analysis/Solver`, and its wiring into `analysis.run` is S2b.
- **Shell element** (`ShellElement`): a flat element of 4 or 3 nodes with 6 degrees of freedom a node. Bending is MITC4 on quadrilaterals (Dvorkin and Bathe 1984–85) and MITC3 on triangles (Lee and Bathe 2004), so thick and thin plates take the same element without shear locking. The membrane uses Allman's interpolation with a true drilling rotation tied by the Hughes–Brezzi penalty (γ = G, after Ibrahimbegović, Taylor and Wilson 1990), so no fictitious θz spring is needed where shells meet bars or each other. Loads are consistent nodal shares. Stress resultants (N, M, Q) come at the centre and the corners.
- **Results at nodes** (`ShellSmoothing`): the corner mean, and Zienkiewicz–Zhu patch recovery fitted on the element centres (Barlow points). At a clamped edge the recovery is 0.9 % from theory where the corner mean is 12 % off.
- **Solver**: `FrameSolver` takes shells next to bars (`FrameProblem.Shells`, `CaseLoads.Surface`, `CaseResult.Shells`, `ShellResult.Combine`), on shared nodes, with reactions, balance, mechanisms and springs as before. It gained iterative refinement, which keeps the balance at 1e-9 on badly conditioned models whatever the elimination order.
- **Mesher** (`ShellMesher`): the convex plane pieces of `ShellPieces` become quadrilaterals. Edges are cut first at hard points (bar nodes, other pieces' corners), then into equal parts no longer than the target size, keyed by their end nodes, so neighbouring pieces and bars share nodes (`ShellMesh.NodesAlong`). A four-sided piece with matching opposite sides is meshed by transfinite interpolation, all quadrilaterals. Any other piece gets a Delaunay triangulation (Lawson flips), safe Laplacian smoothing and best-first pairing into quadrilaterals, which puts 86–91 % of the area in quadrilaterals.
- **Sparse factorization** (`SparseCholesky`): supernodal left-looking Cholesky (Ng–Peyton, Davis). Equations are grouped by node and ordered by approximate minimum degree (Amestoy–Davis–Duff) or by nested dissection (George), whichever predicts less work. Supernodes come from the postordered elimination tree. Large blocks run in parallel on desktop and serially in the browser. It replaces the S1 skyline as `FrameSolver`'s default (`FactorKind.Skyline` stays to compare), with the same results to 1e-9. Frames of 23,814 / 80,724 DOFs take 0.73 s / 4.0 s, against 4.3 s / 76 s for the skyline, and the factor needs 403 MB against 1.1 GB. A clamped plate of 133k / 192k DOFs takes 3.6 s end to end.
- **Tests against closed forms**: simply supported and clamped plates (Timoshenko and Woinowsky-Krieger) converge to 0.1 % at 32 × 32. Thin plates do not lock up to a/t = 10,000. A cantilever wall in its plane agrees with Timoshenko's beam to 0.4 % at 8 × 32, and Scordelis–Lo's roof gives 0.3014 against 0.3024. MacNeal–Harder patch tests pass exactly, every element has six rigid-body modes and no spurious one, and a slab on beams on rigid arms balances and shows composite action. The solver matches dense factorization and the skyline.
- **Development cross-check**: `scripts/fem-check/pynite_plate.py` compares against PyNite's DKMQ quadrilaterals; on these plates Lapin is closer to theory.
- **Tests and the S2b spec**: `ShellElementTests`, `ShellMesherTests`, `SparseCholeskyTests` (big frames and plates timed with `LAPIN_FEM_BENCH=1`). §10.10 specifies S2b: meshing slabs and walls in the analysis, bars cut on the mesh nodes with offset_z as rigid arms, loads on elements with the 45° rule retired, `analysis.contours`, contours in 3D and plan through the visibility resolver, and the guide.

**12.11.2026 — Structural analysis S2b: slabs and walls as shells (decision 514).** `analysis.run` meshes concrete slabs and walls with an E into MITC4/MITC3 shells (0.5 m by default, at least four elements across a narrow piece; `analysis.setOptions {shells, meshSize}`, one undo step, in the results dialog), the bars' and the pieces' nodes hard points; timber floors keep the 45° rule, masonry walls stay line supports (with the mesh's nodes along them). Bars along the mesh are cut at its nodes after their loads are mapped, releases kept at their ends, beams under a slab on rigid arms (OffsetZ, a T beam), and recomposed whole for stations and diagrams (`Members`, `PieceMap`). A shell wall's foot takes its footing's support. Area loads go on the elements (uniform where whole, by shape functions where partly covered), line and point loads by shape functions, self weight on slabs and walls, façade wind on the shell walls in its plane. `analysis.results` gives each surface's extremes (smoothed by patch recovery), `analysis.contours` each surface's mesh and nodal values. In the UI: banded contours on both faces in 3D (`overlays3d` now get the scene), a legend with the sign (a slab's moments bottom-tension positive), the quantity row "Slabs and walls", the largest values in the dialog, and the slabs' bands on their storey's plan (screen only; sheets later). The SAV export places a beam laid in a slab by its profile's Position. Tests: `ShellAnalysisTests` (Navier's plate, mesh convergence, the guide's frame against the 45° rule, a flat slab, a timber floor, masonry walls, concrete walls on footings, contours, options; a large frame timed on request: 223,260 dofs in 5.7 s), `shells.test.ts`; against ifc_structural_mechanics the guide's frame now agrees to 5–9 % on supports and under 5 % on displacements (was 10–17 %). The guide page in every language, scene `analysis` (analysis-slab, analysis-legend). Details in `docs/plan-fem.md` §10.13.


**User-configurable symbols, tables, conversions, line types, rule packs, classifications, formulas, bar shapes and models (decision 515, docs/plan-user-extensibility.md).** Asked by the user on 12.11.2026 with a standing rule: whatever users can configure themselves by drawing or by formulas and live text gets that way too, beside the coded one, and the coded one stays the built-in default with the same output. 2D symbols of the user's own (`Lapin.Platform/Symbols/`): a `LapinSymbol` is a title block's items plus circles and arcs in mm, its insertion point at the origin, annotation or model scale, with attributes; twelve built-ins as data; instances (IfcAnnotation `SYMBOL`) on views and sheets, turned, scaled and flipped across x or y with their texts kept readable left to right or bottom to top (`SymbolRender.LayText`); drafting saved as a symbol and a symbol exploded back; `.lapinsymbols`. Formulas `{= … | format}` in any live text (`Expr`, `LiveText`): a bounded declarative language, never code. The views' marks are slots (`SymbolSlots`): section heads, elevation marks, level markers, grid bubbles, site north arrows, callout heads and view titles take a symbol chosen on the mark or the project, reading the mark's tokens; none chosen draws exactly as before. The table import (`TableImport`): targets registered by modules, headings guessed in several languages with their units, a preview row by row, one undo step through the modules' commands; structural loads (any unit, missing cases made), load cases, property values, classification items and codes. Convert drafting to… (`LineworkConversion`, the modules' converters): walls, floors, rooms, railings, beams, footings, columns and bars (straight, L, U and stirrup recognised) from drafted shapes or a map of a DXF's layers; a DXF's blocks kept as model symbols. Line types of the project's own read from `.lin` and named everywhere a line type is; the drafting standards as a `.lapinstandards` library with choices. Rule packs (`RulePacks`): Lapin's own, Romania (indicative), holds the code's stair, reinforcement and roof values; a project's copy drives new stair types, new bars and the roofs' least pitch, and the check lists what differs. Classifications and keynotes (`Classifications`) with tag tokens and IfcClassificationReference in the export; shared parameter definitions (`.lapinparams`); parameters worked out by formulas (`ParamFormulas`, never stored). Bar shapes as data in the open `rebar` module (`RebarShapeData`: legs whose lengths and turns are parameters or formulas; a bar keeps its definition under a content code), the bending schedule with A, B, C and the BVBS BF2D export after the public guideline 3.1 (`RebarBvbs`). Furniture models of the user's own from GLB (`UserModels`, carried by the furniture library) and a symbol for a furniture type's plan. Interface: Manage → Symbols…, the symbol editor, Save as symbol…, the Symbol tool, marker symbols in the drafting standards and Properties, Import table…, Convert drafting to… and Convert layers…; the rest of the interface (line types, standards library, rule packs, classifications, formulas, bar shapes, models) follows. Tests `SymbolTests`, `SymbolSlotTests`, `TableImportTests`, `LineworkConvertTests`, `DxfBlockSymbolTests`, `StandardsLibraryTests`, `RulePackTests`, `ClassificationTests`, `ParamFormulaTests`, `RebarShapeDataTests`, `UserModelTests`; the `symbols` scene.


**The unified library and families (decision 515, continued, 13.11.2026).** Firm folders for every kind of library (File → Preferences → Libraries; `library.readFolder` and `library.writeFolder` in the host, the host's only), read and saved by each library dialog through its own importer; the Catalog's From the libraries (Lapin's, this computer's, the firm's, the extensions') with Add to project; Save to library at the type dialogs; extension kinds symbols, lineTypes, standards, rules, barShapes, parameters, mapping and families, checked in Lapin and in the catalogue's CI alike. Families (`Lapin.Studio/Families.cs`): a light parametric object of the user's — parameters and bodies whose corners, depths, offsets and conditions are formulas of them, objects with their own values, turned, mirrored and moved as any element, exported as the class the family names — and made from a sketch, its width, height and depth parameters its corners follow. Schema-driven forms were left where they are (gates, fans, families): the hand-written configurators stay, a new one is made from a schema. Tests `FamilyTests`, `FirmFolderTests`; scenes libraries-firm and families.
Mouse navigation styles (decision 516, a tester's report asking for Revit's controls and a selection window in 3D; UI only). `ui/src/app/navStyle.ts` is one table of data: for each preset (Lapin, Revit-like, AutoCAD-like, ArchiCAD-like, SketchUp-like, Blender-like, Rhino-like) which button and modifiers orbit, pan and zoom the 3D view, which pan the 2D views, how a click or a region joins the selection (replace, add, remove, toggle), the region's mode (by direction, or always crossing for Blender), the key for a part, the wheel and the middle button pressed twice. Lapin stays the default and untouched: three's orbit controls keep their mapping, and its one addition is Alt + left drag drawing the region that View → Select region already drew. In the other presets each 3D press tells the orbit controls what it does (or nothing) with its modifiers taken off the event, and a left drag on empty space draws the existing 3D window/crossing (solid and dashed, as in plans). Plans, sections, elevations and sheets read the pan buttons, add/remove keys and wheel direction from the same table. The style is a user preference (`Prefs.navStyle`), chosen from a menu in the status bar (each style with a line of its gestures) or File → Preferences → Display. Tests: `navStyle.test.ts` (the table, click and region selection, window/crossing and forced modes); the `nav-styles` scene; the Interface pages in English and Romanian.
Studio parametric parts planned (decision 517, 13.11.2026, the user's answers in its §8): `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md` writes up the user's direction after a tester asked for sketch constraints like Inventor's. A Studio part becomes a parametric part and a type from day one (the family editor and Studio are one thing), edited in an edit mode as in Blender (the part's tree, sketches edited in place in 3D with constraints, degrees of freedom and a parameter table with formulas and flex) and seen in the model only through its published parameters (values, formulas, links to the model). The constraint kinds follow FreeCAD Sketcher's; the solver is Lapin's own (damped least squares, QR for degrees of freedom and conflicts), MIT. Interop at object level: STEP AP214/AP242 solids both ways and FreeCAD `.FCStd` PartDesign Bodies both ways, written from public documentation. Stages P0, C0–C3, P1–P4, X1–X4 and an optional C5; no code yet.
Studio push/pull on a face split by lines, made clean (decision 518, 13.11.2026, a tester's report): a slanted line on a box's top, the corner (or the rest) pushed down or through, gave the right volume but zero-thickness membranes, loose edges over the corner taken away and a stray triangle on the notch floor (11 faces for 8, 9 for 7, 13 for 8). Three causes, three fixes. A boolean's tool whose faces lie on its target's is moved a hair off them (`Lapin.Geometry/Flush`: `Proud` moves a difference's or an intersection's tool faces that lie on the target's faces, facing the same way and overlapping, 0.1 mm out; `Sunk` moves a union's tool face resting on the target, within it, 0.1 mm in); the corners they share with faces that stay go where the planes meet, so the volume stays exact. Studio's live booleans (`StudioModule.Apply`) use them for every tool, and the face pushed into another element (the `prism`) is now its true size, the whole-prism nudge kept only for a pull. A reference plane's axes lose the sines' rounding (`RefPlanes.Of`: components under 1e-14 are 0), so what is drawn on a level or upright face lies on it exactly; nothing stored changes. The outline of a region pushed or pulled (the solid's sketch on the face's plane) is not drawn in 3D, worked out on every ask (`StudioModule.ConsumedOnFace`: a draft on a face's plane that a Studio solid is made from), so nothing hangs over the part taken away; the line drawn stays. Tests `StudioFlushCutTests` (18: the corner, through, the rest, pulled, edge to opposite edge, a pocket, a notch, two corners, offset and turned 17° and 30°, a wall face pushed in, the plane's axes, `Proud` and `Sunk` on their own): faces, no membranes, edges by Euler, volumes. The Studio guide page says where the region's outline goes, in English and Romanian.

Flush for Studio's own steps and for cut priorities (decision 519, 13.11.2026, the follow-ups decision 518 left open). The push/pull step on a Studio solid's whole face (`Features.PushPull`) no longer nudges the whole prism by 0.1 mm: the prism is the face's true size, sunk into the body where it rests on it (`Flush.Sunk`, a pull) or moved proud where its faces lie on the body's (`Flush.Proud`, a push), so the cut's sides along the body's sides leave no sliver on a turned body (a box turned 17° or 30°, its top pushed, gave 5 membranes). The same for a slice's half-space (`Proud`), an array's copies set face to face (each sunk into the one before, and the last into the first: a row turned 17° gave 2 membranes) and cut priorities (`CutPriorities.Apply`: a cutter's faces on a part's, so a strip footing whose ends lie on a floor's no longer leaves the floor's layers in several bodies, and an IFC file of that house without Lapin's intent now brings the floor in as a native slab recognised from its triangles, within the fidelity gate). `Flush`'s overlap test is a separating-axis test on the two faces' triangles in their plane (they share area when a pair overlaps by more than 1 µm across each of its six edges), so two flush faces crossing like a plus count though neither holds the other's triangles' centroids, and faces touching along an edge or at a corner do not. A corner's move is solved exactly for one, two or three planes (a least-squares whisker only when the normals lie round one axis): the whisker had bent a point midway along an edge off the line by 1e-17, enough for manifold to find the tool not closed (a sweep's top pushed after its end was pulled); and a face in line with a moved one, sharing a corner, moves with it (`Proud`), or the tool is left as it was (`Sunk`). Left as they were: walls' openings (their boxes run 5 cm past the faces, the sill on the wall's base exact and level), wall sweeps, slabs' and footings' own cutters, terrain, steel and timber (each module's own cuts, their tools through or past the faces, tested face by face), the shell's inner body (its open ends 1 mm past), a fillet's or chamfer's piece (out past both faces, not on them) and Follow Me (no boolean with its host). Tests `StudioFlushStepTests` (18: an extrusion's top split and pushed, through and pulled, offset and turned 17° and 30°; its side split by an upright line and pushed in; steps on a primitive pulled, pushed and a side pushed whole, then the face they made split and pushed; a step's top split by a square and pushed; an array face to face, square and turned; a wall turned 17° cutting a block flush with it; two flush faces crossing, an edge and a corner touching), `IfcImportTests` updated for the floor now recognised.
Studio's parts, stage P0's engine half (decision 520, 13.11.2026; docs/plan-studio-parametric.md §3.1 and §9, the user's answers that families become parts). `LapinSolidType` grows into the part: besides its name and description it may carry a part definition (`def`: the light families' schema of decision 515, its parameters also keeping `unit`, `instance`, `published`, `group` and `description` for P1's parameter table). Every other Studio solid is a one-step part with nothing changed: its recipe stays on the solid, a type without `def` is the named type it was. A placed part is an ordinary Studio solid (`Lapin.Studio/Parts.cs`): an `IfcBuildingElementProxy` of `operation: "part"` with `exportAs` the class its definition names (so it is written to IFC as that class with the part's IfcTypeProduct, and `studio.setClass` changes it as any solid's), standing at `at`, `z` above its storey, turned, maybe mirrored, with values of its own, typed by its part through `IfcRelDefinesByType`; moved, turned, mirrored and copied by `StudioPlacement` (a copy keeps its type, now for every Studio solid), raised by a move step, its Studio steps done on each body. `FamilyPartUpgrade` opens a project saved with families as parts: each `LapinFamily` a `LapinSolidType` with its id, name and definition, each `LapinFamilyInstance` a placed part with its id, point, height, rotation, mirror, values and class, one type relation per part whose GlobalId is derived from the part's. New commands `studio.createPart`, `updatePart`, `deletePart`, `placePart`, `setPartValues`, `partFromSketch`, `importPart` (a `.lapinpart`, `{format: "lapinpart", version: 1, name, def}`, or a `.lapinfamily`) and queries `studio.parts`, `partPreview`, `exportPart`, `partTree` (what the edit mode will show: the part, the origin planes, the sketches — a recipe solid's drafted profile, path and rail with their view and plane, a formula part's body outlines on their origin planes —, the steps — the operation and Studio's steps, or the bodies with their `when` — and the parameters with the instance's values); the family commands and queries stay as shortcuts with the same arguments and results, `FamilyTests` passing unchanged. `lapinpart` joins the library kinds (the engine's firm-folder list, the extensions' families kind, the files kept on this computer). The Family section of Properties shows for a placed part, finding its part through the type relation; the `families` scene counts placed parts. Tests `PartTreeTests` (5: a placed part typed by its part, copied and stepped; a recipe solid's tree; a formula part's tree; the `.lapinpart` file and a `.lapinfamily` read as a part; a project saved with families opened as parts with the same ids, bounds and volume).

Studio parts, P0's interface half (decision 520, `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md`): a Studio solid or placed part enters **part edit mode** by Tab, a double-click or Edit part (the Studio ribbon's Part group, the Properties section Part), reusing the edit scope (`ui/src/platform/scope.ts`: the rest of the model faded, not pickable, still snapped to; `tabHandlers` and `doubleClickHandlers` added so Tab still folds the panels when no module takes it); Tab, Esc, Done or Finish part leave it, not an undo step. The part's tree (`ui/src/modules/studio/PartTree.tsx`, `partTreeModel.ts`, from `studio.partTree`, refreshed on every version): Origin (the three planes shown in 3D), Sketches (a recipe sketch opens on its view, a formula body opens the part's editor), Steps (switched off, moved, removed; suppressed ones greyed with their `when`), Parameters (the instance's values edited in place; instance and published shown, editable in P1). Tests `editMode.test.ts` (14); guide page Studio parts (English, Romanian), scene `studio-part`.

An exact test that a union's tool face lies within its host's (decision 521, 13.11.2026, a follow-up to 519). `Flush.Within`, which decides whether `Flush.Sunk` may sink a tool face resting on a host face, looked at the tool's corners and its triangles' centroids only, so a tool spanning the notch of an L- or U-shaped face, or lying over a hole, with its corners and centroids all on the host, was sunk and poked through the notch or the hole. It is now exact in the face's plane: every corner of the tool in or on the host's face (outline and holes, within the 1 µm tolerance), no tool edge properly crossing an edge of the host's outline or holes, no corner of the host's strictly inside the tool's face, and the area the tool shares with the host's triangles (each tool triangle clipped by each host triangle, Sutherland–Hodgman, after a box check) all of its own but a band 1 µm wide round its outline, which catches a notch whose edges lie along the tool's. Consistent with 519's separating-axis overlap: the same triangles in the same plane basis. Tests `FlushWithinTests` (8: tools across a U's notch, three ways, not sunk and the union as before; an L-shaped tool inside an L sunk and joined into 15 faces, a square over the L's notch not sunk; a tool over a hole and over a strip of it not sunk, one beside it sunk; a tool equal to the host's face, a box and an L, sunk and joined into one body; each square and turned 17°); the old test missed the notch strip and the hole strip.

The Wall types dialog reads one way (decision 522, 13.11.2026, forum topic 45, testers Rychlas and Petri). The legend drew the layers reversed (the last layer on top labelled L, layer 1 at the bottom on R) while the table lists them 1 to n top-down, so the picture and the lower part where layers are made disagreed. The legend now runs as the table: layer 1 on top on the R face, the last at the bottom on the L face, the drawn-direction arrow turned to point left so that the right-hand face is truthfully the top one, the band numbers moved to the right. The faces are named **R · exterior** and **L · interior** (the letters follow the interface language, as in plan), with a note that this holds for walls drawn anticlockwise. A **Reverse layers** button runs the rows the other way after a confirmation that walls of the type swap faces once it is saved (`reverseLayers.ts`). **Open in Materials…** in the hatch swatch's panel (Wall types and Floor types) opens Manage → Materials on that material's 2D graphics tab: the single dialog slot is handled by `modules/core/dialogReturn.ts` (the dialog's editor state is kept, `useUi.materialFocus` and `returnTo` carry the target, a **Back to Wall types / Floor types** button returns and the dialog takes its unsaved edits back; leaving Materials otherwise drops them). The per-layer height columns **From** / **Up to** (placeholder *top*) became **Layer from** / **Layer up to** (placeholder *wall top*) so they are no longer read as the wall's top. Interface only: the engine is untouched. New strings in all languages; `docs/user/walls.md` and its Romanian page, the `walls` scene (layer order, Reverse layers, the way to Materials and back), `wallLegend.test.ts` and `reverseLayers.test.ts`.

Studio parts, constrained sketches (decision 520, stages C0 and C1 of `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md`, engine only): **C0** keeps a sketch's constraints on a `LapinSketch` resource entity per view (`{viewId, constraints}`, saved in `.lapin`, never written to IFC; made with the first constraint, removed with the last; `Lapin.Studio/SketchConstraints.cs`). A constraint is `{id, kind, refs: [{shape, part}], value?, formula?, driving}`, its kinds FreeCAD Sketcher's (coincident, pointOn, horizontal, vertical, parallel, perpendicular, tangent, equal, symmetric, fix, distanceX/Y, distance, radius, diameter, angle, midpoint), `part` one of start, end, center, vertex:i, edge:i, whole, written one way; lengths in metres, angles in degrees signed counter-clockwise, distanceX/Y signed; a formula names the parameters of the part whose recipe uses the sketch. Commands `studio.addConstraint`, `updateConstraint`, `removeConstraint`, query `studio.constraints`, and per sketch in `studio.partTree`; a platform hook `ICommandFollowUp` carries refs through TRIM, EXTEND, FILLET, CHAMFER, JOIN, EXPLODE (corners kept by coincidences), OFFSET, delete, grip edits, move/rotate/mirror/scale and copies, or drops a constraint with its reason in the command's result (`SketchConstraints.Mapping.cs`). **C1** is Lapin's own solver (`Lapin.Geometry/Constraints`, MIT, clean-room): the unknowns are points' x, y and radii; every driving constraint gives residual rows with analytic derivatives; the system splits into clusters (union–find), each solved by damped least squares (Levenberg–Marquardt, the least-norm step, so what moves moves least) from the current geometry; the rank, from a Cholesky factorisation of the unit rows' Gram matrix taken in order, gives the degrees of freedom, the free unknowns and the redundant rows; a cluster stopped at a least-squares point names its conflicting constraints; sided constraints keep their side, an arc's sweep never passes zero, drag goals weigh 1000 times more and are solved again up to eight rounds; deterministic. **The integration** (`SketchConstraints.Solve.cs`): inside every command that changes a sketch's constraints or shapes, in its undo step, the shapes the constraints name become the solver's points and entities (a line two points, a polyline its vertices and a line per side, a circle its centre, an arc its centre, start and end; shapes are joined only by coincidences, never by sharing a position), each stored kind and pattern its solver form (degrees to radians, formulas worked out with the part's parameter defaults, driven dimensions as references, a fix held where it is at the command's start, a polyline fixed whole by its vertices), and the moved shapes go back with `Drafts.Set` (arcs counter-clockwise), so the solids built on them rebuild; the solve's own writes are marked so the follow-up never takes them for an edit. A conflicting or unsettled solve refuses the command, naming the constraints by kind and id; a delete always goes through and tells `sketch: {status, conflicting}`; removing a constraint never solves. A user's edit of some of a sketch's shapes (a grip, a move) is a drag goal on each point it moved, so it wins where the constraints allow and the rest follows; one it cannot have is refused with the constraints that hold it (a second solve with the goals as hard constraints names them); a whole sketch moved needs no solve. A tangent end to end at a polyline's corner, or between two straight ends, is refused when added (no solver equation). New: `studio.addConstraint {constraints: [...]}` (several in one step and one solve), the query `studio.sketchStatus {viewId}` (`{status, dof, freeShapes, redundant, conflicting, dimensions}`, derived on every ask, unconstrained drafts counting their freedom), `status`/`dof` per sketch in `studio.partTree`, the command `studio.dragSketch {viewId, shape, part, to}` and the query `studio.dragPreview` (the would-be shapes, nothing committed). A staircase of 100 lines and 300 constraints solves inside `updateConstraint` in about 30 ms. Tests `SketchConstraintStoreTests` (14, two adapted to the solve), `SketchSolverTests` (17), `SketchSolveTests` (11).

**Sketch constraints in the interface, C2 and C3 of decision 520 (13.11.2026).** Studio → Constraints has a CAD command for each kind (`CNHORIZONTAL`, `CNFIX`… and the smart `CDIM`, which takes what is picked for a distance, a length, an angle or a radius, then asks for a number with its unit, `=formula`, Enter for what the shape measures, or `Driven`): the part under the cursor (end, vertex, centre, edge, curve) is lit and named as it moves, the tool applies when the picks make a whole pattern of the kind (`viewport/cad/constraints.ts`, mirroring the engine's table) and stays on until Esc. Studio's plan and drawing overlay (`ConstraintOverlay`) draws a glyph for each geometric constraint (fanned out when they stand together), each dimension with its value (driven in parentheses and dashed, a formula's on hover), colours the sketch's shapes by `studio.sketchStatus` (free blue, fixed ink, conflict red, free points dotted) and the drag preview of `studio.dragPreview`; a click selects a glyph or a dimension, Delete removes them, a double click edits a dimension in place. The chip over the view says "N degrees of freedom", "Fully constrained", "Conflicting constraints" or "Redundant constraints", with the eye that hides a view's constraints (kept per view); the part tree shows each sketch's constraint count. Drafting grips of a point in a constrained sketch run `studio.dragSketch` (previewed live by `GripLayer`), and any command's `dropped` list becomes a notice. C2: `session.ts` keeps the points put down in a command with how each was found (the aim's snap, the shape it took the point from, the ortho, polar or tracking line, a typed length or angle, Ctrl or ⌘ held at the click), and after `drafting.draw`, in a sketch (a view that builds a Studio solid, has constraints, or is drawn with a role) `viewport/cad/recorded.ts` turns them into constraints sent as one `studio.addConstraint` batch (one by one if the solver refuses the batch): endpoint and centre snaps are coincident, midpoint is midpoint, on edge, quadrant and intersection are point on object, perpendicular is both, a side drawn on an ortho or polar line is horizontal or vertical, a typed length or angle a driving dimension, a rectangle two horizontal and two vertical sides, a line begun where the last ended is joined to it, a typed radius a radius; a click with Ctrl or ⌘ is free; the CNSTR toggle of the command line (the preference `constrainWhileDrawing`, on by default) turns it off. The constraints are a second undo step after the shape, since the shape's id is known only once it is drawn. Parallel and tangent are not inferred, the inference layer having neither. Tests `constraints.test.ts` (18), `recorded.test.ts` (14), five in `session.test.ts`; scene `sketch-constraints`; guide page `sketch-constraints` (English, Romanian).
A wall's top constraint (decision 523, 13.11.2026, the forum's topic #45, a tester's report approved by the user). A wall's height was a stored number (3 m by default): walls never followed the storey above nor a storey moved, and the topmost storey gave 3 m whatever its height. A wall now has `top`: `"above"` (the storey above its own: the first whose level is higher, as a stair's `TopStorey`; on the topmost storey that storey's own top, its level plus its height), a storey's id, or none (unconnected: its own `height`), and `topOffset`, added to that level. `WallTop.Height` (`Walls.HeightOf`) works the height out on every ask, the target's level plus the offset less the wall's base (its storey's level plus its base offset), and every reader of a wall's height asks it: the body and its arc, the layout the joins and the layers' extensions into floors read, the envelope that cuts lower priorities, lintels, layers' From/Up to (clamped to it), openings' fit and head limits, the opening maker, sweeps from the top, faces, the analytical model, quantities (Qto_WallBaseQuantities) and so schedules, drawings and IFC bodies, gates, balconies and their loggia's back wall (which takes its host's top), the timber frame, MEP sleeves, provisions and fans, finish walls' matching. The geometry cache follows by itself (the storeys are read as a class). A top at or below the base is refused by the commands; one that comes so later (a storey brought down), or a named storey gone, leaves the wall its stored height, never a negative body, and the wall's diagnostics say so (Problems). The stored height stays what an unconnected wall stands to: set to the worked-out height whenever a top is set, so a wall disconnected keeps the height it had. New walls (`addWall`) go to the storey above unless a top is asked (`top`, `topOffset`), a height given (unconnected at it: scripts and tests that give one keep their walls), or their type says otherwise: a wall type's `defaultTop` (`unconnected`, else the storey above), `defaultTopOffset` and `defaultHeight`, set with `architecture.setWallTypeTop`. `architecture.setWallTop` {ids, top, topOffset, height} sets it on every selected wall in one step, the openings checked to still fit; `architecture.updateWall` with a height makes a wall unconnected; `architecture.wallTop` answers the interface. Deleting a storey lets the walls going to it (named or as the storey above) stand unconnected at their height, in the same step (an `IDeletion`). Copy to storeys sends a named top as many storeys up from the copy's storey as the original's went, else unconnected at the original's height; a top to the storey above needs nothing. IFC: the body and quantities carry the worked-out height, `Lapin_Intent` carries `top`, `topOffset` (a length) and the stored height, and Lapin's own file brings the constraint back (storeys keep their GlobalIds); foreign walls stay as their file says (no top). Existing walls had no `top` and keep their stored height: no project upgrade. Guests who may edit run the commands as any other (EngineDispatcher lets every `doc.execute` through for editors). Interface: the wall's Wall section shows Top (Storey above, the project's storeys, Unconnected height), Top offset and the height worked out with the storey it goes to; the generic Height field shows only on an unconnected wall; the Wall and Curved wall tools' bar chooses the top of the walls drawn (as the type, storey above or a storey with an offset, or a height); Wall types has a small Wall top row under the lintels (`WallTypeTop`); Wall profile starts from the worked-out height. Guide: walls.md (EN, RO) section "The wall's top" with the picture `wall-top`, the `walls` scene checking the tool's option, the property and a storey raised. Tests `WallTopTests` (10): a new wall to the storey above following it raised, lowered and undone, and the lower storey lowered (base down, top kept, quantities); the topmost storey's own top and a storey added over it; a storey two up with an offset and a base offset; a top at the base refused and one brought there flagged; unconnected walls, disconnecting keeping the height, a height typed; a type's top and height; several walls in one step and undone; a storey deleted (named and above, one step); copy to storeys (above, one up, none so high); an IFC round trip (the label, the Qto height, the constraint back and still live). Not done: curtain walls keep their own height (another class); Move to storey keeps a named top as it is.

**Decision 524: the box by the cursor for a rectangle, and Rotate by Reference (forum 45, Petri).** The live input beside the cursor (`liveInput.ts`, `LiveBox.tsx`) has two modes for a rectangle tool: Length / Angle and Width / Height (`W`, `H`). Space (or the mode button in the bar) switches, Tab or a comma moves from the width to the height, Enter takes the corner, a field left empty follows the mouse, values are signed as `@2000,-1500` on the command line (a minus can start an empty field); the mode is remembered per tool (`liveBegin(..., { rect: key })`, the plan's rectangle gesture by its tool id, the CAD RECTANGLE step by `Step.rect`). Studio's sketch and 3D tool rectangles already take `w,h` in their one-field Measurements box (decision 374) and were left as they are. ROTATE (`cad/session.ts`, pure parts in `cad/rotate.ts`) gained `[Reference/Copy]`; the ribbon's Rotate and Modify → Rotate start the hidden command ROTATEREF, which opens in the Reference flow (hinge, reference point, target point; an angle typed at the reference step is the plain turn) while the typed ROTATE/RO stays AutoCAD's: Reference takes the hinge, a reference direction (point or typed angle), then the new direction; the turn is the difference (typed angles are read from the UCS axis, decision 445); Copy sends `copy: true` to `element.transform` (the engine already honours it for rotate); the selection turns in the preview and the turn shows at the cursor. The ribbon Rotate is this same command. Interface only, no engine change.
