Tests: `liveInput.test.ts`, `keys.test.ts`, `rotate.test.ts`, `session.test.ts`; `docs/user/drafting.md` and its Romanian page, the `drafting` scene (`rectangle-box`); new strings in all languages.

Forum topic 47 (tester Rychlas), analysed and approved by the user (13.11.2026), decision 525: interface only, the engine untouched. (1) Dark mode: a classless `<button>` (about 59 in the interface, e.g. the dimension section's) took the WebView's light default face under the theme's light text; `theme.css` now has a base rule `button:not([class])` (panel face, strong rule, ink, hover, active, disabled and focus states) that every class or container rule still overrides, and `.props .fields dd` wraps, so a row of buttons no longer clips. (2) Selecting in plans and drawings: the snap marker shows only while a command step wants a point (`cadStep?.want === 'point'`); the cursor is a pointing hand while nothing is drawn or a step wants a pick or a selection; the outline of what a click would take (`pick(rawHover)`, so a click selects only what the view shows, decision 498) lights under the cursor in the plan and in the drawing view (`.plan-hover`); a press that moves more than 4 px but makes a tiny box (8 px or less, `boxOutcome` in `selectDrag.tsx`) is a click at the press point, and a box that takes nothing keeps the selection. (4) Opening properties: "Along the wall" of an opening, a door or a window (`AlongField.tsx`, `openingAlong.ts`) is a distance with two choices, **Measured from** (the wall's start, the nearest face of a wall meeting the host, that wall's core face) and **Measured to** (the opening's start, centre, end), read through `architecture.openingReferences` and written through `architecture.moveOpening` (the centre), nothing stored in the model; the last choice is a user preference (`prefs.openingAlong`). (6) **Line up the axes** passes the selected axis as `to` (tolerated by an engine that does not read it yet). (7) Shift on empty space leaves the selection alone in Lapin's style, drilling or not (`pickByMods`, `pickClick`). (8) In Lapin's style Ctrl or Cmd with a region takes it out of the selection (`regionMode` in `navStyle.ts`, region only; clicks keep Ctrl for the part, Alt stays the 3D region); (8B) Esc with no step, buffer, tool or dialog clears the selection, after a drilled selection has stepped up and after a tool ended in the same press (`keys.ts`, intent `deselect`). New strings in all languages; `docs/user/interface.md`, `doors-windows.md`, `drafting.md` and their Romanian pages; the `temp-dimensions` scene takes the Along field's picture; tests `navStyle.test.ts`, `assemblies.test.ts`, `keys.test.ts`, `selectDrag.test.ts`, `openingAlong.test.ts`.

Plan views turn along the screen (decision 526, 13.11.2026, tester Petri: "I found UCS, but it doesn't rotate the screen"). A plan view keeps an **orientation** (`orientation` on the `LapinView`: the model direction in degrees shown horizontal, or `"ucs"` to follow the UCS's X axis as it changes), the step decision 447 left open. `view.setOrientation {id, angle | ucs}` sets it in one undo step (a level's default plan `plan:<storey>` becomes its plan view with it, its drafting rehomed as in decision 386; `view.create` takes `orientation` too); only plans take one (floor, ceiling, foundation, site and location plans), a section or elevation is refused. The drawing stays in model coordinates (`view.drawing` is unchanged, so picking, snaps, marks and the UI's caches need nothing new); the turn is applied where the drawing meets the screen or paper. On screen, `PlanView` turns by it through the same `toScreen`/`toWorld` pair true north up uses (decision 447), which it overrides: pan, zoom, grips, snaps, window and crossing selection, temporary dimensions all follow; the key plan draws the screen's four corners (a turned box on its north-up map); ortho, polar tracking and the grid follow the screen's axes when no UCS is set (`useCadCanvas({axes})`), typed coordinates stay in the UCS. On paper, `Sheets.Place` turns the view's drawing about the middle the viewport shows (`Placement.Turn`, `ToPaper` composed with it; the box fits the turned drawing; a boundary's box is the one round it turned), and `sheet.cropViewport`/`sheet.panViewport` read paper moves back through the turn (`Placement.FromPaper`; cropping a bound viewport of a plan turned by other than a quarter turn is refused: the boundary is square to the plan's axes). The single-view PDF/SVG page (`HtmlExport.ViewOnPaper`), document pictures (`DocumentImages`) and the DXF in the plan's own coordinates (`Views.Dxf`, internal) print it turned; the base point's and the map's DXF stay in their systems. Texts stay readable everywhere by one rule (`Turning.Upright` in the engine, `uprightText` in `orientation.ts` for the screen, true north up included): turned with the drawing, and a text that would read upside down (outside −90°…90°] is turned over on its own footprint (anchor start ↔ end, its point moved across by its height). The plan's bar has a **Rotate view** button (North up, Rotate view… by an angle, Rotate view to align with an edge, Follow the UCS, True north up), and the command line **PLAN** [Current UCS/World/Angle/Edge/True north] and **ALIGNVIEW**. Not done: tags and room labels follow the plan's turn rather than staying level on the turned view (their multi-line layouts are drawn in model axes); a site plan's scale bar turns with it; a sheet's north-arrow symbol is placed by hand. New strings in all languages; `docs/user/views.md` (Rotating a plan) and `site.md` with their Romanian pages, the `views-plan-orientation` scene; `PlanOrientationTests` (a wall at 30° level on the sheet of a plan turned to 30°, texts upright, one undo step, UCS following, pan and crop through the turn, the DXF) and `orientation.test.ts` (the screen map and its inverse, edges, upright texts, ortho on the screen's axes).
Scripts' panels, menus and palettes (decision 528, asked by the user on 13.11.2026: Python scripts registering interface elements in My tools, all from code). `lapin.ui.panel("Walls")` (a `with` block, `p.tool(…)`, or `panel=`) puts a script's tools, menus and palettes into a My tools group of its own instead of "Scripts", made when missing among the script's other panels; `size="small"` places small; the header takes `panel=` and `size=`. `lapin.ui.menu(…)` is one button whose pulldown lists its tools, each still a tool of its own; the pulldown is generic (`ToolDef.items`, `ui/src/shell/pulldown.ts`, opened under the button in the ribbon and My tools). `lapin.ui.palette(…)` is a small floating panel of the window's own (`PaletteWindow.tsx`, its reader `palette.ts`): headings, texts, separators, the fields `ask` has, buttons alone or in a row; moved by its title, its place and values kept per palette in the browser's storage. A palette's button is one Run and one undo step named after it: `script.run {tool: {name, call, palette, values}}`, the values checked by the window, limited by `ScriptHost.ToolOf` (64 keys, 64 KB), converted by the kernel as `ask` converts and given to the function when it takes a parameter (`_ui.press`). Everything travels as JSON in the existing `{type: "tools"}` message; what older builds stored still reads. Tests: Python `PanelTests` and the kernel's palette run, vitest for the placement, menus and palette rows, xUnit `A_palettes_button_carries_its_values_within_limits`; the `python-tools` scene shows the panel, the menu and the palette.

Visual graphs, stage 1 (decision 527, `docs/plan-visual-graphs.md`; the user's request of 13.11.2026 for "something like Grasshopper but friendlier", in the free edition, on the web from the start, as a side panel). A new open engine module `graphs` (`engine/src/Modules/Automation/Lapin.Graphs`, in `products/lapin.json`): `LapinGraph` entities hold a graph as JSON (format 1: nodes with their kind, place, constants and the inputs that take a list whole; wires output → input), checked when read (unique ids, one wire per input, no circle, 500 nodes, 2,000 wires, 256 KB); `graphs.save`, `graphs.rename`, `graphs.delete` are one undo step each and a copy is written as `graphs/<name>.json` in the .lapin (`LapinPackage.TextFolders`); the core's generic edits are refused on a graph, delete allowed (`IDrivenElements`). The engine evaluates the graph (`Evaluator`): its own nodes (`Builtins`: inputs, lists, maths with `Expr` formulas, points, lines, polylines, rectangles, the model's elements and storeys, a property, a filter, a watch) and the product's commands and queries as nodes (those that import, export, link, open projects or run scripts refused, and no argument naming a path), a list into a single input mapped over by the longest list (a shorter one repeats its last item, one level at a time), a failing node saying why while the nodes after it say which input has nothing and the rest goes on, 10,000 items a list, 2,000 commands and 5 s a run (60 s to bake). `graphs.evaluate` is a query: a graph that runs commands runs them on a copy of the model (a `ModelStore` kept while the model's revision stands), in one transaction given back at the end, and the elements made or changed there are built by the modules' own producers into the ghost (meshes, plan lines, the geometry nodes' points and polylines); the model is never touched. `graphs.bake` runs the same evaluation in its own command (one undo step, "Bake graph"), refused whole when a command node fails. A command node without a storey works on the view's storey (`storey` sent by the window). The interface (`ui/src/modules/graphs`): View → Graphs opens a module view beside the model (split row: the side panel on the web; on the desktop its tab goes into a window of its own, the ghost shared between windows by a Tauri event); a canvas written in SVG (no dependency): typed coloured ports, list wires drawn thicker with their count, double-click to search and add, drag to move or wire, box and Shift selection, Delete, wheel zoom, middle button or Space to pan, the graph fitted when it opens, the inspector for constants (number, text, yes/no, choice, point, storey, the selection for picked elements), errors and values; every finished change is a graphs.save, so Ctrl+Z undoes it; the ghost in every 3D view and plan (`overlays3d`, `planOverlays`). Tests: xUnit `GraphTests` (order and mapping, nested lists, errors per node, limits and refused commands, the preview leaving the model and history as they were with a ghost of three walls, Bake one undo step and refused whole, save/rename/delete, the package round trip, the nodes query), vitest `graph.test.ts` (ports from the catalogue with point pairs, search, wiring rules, editing, drawing, fit); the guide page `graphs` (English and Romanian) and its scene. The web build was not made here (no wasm-tools workload on this machine); nothing in the module is desktop-only. The user's answers to the plan's questions (13.11.2026): all as proposed, except that graphs are the host's, like scripts: `graphs.*` joins `EngineDispatcher.HostOnlyCommands` (alone or in a batch) and the new `HostOnlyQueries` (a guest neither evaluates nor lists graphs, one who looks included), `GraphsModule.Before` refuses a guest every generic command on a `LapinGraph` (delete, rename, group, copy, move), and a guest's window says why instead of opening the panel (xUnit `Graphs_are_the_hosts_a_guest_neither_runs_evaluates_nor_touches_one`).
**Decision 529: the type configurators' preview, drawn by the engine (stage 1: the engine query and walls).** Approved by the user: a type configurator shows Plan · Section · 3D as the drawings draw them, not a schematic of its own. `types.preview {class, type, sample, views, scale, detail, styleId?, layers?}` (`TypePreviews.cs`) stands the type being edited, unsaved (its create command's arguments; `id` when it edits a saved one), up in a scratch project: a `Session` of its own on the product's module catalogue, seeded with the open project's set-up (its project attributes: drafting standards, view styles, pens, hatch patterns; its resources: materials, the cut order, line types; no element, type, view or storey), a storey of the sample's height and one above it. The module's `ITypePreview` makes the type and its sample with its own commands, so the sample is built by the same code as any element: walls (`WallTypePreview`) make the type with `architecture.createWallType`, a 3 m wall drawn right to left (its R face, layer 1, on top in plan) and a 1.2 m wall of the type joined to its middle in T on the L side (`addWall` joins it), each going up as a new wall of the type does (a saved type's top defaults copied), or to `sample.height`. A section across the long wall (a fifth along, looking away from the T, R on the right) and a plan are made with `view.create`, their level of detail and style (`styleOverrides.detail`, `viewStyle`; every layer drawn unless `layers: "style"`) set, and drawn with `Views.Render` → `Views.DrawingOf`, so Visibility / Graphics, the view style, the material hatches and pens at the scale and the level of detail are the drawings' own; `Rendered.Generated` gives each cut area's part key, returned per view as `parts {key: loops}`; `mesh` holds the sample's parts tessellated with their keys and materials. The open project is only read: no entity, no undo step, its caches untouched (the scratch session is dropped). Answers are kept per project (24, by the question's JSON, cleared when its revision changes), and the seed with them. A wall's preview takes about 12–20 ms (Debug, tests), about 1 ms from the cache. The interface's `TypePreview` (`ui/src/modules/core/TypePreview.tsx`, state and helpers in `typePreviewState.ts`): the three panes side by side (tabs under 640 px), scale 1:20/1:50/1:100, coarse/medium/fine, the sample's length and height, kept on this computer for every configurator; the plan and section drawn by `DrawingLayer` (the drawing views' renderer, the graphite theme's ink), the 3D in a small three.js canvas with orbit; asked 220 ms after the last edit, the last answer shown meanwhile. In Wall types it sits under the legend (kept), names R · exterior (top in plan, right in section) and L · interior, and a hovered layer row lights its layer in the plan, the section (its cut areas) and the 3D (emissive). Tests: `TypePreviewTests` (5: the three layers hatched per layer at 1:50 in plan and cut in section at their thicknesses, a closed mesh per layer; the project unchanged; the cache; a refused type's problem; coarse draws fewer outlines), `typePreviewState.test.ts`; `docs/user/walls.md` and its Romanian page, the `walls` scene (`wall-type-preview`); new strings in all languages; the command catalogue. Next stages: floors, ceilings, roofs (a slab or roof sample: a plan and a section across its layers, a sloped roof's section along the fall); columns, beams and curtain walls (a member's sample on a storey, its profile at LOD 400; a curtain wall's grid in a short run with a corner); stairs, joinery, casework and the rest as tabs, their bespoke previews (`stairPreview`, `curtainWallPreview`, `joineryPreview3d`, `roofPreview`…) moved onto `types.preview`.

**Decision 530: garage doors (forum topic #48, a tester in Australia: "a steel sectional panel lift door").** A garage door is a door type whose one panel has a garage door's operation, added to `Joinery.DoorOperations`: `sectional-overhead`, `roller`, `up-and-over`, `sectional-side-left` / `-right`; its settings are `JoinerySpec.Garage` (`JoineryGarage`: panels, null from the height at about 610 mm; finish ribbed, flat, V-groove or glazed top; a wicket door and its side; the track standard, low headroom or high lift with its extra lift; headroom, backroom and sideroom, null the kind's indicative values; a roller's box inside or outside; an up-and-over door's canopy or retractable mechanism; tracks in 3D). `GarageDoors.cs` owns the rules (`Problem`: one panel filling one column and row, framed, a rectangle), the body (the frame without a bottom member; a sectional door's panels behind the frame with ribs, grooved boards or a band of windows, the wicket's frame and handles, two tracks up the sides, a four-piece bend and back under the ceiling to the backroom with a cross angle; a roller's slat curtain, bottom rail and roll box; an up-and-over panel with its T handle and its arms or tracks; a side-sliding door's vertical panels and its track round the corner along the side wall), the plan (the curtain cut, a line at LOD 100, and dashed overhead what CAD libraries draw: the tracks to the backroom with the door open under the ceiling, the roll box, the open panel, the track round the corner and the parked door), the elevation (joints, ribs, windows, the wicket, the slats, the box, the way it runs), the schedule's Opening and Hanging words and the quantities (`Lapin_GarageDoor`: curtain area, panels, headroom, backroom, sideroom). The presets (`JoinerySpecs`): sectional and roller 2400 × 2100, up-and-over and side-sliding 2500 × 2125, side-hinged doors (the double door at 2400 × 2100); the catalogue lists the common sizes, Australia's 2400–5400 × 2100 and Europe's 2375 × 2000 and 2500–5000 × 2125, marked indicative. A garage door hangs behind its opening: `JoineryPosition` gains `"interior"` (the frame's inner face that far from the wall's interior finish), which the presets use. IFC, checked against buildingSMART's IFC4.3 sources (IFC4 has ROLLINGUP, IFC4X3 adds LIFTING_HORIZONTAL, LIFTING_VERTICAL_LEFT/RIGHT, DOUBLE_DOOR_LIFTING_VERTICAL; IfcDoorPanelOperationEnum has no lifting value): a roller door ROLLINGUP in both schemas; a sectional or up-and-over door LIFTING_HORIZONTAL in IFC4X3 and USERDEFINED with SECTIONAL_OVERHEAD or UP_AND_OVER in IFC4; a side-sliding one USERDEFINED SECTIONAL_SIDE_LEFT/RIGHT in both; panels ROLLINGUP, SLIDING or USERDEFINED. `IfcJoineryType.Operation4X3` and `In(ifc43)` give the export the operation per schema (the user-defined text only with USERDEFINED). Lapin's own file comes back whole through `Lapin_Intent`; `JoineryIfc.FromIfc` reads ROLLINGUP, LIFTING_HORIZONTAL, Lapin's names (and a few common ones) and a door whose only panel rolls up as a garage door, so recognition of foreign doors makes them too. The configurator: + New garage door, + New side-hinged garage doors, a Garage door section (kind, common size, finish, panels, track and lift, wicket, roll box, mechanism, the three clearances with Auto, tracks in 3D), From the interior finish in Position in the wall; strings in every language; Ask Lapin's `garage-doors` entry; the guide's Garage doors section (EN, RO) with the `garage-door` scene (the configurator, the plan, 3D). Tests: `GarageDoorTests` (closed bodies and walls for every kind, panels and tracks to the backroom, the plan's dashed tracks, IFC per schema and read back, the schedule and quantities, the presets), `garageDoors.test.ts`, the FAQ. Open: the IFC4X3 reading of LIFTING_HORIZONTAL as a door that lifts and lies flat overhead is ours (the specification gives only the name); the opener's motor and rail are not drawn; tracks are bodies in 3D whatever the view's LOD (a type setting turns them off).

Moved and copied walls join what they meet; axes line up to the one selected (decision 531, 13.11.2026, forum topic 47, tester Rychlas, items 9 and 6). A wall copied so its end landed on another stayed unjoined: `element.transform` only copied the joins among the copied walls and never called `Joins.Auto`, which only drawing and an edited wall's ends ran. Now `ElementTransform.JoinWhatMeets` runs after every move, turn, mirror and copy (the command, so the 2D and 3D drag paths too) and after an array's items are placed (`Arrays.Place`, made or updated): each path element carried (walls, beams, strip footings, through `IPathElements`) has its joins with what stayed worked out again (`Joins.Refresh`, a join that no longer meets goes, as `updateWall` does) and joins what it now meets (`Joins.Auto`), in the same undo step. What was carried together keeps its joins among them as they were (`Joins.Auto`'s new `skip`), so a pair left unjoined stays unjoined and a copy in place joins nothing; an array's items (the original among them) and the path it follows count as carried together, so the items join what they land on outside the array but never each other or their path, and each stays where the rule puts it (`ArrayArrangementTests` keep their numbers). An apartment type's inner walls are left to their instances' own stops (`Components.UnitMembers`, docs/plan-units.md), as before. A wall recognised from an IFC (its `imported` record) moved keeps the file's joins and gets none of Lapin's (the import reproduces the file); a copy of it is the user's own new element and joins. `Joins.Auto` now tries only the elements whose axis comes within six times the thicker's thickness (a join needs two; the rest covers an end an earlier join moved), with thicknesses kept across one command: moving 200 of 400 walls costs about 50 ms more. Grid axes: `drafting.alignAxes` takes `to` (an axis's tag, or its id `grid#tag`): the axes of its family (those in `tags`, when given) take that axis's start and end lines and it stays as it is; without `to` the family's envelope as before; an unknown tag is refused. The Properties button passes the selected axis (decision 525, the interface's). Command catalogue regenerated. Tests: `TransformJoinTests` (the T from the picture, an L corner, a move that drops a crossing and makes a T, rotate and mirror, carried together, an array, beams and strip footings, one undo step), `IfcRecognitionTests.A_recognised_wall_moved_joins_nothing_new_and_its_copy_joins_as_the_users_own`, `AnnotationTests.Axes_line_up_to_the_one_named_and_it_stays_as_it_is`. Guide: `walls.md`, English and Romanian (the grid button's page was written with decision 525; no scene shows either).

Forum topic 47 (Rychlas), points 3 and 5 (decision 532, 13.11.2026), interface only. A length, angle, width or height typed in the box by the cursor is no longer ignored by a click: `liveTaken` (`liveInput.ts`) gives the point the preview shows, the click giving only the direction (or the sign of an empty field), and the plan, the drawing views and the CAD steps use it; a click with nothing typed, Esc and the grips are as before. With every snap on, `nearest` no longer beats the grid: `resolveAim` slides the point along the snapped shape to the nearest grid line (one axis the object's, the other the grid's), in the UCS or the turned screen's frame, while endpoint, midpoint and intersection still win. Drafting page in both languages; `typedClick.test.ts`.

List nodes for the visual graphs (decision 533, a follow-up to 527). The Lists group gains List (a list typed by hand: items between semicolons or on lines of their own, a decimal comma accepted; Kind Auto gives numbers when every item is a finite number, else texts; Numbers and Texts force it), Join lists (A to D taken whole, one after the other; a single value is a list of one, so it also gathers wired values into a list; an empty input is left out), Sort (numbers by size, texts in natural order ignoring case; By sorts by another list's values place for place, Descending, a stable sort with nothing last; Order gives the original indices), Part of a list (Start and Count; a negative start counts from the end, no count runs to the end), Unique (first-seen order with How many; numbers and points equal within 1e-9, texts exactly) and Every with every (A repeated, B cycled and the pair count, so that the next node runs on every combination; more than 10,000 pairs fails on the node). The rules are in `Lapin.Graphs/Lists.cs`; `GraphTests` covers each node (empty lists, mixed kinds, the limits, walls sorted by height, negative slices, every combination of X and Y made as walls by a command node); the labels are in every language; the user guide's List nodes section (EN, RO).

Graphs read the model's geometry as references (decision 534, a follow-up to 527). The Model group gains Axis (walls straight and curved, beams at their top, strip footings, railings, every axis of a grid, steel members' sticks; arcs through `Chords`), Outline (the closed `contour` of floors, rooms, roofs and plantings at their level), Location (`at` and `rotation`; doors and windows on their wall's axis at their sill, turned with it), Bounding box (from the geometry the engine builds, on the model being evaluated) and Drafting geometry (lines, polylines, rectangles, arcs, circles and a DXF's linework, on their view's plane as the 3D view places them, through the new platform service `IDraftedGeometry` that the drafting module implements). Each takes an element or a list; one of the wrong kind fails on its item with a reason. `NodeCall.Query` gives nodes the geometry and services of the model the run reads, so the preview follows a moved element. Tests in `GraphTests`: every node, curved walls, drafted arcs and circles at an upper plan's level, walls along a drafted rectangle and columns at a drafted line's division points baked, a moved wall moving what the graph makes. Strings in every language; the guide's Reading the model section (EN, RO).

Graph fixes found while filming (decision 535). A command's list argument (Pad footings under columns given 12 picked columns answered „11 of 12 failed”, run column by column) is taken whole by the engine whatever the graph noted: `Evaluator.ListArgument` names the arguments the commands read as lists (ids, points, pts, shapes, every …Ids), and such an input maps only over a list of lists, a list of [x, y] points staying one list; the interface gives the catalogue's untyped ones a list port too. A node's input row shows a chosen storey by its name and picked elements by their count, cut to the room before the output label on its row. The canvas has a Fit button (F, Home) and fits again as the panel is resized until the user pans or zooms. The inspector writes numbers with a point and reads them with the app's number rule (a decimal comma, sums), as the nodes show them. The 3D pane's blank frame while dragging the divider comes from `Viewport3D` (the renderer resized in a ResizeObserver callback, drawn only on the next animation frame): noted, not changed here.

**Sketch constraints: one undo step while drawing, dimensions placed (decision 520, 13.11.2026).** A shape drawn in a sketch and the constraints recorded for it (C2) are now one command, so one Undo takes both: `studio.drawConstrained {viewId, layer?, role?, shapes, constraints}` runs `drafting.draw` inside its own command context (as `edit.batch` does; Drafting still knows nothing of Studio), then adds the constraints, whose refs name the new shapes as `{new: i, part}`, and solves once. A batch the solver refuses is taken back and tried one by one in the same step; what does not hold is reported (`refused: [{index, kind, reason}]`) and never refuses the drawing. The interface sends it only when something was recorded and falls back to `drafting.draw` where it cannot run; Ctrl for a free point and CNSTR are unchanged. A dimension can carry `place` (`SketchConstraints.Place.cs`, §4.2 of the plan): `{offset}` for a distance (signed, across its direction, left positive) and an angle (its arc's radius), `{offset, angle}` for a radius or diameter (beyond the rim, on a ray), set by `studio.updateConstraint {id, place}` (one step; null back to automatic), carried by a move, turn, mirror, scale or copy of all its shapes and otherwise kept relative to what it measures. In the view a dimension is dragged by its value or its line (previewed, set on release); one not placed stands outside its shapes, away from their middle, clearing a shape small on screen whole (`autoPlace`). Tests `SketchDrawPlaceTests` (5), `constraints.test.ts`, `recorded.test.ts`, `session.test.ts`; the guide's page in English and Romanian. Open: no command to return a dragged dimension to automatic from the interface (the engine takes `place: null`); the guide's scene does not show a drag yet.

Constraints UI fixes (decision 520, stage C). Parallel and tangent are now inferences while drawing: `ui/src/viewport/cad/relations.ts` holds a line to a drafted side near the cursor when its direction is within 3 degrees (a Parallel hint, a dashed guide from the side), and to the tangent of an arc or circle when the base lies on it, or at the point where a line from the base just touches it (a Tangent hint); `resolveAim` takes them after the object snaps and before the ortho and guide lines, never with ortho on, and `Aimed.lock`/`Inference.to` carry the side or curve into the trail. `recorded.ts` turns them into `parallel` [L,L], `tangent` [L,C] (with `pointOn` when the end lies on the curve), and `tangent` [P,P] when a line begins at an end of an arc; a rectangle typed as `@w,h` also records its two sides as driving distances. The Constraints group moved to third place in the Studio ribbon (after Solids and Thick; the Sketch group is 909 px wide, so it ends at 1379 px of 1440), and Coincident has its own icon (`coincident` in icons.json). The constraint tools' command-line prompts, refusals, input errors and part labels go through `viewport/cad/prompts.ts` (`tp`, `orList`, on `t()`) and are in every language; keywords (Driven, X, Y, D, R) and command names stay. Tests: `relations.test.ts`, `prompts.test.ts`. Open: an arc drawn from a line's end tangentially (the three-point ARC has no tangent hint yet), a typed arc radius, and the live rectangle box (width and height typed in the field by the cursor) record no dimensions; only the command line's `@w,h` does.

**A part's formula corners become constraints (decision 520, §9 point 2 of `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md`, 13.11.2026).** A part definition may now hold sketches of its own (`def.sketches`: `{id, name, plane, offset?, shapes, constraints}`, shapes in the part's coordinates, constraints in the sketch store's schema with refs naming the sketch's shape ids, formulas naming the part's parameters), and a body may take its outline from one (`{sketch, shape?, holes?: [shape ids], depth, …}`) instead of `points`; bodies of `points` stay as they were. The stored shapes are the sketch solved with the defaults; each placed part solves it again with its own values by Lapin's solver, cached per sketch and per set of the values its formulas name, so instances sharing values never solve twice. A formula corner is a driving `distanceX` plus `distanceY` from the sketch's origin (FreeCAD's Lock), not a `fix`, which would hold the point where the solve starts against the formulas. `studio.constrainPart` converts a part's `points` bodies to such sketches with the same bodies and volumes; nothing is converted on opening (no `IProjectUpgrade`), and `familyFromSketch`/`partFromSketch` still make `points` bodies, since the Families dialog edits corners. `studio.partSketchAddConstraint`, `partSketchUpdateConstraint`, `partSketchRemoveConstraint` and `partSketchSetShapes` edit an own sketch (one undo step each, solved with the defaults; a conflict, or a placed part's values that would no longer build, refused; constraints a shape edit leaves without their shapes dropped and listed); `studio.partTree` shows each own sketch with its solved shapes, resolved constraints (`value`, `measured`), `status`, `dof` and free points. The store's checks and solver translation are reused on shapes that are not drafted entities (`SketchConstraints.Local.cs`, a new partial file, so the view store is untouched); the part side is `PartSketches.cs`. IFC is unchanged: the solved bodies go out, the definition (with its sketches) rides in `Lapin_Intent` as before. The Families dialog's model keeps `sketches` when it saves. Tests `PartSketchTests` (5): the bracket converted gives the same bounds and volumes for four value sets and undoes in one step; a corner's distanceX replaced by a vertical side still solves for every instance, a horizontal one is refused as a conflict; instances sharing values solve once; values a sketch cannot take refused in `setPartValues` and in an edit; a shape edit within the constraints, a triangle dropping two dimensions, a hole from a sketch circle. Open: the edit mode's interface for own sketches (drawing and grips through `partSketchSetShapes`, glyphs and dimensions through the constraint commands, the DOF chip), a Constrain part button, the Families dialog showing a converted body as drawn from a sketch.
Visual graphs, stage 2: the automatic palette (decision 536). A graph is a tool of My tools when its "Show in My tools" is on (the graph's own `tool`: the button's name, icon, tooltip and group, "Graphs" by default), put in once like a script's tool (`addNewToolsTo`, moved to `app/myTools.ts`), and its button opens a floating palette made of its input nodes: Number (unit, and a slider with a least and a most), Whole number, Text, Yes or no, Choice, Elements picked (Use selection), Storey (the view's unless chosen) and the new A type node. Each input node has palette settings (shown, label, order, least, most, step); Watch nodes and nodes marked "Show in palette" show their values as a value, a short list or a small table. A field being changed previews through `graphs.evaluate {values}` (no step); let go of, it is written by `graphs.set` (one step, "Change graph inputs"); Bake from the palette writes the values and makes the elements in one step (`graphs.bake {values}`). The values belong to the graph, so the canvas, Bake and colleagues see the same; graphs stay the host's (no palette for a guest); it works on the web. The graph JSON is format 2 (palette settings, the tool, groups), format 1 read by `GraphDoc.FromFormat1`. A selection collapses into a group (G) with its crossing wires as ports and expands back; the engine evaluates a group as its nodes, results by path. The ask fields, their `Field` and the tool icons moved from the scripting module to `ui/src/shell/` so graphs share them. Tests: `GraphTests` (format 1, settings kept, preview without a step, set and bake with values, the type node, groups), vitest for the palette and collapse/expand; the guide's "A graph as a tool" and "Groups" (EN, RO), the `graphs` scene extended with the palette.

A part's own sketches and bodies edited in the engine (decision 520, §9 of `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md`). A point of a part's sketch is dragged as a view's is: `studio.partSketchDragPreview` solves the sketch with one drag goal and the placed part's values and keeps nothing, `studio.partSketchDrag` keeps it solved with the defaults, refused with the constraints that hold the point (`SketchConstraints.DragLocal`, the store's own drag on the part's shapes). Sketches are added on an origin plane with an offset of numbers or formulas (`addPartSketch`), renamed and removed, never while a body is drawn from them; bodies are added from a sketch's closed outline with a depth and a condition of the parameters (`addPartBody`), changed (`updatePartBody`: depth, condition, name, outline, holes, sketch) and removed, never the last. A body's outline may now be a closed chain of lines, arcs and open polylines meeting end to end in any order and direction, besides a closed polyline or a circle; arcs are faceted by `Chords`, so a slot's volume is the faceted (πr² + 2rL)·depth. `partTree` tells which bodies use each sketch and, per shape, whether it is in a body's profile or in any closed loop. A sketch's shapes are (u, v) on its plane, and the tree's frame takes them to the world. Tests `PartSketchEditTests` (4). Open: the edit mode's interface for these commands (other sessions), a search instead of the greedy walk for branching chains.


Constrained sketches, three small fixes (decision 520): a placed dimension goes back to automatic (select it, A, or the A button beside it: `studio.updateConstraint {place: null}`); the constraints the solver refuses in `studio.drawConstrained` (`refused`) are said in a notice with the first reason, as `dropped` is; and ARC has a tangent continuation (Tangent keyword or Enter at the first prompt: starts at the end of the last drawn line, open polyline or arc, only the end point is picked, `arcTangent` gives centre, radius and angles, the tangent hint is previewed) that records a coincident and a `tangent` end to end through `recorded.ts` (`DrawHints.tangentFrom`, request shape unchanged). Tests: `arcTangent` (left and right turns, degenerate), the recorded mapping; strings in all ten languages; guide EN and RO.


A part's own sketches edited in place (decision 520, `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md` §3.2): in the part's edit mode, *Constrain part* (tree and Properties) turns corner bodies into sketches; a click on a sketch in the tree, or a double click on the part, turns the 3D camera square to its plane (animated, restored on leaving: Esc, Tab or Finish sketch) and draws the sketch as an SVG layer over the view projected from its frame (`PartSketchEdit.tsx`, `partSketchModel.ts` for the pure frame/pose/shape-list/tool logic), reusing `ConstraintOverlay` through a `local` source (glyphs, dimensions, DOF colours) and the CAD constraint commands through a `CadHost.addConstraint` hook that calls `studio.partSketchAddConstraint`. Grips drag with `partSketchDragPreview` live and `partSketchDrag` on release; Line, Rectangle, Circle, Arc (3 points) and Delete send the whole shape list with ids kept (`partSketchSetShapes`). The tree gets + Sketch (plane), rename, remove, + Body from sketch (depth number or formula), a body's depth, *made when* and removal; the Families dialog shows *Drawn from sketch <name>* with a button into the part. Tests `partSketchModel.test.ts`; strings in all ten languages; guide EN and RO and the `studio-part` scene extended.

A part's sketch edited on a placed part with its own values (decision 520): editing a part's sketch edits the part's definition, so `studio.partTree` gives each local sketch a `stored` object (its shapes, constraints, status and frame solved with the part's defaults; `shapes` stay the object's own) and the editor draws and sends those — before, the instance-solved shapes sent back looked "moved" against the stored ones and the edit was refused. The object's own outline is drawn dashed behind, and the bar says *Editing the part: values shown are its defaults* when they differ; `partSketchDragPreview` takes `values: "defaults"` so the preview is what `partSketchDrag` keeps. Part-sketch dimensions keep a `place` (add, update, `null` = automatic) as a view's do. `partSketchSetShapes` takes `constraints` (refs `{new: i, part}` to the new shapes) in the same undo step: a point drawn on an existing grip is coincident with it (Ctrl places it free), a rectangle's sides horizontal and vertical; what cannot hold is reported as `refused`. The sketch bar stands beside the part tree, not over it, and a body's depth and condition sit in two tidy rows. Tests `PartSketchStoredTests` (also `when: null` clearing a condition) and `partSketchModel.test.ts`; strings in all ten languages; guide EN and RO, the `studio-part` scene keeping W = 1600.
Visual graphs, palette values shared or each user's own (decision 537, the user's answers of 13.11.2026 to stage 2's two questions). A graph's tool has a new setting, **Palette values** (the tool's `values` key in the graph JSON: `"shared"`, the default and what a graph without the key reads as, or `"own"`; anything else dropped; the format stays 2). Shared is stage 2's behaviour: what the palette lets go of is written by `graphs.set`. Each user's own: the graph's values are only the defaults; what a user lets go of is kept on their computer, per project (the IfcProject's GlobalId) and per graph, in the browser's storage (`lapin.graphValues.<project>.<graph>`), never written into the graph. Kept in the browser's storage and not in the engine's user folder because the web's engine, in a Web Worker, has no user folder, and the values are the interface's: the engine gets them with each evaluation and Bake. The palette shows the user's values that still fit (a value whose input node is gone or of another kind, a choice no longer offered, a number outside its least and most, a storey or type gone falls back to the graph's; elements deleted drop out of a pick), a dot beside each field that differs from the graph's, **Reset to the graph's values** and **Save as the graph's values** (`graphs.set`, one undo step). Preview and Bake use the user's values; `graphs.bake` takes `write` (default: true, false for an "own" graph), so Bake from an own palette writes only the elements and its undo label stays "Bake graph". The other answer: "Show in My tools" stays off for new graphs. Guests still get no palette. Code: `ui/src/modules/graphs/ownValues.ts`, `PaletteWindow.tsx`, the setting in `GraphView.tsx`'s tool settings, `GraphDoc.EachUsersOwnValues`; the 11 new phrases in all ten languages. Tests: `GraphTests` (the setting kept only when known, Bake with own values leaving the graph's constants and its undo label, `write` both ways), `ownValues.test.ts` (fits, fallbacks, merging, storage); the guide's "A graph as a tool" (EN, RO).

**The messages console (decision 538, 13.11.2026).** A strip docked at the bottom of the graph editor and of the Script panel, like the CAD command line: rows with a kind (error, warning, info), a time, a source and the text; Errors / Warnings / All with counts; Copy as plain text and Clear; collapsed it is one line with the latest message and the error count; its height is dragged, and its state (open, height, filter) is remembered per place in localStorage. Messages only, no input line; the Script panel's REPL is untouched. One shared component (`ui/src/shell/MessageStrip.tsx`) over a pure model (`ui/src/platform/messages.ts`: repeats counted, rows replaced by group, filters, plain text, the remembered state; at most 500 rows). The graph editor feeds it after each evaluation (`modules/graphs/messages.ts`): each node's error or warning with its label (a group's inner node as `Group / node`) and the reason, nodes with nothing to work on (the engine's "has nothing" and "Not reached") as warnings after the root causes, a cut ghost and the calls limit as warnings, and "Commands: N, M ms"; a click selects the node and centres the canvas on it (`focus` in the graphs store); Bake adds "Elements made: N, one undo step" or its refusal. One evaluation is one group: the next replaces it, and a row that stays keeps its time and is not told to screen readers again. The Script panel feeds it from the kernel's `script.output` events (`modules/scripting/messages.ts`, `scriptLog` in the store, kept while the panel is closed, so tool and palette-button runs are logged too): started, done with its duration, stopped, failed with the error's last line, `name:line` from the traceback, and a click opens that script at the line. Console runs (typed lines) are not logged: the REPL has them. The strip open adds its height to the panel's. Interface only; no engine change. Tests: `platform/messages.test.ts`, `modules/graphs/messages.test.ts`, `modules/scripting/messages.test.ts`; the guide's Messages paragraphs (EN, RO); the new phrases in all ten languages.
**External geometry, step L1: bodies a program computes (13.11.2026, decision 539).** The user approved working with external geometry programs (the first is Lite-STEP, a Python DSL whose licence is unknown, so nothing of it is copied) through Lapin's open Python API rather than embedding them; `docs/plan-external-geometry.md` is the contract: what an external program may do through `lapinbim`, the four steps, and what Lapin deliberately does not do (route IFC through an external engine, shared-memory Arrow IPC or ghost frames, AST patching of the user's script). L1 adds `element.createBody` and `element.setBody` to core (`ExternalBodies.cs`): a triangle mesh, planar faces with holes, or coloured parts of either, in metres, checked (finite, indices in range, 2 million triangles, 10 km, faces planar to 1 mm; closed or open reported, never required), stored the way Lapin already stores a body that came with an element: a `LapinSharedBody` found by the SHA-256 of its content, the element an instance placed by its 12 numbers (a 4×4 matrix or `{at, angle}`), so one body set on many elements is kept once and a body nothing uses goes in the same step. The class is any IfcElement subtype of IFC4 or IFC 4.3 (`IfcElementClasses`), else a proxy with a note; a pinned GlobalId is validated and refused when taken; `owner` and the program's `attrs` are kept on the element, written into `Lapin_Intent` and read back by the IFC import with the class, so Lapin's own IFC (IFC4 or IFC4X3, B-rep or face sets) brings back the same element with the same id. Being a stored body, it is drawn, cut in plans and sections, picked, moved, turned, copied (sharing its body, a new GlobalId) and deleted like a reference; it measures `BaseQuantities` (volume of a closed body, surface, box). Its shape is the program's: `element.update`, Studio's *Make Studio solid* and *Convert to native* refuse it in plain words, and Properties says *Made by a script: <owner>*. Lite-STEP, read once its licence was known (MIT, 13.11.2026; still an external process: it needs ifcopenshell), shaped the input: `units: "mm"` (it models in integer millimetres), a mesh as rows of three (`vertices`, `faces`/`triangles`, as numpy and manifold3d give them), and `lapinbim.global_id(name, namespace)`, a stable GlobalId from a canonical name (UUID 5, compressed as IFC writes it), since its own GlobalIds change at every compile. `lapinbim` has `create_body`, `set_body` and `global_id`. Tests: `ExternalBodyTests` (12: millimetres as rows of three; create, set and undo; a pinned and a taken GlobalId; one body kept once and placed; faces with a hole and an open mesh; bad input; refused reshaping, move, copy and delete; the plan's cut; IFC4 B-rep, IFC4 and IFC4X3 face sets read back; save and open) and `python/tests/test_live.py`.

**External geometry, step L2: the selection and measures for clients (13.11.2026, decision 540).** The window now tells the engine's host what it selects whenever it changes on the desktop (it did so only with Developer extras on), and the host sends every local connection a `selection` event with what each element is: its GlobalId, IFC class, name, storey, assembly path (`Assemblies.PathOf`) and the box of its body as built; `script.selection {details: true}` answers the same, `lapin.selected()` reads it. Named measures are the host's methods (`measure.define`, `measure.get`, `measure.remove`, `measure.list`; `LiveLinks.cs`): a catalogue query with its arguments, a formula in Lapin's `Expr` over one element (`attr.<field>`, `qty.<Quantity>`, `bbox.*`), or a built-in (`storey.<name>.elevation` and `.height`, `element.<id>.bbox`, `wall.<id>.top`). They belong to the connection that made them; after each change (a command, an undo, a delivery, a rollback) the host recomputes only those that read an element the diff touched (its changed nodes and the bodies it rebuilt, which already follow what each body is built from) and sends the `measure` event `{name, value, was}` to that connection when the value changed. `lapinbim` gained `selected()`, `measure()`, `measure_value()` and `remove_measure()`. Tests: `LiveLinkTests` (a measure told when what it reads changes and only then, formula, storey and query measures; the selection with what it is) and `python/tests/test_live.py`.

**External geometry, step L3: regeneration on change (13.11.2026, decision 541).** A connection asks to be told when elements or measures change: `script.watch {ids?, measures?}` gives a watch whose `watch` event `{watch, ids, measures}` comes after another's change (the window's, an undo, another program) touching one of the elements (its attributes, or its body rebuilt) or changing one of the measures; the connection's own edits never fire it, so a program that updates its bodies on the event cannot loop. A project script's watch is kept in the project: `script.watch {script, …}` runs `scripting.watch {id | name, ids, measures}` (one undo step, on the `LapinScript`), and when what it watches changes the host runs the script again in the Script panel's Python, only when it is trusted on this computer (by its SHA-256, decision 489 §7; otherwise a translated note in the Script panel says how to trust it), as its own undo step *“<script> regenerated”* right after the step that set it off. No loops: what a script run changes (any connection of the kernel, and its rollback) never sets off a project script; it waits 300 ms after the last change, a run going on puts it off (each second, a minute at most), and one taking more than 60 s is stopped with nothing kept. Lapin's own notes come to the console as `script.output {type: "note"}`. `lapinbim` gained `watch()` and `unwatch()`. Tests: `LiveLinkTests` (a watch told of another's change, never its own, through undo, ended, gone with its connection; a project script's watch kept in the project and an untrusted script not run) and `python/tests/test_live.py` (a connection's watch; a trusted project script run again by the real Python as its own undo step, not set off by its own change).

**External geometry, step L4: a public example and the developer page (13.11.2026, decision 542).** `python/examples/truss_over_room.py` (MIT, written from scratch) connects with `lapinbim`, takes the room selected in Lapin (else the first), builds a planar Warren truss over the room's short side with its bottom chord on the ceiling as one `IfcMember` body of square bars (`create_body`, owner `script:truss_over_room`, its GlobalId made from the room's so a second run updates it), watches the room and builds it again with `set_body` when the room changes, keeping the GlobalId; `--once` builds and stops. `python/tests/test_live.py` runs it against a test engine: once over a 6 × 4 m room (spanning the 4 m side, on the ceiling), then live, following the room raised by 0.5 m. Once Lite-STEP's licence was known (MIT; it needs ifcopenshell, LGPL-3.0, so it stays an external process) its repository was read and `python/examples/litestep_bridge.py` sketches the bridge: the model compiled by Lite-STEP's own compiler, each product meshed by ifcopenshell and pushed with `create_body`/`set_body` in one step, GlobalIds from the products' names, products gone deleted, `--watch` at every save; it imports `lite_step` and `ifcopenshell` only when it runs, so no dependency is added. `docs/python.md` has the section *External geometry and live scripts* (the contract, the arguments, events, measures, watches, the examples); the user guide's Python page (English and its eight translations) has *Shapes made by a script*; `docs/plan-python-scripting.md` §10 records the licence.

**The engine's port: 14912, then the next free (13.11.2026, decision 543).** Asked by Lite-STEP's author, who integrates through `lapinbim`: a user cannot be sure of a port the system picks. An engine started without a port of its own (`--port auto`, also none or 0; the desktop app now passes `auto`) binds 127.0.0.1:14912 (L A P I N, the letters' places 12 1 16 9 14), else the next free up to 15011, moving on when the bind itself finds the port taken (`EnginePort.StartAsync`, the server built again for the next port); with all taken, a system port, said on stderr. `--port N` stays exact; `pnpm dev` stays on 47810 so a dev engine never takes the app's port. `GET /health` (loopback Host only) and the new WebSocket call `engine.hello` (past the queue of calls) say `{"engine": "lapin", product, version, pid, port, project, …}`, so a probing client never takes another program for Lapin. Each engine writes `hosts/host-<pid>.json` beside `host.json` (the latest, kept for older readers), removed when it stops; stale files (a dead pid) are skipped by readers and removed by the next engine (`EngineHostFiles`). `lapinbim`'s discovery is `LAPIN_PORT` → host files → ports 14912–15011 and 47810 probed in parallel, each confirmed; `connect()` takes the only engine, the one whose `project`/`product` matches, or raises listing them; `lapinbim.engines()` and `python -m lapinbim engines` list them. The MCP bridge finds engines the same way, picking with `LAPIN_PROJECT`/`LAPIN_PRODUCT`. Tested by `EnginePortTests` (two servers without a port take the first two free from 14912 while the test holds one; an explicit port kept; a full range falls back), `python/tests/test_discovery.py` against fake engines, and the real engine's `/health` and `engine.hello`; checked by hand with two engines (14912 and 14913, their host files removed at exit) and the bridge.

**Transform nodes and the twisted tower (decision 544, 13.11.2026).** The geometry nodes the classic twisted-tower demo needs: **Rotate** (points or polylines about the vertical through a centre, the origin by default, by an angle in degrees, anticlockwise), **Scale** (about a centre by a factor, with an optional Z factor that defaults to the same), **Centre** (the middle of the box round the points or polylines given, all together), and a **Start angle** on **Points on a circle** (0 by default, so existing graphs give the same points). Rotate and Scale map by the usual list rules: a list of angles × one outline gives one turned outline per angle; a list of outlines × a list of angles pairs them. The gap found: a list of polylines on a command's `points`/`pts` argument was passed whole (one floor with nested points, an error); it now runs once per polyline, as a list of lists does (decision 535's rule), so Rotate → Floor with its Storey from a New storey node makes one floor on each storey. The ready graph `engine/tests/Lapin.Engine.Tests/Fixtures/graphs/twisted-tower.graph.json` (Floors, Floor height, Total twist, Width, Taper with sliders; Show in My tools; floors and columns at the corners of the outline scaled to 0.85) is the one the video tutorial loads. Preview (Debug build, warm): 12 floors with columns ~75 ms, 30 floors ~270 ms. Tests: `GraphTowerTests`. The guide's Geometry nodes and "A twisted tower" (EN, RO); the new strings in all ten languages.

**Floor types: move a layer, Reverse layers (decision 545, 13.11.2026).** Forum topic 52 (benlungu): a floor type's layers could not be moved. In Floor types each layer row now has Move layer up / down buttons (disabled at the ends, reachable with Tab) and the dialog a **Reverse layers** button like Wall types', asking first because every floor of the type turns over once it is saved. The reorder is one helper, `moveLayer` (beside `reverseLayers`), used by both Wall types and Floor types and tested in `reverseLayers.test.ts`. Roof and ceiling types have no layer table, so nothing was missing there. Guide: Floors page (EN, RO) and the scene `floor-layers`; new strings in all ten languages.


**Studio parts, P1 engine: the parameter table (13.11.2026, decision 520).** A part's parameters grew into Inventor's and FreeCAD's table (`docs/plan-studio-parametric.md` §3.2, P1): kinds `text` and `integer` beside length, number, angle and yes/no; `min` and `max`; derived parameters (a `formula` of the others, worked out on every ask in the order the formulas need, cycles refused with their path, never an instance's own value); type parameters (`instance: false`, one value for every placed part). `Lapin.Studio/PartParams.cs` holds the resolution (`Resolve`: defaults, the instance's own values, a flex's values, then the derived ones, with each value's source and problems) and the commands `studio.addPartParam`, `updatePartParam` (a new key renamed in every formula of the part, its sketches' constraints and every placed part's values, the scan the family editor's `renameInFormula` uses), `removePartParam` (refused while named, listing where), `movePartParam`, and the query `studio.partValues`; each goes through `PartSketches.Commit`, so the definition is checked whole and every placed part must still build. `studio.partPreview` takes a placed part and gives world edges for the flex ghost, and says `ok: false` with the reason instead of throwing. Tests `PartParameterTests` (10).

**Part parameters, the interface (stage P1, decision 520; 13.11.2026).** In a part's edit mode the tree's Parameters section keeps its compact rows and gains **Parameters…**, which opens a wider table beside the tree (`ui/src/modules/studio/PartParams.tsx`, logic in `partParamModel.ts`, tested in `partParamModel.test.ts`): rows grouped by `group`; name and key; a default cell that takes a value or `=formula` (a derived parameter shows its formula there and `= value` as its own); this object's own value for an instance parameter (a type one shows its default only); instance and published toggles; a row menu (key rename, label, description, group, minimum, maximum, kind, unit, move up and down, delete) with the engine's refusals shown under the row; **+ Parameter** with a kind picker (`studio.addPartParam`, `updatePartParam`, `removePartParam`, `movePartParam`, `setPartValues`). **Flex** is a switch in the table: while on, typed values only go to `studio.partPreview` (debounced 250 ms) and its edges are drawn as a dashed ghost in 3D (`PartFlex3D`, an SVG overlay projecting the model's coordinates as `PartPlane3D` does), with the volume or the engine's reason under the table; **Keep** sends the instance values and the type parameters' defaults as one `edit.batch` (one undo step), **Put back** drops them. In model mode a placed part's Properties show **only the published parameters** (`PublishedParamsSection`, from `studio.partTree` and `studio.partValues`): instance ones editable per object, type ones read-only with a *type* badge, derived ones read-only with their formula on hover and a *formula* badge, and an *Edit part (Tab)* button; the Family section no longer lists every parameter. `usePartTree` moved to `partTreeStore.ts`. Guide: `docs/user/studio-parts.md` and `ro/`, the `studio-part` scene (a derived parameter, Flex on Width, Keep), the `family-properties` step of the families scene. Strings in all ten languages.

Part parameter table, follow-ups (decision 520): the Families dialog's `normaliseFamily` now keeps a parameter's formula, instance, published, group, description, unit, min, max and the text and integer kinds, so saving a family no longer turns a derived parameter into a plain one. Clearing a bound sends `null`, which `updatePartParam` already takes as clearing (checked in PartParams.cs). The part tree's rows (`partTreeModel.ts`, the Properties parameter list) type values as number or string: a text parameter is shown and edited as text and compared exactly. In Flex, the real part is drawn faded (looks source `part-flex`, opacity 0.25) while the ghost shows, and restored on Keep, Put back or leaving.
**Each end joining automatically or not (decision 546, forum topic #50, 13.11.2026).** A tester had masonry between RC columns imported from IFC; any change to a wall made Lapin's automatic joins, which moved its ends, and the openings, placed from the wall's start, moved with them. Each end of a path element (wall, beam, strip footing) now has `autoJoin {start, end}`: not stored, a drawn element's ends join and a recognised IFC element's do not, so the import keeps reproducing the file. `Joins.Auto` skips an end that is off and never brings another's end to it; an element with both ends off makes no new join (an end of another may still stop on it as a T, leaving it whole); joins already at an end that is off stay. The transform path of decision 531 goes through the same rule; a copy of a recognised wall gets both ends on. `element.setAutoJoin {ids, start?, end?}` (core) sets it on a selection in one undo step, and an end turned on joins what it meets at once. Properties has a Joins section with two toggles for every selected wall, beam or strip footing; right-click an end grip for Don't join at this end (a new `Grip.menu`). Openings are stored by `along` from the wall's start; on a straight recognised wall `updateWall` now keeps them where they stand in plan when its start moves (and on its joined recognised neighbours), while a drawn wall's still go with its start. Tests: `AutoJoinTests`, `IfcRecognitionTests.A_recognised_wall_edited_keeps_its_ends_and_its_openings_and_joins_nothing`, `autoJoin.test.ts`. The guide's Walls page (EN, RO) and the `walls` scene; the new strings in all ten languages.

**A view placed again at another scale (decision 547, forum topic #31, 13.11.2026).** A tester wanted a plan at 1:100 and the same plan cropped at 1:25 on one sheet, which took a duplicate, a scale and a crop by hand. `sheet.placeAtScale` does it in one undo step: the viewport's view duplicated with its annotations (`Views.Duplicate`, taken out of `view.duplicate`), given the scale, cropped to a rectangle dragged on the viewport (or the viewport as it is cropped), its middle found through `Placement.FromPaper` so a turned plan works too, and placed on the same sheet beside the original (right, else left, below, above, as the frame has room clear of the title block and the other views). The copy is named "<view> 1:<scale>". Right-click a view on a sheet for Place again at 1:N… (the project's other standard scales), then drag the part (a click takes it as cropped); right-click a view on a sheet in the project browser for the same with its crop, and its sheet opens. Tests: `PlaceAtScaleTests`, `placeAtScale.test.ts`; the guide's Sheets page (EN, RO) and the `sheets` scene; the new strings in all ten languages.
Doors and windows in one object (decision 548, forum topic 34, testers Robby and LVK). Door leaves and window sashes share one frame in both kinds: an element is a door (IfcDoor / IfcDoorType) when it has at least one door leaf, a window when it has opening sashes and no leaf, else what it was made as (`Joinery.KindOf`; frameless glass and bay windows keep their kind). `createJoineryType` and the configurator's preview take that kind; `updateJoineryType` refuses a change of class (entities never change class), and the dialog offers *Create as a new type*. One mark, one schedule line (the operation row lists every pane's). Body: a sash in a door's bottom row stands on a bottom rail the frame's width over the threshold (`Joinery.RailUnder`, `PanelRect` raised by it); a door's window boards run only over a solid fixed panel at a column's foot (`ParapetTop`, on the transom over it), none where glass or a leaf reaches the floor; a sash in a door has a window handle (`HandleStyle(s, op)`) and its drip. New door operations `lift-slide-left/right` (HS) and `tilt-slide-left/right` (PSK): a glazed leaf framed like a sash on the frame's inner track, flush with its room face, the fixed panes beside it on the outer track; handle inside on the stile; bottom row only; plan: the leaf cut on its track and an arrow; elevation: the run arrow with a lift arrow (HS) or the bottom-hung triangle (PSK); IFC SLIDING_TO_LEFT/RIGHT, panel SLIDING. Glazing: `JoinerySash.Glazing` ("4-16-4", "4-12-4-12-4", "33.1-16-4"; a laminated pane AB.n is A+B mm plus n × 0.38 mm; letters after a number are notes) parsed by `GlazingBuildUp`, setting `GlassThickness` when the spec is read; refused when unreadable or thicker than the sash and frame hold. IFC: every panel is written now (a door's transom and side panes were dropped): leaves their operation, fixed FIXEDPANEL, sashes USERDEFINED (they were FIXEDPANEL); OperationType as before (a full-height sash beside one leaf counts as a fixed side pane for SWING_FIXED_*); the type's `Lapin_Joinery` gets PanelOperations (each panel's term in panel order, sashes in IfcWindowPanelOperationEnum, HS/PSK as LIFTANDSLIDE/TILTANDSLIDE), Opening and Glazing, merged into the user's own `Lapin_Joinery` set when there is one (`IfcJoineryType.Properties`); elements with a build-up get `Pset_DoorWindowGlazingType` (GlassLayers, GlassThickness1–3, IsLaminated, SolarHeatGainTransmittance from the g-value). The import reads PanelOperations over the panels' terms and `JoineryIfc.FromIfc` takes sash terms, LIFTANDSLIDE/TILTANDSLIDE and Lapin's user-defined text with sashes; IfcDoorLiningProperties has no mullion offsets, so a door's stay in Lapin's intent. Plans: `joinerySymbols.windowSwing` (auto, none, dashed, solid; `Joinery.WindowSwing`) draws side-hung and tilt-and-turn sashes' swings: auto dashed for those whose bottom is within 150 mm of the floor (the element's sill plus the pane's bottom), the default. Presets: balcony door + window, door with sidelights and transom, lift-and-slide portal, French window (`JoinerySpecs`, in the catalogue's doorPresets). UI: the Opens list grouped (Door leaves, Window sashes, Garage doors) for both kinds (`operationGroups`, `kindOf` in joineryGrid.ts), a Glass section (build-up with common ones, thickness, a pointer to Data), the window-swing choice beside the symbol convention, sills over a parapet for doors, IfcDoor/IfcWindow under the drawings. Tests: `DoorWindowCombinationTests` (kind, closed bodies of every preset, rail and boards, IFC terms and an IFC4/IFC4X3 round trip, swings, HS, glazing, schedule); `JoineryIfcTests` reads panels as the import does. Guide: Doors and windows, "Doors and windows in one frame" (EN, RO), scene `joinery-combination`; strings in all ten languages.

Example graphs (decision 549): the Graphs panel's **New from example…**, beside New graph and on the empty panel, lists the graphs bundled in `ui/src/modules/graphs/examples/*.graph.json` (`examples.ts` holds the list, name, one line and icon of each; `ExampleMenu.tsx` the menu); picking one saves a copy into the project under a name not taken (`graphs.save`, one undo step; `uniqueName`) and opens it, fitted. The files are lazy imports (`import.meta.glob`), so the web and the desktop alike, and the engine's tests read the same files (`GraphExamplesTests`: each parses, evaluates without an error on a new project, bakes in one undo step; the tower gives 12 storeys, 12 floors, 48 columns; the grid 20 columns; the rectangle 4 walls) so no copy can drift: the twisted tower fixture moved there from `engine/tests/.../Fixtures/graphs/`. The tower example changed for a real project: its twist step is the Formula `a / max(b - 1, 1)` (the top floor turns the whole Total twist, 90° not 82.5°), its levels start one floor height up (Floors − 1 new storeys), and the first floor stands on the project's own storey at level 0 (Storeys → Compare `=` 0 → Filter → Join lists before the new storeys' ids), found by its level, so a project with a basement and a storey above works too. Examples: Twisted tower, Columns on a grid, Walls round a rectangle. Guide: "Examples" in `docs/user/graphs.md` and `ro/`. Strings in all ten languages.

Graphs panel, follow-ups from the tower filming: the inspector's Least / Most / Step fields (`.graphs-limits`) and the X / Y / Z row wrap or shrink to the panel's width (grid tracks `minmax(0, 1fr)`, the inspector no longer scrolls sideways); the tool icon of a graph resolves through `scriptIcon` like a script tool's (`building` is lucide's Building2; a vitest checks that the examples' icons are drawings, not the fallback letter).
Texts in formulas (decision 550, forum topic #38): the formula language (`Expr`) works with a value that is a number or a text (`XValue`). Texts are written in quotes, joined by `&` or `concat`, and the new functions `text(x; "00")`, `pad`, `upper`, `lower`, `trim`, `left`, `right` and `len` shape them; comparisons of texts are case-free. Numbers read as before: the numeric `Expr.Eval` refuses a formula that gives a text, and a model text starting with a number still reads as that number. Live text (`{= …}`) prints a text or a formatted number, a bar or a brace inside quotes left to the text.

Fields and formulas that read other elements, and text parameters worked out (decision 551, forum topics #36 and #38): a name with a scope before a dot reads another element as the element itself (`ParamScopes`): `storey.`, `building.`, `site.`, `project.` (its information and its own parameters), `type.`, `group.`, `assembly.`, `host.` (a door's wall), and from the architecture module `room.`, `fromRoom.` and `toRoom.` (a door's rooms on either side, swapped by a flip). The project's own parameters are global, read by their key alone. Tags, room stamps, `{= …}` and parameter formulas read them; a text parameter's formula may be an expression with texts or a template with fields (`{storey.code}.{Hall}.{Flat}.{number}` → K1.A.3.1). Nothing is stored, so a storey's change shows at once everywhere; a formula reading itself through other elements gives no value. The IFC export now writes formula values, and the room schedule has a column per room parameter.

Tags drawn by the user (decision 552, forum topic #37): a tag type, or the room stamp, may name a symbol of the user's (decision 515's editor) and is then drawn as that symbol at the tag's point. The symbol's texts are its slots: each holds fields as a tag template does, read from the tagged element (`{storey.code}.{Hall}.{Flat}.{number}`, `{name}`, `{area}`, `{Floor finish}`, decision 551's scopes, `{= …}` formulas), while the symbol's own token sources keep theirs. Placed tags are drawn again on every view drawing, so they follow their element and whatever it reads; a symbol tag keeps its leader, rotation and grips, and a deleted symbol leaves the type's text. Drafting standards → Tags has a symbol picker per tag type, Room tag… a "Drawn as" choice, and the symbol editor's Fields list offers the tagged element's fields and the storeys' parameters.



Studio parts as types everywhere, stage P3 of decision 520 (docs/plan-studio-parametric.md): placed parts with the same values share one body, built once per part definition and values (`Parts.SetOf`, keyed `part:<id>:<hash>`), and are drawn as instances of one mesh (`SharedInstance.Local`, tessellated once by the session); a filter that changes an element's parts now drops its instance. IFC: a part goes out as its class's IfcTypeProduct with one representation map, its defaults' body (IFC4's maps are views of one block, not variants); placed parts of the type's values are IfcMappedItems of it, the others their own B-rep; parameters in `Lapin_PartParameters` (type ones on the type, instance and derived ones on each placed part, measures by kind), `LapinPartId` in the type's intent (hook `IIfcTypeShapes`; `IfcFurnishingElementType` written without a predefined type). Lapin's own IFC brings the parts back, each measured by the fidelity gate; a changed map leaves its placed parts references. Parts keep reading instance defaults on every ask rather than copying them (`TypeDefaults`), a difference written in the plan. Tests: `PartTypeIfcTests`; docs/spec-ifc.md, "Piesele Studio ca tipuri".

Class names in their own context: the engine's class labels (the Properties header, the selection card, Studio's IFC class list) carry `data-i18n-ctx="class"`, so the translator looks up `class|<label>` before the plain word. A Studio solid (IfcBuildingElementProxy, "Solid") reads "Solid" in Romanian instead of the panel word "Pline" (a bay's solid sides); the same for Bar (rebar, not the handrail), Issue (a CDE issue, not an edition), Grid (the drawing grid) and Covering (a finish, not a roofing) in every language that had the clash. Other labels (Member, Plate, Opening, Floor, Array, Space) were checked and read correctly.

A room measured to its finishes (forum topic 82, decision 554). A tester laid a finish with 1.5 cm of tiles on the walls in a room and its area did not change: rooms measured only the outline between the walls' faces. Now a finished room's net floor area, perimeter and volume are measured to the finished face of its walls (`Rooms.MeasuresOf` → `RoomFinishes.FinishedOf`, derived from the model on every ask): the outline taken in along every side by the walls' finish at the floor (`WallFace`, the innermost face of its layers' first pieces), the islands grown by it, the volume up to the finished ceiling. The gross measures and `Height` stay the walls' outline; `FinishCeilingHeight` and `FinishFloorHeight` are added for a finished room. A door's gap stays out of the room's area even when the floor runs through it. The stamp, the room schedule, the colour legend, the documents' usable areas and the fan schedule read the net; the Quantities panel shows the gross area, net perimeter and finished ceiling height where they differ. `RoomFinishAreaTests`.

A spiral stair's railing follows its treads all the way (forum topic 86, Casa Curutchet's stair): a spiral that turns more than once passes over itself, and the first turn's balusters stood on the highest surface over their footprint, the treads a turn up (or the floor above, where the top stands over the foot), so they were cut to 5 cm stubs under the handrail, which floated over the lower half of the stair in 3D and in sections. A post's ground is now only what lies below its top (`StairBuilder.GroundUnder` takes the handrail's height as a ceiling, `SurfaceAt` an upper bound), so every post stands on its own tread and reaches the handrail, on either side, turning either way, through one, one and a half or two turns (decision 553). Tests: `SpiralRailingTests`.

Forum topics 70, 72, 76, 77 (tester Rychlas). 70: the status bar is one line of a fixed 28 px whatever the width (it wrapped when a long file name met the cursor coordinates, which shrank the view and flipped ~20 times a second): the file name is cut with an ellipsis (full name in a tooltip), the coordinates keep a fixed width (tabular numbers), the module list ellipsises with the whole list in a tooltip. 72: a wall's bare opening has only a hit area in the plan (its gap), which a selection box and lasso skipped, so no box took it; `regionIds` now takes an element that has nothing else by that area's outline (a door keeps its own lines). A click in the gap already took it (planPick tests). Not done: sections and elevations have no pick mark for an opening (the engine's `PickMarks` knows only cuts, lines and fills of the drawing). 76: the status notice and the engine notice sit at z-index 500, above dialogs and menus (60); the notice has `pointer-events: none`, so it never covers a control. 77: in 3D the command line is `floating`, along the view's bottom edge above the overlays, as in a plan (it was an unpositioned block that collapsed to a small box beside the HUD).

Small interface ideas from the forum (topics 62, 63, 67): Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab (and Ctrl+Page Down/Up, Control on a Mac too) bring the next and previous view tab in front (`cycleTab` in `app/store.ts`, commands `app.nextTab` and `app.previousTab`, reassignable, never inside a field). A 3D tab keeps its camera (pose and projection) and its section box while another view is in front: the viewport is unmounted then, and `viewport/viewState3d.ts` holds one entry per 3D view for the session (written as the camera or the box changes, read when the viewport mounts or its view changes, emptied when a project opens), where the section box used to be reset at every mount; a saved view's own camera and box apply only when nothing was kept. The panels could already stand on either side (View → Panels, decision 427); the same four choices now also stand in View → Interface… as Panel sides.

Openings picked in every 2D view (forum topic 72, Rychlas): a bare opening (an IfcOpeningElement with nothing filling it) draws nothing of its own, so sections and elevations had no pick mark for it. `Views.OpeningPickMarks` now adds one per opening a section or an elevation shows: its void's body (`IVoidShapes`, the host's module's) cut by the view's plane where the view cuts it, else its outline seen on the view, as PICK lines (box selection) and `data.loops` (a click inside), laid at each piece's station in a developed section. Shown means the host draws lines on that outline (the jambs, the cut's edges at the sill and head; beyond a cut, seen lines only), so an opening behind another element, in a host the view's Visibility / Graphics hides, or hidden in the view has none. In plan, `Openings.BareGeometry` gave a hit area only to a wall's gap; an opening with its own outline (a floor's, a shaft's, in any host) now has a hit outline closed on itself (its edge picks it, a stair in its stairwell keeps its clicks; its outline drawn in 3D when selected at the floor's top), and one in another host without an outline the plan footprint of its void's body. The UI's drawing picking takes the marks as they are. Tests: `OpeningPickTests`.

A material for Studio solids and materials that stay out of joins (forum topics 59 and 60, tester Mamah, decision 555). Every Studio class and the solid type (`LapinSolidType`) have a `material` field of a new kind, `materialRef`: a project material by its id, chosen in Properties by name (`MaterialPicker byId`; several solids at once), `element.update` taking an id or a name and keeping the id (`Fields.Check(f, value, model)`). `StudioModule.MaterialOf` works it out on every ask: the solid's own, else its type's; a placed part's own material takes all its bodies, its part type's the bodies without one in the definition (`BodySet.Local(m, mirror, over, fallback)`, the shared body's key carrying the material, so parts of one material still share one mesh; the IFC type map takes the type's). The 3D colour, the cut's hatch and fill (white only without one), the takeoff by material, the cost, the cut order by material (`CutOrder.MainMaterial`) and the IFC export's IfcRelAssociatesMaterial read it; Lapin's IFC brings it back. A material's `joins` (default true, kept only when false; the Materials dialog's "Joins with other elements"): `Materials.Joins(m, e)` by the element's main material; false, `Joins.Auto` neither joins the element nor joins others to it, `ICutters.For` neither cuts it nor lets it cut, `MaterialDrawing` gives its cuts no join key (its own outline, no seams, as `drawJoints`), and a beam runs whole through the columns it passes (it still stops at the faces of those its ends stand in). `material.update` turning it off takes off the joins of the walls, beams and strip footings mainly of that material, on again joins them (`Rejoin`), one undo step. Tests: `StudioMaterialTests`, `MaterialJoinsTests`.
