a rounded balcony under 5 cm deep builds; commands that answered with an internal error give a clear message. *Links*: a copy made by element.transform or an array points to the copies of what its original pointed to, through Copy to storeys' remap (`CopyLinks`), and modules are told before element.delete removes anything (`IDeletionFollowers`): railings and skirtings drawn in plan move, a room's finish follows, is deleted with and copied with its room and keeps its GlobalIds when its type changes, downpipes are no longer doubled, stairs keep one opening and mirror their turn, halls and deleted hosts leave no dangling connections or bars, a ceiling's grid moves and turns with it. *IFC*: a foreign material's measures in the project's units of their kind (an area in m² in a project in mm was up to 10⁶ too small), all twenty SI prefixes, trimmed lines and ellipses by parameter or point, carried profiles followed into their outlines or left to Lapin's export (never a `$` in a required attribute), durations in the calendar's working hours, task dates as written, no empty containment, empty typed values and complex instances no longer failing an import, one STEP string decoder (`StepText`). *Planning, coordination, quantities*: indent and outdent drop the links they would leave between a summary and its task and the critical path ignores them; one open mesh no longer fails a 3D view, its sheets and PDF, nor the openings check (the pair is reported unchecked); stairs, ramps and balconies give the takeoff and estimate their concrete, slab and railing with material ids; money rounds in decimal; BCF names differing by case; faces above 46 340 vertices triangulate; CLT joints stay out of overlapping openings; a batch's steps report only their own sketch constraints. *Shared sessions and desktop*: protocol 2, a salt from each side per connection, so a relay can no longer replay a guest's session (guests of another version are told to update); a guest survives a bad frame; an edit running past 30 s is no longer reported failed; the desktop shell opens only folders and documents, reaches only Ollama's port and gives the updater to the main window. *Interface*: a colour pick sent once; dialogs close only on a press that starts on the backdrop and on Escape from a field, focus moved in and back, aria-modal; confirmations and prompts in the chosen language (`shell/prompts.ts`); numeric fields commit on blur or Enter with a decimal comma and honour min and max; 3D overlays ignore keys in fields and dialogs; undo during a gesture cancels it or asks to finish it; selectors use a by-type index; stale replies guarded; drags end on pointercancel; the graph and palette storage capped. Left for later: the window does not yet show the engine's new save warnings nor the openings check's unchecked pairs; `storey.duplicate` still copies without the link remap; MS Project's import parses dates as local time; the ear-clipping triangulator is O(n²).

**Make a layered wall from solids (forum topic 101, decision 600).** A wall another program modelled as separate parallel solids, one per layer (Slawomir's timber-frame house wall in SketchUp: cladding, battens, boards, insulation with its studs, gypsum), stayed those solids after an import (references, or Studio solids with Everything editable), since an import reproduces the file and recognises a wall only when it is one element. **Architecture → Wall from solids** (`WFS`, and **Make a layered wall…** on the right-click of two or more solids) makes them one Lapin wall, an explicit command after the import, never done by it. `WallsFromSolids` (architecture) reads each solid from its triangles (a stored body's, or its producer's for a Studio solid): its plan's smallest rectangle gives its direction, length and thickness; wall-long solids (≥ 0.3 m, three times their thickness) running the same way, overlapping along and up, side by side with at most 10 cm between them, are one wall, each set another; the rest (studs, battens, a door's or window's solid) go into the wall they stand in. Across a wall, solids sharing half a thickness are one layer, of the material most of it is made of (the solid's material, else its name without its copy number, else a placeholder), the pieces of another material inside it reported (studs: their width and spacing), or, with **Timber frame layer** and the wood module, the layer made the core and the wall type a timber frame of their section and spacing (`wood` on the type, as `wood.setWallFrame` writes it). Gaps become an air layer or go to the thicker neighbour; overlaps come off the thicker one; thicknesses round to a millimetre. The core: the user's, else the timber frame, a structural layer by its name, the stud layer, the thickest. A hole is read from the faces looking down inside a layer across its whole thickness over the highest face looking up under it (or the floor); one in every solid layer that runs past it is the wall's opening, as large as the smallest, a door's or window's solid in it (by class or name) becoming a door or window of that size with nothing of Lapin's (no reveal, lintel, boards); one in some layers only is listed and the wall left whole there. The type has no lintels. A preview query (`architecture.wallFromSolids`) shows the walls found, their layers, openings, and each solid that differs from the wall by more than 1 mm or 0.5 % of its layer's volume (RecognitionFidelity's numbers), before **Make** (`architecture.wallFromSolids`) makes the type, the wall on the core's middle, its openings, puts it in the solids' group and joins the walls made together (and to walls they meet). The solids are kept hidden (`supersededBy`, `Superseded` in the platform: the one resolver, `VisibilityGraphics.Hides`, and the IFC export leave them out while the wall exists; deleting the wall brings them back; **Show the original solids** in the wall's properties, `architecture.showOriginalSolids`), deleted, or kept. One undo step. Tests: `WallsFromSolidsTests` (five boxes → five layers in order and thickness, the wall's body theirs within a millimetre; a 5 mm gap as air or absorbed and reported; a window's hole through all layers → an opening 1200 × 1200 at 900 with a window; a gypsum 2 mm short reported, an OSB 0.5 mm short not; undo with the solids deleted; two walls at a corner joined; Studio solids with their materials and the concrete core; studs in the insulation reported, then a 45x145 frame at 600). Guide: walls.md, import-export.md (en, ro), scene `wall-from-solids`. The import's hint ("these solids look like one layered wall") was left for later.

**Dimension values placed clear of one another, and a grid axis's temporary dimensions (decisions 601 and 602, 15.11.2026; forum topic 78, Rychlas, after the exterior dimensions).** A dimension value that does not fit between its witness lines (its Helvetica width plus 0.4 paper mm a side, `BuildupText.Measure`, in paper mm times the view's scale) is now moved by the engine when the string is drawn (`DimensionTextPlacement`): just outside its segment on the free side (the string's end, or a neighbour whose own value fits, the one with more room first), else onto the rows above and below the line in turn (one text height and gap each, further out first; three each way), the first place that overlaps nothing; staggered places off a row's middle cost a little, so a value keeps over its segment when it can. `Dimensions.Marks` first puts everything that stays into a view-wide `TextRoom` (every string's dimension line, the other strings' extension lines as soft obstacles, the values that fit or were moved by hand, the opening heights), then places each string's moving values, adding them as placed, so the strings of an exterior or room dimension group and any other dimension of the view keep clear of one another; a value never goes across another string's line. Past `textLeader` paper mm from its place it gets a leader from the segment's middle to its nearest point. A value moved by hand (`textAt`) keeps its place and leader as before; **Texts back on the line** hands it back to the placement; opening heights stay where they are under a value placed automatically. The mark's data carries `textAuto` (how far each value was moved), so the value's grip, dragged, moves it from where it is shown. Settings: the project's `dimAutoText` (default on) and `dimTextLeader` (5 mm) in the drafting standards (`standards.options`, carried by `.lapinstandards` files), shown under the tabs of **Annotate → Dimension rules…** and saved in the same undo step as the rules; each dimension style's **Text placement** (`autoText`: project, on, off) and **Leader beyond mm** (`textLeader`, empty: the project's) in Drafting standards → Dimensions, and as overrides (`o.autoText`, `o.textLeader`). On for existing projects too: a value that fits never moves, and `DimensionTextPlacementTests` proves a view whose values all fit (walls, an opening's height, strings above, on and below the line, a vertical string with a typed text and a moved one) draws byte for byte the same with the placement on and off; no `IProjectUpgrade`. Parts' derived dimensions (decision 581) place theirs within their own string. **Temporary dimensions for grid axes**, as doors' and windows' (decision 511): one axis selected in a plan shows its distances to the parallel axes either side (`modules/drafting/gridDims.ts`, `GridAxisDims.tsx`, the drafting module's first plan overlay), standing halfway along the stretch they share; a value typed (sums too) moves the selected axis through `drafting.updateAxis`, the neighbour staying, in one undo step (`edit.batch`), and dimensions held on the axes follow; **move the axes beyond too** beside them is the Properties panel's spacing tick (`useAxesBeyond`, shared), moving the axes past the selected one, away from the neighbour measured to; the padlock makes an aligned dimension held on both axes. An axis of a radial grid (axes from one centre, none parallel to it) shows the angles to its neighbours round the centre, typed in degrees, the axis turned about the centre, the axes beyond up to the fan's widest gap with it. The temporary dimensions' drawing (`Marks`, `Shown`, `ROW_PX`) moved to `viewport/TempDimMarks.tsx` for both, with a value's own format and parser (degrees). Tests: `DimensionTextPlacementTests` (a short segment's value outside it, two short neighbours staggered above and below, a leader past the set distance and the styles' own settings, a moved value untouched, another string's line kept clear of, the unchanged drawing, older standards read on), `DimensionStringTests` (a moved value from its own place), `gridDims.test.ts`; guide: drafting (Automatic text placement, Grid axes → Temporary dimensions), both languages; scenes `dimension-text-placement`, `grid-temp-dimensions`.

**A georeferenced IFC (15.11.2026, decision 603; GitHub issue 27, forum 85).** A model whose location was set still landed at 0,0 in a GIS program: the export wrote IfcMapConversion and IfcProjectedCRS only from a survey point, so a project with a coordinate system but no survey point (or one added and left at 0,0) went out unplaced; IfcSite never carried RefLatitude and RefLongitude; IFC 4.3's map conversion lacked its ScaleY and ScaleZ (eight attributes of ten, refused by a strict reader) and every map conversion left Scale unset; and an imported IFC's map conversion was not read at all (only Lapin's own intent brought a location back). Now the base point takes a latitude and longitude (Manage → Location, WGS 84 degrees; `project.location` `base.latitude/longitude`, null clears them, out of range refused); with a projected system and no survey point, `Location.Survey` is the base point standing on their eastings and northings in that system, so plans, spot coordinates, the map DXF, point clouds, the sun and every export read one tie (`project.location` returns it as `tie`, the interface's `surveyPoint` takes it). `GeoRef.Tie` is the one tie the IFC and Fragments exports write: the survey point, else the latitude and longitude in the project's system, else in the WGS 84 UTM zone of the place (with a note); IfcProjectedCRS by its EPSG name with datum, projection, UTM zone and the metre; IfcMapConversion with Scale 1 (and IFC 4.3's two more scales unset); the site's RefLatitude/RefLongitude as IfcCompoundPlaneAngleMeasure; a note when a survey point in a known system is left at 0,0. The import (`IfcImport.Georeference.cs`) reads IfcMapConversion with its map unit (else the project's), or IFC2X3's ePSet_MapConversion and ePSet_ProjectedCRS on the site or the project, into a survey point at the plan's origin, the angle to true north, the height and the EPSG system Lapin knows, only into a project not yet tied and never over Lapin's own intent; a site's latitude and longitude alone become the base point's. Lapin has no IFC2X3 export, so the ePSets are read only. The angle to true north stays the angle to the map's grid north, as the plans' map coordinates already used it (grid convergence not applied). Checked against IfcOpenShell (a dev tool in a scratch venv, not a dependency). Tests: `IfcGeoreferenceTests` (Stereo 70 from latitude and longitude within 1 cm of PROJ, UTM fallback, IFC 4.3, nothing without a location, IFC4 and IFC2X3 imports, a tied project kept, the round trip). Docs: `docs/user/site.md`, `import-export.md` (en, ro), `docs/spec-ifc.md`.

**Solids behind a wall made of them counted once (decision 604, after decision 600).** The solids a layered wall was made of and keeps hidden (`supersededBy`) were left out of views and exports, but still measured, scheduled, priced, clashed and planned beside the wall. `Superseded.Counts` is now the one rule, read where the model's elements are gathered for everyone after: `Session.Quantities` (which the quantity takeoff, quantity schedules, the cost estimate and the planning's quantities read) gives them no quantities; `ElementFilter.IsListed` (field schedules, the fields queries, every filter) does not list them; `AnalyticalModel.Build` drops what any module contributed for them; clash detection (`ClashModule.IsElement`) and a planning task's elements (`TaskElements.IsCandidate`), which gather elements by rules of their own, skip them; views, mesh exports and the IFC export already did. While the wall exists; deleted, or the solids shown again, they count as before. Test: `WallsFromSolidsTests.The_wall_and_its_hidden_solids_count_each_volume_once` (three Studio solids, 5 × 3 m, 315 mm: the model's volume 4.725 m³ before and after, the solids measured nothing, the takeoff and a field schedule list the wall alone, clash detection takes the wall and not the solids).

**The free edition audit, follow-ups of stage 1 (decision 605, after decision 599).** What stage 1 left for later. `storey.duplicate` copies as Copy to storeys does (`StoreyCopy.CopyOnto`, through element.transform's copy and its link remap): a wall's copy names its roof's copy, a roof's downpipes are copied once with it, a finished room keeps its finish, a stair's copy has its own opening in the floor above, parts go with their assembly (`StoreyDuplicateLinksTests`). MS Project XML dates are read as written, a `Z` or an offset kept out (`DateTimeOffset`, as `IfcImport.IfcWhen`), so a task late in the day no longer falls on another day in the machine's time zone (`MsProjectDatesTests`). A reply's warnings and coordination.runCheck's report (its unchecked pairs) are described for the MCP bridge and Python: `lapin_save` lists a save's warnings, `lapinbim` raises them as `LapinWarning`; the command catalogue reads `a?["key"]` and helpers given `ctx.Model` (40 commands' arguments more). A retaining wall's and a beam-and-block floor's whole set give their `NetVolume` and `GrossVolume` (`ConcreteVolume` kept), a retaining wall's with its material's id, so a quantity takeoff and the estimate count their concrete (audit M2; `ConcreteVolumeTakeoffTests`). The ear clipper is no longer quadratic: it walks a doubly linked ring and finds the corners that could stand in an ear through a z-order index (the published earcut algorithm, reimplemented), and a hole's bridge passes edges over by their bounds and tries the nearest ring vertices first; a ring of 50 000 vertices triangulates in 0.1–0.4 s instead of one to four minutes, 40 000 vertices with 25 holes of 400 in about 0.6 s, the triangles valid as before (`TriangulatorLargeFaceTests`, `GeometryRobustnessTests`). Curves are still divided by `Lapin.Geometry/Chords` alone; the triangulator only takes the rings it is given.

**The free edition audit, stage 2: one element filter and one condition editor (decision 606; audit D3, D4, S10).** Five filter engines chose elements differently by where the filter was written (a type, a door's storey, the materials, an empty rule). Characterization tests were written first (`FilterCharacterizationTests`: what view filters, Quick select, field schedules, task rules, clash search sets and article rules took on one varied model). Then `ElementFilter` + `FieldCondition` (`Lapin.Platform/ElementFilter.cs`) became Lapin's one filter: the dimensions (classes, types, storeys, materials, groups, phases, demolished; by id or name), conditions on any field or facet (`@class`, `@type`, `@storey`, `@material`, `@link`, `@phase`… and `@text:<field>`, read as a tag writes it), fourteen operators (lists, ranges with an open end), all or any, nested groups. View filters and Quick select (`ViewFilters.ToFilter`), task rules (`TaskRule.ToFilter`), the generator's steps and clash search sets (`SearchSets.ToFilter`) are adapters over it, their rules kept in their own words; field schedules use it directly; article rules (a material's category) stay as they were. One storey rule (D4): `Spatial.StoreyOf(…, followHost: true)`, a door or window on its wall's, an opening on what it voids. One element type (`Types.Of`), the copies in the modules over it. What the tests show taken differently is on purpose and written beside them: a reference's file type counts, a door off every storey is on its wall's, a class by its name without Ifc or its label, "1,200" is 1200, an unphased element is in the default phase, task rules name types, storeys and materials by name too, clash sets read parameters as tags write them and an element's layers' and parts' materials. `ViewFilterUpgrade` (an `IProjectUpgrade`, `filterRev` 1) turns a saved view filter's "phase has a value / has none" into the same question on the stored attribute, so it keeps taking what it took. The interface has one editor (`ui/src/shell/ConditionGroupEditor.tsx`, the stored shapes converted by `conditionGroup.ts`) in Visibility/Graphics → Filters, Quick select, clash detection's search sets (with groups), a field schedule's filter (all or any, with groups, stored as `match` and `nested`), a task's rule and a generator's step (all must hold): a field from the picker (the element's facets first, then every field of the project), an operator, a value suggested from the model, two ends or a list; a stored rule goes back unchanged until it is edited, and conditions still being typed are not sent. Tests: `FilterCharacterizationTests` (8), `conditionGroup.test.ts`, `quickSelect.test.ts`; guide scenes `interface-quick-select`, `views-vg-filters`, `clash`, `field-schedule`, `planning-fields`. Docs: `docs/user` interface, views, clash, schedules, planning (en, ro).

**The free edition audit, stage 2: one reader each (decision 607).** The copies the audit of 15.11.2026 found reading the same things in different ways now go through one each. Numbers: `Numbers.Parse` (strict, grouped, leading, first number), so „1 250” is 1250 everywhere and „1,200” always 1.2 (D2). Steel profiles: `Section.Named` reads the built-in library only, so „IPE 300” and „IPE300” are the same EN section with its root radius and an UPN's sloping flanges; `CatalogueSectionUpgrade` brings a section an earlier build stored from the old four-size catalogue to the library's when the project opens, tested on a project saved by the build before (`Fixtures/old-steel-catalogue.lapin`) (D1). XML: `DocText.Xml` for the workbook, SVG and LandXML writers, so a name with a control character no longer gives a workbook Excel refuses (D6). CSV: `CsvText` reads and writes every table (D7). Points: `Args.PointOf`, `TryPoint` and `StoredPoints` for the modules' 40-odd copies, so a coordinate that is not a number is never read as 0 (D8). Colours: `HexColor.IsColour` and `Read`, never `#zzzzzz` (D9). Free names: `Names.Free`, „Name 2”, and „Brick 25 (2)” when the name ends in a digit; Duplicate follows it, an import's copy kept beside stays „Name (2)” (D12). Smaller: `Spatial.Elevation`/`Spatial.Base` and `Numbers.Mm` (half to even, as the copies did). Left, in the audit's §9: three point readers on purpose, three millimetre formatters, D13.

**The free edition audit, stage 2: one set of polygon helpers and one rule for curves (decision 608).** The clips, point-in-polygon tests, areas, centroids, segment distances and line meetings the modules had each written for themselves (audit D10) now go through `Polygon2` and `Polyline2`, and the circles made with a fixed number of sides (D11) through `Chords`. First a pin: `GeometryPinTests` takes a small house (an L of layered walls, one askew, a door, two windows, a floor, a room with its skirting, a ceiling, a roof, a stair and its storage under it) and four views, and checks its quantities, volumes, plan items and drawn lines against the numbers fab28778 gives (408 of them, to 1e-6); it held through every step. The helpers: `Polygon2.ClipHalf` (2D, 3D and any vertex, with a tolerance), `ClipBox`, `Contains` within a tolerance, `Centroid` (about the first vertex, so it holds at georeferenced coordinates); `Polyline2.LineIntersection` (parallel by the sine of the angle, whatever the vectors' lengths) and `SegmentsDistance`; `Chords.Ring` (inscribed, or circumscribed so a pipe still clears its opening) and `Chords.Arc`; `Prism.ExtrudeOriented`; `Measure.Bounds` of several solids; a volume measured about its first vertex, not the origin, so a small body at Stereo 70 coordinates keeps the digits of its volume. While moving the analysis solver onto the shared clip a fault in it showed: a crossing whose fraction rounds to the end was dropped next to the vertex left out, so a slab's tributary piece lost its corner and half its load; fixed in the helper, with a test. Over them: walls, ceilings, roofs, rooms, skirtings, stairs, curtain walls, openings' references, site, scaffolds, underlays, tags, pipes, hatches, linework, sheets, surface hatches, 3D view drawings, room dividers, IFC recognition, mesh clips, the solver's tributary areas, shell pieces, section properties and climate loads, and outlines with arcs (`ArcOutline.Simple` by `Polyline2.Crosses`). By `Chords` now: round openings from clashes, MEP and fan provisions (circumscribed), MEP plan symbols, joinery symbols' swings and roses, Studio's thick rings, the stair's eye, plan builders' and view marks' circles when no count is given; the tests that counted the old sides count Chords'. Dead code removed: `JoineryElevation` (its `Stroke` kept in a file of its own), `Tessellator.TessellateMany`, `Measure.TaggedArea`, `AlignmentJson.ReadVertical`, `PiAlignment.FromPolyline`, `Openings.TypeSpecOf`, `SiteGeometry.Oriented` and `Densify`, `Stations.Displaced`, `Linework.Crossing`, `HatchRegion.Contains`, `SurfaceHatches.ClipBox`, `Standards.LayerLocked`, `CdeRevisions.Preliminary`, `FieldAliases.IsAlias`, `LineworkConversion.Option`, the LAZ decoder's `ReadBit`, `PointStore.SpacingOf` (none had a caller here or in the private modules); `Chaining` stays, the rail module uses it. Left, in the audit's §10: the hidden-line clips in `Projection.cs` (shared, a section's roof lines came out 5 cm shorter in the pin), a curved section's pieces (`SectionPaths.SampleArc`, 7.5° steps; Chords would give three times as many cuts), and the 14 catalogue commands nothing calls. Tests: `GeometryPinTests`, `Polygon2SharedTests`, `SharedHelperTests`, `RoundOpeningChordsTests`.

**Offset outline and 3D face and height grips (a user's request: "live offset the edges of walls, slabs, roof contours and snap them in 3D the way Archicad does it"; decisions 609 and 610).** `ArcOutline.Offset` moves every edge of a closed outline at once, outward or inward (Archicad's Offset all edges): straight edges parallel, meeting again at a mitre; an arc concentric about its own centre; where an arc meets an edge at a corner the offset edges meet where they cross when the outline shrinks, and a round of the distance about the old corner joins them when it grows (that corner becomes two; `From` tells each new edge's old one, so a roof's edge rules follow). It keeps the outline's way round and refuses an edge that would vanish or turn back, an arc shrunk to nothing, an outline crossing itself or turning inside out. The core query `outline.offset` {points, arcs?, distance} returns it (or `{problem}`) without changing the model; the element's own update command writes it, one undo step. In the interface every `outlineGrips` edge middle lends `offsetAll` (the outline, its outward normal, its save): Shift-drag offsets the whole outline live in plans and in 3D, the engine's result previewed solid with the distance at the cursor (or the refusal in red), the distance typed too; the selection's right-click menu (plans and 3D) has **Offset outline…** (+ outward, − inward) for floors, floor openings, ceilings, roofs, rooms, balconies, platforms and plantings, several at once in one edit. A new module contract `grips3d` (Grip3D: a point, the line it moves along, a step by a distance) gives 3D-only grips, drawn by `Grips3D.tsx` as a disc with a double arrow and dragged along their line (the line's point nearest the cursor's ray, to the centimetre), snapped by `snap3d` to other elements' corners, middles and edges (and, across a wall, their faces), the distance typed in the live box: a wall's top (its top's offset when tied to a storey, else its own height, `setWallTop`), its two faces (only the one turned to the camera; the wall moved sideways with its joined ends following, a curved wall's radius about its centre, `updateWall`), a floor's top (its offset from the storey; its thickness stays its type's, as Properties has it) and a roof's eave (its `eaveOffset`). Plan behaviour is unchanged. Tests: `OutlineOffsetTests`, `grips3d.test.ts`; guide scene `grips-3d-offset` (floors-roofs, views, walls pages in every language).
Web apps on other sites connecting to the desktop engine, stage 1 (16.11.2026, decision 611, `docs/plan-web-connect.md`). The first is ginnung.tech; nothing third-party is in the code. `/ws` and `/health` now check the Host header (a loopback name and the engine's port, against DNS rebinding), and origins are compared exactly (`WebOrigins`: scheme, host and port parsed and equal, as a browser serializes them), so `http://localhost.evil.com` and `http://127.0.0.1.evil.com`, which the old prefix test let through, are refused. The app's own pages and programs without an Origin work as before; any other https origin is accepted but paired with nothing (`WebConnect`): it may call only `engine.hello`, redacted to engine, ok, product, version and its pairing, and `pair.request`, which asks this computer's windows (event `pair.ask`) until the user answers (`pair.answer`: once, always or deny; read, with edit as a separate grant; 2 minutes, then `timeout`; a refused or revoked site is not asked about again for 10 minutes). Let in, a site is a guest like a shared session's, through `EngineDispatcher.AllowedRemote`: the new role `reader` (a viewer that does not even mark site progress) or `editor`, with its own participant and undo; the host's own calls are closed to it but hello, asking and presence (`WebConnect.PairedOwnMethods`), and a connection not let in has the role `none`, refused everything below the gate too. "Always allow" writes `connected-sites.json` in the user's folder (`ConnectedSites`), which `sites.list`, `sites.set` and `sites.revoke` manage (revoking closes that origin's connections at once, 1008); the `sites` event keeps Preferences up to date, which is the next stage, with the prompt's dialog. Incoming messages are bounded (`EngineHub.MaxOwnMessage` 512 MB, `MaxSiteMessage` 100 MB, `MaxUnpairedMessage` 64 KB), the connection closed with 1009 past them. Tests: `WebConnectTests`.

**A curved section laid by the one rule for curves (16.11.2026, decision 612; the audit's §10).** A section along an arc was cut into pieces of 7.5° (4 to 48); it now follows `Chords` like every other curve, through `PathCurve.Stations`: no piece more than a millimetre off the arc, so a large arc gets more pieces and a drawing that matches the plan's own curves. The user chose it when the audit's geometry stage left it open. `SectionPathTests` counts the pieces by the rule; sections, sheets and the geometry pin are unchanged otherwise.

**The free edition audit, step 7: the fixed schedules as shipped field-schedule templates, parts as rows (audit S1, decision 613).** The schedules that list one row per element or per group of elements are field schedules made from templates the modules ship (`ShippedScheduleTemplate`, a service: id, label, the fixed kind it stands for, a `.lapinschedule` object): sunshades, gates, balconies, skirtings (architecture), elevators (transport), furniture, MEP equipment, scaffolds (site works), plantings (civil), roof timber (architecture) and timber (wood). First the characterization: `ShippedScheduleTemplateTests` builds a varied model of each kind and checks that the field schedule the template makes gives the fixed schedule's headings, rows, order and totals cell for cell. To give them the field schedule grew, all optional and kept by `.lapinschedule` templates: `source`, the parts it lists instead of elements (`IPartSource`: a module's parts of an element, `ElementPart` with fields of their own, read before the element's through `ElementFields.Own`; rows keyed `<element>#<part>`); `groupColumns: false` (only the columns, a group's value through `first`); `sort` (fields, ascending or descending, ties in the model's order); `subtotals` (a totals line per value, after the rows: timber per section and grade); `totals: "always"` (under one row too); the `rows` aggregate (the items in a row); a column `format` (`0.##`); and decimals written as Lapin's tables write them (`0.00`, a half rounded at 15 digits, not `F2`). What the fixed tables worked out is now fields any tag, filter or schedule reads (`DerivedFields`: `shadeKind`, `gateSize`, `liftCar`, `balconyArea`, `skirtingHeight`, `furnitureSize`, `mepDuty`…; `QueriedField` for those needing geometry: a piece of furniture's room, a scaffold as built). The parts sources: `plants` (a planting's plants), `roofTimber` (a framed roof's members) and `timber` (a timber-frame wall's, a joisted floor's and a roof's, a CLT roof's wall plates only). `architecture.importSchedule {builtin}` makes one (by its id or the fixed kind), `schedule.shippedTemplates` and `schedule.partSources` list them; the ribbon buttons, the browser's **+** list and Construction → Schedules… make the field schedule (`createScheduleOf`), their tool ids unchanged, so shortcuts and My tools keep working; `architecture.createSchedule {kind}` still makes the fixed table for scripts. No project upgrade: a saved fixed schedule keeps its kind and opens as it was, with what was typed in its cells (keyed by type, which a grouped field row is not); tested on a project saved and opened again. The one difference left: the MEP equipment total no longer writes "pcs" under the units. Not converted (they need a form a field schedule has no words for): the joinery and lintel cards, the bar bending schedule (its totals per diameter in the shape column), formwork (its sets and purchases per type), the cost forms, and the module tables that compute across elements (kitchen units, cut list, wall sweeps' totals per kind, fans' rooms, MEP runs, steel, CLT, boards, panels, rooms, finishes, under-stair). In the interface the field schedule's editor lists the parts (**Rows**), sorts, subtotals, hides the grouping columns, keeps the total under one row and takes a format; **From template…** lists **Shipped with Lapin** first; the user's own templates stay as they were (Template…). Guide: the schedules page (English, Romanian) and the scene `shipped-schedules`.

**Convert to… as the one place that makes something of drawn shapes (audit S12, decision 614).** The audit found eight places that make something of drafted shapes: Draft → Convert to… and Convert layers…, Studio's RING/BOX/TWALL laying native walls on a plan (`architecture.wallsAlong`), Steel → Convert to member (`steel.convertSketch`), Site → Road from linework and Breakline from linework (`civil.fromLinework`), Save as symbol…, Make family from sketch… and Profiles → From a drafted outline. They now go through Convert to…: Studio's thick commands on a plan run `drafting.convert` with the walls converter (which gained the `bottom` and `top` floors of a closed shape, BOX's), the drafting kept as before; Convert to member opens the dialog on the new **Steel members from outlines** converter (a band read by `SketchMembers`, through `steel.convertSketch`); Road from linework and Breakline from linework open it on the shape picked, with **Road**, **Path** and **Breakline** converters that group the shapes by the chain `LineworkElements` follows (one `civil.fromLinework` per chain; a module's own kind, rail's track, still goes straight there, and `Lapin.Civil.LineworkConverters.Along` is the hook for its converter); symbols, families and profiles are listed below the elements as hand-offs (`ui/src/modules/core/convertHandoffs.ts`) that run their own dialogs. The tool ids, shortcuts and commands stay. New converters, each in its module: **Ceiling** (a circle round, by `ArcOutline.Circle`'s arcs; floors too now), **Roof** (the style chosen, choices with their names: `LineworkOption.ChoiceLabels`), **Balcony** and **Skirting** in architecture; **Terrain**, **Platform**, **Road**, **Path**, **Breakline** in civil; **Steel members** (each straight piece of the profile chosen, `steel.addMember` taking a `section` name), **Steel columns** (`structure.addColumn` taking a `section` name: the project's column type of it, else a new one, as Steel's Column tool names it) and **Steel members from outlines** in steel. A roof, a balcony, a skirting and a steel member refuse an arc or a circle with the reason (`LineworkConversion.Straight`). The platform gained `LineworkConverter.Together`: a converter that makes one element of many shapes (a terrain of all its contours, a road of lines end to end) checks each shape, then gives each group's steps to its first shape, the others marked `joined` in the preview ("Made with row n"); `LineworkShape.Heights` carries a DXF's heights. The dialog groups the targets by module and opens on the target a shortcut asks for. Tests: `LineworkConvertTargetsTests` (14, one for shapes drawn on a level's default plan converted from its plan view), `thick.test.ts`; guide: drafting (Convert to…), steel, site pages in English and Romanian, scenes `convert-targets` (new) and `steel` (Convert to member through the dialog).

**One envelope and one conflict handling for the library files (audit D13; decision 615).** Lapin reads and writes eighteen kinds of library file for the user's own content, each with its own reader before: symbols (`.lapinsymbols`), type packs (`.lapintypes`), the wall and floor types' JSON (`lapin-types`), drafting standards (`.lapinstandards`), AutoCAD's `.lin` and `.pat`, rule packs (`.lapinrules`), shared parameters (`.lapinparams`), field aliases (`.lapinaliases`), title blocks (`lapin.titleBlock`), bar shapes (`.lapinbarshapes`), parts (`.lapinpart`), families (`.lapinfamily`), schedule templates (`.lapinschedule`), My furniture (`.lapinlib`), profile libraries (`.lapinprofiles`), price lists (`.lapinprices`) and mappings (`.lapinmapping`); only the type libraries shared a conflict handling. `LibraryFile` (Lapin.Platform) is the inventory (`Formats`: format id, extension, what it is called, the payload's version, its owner) and the one envelope: `format` and `version` stay where every build reads them, and a header `lapin: {envelope: 1, app, created, source}` follows (the app version, the UTC moment, the project's name); `Read` validates (JSON, an object, the format, not a newer payload or envelope: one sentence for every kind, naming its extension) and applies a format's upgrade steps (`Upgrade(format, from, step)`), `Write` puts the header first after format and version. A file without the header is envelope 0 and reads as it always did; AutoCAD's text formats carry none. Every engine writer goes through it (symbols, types, standards, rules, parameters, aliases, title blocks, bar shapes, parts, families, schedule templates, furniture, the profile libraries a firm folder is written), and so do the interface's (price lists, mappings, title blocks this computer keeps; `ui/src/platform/libraryFile.ts`). `LibraryMerge` is the one conflict handling: an item is new, the same, the same name (keep both, „Name (2)”, by default) or the same thing by its id (a drafting standard's style, a line type, a pattern, a shared parameter, a field alias, a bar shape's code, a furniture entry: updated in place by default); the choices are always `both`, `update` (read „replace” too) and `skip` (read „keep” too), a row may offer fewer (a bar shape and an alias have no keeping both), and `Decide` takes the item's choice, else the one for all, else the command's default from before, else the row's. Each kind registers an `ILibraryFormat` (its preview rows and its import), so `library.preview` {text, ext?} and `library.import` {text, ext?, choices?, all?} serve every one of them, and `library.formats` lists the inventory; the kinds' own commands keep their arguments and defaults (without choices `rules.import`, `titleBlock.import`, `studio.importPart` and `architecture.importSchedule` still add, the copy now named „Name (2)”; the symbols', standards' and line types' copies are „Name (2)” too, a pattern's stays `NAME_2`), and My furniture has `furnitureLibrary.preview` and choices on `furnitureLibrary.import`. In the interface one component shows it everywhere (`ImportPreview`, `ChoiceSelect`, `ChoiceAll`, `ChoicesNote` in `ui/src/platform/ImportPreview.tsx`): inline in Symbols, Drafting standards, Line types and the type libraries' choices, and as the **Import preview** dialog (`LibraryImportHost`, over any other) that the firm folders, the Catalog, the extensions' Add to this project and the Rules, Shared parameters, Field names, Title blocks, Bar shapes, Families and My furniture imports open when something differs (straight in otherwise), with the file's header (*Written by Lapin … on …, from …*) and what was done counted after. The fixtures are the files the build before saved (`engine/tests/Lapin.Engine.Tests/Fixtures/libraries/v0`, every kind): `LibraryFileTests` (each read as envelope 0 and brought in, each written now with the header and taken back as the same, newer files refused, an upgrade step, the inventory, the choices by name and by id, a type pack's material, My furniture) and `libraryFile.test.ts` (every reader of the interface on the same files with and without the header, a price list's and a mapping's round trip, the preview's defaults, the browser's furniture merge). Of the three millimetre texts left from stage 2, a bar's diameter (`RebarShapes.Dia`) now goes through `Numbers.Mm` (the same text for every diameter, `MillimetreTextTests`); the joinery schedule's local `Mm` and `DocNumbers` under 1 cm stay: the one way writes „0” where they write „-0” for a value between −0.5 mm and 0. Guide: Interface → *Importing a library file* (scene `library-import-preview`), Drafting (symbols, line types, standards) and My furniture, in both languages.


**The free edition audit, stage 3: one Opening tool, one Place catalogue, Create similar everywhere, one rule for Enter and Esc (decision 616; audit S14, S15 and the mismatches users feel).** UI only; the engine's commands and saved projects are unchanged. **Opening** (`architecture.opening`, `ui/src/modules/architecture/openingTool.tsx`) replaces seven buttons: its first choice, in the command line's bar and at the head of Properties, is what to put by host (in a wall a door, a window, a bay window or an opening of a shape, the shape now also a Circle, which the engine already took; in a floor an opening; in a roof a roof window or a dormer). Each kind is the tool it was (its gesture, ghost, type, Space, command), read through getters and a `mode` so a change starts the gesture over; the old ids (`architecture.door`, `.window`, `.bayWindow`, `.wallOpening`, `.slabOpening`, `.roofWindow`, `.roofDormer`) are `legacyTools` that set the kind, with their letters (DR, WN, BW, WO, SH), their `typed` (the catalog's Door and Window cards) and the default quick slots. Shaft openings (elevators) and the clash report's openings stay where they are: they are made from other elements, not drawn. **The Place catalogue** (`shell/PlaceCatalog.tsx`, Manage → Place from catalogue…) gathers a new module contract, `placeSources` (an id, a label, a hook giving entries, the editor dialog): the project's types (as the browser's Catalog tab), the symbols (Lapin's, the project's with the DXF blocks, this computer's), the components (a card copies an instance from a base point), the families, the furniture (the project's types, My furniture, Lapin's kinds) and the MEP equipment (every role of Place node), with one search over all (every word in the name, the group or the description); Manage → Symbols…, Annotate → Symbols…, Manage → Components, Studio → Families… and Interior → My furniture… open it on their source, and its button opens the old dialog, which stays the editor. **Create similar** asks `similarFor(node)`: a module's `similar` by class, then a new `similarFor` (MEP nodes by their role, runs by system, fans and carriers by type; Studio's placed parts and primitives), then any element of a type the tool placing that type's class (`typed`); new `similar` entries for structure (columns, beams and pad footings now take a type in the bar, `structure/typePick.tsx`; a strip with its wall), furniture (the Place tool set to the piece's kind and type, a kitchen's tool), civil (platforms, ditches, paths and roads, retaining walls, plantings, entourage), rebar (the bar's shape tool, a shape of the project's, runs), loads and wall or floor openings. **Enter and Esc** follow one rule (`viewport/gestureKeys.ts`, in plans, drawings and 3D): Enter finishes (closes an outline, ends a polyline, ends a chain, ends a pick-then-point tool at its pick), Esc cancels the step in progress and then leaves the tool; a chain used to end only with Esc, and a pick-then-point tool took Esc as Enter. **Labels**: Architecture's Join is **Join walls**, the Linework group's Join, Trim, Extend and Reset say **linework** (ids unchanged). **Manage → All types** lists every tab's Types tools in one pulldown. Checked and left: Tag a parameter already uses the app's dialog (stage 1's `prompts.ts`); room colours already have one home (View, the ribbon stage); wood frames have no grips because the frame is a rule on the wall type, worked out, with no element or command to drag (they follow the wall's grips); the dimension string keeps what was drawn on Esc (a tool or command started meanwhile also ends it); the crowded MEP and Studio tabs are untouched. Tests: `openingAndPlace.test.ts`, `gestureKeys.test.ts`; guide scenes `opening-tool`, `enter-esc`, `place-catalogue` and the entry points' scenes (doors-windows, floors-roofs, interface, walls, drafting, families, furniture, groups pages, in English and Romanian).
**Overlapping sun shadows (decision 617, upstream issue #14, third bullet).** Two flush faces that overlap in a view (two walls, a wall and a slab's edge) are neither nearer than the other, so each kept the overlap and each received the same sun shadow there. The drawing fills its shadows even-odd, so the two areas cancelled out and the overlap came out unpainted. `SunShadows.Draw` now hides a face by the flush faces before it in the drawing's order (the same plane and direction, within 0.1 mm), so the overlap is kept once, by the first face's element: the owner of an area is still the element whose face it lies on. The change is local to `SunShadows`; `ViewFaces`, the surface hatches and every visibility rule are as they were (a flush overlap may still be hatched twice). Tests: six in `SunShadowTests`, painted as the renderers do, every outline filled even-odd: overlapping walls paint what one continuous wall paints, with each area's owner; walls with a gap leave it unpainted; a shadow nested in another; a window-shaped hole kept open over overlapping walls; a face in shade whole; an elevation rendered through `Views.Render`. Left in #14: storey plans, developed sections, caching the shadows per view.
**Three upstream issues from outside contributors (decision 618, issues #33, #42, #43).** #33: `element.removePset` removes a whole property set (every parameter in it) from elements or types in one undo step, with a button per set in Properties → Parameters. #42: axis grids now hide in 3D through Visibility / Graphics, and paths and roads hide with the topography. For that, `IFCPAVEMENT`, `IFCKERB`, `IFCROAD` and `LAPINSITEFEATURE` moved into the `topography` category in `LineStyles.CategoryOf`; this is a behaviour change that is not only for 3D: 2D plans, Manage → Object styles and saved graphics sets now treat those elements as Topography (a graphics set that hid or restyled Topography now affects them; one that did not mention them draws them as before). #43: the **Section box** chip has **Hide box** / **Show box**, which hides the box's frame and handles while the cut stays active (`useSectionBox.hidden`). The hidden state is the viewport's and is not saved with a 3D view: the saved box (`view.setSectionBox`) and the session memory (`viewState3d`) hold the box only, and keeping `hidden` would need the box's engine DTO to change. The PRs were merged without their interface strings; the follow-up adds them to all ten languages (the title with the property set's name is a pattern, so the name stays untranslated). Guide: views, site and interface pages in English and Romanian.

**Element edits: Trim/Extend, Split, Offset on walls, beams, strip footings, floors and ceilings (decision 619, audit §6 and §15).** The platform's `PathEdits` gives every path element (walls, beams, strip footings, straight or curved) `element.split` (two elements, the first keeping its GlobalId, the second a copy with type, storey, own layer usage, properties and groups; the far joins move with it and the parts join in line), `element.trimExtend` (Revit's Trim/Extend to a boundary element's axis or near/far face, or a drafted line or arc; curved elements go on round their circle; at a same-class axis the two are joined) and `element.offset` (a parallel or concentric copy, by axis or between faces). Modules keep their own data right through `IPathPieces`; architecture's `WallPieces` moves openings to the part they fall on (refusing one across the cut or cut away), keeps layer ends and extents with their ends, cuts own sweeps and rebuilds type sweeps. `OutlineCuts` splits an arc outline by a line crossing it twice; `architecture.splitOutline` and `trimOutline` use it for floors and ceilings, openings going with their part. UI: Modify → Trim/Extend elements (TRIMEXTEND), Split element (SPLITELEMENT, Intersection), Offset element (OFFSET takes elements); TRIM/EXTEND now reach walls from a plan; the Joins group's Trim/Extend became aliases. Left: railings, MEP runs, roads, roofs and rooms, ceiling fixtures and other wall-hosted elements (they stay on the first part), extents on curved slab edges.

**Annotations drawn on a sheet itself (audit §6, decision 620).** A sheet's paper is a view: `paper:<sheetId>` (`PaperViews`), of kind `paper`, at 1:1, its coordinates the paper's in metres from its lower-left corner, y up (paper mm / 1000), so every Draft and Annotate command works on it unchanged and a typed length in mm is paper mm. `Views.Find` makes it like a storey's default plan; `Views.Render` draws nothing of the model on it, only the marks of the annotations whose view it is (the modules' other marks are filtered out), legends and scale bars, and on the screen the sheet as it prints under them (`Sheets.Compose(annotations: false)`, the views' boxes on a `VIEWPORT` layer and the paper's edge, both snapped to with `FRAME` and `TITLE`). `Sheets.Compose` adds what is drawn on the paper over everything, in paper mm, so the sheet, its PDF, Print and DXF print it; a sheet with nothing on its paper composes byte for byte as before. New on any view: a **leader note** (TEXT's `leader`, arrow and bends, moved with the text), a **revision cloud** (a closed shape's `cloud`, its scallop in paper mm; REVCLOUD, Properties → Revision cloud) and a **scale bar** (IfcAnnotation `SCALEBAR`, at the view's scale; on paper a viewport's, its own or the first view's; a round length about 80 mm long). On paper only: texts read the sheet's `{…}` tokens as title blocks do, and a **legend** (`SHEETLEGEND`) lists live the materials the sheet's views cut (fill and hatch swatches) or their lines by role, type and width. What is on a sheet belongs to it: `sheet.duplicate` copies it with the symbols placed on the sheet (decision 591), deleting the sheet deletes them (`PaperViews.Deletion`). The interface: **Annotate** in the sheet's bar (or a double-click outside the views) opens the paper in the drawing pane with a **Sheet** button back, zoom up to 400 000 px/m; Annotate → Sheets has Annotate sheet, Legend, Scale bar and Revision cloud; Text has Leader note. `SheetAnnotationTests` (9), the `sheet-annotations` scene, Sheets in every language and Drafting in English and Romanian. Left: grips for a leader's arrow, symbols placed on the sheet by paper mm still picked only on the sheet (not on its paper), the legend's texts not translated, and annotations not in the issue's drawing hash.
**One Dimension tool, one Tag tool (decision 621, audit 2026-11-15 S9).** Interface only; engine commands, typed commands and saved dimensions and tags are unchanged. Inventory: the ribbon had Aligned, Linear, Angle, Room dimensions and Exterior dimensions as buttons and Dimension rules, Add points, Remove points, Radius, Diameter, Arc length, Slope, Ordinate and Spot coordinate small, Datum → Level, plus Civil → Spot level (a terrain element, left), a chain mode (Continuous) and the generated grid-axis and opening temporary dimensions (overlays, left); tags were Tag (TAG, with field, any parameter and tag types, the build-up types among them), Tag a parameter, Door & window tags (all of a plan), Room tag… (what every room stamp shows) and keynotes only as a `{classification.code}` field. Baseline dimensions did not exist. **Dimension** (`drafting.dimension`, DI, the new typed **DIMENSION**/**DIM**; DIM used to be an alias of DIMALIGNED) starts smart: two points make an aligned dimension (linear when the bar says so), a drafted line or polyline side clicked off its snaps is measured end to end and a second line clicked instead of the dimension line's place gives the angle between them, a circle or arc (clicked on, or snapped to its centre or a quadrant) gets its radius, diameter or arc length as the bar says, and Enter after the first point writes that point's level. The bar (`SmartDimensionOptions`) chooses any kind outright, the string mode (one by one, chain, and the new **baseline**: each next point measured from the first, a dimension of its own 7 mm on paper further out, one undo step each), and holds Rooms, Exterior, Add points, Remove points and Rules…; typed kind words (AN, RA, LE…) and B/C/S work at the first point; every kind asks for the next one. The old kind buttons are `legacyTools` that start the tool at their kind (ids, letters, quick slots, My tools); Datum → Level stays and starts it at Level. **Tag** (`cad.tag`, TG) has a source first: element (as before, plus All doors & windows), room (a room's field, clicks outside rooms ignored, Room stamps… opens the stamp dialog), parameter (typed in the bar, no dialog), material / layers (a build-up tag type) and keynote (`classification.code` or `.title`); Tag a parameter is an alias at the parameter source. New strings in all ten languages; guide: drafting (all languages that have it), site and doors-windows mentions; scenes `dimension-tool`, `tag-sources`; tests `smartDimension.test.ts`, `dimensionsAndTags.test.ts`.

**Tool Properties without the options twice (16.11.2026, decision 622; the user's choice).** The Tool Properties tab (forum #122) drew each tool's options a second time beside the command line's bar, so fourteen guide scenes found two of every control and the Opening tool's kind picker was gone from the panel. Now the tool's options stay in the command line's bar only; the tab shows what the new element will be: the tool's own properties panel (its type and values), else its type picker, in the same `aside.props` panel as Properties.


**Outlines, wave 2 (audit S4, decisions 623–627).** The rest of S4 after decisions 589/590/609, five pieces, each tested and committed on its own.
(1) **Arcs on the other outlines** (623): drawn rooms, drawn balconies, platforms and parcels keep `arcs` beside their corners as floors do (a parcel `parcelArcs` beside `parcel`), laid in chords by the one rule and measured exactly (a room's GrossFloorArea and the IFC space's profile as the true curve, a balcony slab's area, ParcelArea and ParcelPerimeter; a platform levels the ground along its arcs). `architecture.roundCorner`/`chamferCorner`/`setEdgeArc` take drawn rooms and balconies (a balcony keeps its drawing order, its first edge against the building); `civil.roundCorner`/`chamferCorner`/`setEdgeArc` take platforms and parcels. A room found from its walls takes no arcs. Roofs still refuse them: their edge rules are per straight edge.
(2) **Holes of their own** (624): a floor, a ceiling or a platform keeps islands of its outline (`holes`, `holeArcs`), not floor openings: floors are built round them layer by layer (`Prism.ExtrudeFootprint` with holes), ceilings (a plain board round them, a grid cut by them), platforms keep the ground inside them; areas leave them out exactly. Each hole is checked inside the outline and apart from the others; an outline changed so that it no longer holds one is refused. `addOutlineHole`/`removeOutlineHole` (architecture, civil), the corner commands with `{hole}`, transforms carry them, IFC writes them as more curves of the footprint. **Edit sketch** draws every loop and finishes several closed loops as the outline (the largest) and its holes; drawn rooms and platforms are edited as sketches too. UI: the Opening tool's **Hole in the outline**, Site's **Hole in platform**, hole grips with **Remove hole**.
(3) **One outline gesture with sources** (625): every outline tool shares the bar (polygon with arcs, rectangle, circle, ellipse where the element takes one) and takes a whole outline with a click: **Inside walls** (`architecture.outlineInside`), **From drawn shape** (the smallest closed drafted polyline, circle or ellipse round the point), **From room**.
(4) **A true ellipse** (626): Draft → **Ellipse** (`ELLIPSE`, `ELL`; an elliptical arc with A), stored as an ellipse, laid by `Chords.EllipseArc`, snapped at its centre and axes' ends, with grips; Trim cuts it into elliptical arcs, Offset refuses it, Extend and Fillet take lines and arcs only. DXF import keeps ELLIPSE as an ellipse. Outlines take elliptical edges (`ellipses`: centre, major semi-axis, ratio, way round) with an exact area (½∮x dy − y dx) and length (Gauss–Legendre): floors, floor openings, ceilings and drawn rooms, through the bar, From drawn shape, Convert to… and Edit sketch (elliptical arcs there are left unconstrained: the solver knows no ellipse). IFC writes the outline as an IfcCompositeCurve with IfcTrimmedCurve on IfcEllipse. Left: DXF export still writes every curve (circles too) as short lines; an elliptical edge in a hole; offsetting an elliptical outline.
(5) **A wall opening of its own outline** (627): `addWallOpening {wallId, profile, arcs?}` (along the wall, up from its base): the bounds are its place and size, the outline is kept relative to them (`shape: outline`, `profile`, `profileArcs`), the joinery outline code cuts the wall by it, a resize stretches it, another head replaces it; the IFC opening's body is its prism and its intent brings it back. **Convert to… → Wall opening** on a section or an elevation makes it from a closed drafted shape in the straight wall the view looks at. Left: a drawing gesture for it directly in an elevation (the Opening tool works in plan).
Saved projects open unchanged: every new attribute is absent when unused. Tests: OutlineArcsTests, OutlineHolesTests, EllipseOutlineTests, DraftEllipseTests, OutlinedWallOpeningTests; scenes rooms-curved, floors-holes, outline-sources, drafting-ellipse, wall-opening-outline.

**Drafting, wave 3: the rest of sheets, dimensions and outlines (audit §16–§18; decisions 628–631, 16.11.2026).** (1) **DXF writes real curves** (628): a pen keeps, beside its chords, the exact curves they were laid along (`PenCurve`: a centre and conjugate axes, so every affine step on the way — a sheet's scale and shift, a turned plan, the paper's flip, map coordinates — maps it exactly); the DXF writer writes the chords that still lie on a curve as that curve (CIRCLE, ARC, or ELLIPSE by its principal axes, a mirrored one run the other way) and the rest as lines, so a viewport that cuts a circle keeps its piece as an arc. Drafted circles, arcs and ellipses carry them, and on plans so do the arc and elliptical edges of drawn outlines (floors, ceilings, openings, their own holes). The screen, PDF and SVG still draw the chords (one rule: Chords); a DXF written and read back keeps its circles, arcs and ellipses. Left: joining lines and arcs into bulged polylines. (2) **Ellipses edited** (629): Offset gives a polyline within a millimetre of the true offset curve (each chord halved until its middle lies that close), refused inwards at or past its tightest radius of curvature; a line meets an ellipse exactly (Trim by it, Extend to it); an elliptical arc extends round its ellipse; Fillet joins a line and an ellipse by the circle touching both on the sides picked (found on the ellipse's offset curve). A floor's or a ceiling's own hole may run along an ellipse (`holeEllipses`): measured exactly, built, moved and mirrored, kept by grip edits and Edit sketch, IFC IfcEllipse, DXF ELLIPSE, drawn with the Ellipse source of Hole in the outline. An outline with an elliptical edge is not offset as a whole (refused with its reason; its grips do not offer it). Left: ellipses in the constraint solver. (3) **Sheet annotations** (630): grips on a note's arrow tip and bends (`drafting.moveLeaderPoint`); a legend's own words in its language (`lang`, from the interface's when placed; `DrawingWords`); a sheet's own symbols taken by a click on its paper and moved there in paper mm; the issue's drawing hash counts what is drawn on the paper and the sheet's symbols by their attributes, so a sheet changed only by them is flagged since its issue, a new date changing nothing, and a sheet with none hashing as before. (4) **Smart Dimension** (631): in a section or an elevation, a bare click on a level's line writes its spot elevation there, without Enter. Saved projects open unchanged. Tests: DxfCurveExportTests, DraftEllipseEditTests, OutlineHoleEllipseTests, SheetAnnotationRestTests, smartDimension.test.ts (DxfImportTests and DraftEllipseTests updated for the new behaviour); scenes floors-holes, sheet-annotations.

**Families replaced by parts, and parts holding native elements (audit S6, decisions 632 and 633, 16.11.2026; `docs/plan-studio-parametric.md` §10).** (1) The Families dialog and its corner editor are gone: Studio → Parts… lists the project's parts (new, import of `.lapinpart` and `.lapinfamily`, export, keep, firm folders, rename, IFC class, delete, place), and Edit part opens the part's edit mode on a placed part (placing one at the origin when none stands). `studio.family`, `studio.families` and `studio.familyFromSketch` stay as aliases of the part tools; the engine's family commands are unchanged. Saved parts of formula corners open drawn from sketches of their own (`PartSketchUpgrade`, the same ids, values and volumes), and what the interface makes is sketched at once (`constrain: true` on `createPart` and `partFromSketch`; the library import). (2) A part holds native elements: `studio.addPartMembers` takes walls (with their doors), floors, furniture or equipment standing beside a placed part into its definition as snapshots in that part's frame; every placed part builds them as real elements aggregated by it (`IfcRelAggregates`, ids kept per slot), carried by the platform's own move from the frame they were taken in, so they move, turn, mirror, copy and delete with their part; an edit by any command in one placed part is written back and reaches the others in the same undo step; `setPartMemberDrives` drives a member's length or own numbers by formulas of the part's parameters; `removePartMembers` lets them go. IFC writes them as their own classes, parts of the placed part (a part of members only as an IfcElementAssembly), counted once. Interface: Add to part, the part tree's Elements section with drives, members pickable in the edit mode and picking their part outside it. Components, groups and apartment types stay separate (their semantics differ, §10.3); they already share the Place catalogue and the transforms. Tests `PartMemberTests` (8); `PartTypeIfcTests`' package check now compares the reopened keys between two openings (the definition is sketched once). Left: joins to walls outside a part, hand edits of driven values, members' types in another project, members through an IFC round trip, nesting.

**Element edits, the rest of decision 619 (decisions 634–637, audit §14, §15 and §21).** Split, Trim/Extend and Offset now take elements along a polyline of points and arcs through the platform's `IPolylinePieces` (`PolylineEdits`, behind the same `element.split`, `trimExtend`, `offset`): railings (an arc cut into two arcs of its circle), MEP runs (levels, branches and end connections on their part; a split puts a coupling, the new inline role `coupling`, IfcPipeFitting CONNECTOR, whose ports sit at its middle so the parts' lengths add up; paired runs or `coupling: false` leave open ends; a trimmed end lets go of its port) and corridors (paths, roads, retaining walls, ditches: stations go on from the cut, PVIs kept whole, sections and joints on their part, curves at inner vertices; a cut in a curve, and axes of IFC 4.3 segments, refused; the terrain cut by each part). A platform `ISplitFollower` tells the modules of the parts: architecture's `HostedParts` puts finishes on straight wall sides and floor faces on their part (re-measured, running on across the cut unless a split line stops them), sunshades along a wall (across the cut: refused) and balconies (cut away: free); rebar is hosted by the part its point lies on, formwork forms each part. A ceiling's grid keeps its origin and its fixtures go to their part; a floor's layer extents on curved edges follow their arc (`RemapEdges` with rises). The outline cut moved to the platform (`OutlineSplits`, `element.splitOutline` / `trimOutline`, `IOutlinePieces`; `architecture.splitOutline` stays): own holes go to their part, elliptical outlines are refused, and it takes roofs (each part's edge rules written out, the cut a gable without overhang, allowed only where each part's solved planes stand within a millimetre of the roof's; barrel roofs refused; openings, downpipes and walls attached by name follow), drawn rooms (next number), platforms and parcels (trim only). Wood: a wall's own exceptions to its type's frame (`frameStuds` {add, remove}), `wood.editStuds` and `wood.frameGrip`, the frame elevation's grips for studs, outer kings and outer jacks (the opening's own rule); a split wall keeps them on its parts. Saved projects open unchanged: every new attribute is absent when unused. Tests: PolylineEditTests, HostedSplitTests, WoodStudEditTests; scenes railing-split, roof-split, wood-frame-grips. Left: a curved wall's finishes stay on its first part; MEP couplings for paired runs; cutting IFC 4.3 alignment segments; roofs cut where the planes would change; adding a stud by a grip (only by command).

**Audit wave 2: schedules, imports, Convert to…, crowded tabs (16.11.2026; decisions 638–642, audit §22).** (1) **Part sources** (638): the CLT panels (`clt`), the steel members (`steelMembers`: each member's row of the members list, its weight and paint), the steel connections' plates, bolts, anchors and welds (`steelParts`) and the reinforcing bars (`rebar`) are parts field schedules list. `IPartSource.LooseParts` (a default member) gives parts whose owner a schedule does not list: the bars and distributions drawn on views (annotations), read through themselves, filtered by their owner; a type's stirrups and column bars are read through their beam or column. Shipped templates: **CLT panels** stands for the fixed table, cell for cell, and its button now makes it; **Steel members** (no per-hall totals: a field schedule's subtotals come after all the rows), **Steel parts** (its totals line counts the welds too and leaves the length blank without welds) and **Bars by mark** (the bar bending schedule's rows; its per-diameter totals put „Total” in the Mark column and add up the numbers and lengths too; no note, no partition column) are offered beside fixed schedules that stay. The members' marking and the parts' order are worked out once per model revision. (2) **From template… previews** (639): `schedule.templatePreview {builtin | text}` gives the template's one row (new, the same, or the same name set up otherwise) and the templates dialog asks through ImportPreview, importing with the choice (keep both, update, skip); one the same is opened. (3) **Extension installers** (640): price lists, profile libraries and hatch files of the same name that differ go through the import preview (`chooseOnComputer`): keep both („Name (2)”), replace or skip; the same one is taken without asking. Project templates still confirm. (4) **Convert to…** (641): **Stair** (a line or polyline: foot, direction, the turn of an L/U type; a closed outline's first edge its foot and width), **Floor opening** (cut in the floor of the plan's storey under the outline; arcs and ellipses kept) and **Parcel** (the terrain under the outline, arcs kept, straight edges given as zero arcs so the old ones go). Rail's track hook stays in its private repo. (5) **Crowded tabs** (642): MEP in three groups (System, Runs, Carriers and fans) and Studio in six (Sketch, Constraints, Solids, Families, Modify, Boolean), the rest under pulldowns (`pulldownTool`); `allTools` and the command list read a pulldown's items, so ids, letters, My tools and Commands… are unchanged; the ribbon marks a pulldown and lights it while one of its tools is on. Room separation line's RS had never been live (inside a pulldown) and clashes with Stirrup: it takes LS. Tests: ShippedScheduleTemplateTests (CLT, steel members, steel parts, rebar, template preview), LineworkConvertTargetsTests (stair, floor opening, parcel); scene convert-targets (convert-stair).

**One sketch canvas, stage 1 (audit S11, decision 643, 17.11.2026).** The audit counted eight 2D drawing boards, each with its own tools, snaps, keys and undo. Stage 1 puts Draft, the Studio sketch and the part sketch on one canvas (`docs/plan-sketch-canvas.md`): the canvas is a view drawn on with the CAD session (the Draft commands, snaps, grips, constraint and dimension tools, the Enter/Esc rule), shown flat or over the 3D view on its plane; a board is a view a module claims (`viewport/sketch/board.ts`: `registerBoard`, `boardOf`; its frame, tools, constraints on or off, Finish, Cancel, Esc), with one bar for all (`studio/SketchBar.tsx`: whose sketch, the constraint chip, Draft's tools, the constraint tools, Finish sketch, Cancel; Look at plane and Open in 2D over 3D, Back to 3D flat). Undo: one step per command while editing, one more at Finish that writes the board's payload. Engine: `studio.editPartSketch {partId, sketchId}` opens a part's own sketch on a reference plane of its own where the placed part puts it (`partSketchOf`; drafts remembering their shapes' ids, `partShape`; constraints with their formulas, which `SketchConstraints.ParamsOf` works out with the part's defaults on such a view), `finishPartSketch` writes it back in one step (shape and constraint ids kept, solved with the defaults, through `Commit`; ellipses, hatches, texts and construction lines refused), `cancelPartSketch` drops it. Interface: the part sketch's own mini-board (Line, Rectangle, Circle, Arc, Delete) is gone; the part's sketch is the 3D work plane, drawn with Draft's commands, its constraints drawn on the plane (`Board3D.tsx`), Esc, Tab or leaving the edit mode finishing it; Edit sketch on outlines and the sketch on a face take the shared bar (the face's gains Cancel: what was drawn since the sketch began goes in one step); the outline and part boards record constraints while drawing from the start. Draft on plans, sections and sheets is unchanged. Nothing stored changes (no project upgrade): a sketch opened and finished unchanged is the same JSON. Tests `PartSketchBoardTests` (3), `board.test.ts`; the guide's studio-part scene draws with LINE on the canvas. Stage 2 (wall profile, profiles, symbols, title blocks, joinery outlines, in that order) is planned in the same page.


**Select all on Ctrl+A (forum topic 130, decision 644).** Ctrl+A had no command, so the browser selected the whole page's text. `app.selectAll` (Ctrl+A, `ui/src/shell/selectAll.ts`) takes in a plan, section, elevation or drawing view all that the canvas's selection window would take over the whole view (the host's `inRegion`, groups and assemblies whole through `pickRegion`), and in 3D the editable elements shown (not hidden by the view, Hide or Isolate). In a text field the field's own Select all still runs. Tests `selectAll.test.ts`; the guide's interface page (EN, RO).

**Symbol layers kept and drawn (issue #70, forum #120, decision 645).** The symbol editor's layers (16.11) lived only in its canvas: `SymbolDef` dropped the `layers` list on save, and `SymbolRender` read no item's `layer`. The definition now keeps them (the same `Layers` shape as the boards work of audit S11 stage 2b), and every placed copy works out each layer's visibility and opacity from its own attribute values by the editor's rules (`SymbolLayers`: a number 0–100, true/false, `{attr.key}`, `{key}`, `attr.key`, `{= formula}` over live text; what reads as none keeps the layer shown and opaque). A hidden layer's items are left out; the others carry their layer's opacity as `#rrggbbaa` on strokes, fills, hatches and texts, in views, sheet pens, the PDF (a graphics state for strokes and texts too, as fills had) and the SVG (`stroke-opacity`, a text group's `fill-opacity`). The editor's layer strings in every language. Tests `SymbolTests` (4 new).

**The wall profile and the section profile on the sketch canvas (audit S11 stage 2, items 1 and 2, decision 646, 17.11.2026).** Two more editors became boards of the shared canvas (`docs/plan-sketch-canvas.md` §4). Engine (`Lapin.Studio/ProfileBoards.cs`): `studio.editWallProfile {id}` opens a wall's top or a curtain wall's boundary on a reference plane standing on its axis (x along the wall from its start, y up from its base, so the stored [u, v] points are the drafts' coordinates, copied), marked `wallProfileOf`, the wall hidden there, the top drawn as one polyline (the stored profile, else the flat top at the wall's height from `architecture.wallTop`) and the base and ends as construction lines; `studio.finishWallProfile {viewId, flat?}` chains what is drawn into one line from start to end (lines, polylines, arcs and elliptical arcs; curves in chords by the one rule) and writes it through `architecture.setWallProfile` or `setCurtainWallProfile` in one undo step, refusing a top that branches, closes, is in pieces, turns back along the wall, runs past its ends or drops under 50 mm (the board kept open); a top finished as it was opened leaves the wall untouched. `studio.editProfile {profile?, rings?, name, material, library}` opens a section on a level plane of its own far under the model with a 1 mm depth (a blank sheet in the section's metres, 1:1), marked `profileOf`, each ring a closed polyline and the axes as construction lines; `studio.finishProfile` takes every closed shape (closed polylines, rectangles, circles, ellipses, lines and arcs end to end) as a ring, checked and centred as `profiles.fromRings` does (`CheckRings`, `FromOutlines`, `WithSummary`), and answers `{profile, library, changed}`; rings finished as opened give the profile back as it came, any kind (an IPE stays an I), renamed if asked. `cancelWallProfile`, `cancelProfile` drop the board; `studio.setBoardShapes` replaces a board's shapes in one step (the starters, the typed points); `studio.profileBoard` reads what the drafts make now. Interface: the `Board` contract gains `panel`, a side form over the view's right side, and `rememberReturn`/`takeReturn`/`frameBoard` (the view a board was opened from; framing its shapes with room for the bar and the form). `architecture/wallProfileBoard.tsx`: Profile… on a wall or a straight curtain wall opens the board, its side form keeping the dialog's Gable/Slope/Steps to a height, the table of points in mm and Flat top; a curved curtain wall and a product without Studio keep the dialog. `core/profileLibraries/profileBoard.tsx`: Draw a profile… and Edit its outline… open the board, its side form keeping name, material, library, the starters with their sizes, the chamber, size and area and a ring's points; Finish goes back to the libraries' naming step as the editor's Make did, Cancel back to the libraries; without Studio the inline editor stays. Nothing stored changes, no project upgrade. Tests `ProfileBoardTests` (7: frames, every profile kind and a stored wall profile unchanged = the same JSON, flat walls stay flat, arcs in chords, refusals, curtain walls, undo, cancel), `wallProfileBoard.test.ts`; scenes wall-profile, curtain-boundary and profile-editor draw on the boards; walls and profiles pages in every language.

**Symbols, title blocks and a window's own outline on the shared sketch canvas (audit S11 stage 2, items 3–5; decision 647).** The last three 2D editors of the audit's inventory became boards. A symbol and a title block open on a paper of their own (a `paper` view of no sheet, the paper view of decision 620 without a sheet: 1:1, metres = mm / 1000, so a typed length is their mm), their guides drawn on the screen only (`boardUnderlay`: the insertion point's red cross and arrow, the block's dashed box). `ItemBoards` lays each item as one drafted thing remembering its index and a digest of what it was laid as, and reads the board back: untouched items byte for byte, changed ones with their look kept, new drafting converted as Save as symbol does (which now keeps an ellipse as its points), construction lines left out, a dimension or a placed symbol refused at Finish. `drafting.editSymbol` (a project symbol, a new one made at Finish, or one of this computer's library given back to the interface), `setSymbolBoard`, `symbolBoard`, `finishSymbol`, `cancelSymbol`; `drafting.editTitleBlock`, `setTitleBlockBoard`, `titleBlockBoard`, `finishTitleBlock` (through `titleBlock.update`), `cancelTitleBlock`. The symbol's layers are kept with its definition (`SymbolDef.Layers`, the same shape as issue #70's rendering). A window type takes an outline drawn on a board of its elevation (`architecture.editJoineryOutline`/`finishJoineryOutline`/`cancelJoineryOutline`): one closed outline, chained from lines, arcs and polylines when it is in pieces, becomes `shape: outline` with its loop in the unit box (serialised as [[x, y], …]) and the type's width and height; such a type is `JoineryShapes.Ringed` like a round window (a frame following it, one fixed light, the surround and elevation lines following it); a rectangle drawn back gives the rectangle; a door is refused. Interface: `core/boards.tsx` (the two boards and their side forms: name, scale, category, preview, layers, attributes, fields; box, corner, frame, revision table, logo, fields), `architecture/joineryBoard.ts` and Draw outline… in the configurator; the bar gains Text and Hatch; Manage → Symbols… and Title blocks… open the boards, the old editors staying for an edition without drafting or Studio. No project upgrade. Tests `ItemBoardTests` (4), `JoineryOutlineBoardTests` (2); scenes symbols, title-blocks and window-outline; drafting, sheets and doors-and-windows pages in every language.

Axis temporary dimensions near the click (issue #65, decision 648): the editable dimensions of a selected grid axis stand at the point clicked on it, kept within the stretch the axis shares with its neighbours, instead of always at the middle of that stretch; selected from the project browser (no click) they keep the middle. UI only: `PlanView` notes the click (`noteAxisClick`), `axisDims` takes it and `stationAt` places the station; radial grids, editing, the padlock and "move the axes beyond too" unchanged. Tests in `gridDims.test.ts`; the drafting guide page in English and Romanian.
**Renumber axes and Axis grid either way, counting up or down (issue #64, forum 132, decision 649, 17.11.2026).** Renumber axes only numbered ascending, left to right and bottom to top. `drafting.renumberAxes` takes `reverse` (the family ordered right to left, or top to bottom: the sort key across it negated) and `descending` (the labels count down from `start`, 10, 9, 8… or E, D, C…, I and O still left out; with no start they end on 1 or A, the ascending labels the other way round; a start too low is refused). `drafting.makeGrid` takes `reverseX`, `reverseY`, `descendingX`, `descendingY` for its first labels (counting down with the new `Grids.Prev`, Next's inverse: AA → Z, A2 → A1, 05 → 04; `Grids.Series`). Without the flags both commands do exactly what they did. The Renumber dialog has **Order** (named for the family: the selected axes' own, `runUpThePlan`) and **Counting**; the Axis grid dialog has **Order** and **Counting** for each family, a default first label of 1 or A becoming the one that ends the family on it when switched to descending (`firstForCounting`). Tests: `GridRenumberTests` (three new), `gridSpec.test.ts`; the catalogue, the 10 languages and the guide's Drafting page (EN, RO) updated.
