**Dimension styles: extension lines left out, fixed-length extension lines, their own pens (issue #66, forum 131, decision 650).** As AutoCAD's dimension styles, a `DimStyle` gains `extSuppress` (a string's first extension line, its last, or both left out; a dimension's own `extSuppress` attribute, "none" too, goes before its style's through `o.extSuppress`), `extFixed` (every extension line that long on paper, measured back from the dimension line, never nearer the point than the gap), and `extWeight`/`extColor`/`extLineType` and `dimLineWeight`/`dimLineColor`/`dimLineType` (the arrows and ticks take the dimension line's colour, the values the style's). Every field is optional and left unwritten when unset, so existing projects and styles draw exactly as before and no project upgrade is needed; `.lapinstandards` files carry them. `DimStyle.SuppressesExtension` and `DimStyle.Extension` are shared by strings (`Dimensions.Draw` and the view's text room in `Obstacles`, so a left-out line is no obstacle), angular and arc-length dimensions (`DimensionKinds`) and a part's ordinates (`PartDimensions`); the engine draws them, so the plan, sheets, the PDF and the DXF agree. Interface: eight columns in Drafting standards → Dimensions (pens and fixed length empty: as before; the colours clearable), Extension lines off in a dimension's properties (`core/dimensionLines.ts`), the Annotation class's `extSuppress` parameter (Match properties copies it). Tests `DimensionExtensionLineTests` (8), `dimensionLines.test.ts`; scene `dimension-extension-lines`; the drafting page in English and Romanian; strings in every language.
**Door and window markers drawn by a symbol; tag rules (issue #72, forum topic 114, decision 651).** A tag type shaped "opening" may name a symbol (`TagType.Symbol`, already read for every shape; the Tags tab's symbol picker now shows for markers too): `OpeningMarkers.Symbol` lays it beyond the marker line's exterior end, clear of it by its box's reach along the line, turned as the texts lie (along the wall, or the line), its texts filled by `TagSymbols.Lay` from the door or window with the marker's own `{width}`, `{height}`, `{sill}` (in the type's unit) as extras, any field and formula besides; it takes the outer text's place and its part `o0` (moved, turned on screen as a text line is); the line and the inner text stay; a symbol gone gives the outer text back; without a symbol the marker is drawn exactly as before (a snapshot of every printed pen, unchanged). Tag rules: `TagRule(Id, Class, When, TagType)` in the standards (`StyleBook.TagRules`, `tagRules`, null when none so the stored shape of existing projects is unchanged and no project upgrade is needed), read tolerantly, edited through `standards.set`/`standards.remove` with kind `tagRule` (its condition checked as a formula, its type one of the standards'), ordered by the new `standards.move` (an entry to an index), dropped with their tag type. `Lapin.Platform/TagRules`: the condition is an `Expr` formula over the element's fields as `{= …}` reads them (`ParamFormulas.Read`), written bare or in braces, plus what a module measures (`TagRules.AddMeasure`: architecture gives doors and windows `width`, `height`, `sill` in metres); per class in their order the first that holds gives the type (never a build-up), over the tag's own, on every draw (`Tags.Marks`, `OpeningMarkers.Marks`, room stamps in `Rooms.Marks`), and `architecture.tagOpenings` stores the rule's type for a new marker. A tag's type chosen in its properties (`drafting.updateTag` tagType) sets `typeFixed` and the rules leave it; `byRules` gives it back. A tag switched off (`updateTag` `shown: false`, attr `off`) prints nowhere and is drawn on screen only, on the construction layer as a faint outline of its lines and text boxes, its pick box kept (`TagRules.Ghost`). The rules travel in `.lapinstandards` as entries (`tagRule`, named by what they say in the import preview). Interface: `TagRulesEditor` under the tag types (class, condition, tag type, up/down within the class via `tagRules.ts` `moveIndex`, remove, New rule); a tag's properties gain Shown, a marker's Tag type among the marker types, By the rules. Tests `TagRuleTests` (6), `tagRules.test.ts` (4); scene tag-rules; docs/user/drafting.md (EN, RO).
**Detail callouts' boundaries and inset views' frames: pen weight, colour and line type (issue #73, forum 113; decision 652).** A callout's boundary was a fixed 0.35 black line (grey and dashed while it refers to no view) and an inset's frame a fixed 0.18 grey one, on or off. Now each is drawn with a pen weight (paper mm, 0.05–2), a colour and a line type (Lapin's or the project's own, dash-dot among them), each field resolved by one rule (`Lapin.Drawing/FrameLines.cs`): the element's own (`lineWeight`, `lineColor`, `lineType` on the `LapinCallout` or `LapinInset`, set by `view.updateCallout` and `view.updateInset`, null clearing it), else the project's in the drafting standards (`StyleBook.CalloutLine`, `StyleBook.InsetFrame`, kept as `drafting.calloutLine` and `drafting.insetFrame`, set by `standards.frameLine` {kind: callout | inset, weight, color, lineType}), else Lapin's own exactly as before, so a project that sets nothing draws the same. The engine draws them in the view's marks (`Callouts.BoundaryPen`, `Insets.FrameOf`), so views, sheets, PDF and DXF agree; the head, its leader and the inset's title keep their ink, and Visibility / Graphics' category colour still goes over them. The defaults read tolerantly (an unknown line type or a weight out of range is dropped) and round-trip a .lapinstandards file, brought in with its options (`StandardsLibrary.Import`). Interface: `core/frameLine.tsx` (`FrameLineFields`: Pen weight, Line colour, Line type, each "default" to inherit) in the callout's and the inset's properties (the inset's while it is framed) and in Drafting standards → Project (Detail callout boundary, Inset view frame); `frameLineModel.ts` the pure side. Nothing stored changes, no project upgrade. Tests `FrameLineTests` (5: unchanged defaults, project default and own override, the project's own line type, sheet pens, insets, a standards file), `frameLineModel.test.ts`; views pages in English and Romanian; strings in every language.
**Drafting tables, stage 1 (issue #71, forum 119, decision 653).** A table of the user's own, beside the schedules: an `IfcAnnotation` TABLE on a view or a sheet's paper (`paper:<sheet>`), its top-left corner at `at`, turned by `angle`, sized in paper mm; its grid in `attrs.table` (columns with a width or fit within min/max, rows with a height that fit their text by default, cells row-major with text, spans, own style and sides, the table's style and preset). Cells hold multiline text and `{= …}` formulas reading the table's cells by address (`A1`, `AA12`) and ranges among a function's values (`sum(A1:A4)`, `average`, `count`: `Expr` takes an optional range reader, new functions `sum`, `average`, `count`; every other formula reads as before); a cell reading itself prints `<…>` and the table says why; on a sheet a cell reads the sheet's tokens. Rows and columns put in or taken out rewrite the formulas as a spreadsheet does (a reference to a cell taken out becomes `REF`). Merge and split, borders (all, outside, inside, a side, none, the table's), text and background colours with opacity, bold, italic, alignment, presets (header row, grid lines, minimal, none). The engine draws it (`DraftingTables`, `TableCommands.Marks`): fills, joined lines, texts, so the view, the sheet, its PDF and DXF print the same; it moves and turns with the Modify tools, counts among the texts in Visibility / Graphics, saves as plain attributes (no project upgrade). `drafting.addTable`, `drafting.editTable` (ops, one undo step a call), query `drafting.table`; in the command catalogue. Interface: Annotate → Text → Table (TT) with rows, columns and preset in the bar, the table editor (a grid scaled from the engine's sizes, keyboard and paste like a spreadsheet, the bars of buttons), Properties with Edit table…, double-click opens it. Tests `DraftingTableTests` (16), `tableModel.test.ts` (6); strings in every language; docs/user/drafting.md → Tables (EN, RO), scene `drafting-table`. The plan and the later stages (other tables' cells, user presets and rotated text, tables in symbols, long tables and CSV/XLSX): docs/plan-drafting-tables.md.

**Offset outline on an elliptical outline (fix, decision 654).** Found while filming on 0.1.1335: Shift-dragging an edge's middle grip of a floor drawn with Ellipse left a wedge with an arc. The whole-outline offset was withheld from outlines with an elliptical edge (the grip lent no `offsetAll`, `outline.offset` refused `ellipses`), so the Shift-drag fell back to the plain edge drag: the edge's two corners moved off the ellipse, the three edges touching them turned straight and one quarter stayed elliptical. Rooms' grips never withheld it and sent the four corners without their ellipses, offsetting a diamond. Now `ArcOutline.Offset` takes the outline's `ellipses`: an elliptical edge is laid in chords by the one rule, each halved until the curve at the distance lies within `Chords.Tolerance` of its chord (as Draft's `Ops.OffsetEllipse`), offset with the rest, and every corner beside it put where the offset lines meet with the ellipse's own tangent for its chord (on the true offset curve between two of its chords, the true mitre against a straight edge); inward past the tightest curvature (b²/a) it is refused. The result has straight edges only (the curve at a distance from an ellipse is no ellipse), so the element's update drops its `ellipses`. The UI lends each edge grip the outline with its ellipses (`withEllipses` in gripKit: floors, floor openings, ceilings, drawn rooms, outline holes), so Shift-drag and Offset outline… work in plans and 3D. Tests: `OutlineOffsetTests` (3 new: a whole ellipse out and in within the tolerance, its area A + P·d + πd², the fold refused; a half ellipse mitred with its straight edge; an elliptical floor through the query and `updateSlab`, one undo step), `OutlineHoleEllipseTests` (the refusal test now asserts the offset), `grips3d.test.ts`; docs/user/floors-roofs.md in every language.

**Commands pick in the pane clicked, with the view split (decision 655).** Found while filming: with the view split into a plan and a 3D view, Trim/Extend, Split element, Fillet and every command that picks elements answered "Nothing to pick there." on the plan's walls. Each 2D view lends the command line a host (`useCadCanvas`, aim.ts), and the 3D view's work plane (Draft3D) lends one too, but only while a command runs: starting a command registered it last, and the last registered won, so the plan's clicks were picked among the work plane's drafted lines (no walls there). Now the hosts are kept per pane (`viewport/cad/hosts.ts`): the command line works on the active pane's newest host, else the newest of all, and follows the active pane, which a click makes the one it lands in before the view sees it. Tests: `hosts.test.ts`; checked in the app: split, Trim/Extend and Fillet on two walls in the plan pane, and Split element clicked in the plan while the 3D pane was in front.

**Drafting tables, stages 2–5 (issue #71, forum 119, decisions 656–659, 17.11.2026).** The rest of docs/plan-drafting-tables.md. The table model moved to the platform (`DraftingTables`, `TextWrap`) so symbols draw tables too. *Stage 2, other tables' cells:* a table named with one word is read by any other, `table.Areas.B4` in formulas, `{table.Areas.B4}` as a token, ranges `table.Areas.B2:B9` among a function's values; `TableScope` works each table out once per drawing with its own sheet's tokens and keeps the cycle guard across tables (a circle says so in both). A name is found on the same view first (a duplicated sheet reads its own tables), else the only one of the project. `drafting.renameTable` and `element.rename` (through `IDrivenElements`) rewrite the references; a deleted table leaves `REF` (`IDeletionFollowers`); rows and columns put into a table or taken out move the references other tables make to it (`TableEdits.MapRefs`, one rule for local and qualified references). The editor has the table's name and an *Another table* picker. *Stage 3, user presets:* *Save as style…* keeps a table in the drafting standards (`StyleBook.TableStyles`, carried by `.lapinstandards` files, Manage → Drafting standards → Tables), its look, sizes, header rows and with its cells a template; applied as a preset is (a copy, never a live link), its header on the first rows and its body rows in turn; the built-in presets keep their output. A cell's text turns 90° or 270° along its row, which fits its length; `pad` is a cell's own padding; `header` marks header rows; `DraftingTables.Placements` places every line for views and symbols. *Stage 4, tables from the model:* a symbol's `table` item reads each placed copy's live text and a tag's element fields, so each copy lays its own sizes (a room tag that grows with its name), drawn on the symbol's board with Annotate → Table, taken by Save as symbol, exploded into lines and texts; a cell, a row or a table is linked to an element by its id and `el.<field>` reads it through `ElementFields` (a token as a tag writes it, a formula in SI); `schedule.<name>.count|total.<col>|sum|average|min|max.<col>|<cell>` read a schedule as laid out (`IDocumentTables`) and `filter.<name>.count|sum.<field>` a view filter's elements (`ElementFilter`); `ModelCells` reads them on every drawing, nothing stored; a gone element keeps its link and says so; ops `link` and `linkRows` (a row each, the first row's texts filled down), the editor's *Link elements…* bar, *Unlink* and the linked element's fields. *Stage 5, long tables and exchange:* `split` breaks a table after so many rows or at a height into parts laid right or down, each with its header rows again, never through a merged cell; `TABLEPART` (`drafting.placeTablePart`) puts a part on another sheet, drawn live, gone with its table; CSV and XLSX out (`drafting.tableExport`, as read or as typed) and in (op `load`, the table growing); grips on the drawing for column widths and row heights. Saved data only gains optional attributes (no project upgrade). Tests: `DraftingTableTests` (26), `DraftingTableModelTests` (10), `DraftingTableLongTests` (5), `tableModel.test.ts` (14); strings in every language; docs/user/drafting.md → Tables (EN, RO) and the symbol editor; scenes `drafting-table-refs`, `drafting-table-styles`, `drafting-table-model`, `drafting-table-long`.

**Lapin Extensions, stage 2a: paid extensions sold by their authors (decision 660, 17.11.2026).** The user approved every recommendation: content and Python scripts may be sold (an explicit `PAID_KINDS` list, so JS plug-ins and C# modules wait for a sandbox), through the author's own shop, 0 % commission, the author the seller. A manifest's new `purchase` (`provider`, `productId`, `checkoutUrl`, `terms`, `price?` shown as indicative) is checked the same in lapin-extensions' `tools/lib.mjs` and schema, the store's copy and `manifest.ts`; a paid package has no public copy: its `package.url` is the store's `/api/licensed/<id>/<version>`, no mirror or counting address, still signed. The store keeps it in `/data/paid/` (from the dashboard's new *Sold through your own shop* section, or handed over privately into lapin-extensions' git-ignored `private/` and uploaded by `publish.mjs`), and gives it only after the store server checked the key with the provider (`providers.mjs`: Gumroad's verify without counting a use; refunded, disabled, expired, disputed or ended → revoked; a down provider → unavailable, nothing concluded; 10 s timeouts) and only if the file's size and SHA-256 are the index's. Keys are never kept or logged by the store, nor addresses: one line per check (extension, provider, answer); 20 checks a visitor an hour, 60 provider calls a minute. In Lapin the dialog marks Paid with the price, says who sells it through what, opens Buy and the seller's terms in the system browser, asks for the licence key at Install (desktop through the engine's new `extensions.post`, a POST with the key in its body and its answer returned whatever the status; browser by fetch with CORS), keeps the key on the computer with the `lapin.*` settings, checks it again when an update is offered or weekly, never blocks anything offline, and on a revoked key offers no more updates and says so, removing nothing; Forget licence key drops it. The legal drafts (0.2, English and Romanian) gained the selling paragraph, the buyer's side and the key's processing; the site shows Paid, Buy and "Sold by … through …" without a download. Lemon Squeezy, first built beside Gumroad, was removed the same day at the user's answer (plan §12.9): it closed sign-ups on 16.11.2026 and stops processing payments on 31.03.2027, and Stripe Managed Payments, where it sends its sellers, gives no licence keys; Gumroad is the only provider, and store-issued keys for merchants without a licence API (a webhook per sale, the store keeping the keys, with what that means for privacy) are recorded as a later stage (§12.10), not built. Tests: `paid.test.mjs` (14, providers faked), `licence.test.ts` (8), `ExtensionFetchTests` (2 more), lapin-extensions `catalogue.test.mjs` and the paid cases of `lib.test.mjs` and `build.test.mjs`; the guide's Extensions page in every language and its scene with a paid entry (`extensions-paid`). Nothing deployed; the lapin-extensions branch `feat/paid-extensions` awaits review.

**Drafting tables: common measure names, suggestions while typing, AutoSum (issue #71, decision 661).** Found while filming the tables tutorial. *Measures by their common names:* a room carries its area as `NetFloorArea`, so `{el.NetArea}` and `{el.area}` read empty; `QuantityNames` (Platform) now says which quantities a common name stands for, the first an element has — `Area`/`NetArea` the net side, net, net floor or net surface area (a door's `Area`), `GrossArea`, `Perimeter` (`NetPerimeter`, `Perimeter`, `GrossPerimeter`), `Volume`, `Weight` — and `ParamFormulas.Quantities` falls back on it after the exact name, so formulas, tags, schedule columns, filters and table cells read them alike; the quantity takeoff's columns take their lists from it (no parallel alias table; the office's `FieldAliases` still come first). `fields.available` lists the common names in an element's quantities, and the table editor's *Insert a field…* now lists `fields.available` (its quantities included) instead of `params.fields`; the help shows `{= el.Area | 0.00}`. *Suggestions:* `drafting.tableSuggest {id, cell, text, caret}` answers what fits at the caret inside `{…}` / `{= …}` as the evaluator reads it — the roots, the functions with a signature (`Expr.Signatures`), `el.` + the linked element's fields with their values, `schedule.<name>.` + count/total/sum… + columns written as formulas read them, `filter.<name>.` + count/sum.<number field>, `table.<name>.` + cells with what they read — the word before the caret filtering it (prefix, then contains) and being what an accepted item replaces; `shell/SuggestPopup.tsx` (with `suggest.ts`) is a reusable list under any textarea (↑ ↓, Enter/Tab, Esc, click; an item ending in a dot asks again), used by the cell input and the formula bar. *Errors while typing:* the cell being typed shows no errors of its old text; `drafting.tableTry` tries the text without keeping it once typing pauses (0.7 s), so a half-typed formula is not flagged at each key. *The missed double-click after Row below:* the "It reads …" line above the grid appeared or vanished with the selection, so the first click of a double-click on a row read differently (the new, unlinked row) moved the grid under the pointer and the second click hit another cell; the line now keeps its height, and the grid takes focus without scrolling the dialog (`preventScroll`). The link bar (*Select the elements the table reads…*) was shrink-wrapped to half the window by `left: 50%`; it is `max-content` wide now, wrapping only past the window. *AutoSum:* Σ and Alt+= (op `autoSum {range, fn}`, sum/average/count/min/max), worked out by `drafting.editTable` on the table as it reads (`TableAutoSum`): one empty cell takes the numbers right above it up to a header row, an empty cell or a text, else those left of it; several cells get a total under each numeric column in the first empty row below, a row added if none; one undo step. Totals follow growing lists: `linkRows` now fills the empty or linked rows from its start and puts the rest in above the first row with a text of its own (it used to overwrite a total row), and inserting rows right below a range extends ranges in the cells under them (`TableEdits.Extend`). Tests: `DraftingTableModelTests` (+8: room/wall/door names, the fields list, suggestions, tryout, three AutoSum), `suggest.test.ts` (4), `tableModel.test.ts` (+3); strings in every language; docs/user/drafting.md (EN, RO); scene `drafting-table-suggest`.

**Layer lines: the lines between a cut element's layers styled by the user (forum, decision 662, 17.11.2026).** A forum user asked to change the weight and colour of the lines that separate a wall's or a floor's material layers. The engine already drew them in a role of their own (`LineRole.Separation`, picked by `Pens.Separations`) and `LineStyles.For` resolved it at three levels, but nothing in the interface set it. Now Visibility / Graphics has a **Layer lines** column per category (pen 1–16, line type, colour, × to clear; `lines.separation` through `view.graphics`, `withLayerLines`) and Manage → Object styles names its existing separation column **Layer lines**. The one engine change: `Pens.Of` lays a layer line's own style over the cut lines' field by field (before, any separation style replaced the cut style whole), so what is left unset still draws as today: 0.25 mm, two ISO steps under a category weight, or the category's cut pen when one is set. Nothing set: the drawing is unchanged; only optional keys, no project upgrade. An element's own `separation` works through `view.graphics` with `element` (no interface for per-element line overrides exists yet). Tests: `LayerLinesTests` (5: each level and their priority, a view's layer lines change only the between-layer lines and not the contour, object styles with the cut pen filling the gaps, a category weight still thinning them unless they have a pen, an element over its category), `VisibilityDialog.test.ts` (+1); strings in every language; docs/user/views.md (EN, RO), docs/view-styles.md; scene `views-vg` sets the walls' layer lines.


**17.11.2026, downloads and updates at lapinbim.com (decision 663).** The public editions (`lapin`, `lapin-architecture`, `lapin-architecture-structure`) are served from lapinbim.com: `/download/<edition>/` (the fixed names) and `/updates/<edition>/` (manifests and packages), the same server folders as `ciuntucbimstudio.ro/downloads/`, which stays served for good because builds installed before 0.1.1355 only know it. lapinbim-site was deployed for the first time (lapin-web recreated with two read-only volumes, `lapinbim.conf` beside `default.conf`; backups `/opt/lapin/*.bak-20261117`). `publish-update.mjs` writes manifests naming lapinbim.com and refuses to publish while lapinbim.com does not answer (`LAPIN_UPDATE_HOST=old` forces the old address); `build-product.mjs` and `tauri.conf.json` list both endpoints, lapinbim.com first; other editions keep the company site. The app's links (guide, site, source, join page) name lapinbim.com (`Support.cs`, `Sharing.cs`, `support.ts`); the relay and the report form stay on lapin.ciuntucbimstudio.ro (lapinbim.com does not proxy them). 0.1.1355 (7cd818e7) published on macOS (notarized again after the `lapin-notary` keychain profile had to be stored anew), Windows and Linux, the web app and the guide (only the 7 scenes that changed, retaken and rsynced); 15 tutorials added to `docs/user/videos/videos.json` and placed on their English pages; each release is mirrored as a GitHub Release (`lapin` only) by the user. Lesson: `pnpm build:product --target windows` empties `build/<id>/bundle`, so keep each platform's packages aside before the next build; `build-linux-docker.sh` must run from the main checkout, not a worktree.

**17.11.2026, door and window sizes per element (forum topic 140, decision 664).** A door or window now carries its own `width` and `height` over its type's, so one type (the MSE window of the topic) is placed at 1200 × 1200, 900 × 1500 and 1800 × 1200, every element linked to it. `Openings.SpecOf` returns the type's spec at the element's size, and everything that read the size already went through it (the opening and its cut, the plan symbol, 3D, elevation symbols, quantities, tags, room openings); the schedule lists a type once per size (as curtain wall doors were), and `JoineryIfc.Size` gives the IFC export the occurrence's OverallWidth/OverallHeight. `architecture.setJoinerySize` sets or resets it in one step (checked with `Joinery.Problem` at that size and `CheckFits`); `addDoor`/`addWindow` take `width`/`height`; `updateWallOpening` on a filled opening sizes its door or window, so a door's or window's jambs are grips now. The properties panel shows Width and Height as fields and **Type's size** to give them up. No project upgrade: without the attributes an element is its type's size, as before. Tests: `InstanceJoinerySizeTests` (11), `joinerySize.test.ts`.
**17.11.2026, scaling an imported image or PDF to a known distance (forum 143, decision 664).** A user compared Lapin with Edificius, which aligns a traced image with a true measure or its graphic scale; Lapin's Draft → Scale image (decision 371) did that for an image but was hard to find and did not take a PDF. Now the import dialog's „Then scale it to a known distance” (on by default) goes straight on to the tool with the image or the PDF's group selected (Esc skips); the tool's gesture is `twoPoints` with `measure` (`liveBegin(..., { measure: true })`): while the second point is picked the box at the cursor shows the distance the two points are apart (↔) and takes their true distance typed beside it (`2400`, `50m`, `3000+250`, the project's unit unless written), which no longer moves the second point; clicked without one, it is asked for after (metres). `drafting.calibrateImage` takes a group of images and drafting (an imported PDF's) and scales it whole about the first point, images by their corner and size (`ViewImages.Scaled`), drafting through drafting.transform's scale (its body is now `DraftCommands.Transform`), one undo step, ids and group kept; a group holding anything else is refused. The tool is also on the right-click menu of a selected image or PDF and in its properties (Scale to a known distance…), in Ask Lapin (`scale-image`), in the guide (drafting, English and Romanian) and in the scene `trace-scale`. Tests: `PdfTraceTests` (two new), `scaleImage.test.ts`.
**17.11.2026, housekeeping: timing tests and Linux packaging (decision 666, issues #81 and #23; branch `feat/housekeeping-81-23`, not merged).** The two engine tests with a wall-clock limit, which failed under a full parallel run (43 s against 5 s alone) and in CI, now check the work instead of the time. `Many_native_elements_import_in_linear_time` imports 1500 and 6000 native elements in the same run, three times each in turn, and compares the best times (about 4×; the per-element scans it guards against gave 16×; the limit is 9×). `Five_kilometres_with_five_curves_are_realigned_in_seconds` counts the least-squares steps (24 across and 34 up; at most 40 and 60) and compares its time with the 1 km design's measured in the same run (about 30×; the limit is 100×). Both moved into nested `Timing` classes in the xUnit collection `Scaling.Serial` (`DisableParallelization`), which runs alone after the parallel tests; the helper is `engine/tests/Lapin.Engine.Tests/Scaling.cs`. The full engine suite ran twice in a row while other sessions loaded the machine (load average 85 at the start of the first run, 14–19 in the second): 4253 of 4253 passed both times (7 min 30 s and 6 min 3 s); with the load at 17 the two ratios were 4.2 and 32.3. A run before it failed only the 7 `SharingTests`, because the fresh worktree had not built the relay (`dotnet build engine/Lapin.slnx` first). Linux: `build-product.mjs` gives Tauri a desktop entry template of its own (`desktop/src-tauri/linux/app.desktop.hbs`: Name, GenericName, Comment, `Exec=lapin-desktop %f`, `Icon=lapin-desktop`, `Categories=Graphics;Engineering;`, Keywords, `MimeType=application/x-lapin-project;`, `StartupWMClass=lapin-desktop`), used by the .deb and, through the .deb's data, the AppImage, and installs the `.lapin` MIME type (`linux/lapin-project.xml`, a subclass of zip) in `/usr/share/mime/packages/<product>.xml`; the file association is set on Linux only (on macOS and Windows the app does not yet take a file opened that way). The shell's `startup_file` takes the first argument when `LAPIN_OPEN` is not set, so a `.lapin` double-clicked in a file manager opens. The rendered entry passes `desktop-file-validate` (0.26 and 0.27, in Ubuntu containers), and `xdg-mime` resolves `*.lapin` to the type and the type to `Lapin.desktop`; a real Linux build and the themed menu were not checked. GitHub Releases: `scripts/github-release.mjs` prints, never runs, the commands that mirror a published build (the packages under the site's fixed names, `SHA256SUMS.txt`, notes from the commit subjects, `gh release create v<version> --target <commit>`), for the user to run; `docs/release-linux-and-github.md` says how.
**Axis grid: spans mixed, prefix and suffix per family, a hint; several levels at once (issue #83, forum 137, decision 668).** The spans fields have one parser, `ui/src/modules/core/spans.ts` (`parseSpans`): spans separated by spaces or semicolons, `N*span` (or `N x span`, `N×span`) for N equal spans, the two mixed (`100 2*300 200`, which the old split refused), each span a length in the project's units or its own (`6m`, `20' 6"`, its inches kept with its feet), a comma or a dot for decimals; what does not read is named (`Not a length: 2*abc`, `A span is a length above zero: 0`, `The count before * is a whole number from 1 to 200: 0*300`). The Axis grid dialog reads its Across and Along with it (`readSpacings` now wraps it), shows the bad piece under the fields, has a `?` beside each field (hover: the grammar and which labels count: a number at the end counts up, letters at the end run through the alphabet, any other label takes a 1 after it), and a **Prefix** and **Suffix** for each family, sent as `prefixX`, `suffixX`, `prefixY`, `suffixY` to `drafting.makeGrid`, which counts the first label as before (`Grids.Series`) and puts them around each label, never counted; a label holding `#` is refused (it would break `grid#tag`). A **Labels** row shows what the axes will take. **New levels…** (core dialog `newLevels`, opened from the button beside **+** under the browser's Levels and from Annotate → Datum) makes several storeys with the new command `storey.createMany` (`heights`, `elevation`, `name` with `{n}`, `start`), one undo step, the storeys there untouched: the heights are typed bottom up in the same grammar (`3*3000 2800`), the base level is a field of its own (default: the top of the highest storey), names from a pattern (`Level {n}`, numbered from the number of storeys, as `storey.create` counts); several storeys need `{n}` in the pattern. Tests: `AxisGridLevelsTests` (5), `spans.test.ts` (4), `gridSpec.test.ts` (+2); guide: drafting.md and levels.md (EN, RO), scenes `grid` (the mix, the hint, the named error) and `new-levels` (new).
**17.11.2026, types from another project (issue #74, decision 669).** File → Import types from a project…: a `.lapin` picked (by path on the desktop, its bytes in a browser, as a linked project), its types listed with their kind, the materials each needs, how many elements use them there and what each is to the open project; the chosen ones brought in, in one undo step. Built on the type packs: the host method `library.projectTypes` (`Lapin.Protocol/ProjectTypes.cs`) reads the package with `LapinPackage.Read`, runs the modules' `IProjectUpgrade`s in memory (`Session.Upgraded`) into a `ModelStore` of its own, takes its types out with `TypePacks.Export` (those `types.list` lists: not IFC-imported, not a link's) and previews them with `TypePacks.Rows`; the command `library.importProjectTypes` (core, in the catalogue) imports that pack with `TypePacks.Import`. The rules are the pack's (materials by name, the project's kept by default; a type of the same name that differs kept beside as „Name (2)” by default, or replaced, or skipped), with one addition behind a `keepIds` switch that `.lapintypes` imports do not use: a type keeps its GlobalId when the project has no entity of it, and a type the project has under its very GlobalId is that type (row status `id`: reused when the same, its name aside; replaced in place by default when it changed; or skipped, or kept beside under a new GlobalId). `TypePacks` gained `Differences`, `SameId`, `MaterialsOf` and `Closure` (the walk the import already did, now shared with the preview). The source is never written to: the tests hash its bytes before and after. `library.projectTypes` is not in `AllowedRemote` (a guest reads no file through the host; they can bring a `.lapintypes`); the command itself is open to editors like `types.importFrom`. UI: `ProjectTypesDialog` and `projectTypes.ts` (core), the File menu's first section; four new strings in all ten languages; the guide's Extensions page (EN, RO) gains "Types from another project" and Files points to it; scene `project-types`. Tests: `ProjectTypesImportTests` (7), `projectTypes.test.ts` (4).
**Type parameters and the IFC export (issues #29–#32, a CYPECAD MEP user; decision 667, 17.11.2026).** (#30) **Export IFC, Fragments or BOS…** has **Type properties: Also write type properties on each occurrence** (`typePropertiesOnOccurrences` on `project.exportIfc`, `IfcExportOptions.TypePropertiesOnOccurrences`, off by default, the file unchanged when off): a type's own properties (standard sets, the user's sets, `Lapin_Parameters`; not its instance defaults) are also written on each of its elements in the sets of the same names, the element's own value of a property winning (`IfcExport.WorkedOut`). (#32) `architecture.createWallType` takes `params` (checked by `Params.ParseList`, set in the same undo step); **Wall types** has a **Parameters** section: a new type's parameters held in the dialog (`DraftParams`, `typeParamDraft.ts`) and sent with **Create type**, a saved type's edited in its own `ParamsSection`. (#31) An unticked yes/no is `false` and is written `IFCBOOLEAN(.F.)` on the type and on the element alike; only a parameter with no value writes nothing (user guide, IFC page, EN and RO). (#29) With Parts grouped, a wall over its parts (lintels, zones, ring beams) keeps a Body of its own made of its parts' items, each written once and shared by the parent's representation and the part's; roofs, stairs and the other aggregates stay bodiless (IFC: an aggregate is the sum of its parts). Lapin's import in reference mode leaves that body of a wall it wrote (it has Lapin_Intent and parts) and takes the parts, so nothing comes twice; in auto mode the walls are rebuilt from their intent as before. Tests: `TypeParamsIfcTests` (6), `IfcExportTests` (2 assertions turned: a grouped wall now has a Body), `typeParamDraft.test.ts` (3); guide scene `type-params-ifc`.

**17.11.2026, release 0.1.1370 (e2528f92).** Published on macOS (notarized), Windows (cross-compiled, untested on Windows), Linux (.deb and AppImage built in Docker; the .deb's `Lapin.desktop` passes `desktop-file-validate`, its `lapin.xml` MIME file is in place, which closes the part of issue #23 that was unverified), OTA (manifests at lapinbim.com), the web app and the guide. It brings the per-element door and window size (#140), scaling an imported image or PDF to a known distance (#143), the housekeeping package (#81, #23), type properties on occurrences, type parameters at creation and a body on grouped walls (#30, #32, #31, #29), spans mixed in the axis grid and several levels at once (#83) and Import types from a project (#74). The guide took only the scenes that changed or are new (openings, trace-pdf, trace-scale, levels, new-levels, extensions-types, project-types, grid, type-params-ifc) in every language; its pictures went to the server by the same rsync as `deploy.sh`, after a dry run showed nothing deleted. Lessons: `check-web` timed out on a key event while the Linux build ran in Docker, so run the web deploy when the machine is quiet; the `openings` scene failed in two languages when three scenes ran at once (its 400 ms waits) and passed alone, so its waits should become conditions; `deploy.sh` refuses a detached HEAD without `ALLOW_DIRTY=1` (checked: clean and equal to origin/main).
**Terrain: boundaries, split and merge, slope tags, cut/fill grid, 3D points, subregions, platform slopes (issue #82, PRs #84 and #85, decision 670).** T1: `civil.setBoundary` clips a terrain's TIN to an irregular (concave, arced) boundary, or its parcel's; `civil.splitTerrain` keeps the larger half as the terrain itself (its GlobalId and relations) and makes the other half a new terrain like it (`PathEdits.AddLike`), moving to it what lies in it: the elements voiding the terrain (platforms, ditches, paths: by outline centroid or path midpoint) and the placed lists (spot levels, slope tags, subregions); `civil.mergeTerrains` keeps the first terrain in place, re-relates the others' elements to it and gathers their points, breaklines, strokes and placed lists, heights carried as model heights (datum-shifted; strokes, a flatten's level included, are model heights already, so they carry over as they are) and the existing surfaces merged (each one's snapshot, else its survey) when any had a snapshot. Split terrain and Merge terrains tools on the Site tab. T2: chained major contour labels and `civil.addSlope` tags (% and 1:N). T5: the brush's grading measured against the survey or a `civil.snapshotExisting` baseline, a cut/fill grid in plan; the terrain's **CutVolume** and **FillVolume** now include the brush's grading volumes (*SculptCut*, *SculptFill*) beside the platforms', ditches' and paths', so schedules of projects with brush strokes show larger cut and fill than before. T3: `civil.moveTerrainPoint` and its siblings, with 3D height grips on survey points shown by "edit in 3D", capped at the 400 points nearest the 3D view's centre (`ui/src/modules/civil/pointGrips.ts`) at the storey's elevation. T4: subregions (a material over an area, `Lapin_SubregionQuantities`), their skin the terrain's own triangles clipped to the outline (`Terrains.SubregionSurface`, so breaklines hold), outlined in plan; platforms' cut and fill slopes (1:N). Tests: `CivilTests` (split/merge keep platforms, ditches, spots and tags with undo; datums and snapshots merged; the subregion skin within 2 cm of the ground along a breakline), `pointGrips.test.ts`; guide: Site page (EN, RO), scene `terrain-tools`.

**A large IFC export in the browser gave an empty file (report #156, decision 671).** A tester exported a 33-bay steel portal-frame hall to IFC in Lapin for the browser and got a file of zero bytes (SDNF worked). Reproduced: the hall (20 m span, 33 bays of 6 m, purlins, side rails, bracing; 3 283 entities) is a 149 MB IFC (faceted B-reps; 58 MB as face sets). The export came back as base64 in the reply's JSON: 199 million characters, over JSON's cap of 166 million (the desktop engine answered "Internal engine error: The JSON value of length 198616080 is too large"), and in the browser's engine (2 GB of WebAssembly memory) "Internal engine error: Out of memory", measured with the released web build of 17.11 in headless Chrome. Chrome's save picker makes the file when it is chosen, which is before the engine works, so the failed export left that empty file, and the error showed for four seconds. Fixed: (1) a reply carries a file as a buffer of its LPF1 frame (`FrameBytes`, `"t":"u8"`, 4-aligned with what follows) when the caller asks with `binary: true`, as the interface's `exportFile` now does for every export in the browser; other callers get base64 as before up to 120 MB (`EngineDispatcher.MaxBase64Bytes`) and above it a clear error telling them to give a path or ask for binary; `lapinbim`'s frame reader takes `u8` too; (2) the IFC is made with far less memory: `Step` keeps its data as UTF-8 chunks of a megabyte instead of one UTF-16 `StringBuilder`, `IfcExport.Bytes` makes the file once in an array of its size, and a frame's buffers are copied once into it instead of through a growing stream: the hall's export peaks at about 450 MB of managed memory over the model instead of about 880 MB before the reply was even written (where base64 and its JSON added some 600 MB more), 3.6 s; (3) in the browser a failed export removes the file the save picker made (`FileSystemFileHandle.remove`, Chrome and Edge) and says "Nothing was exported: …" in a notice that stays until it is closed (`notifyProblem`); an empty reply is a failure too. Tests: `LargeExportTests` (the 33-bay hall exported through the dispatcher with `binary`, over 125 MB and whole, and the text reply's clear error; a small export the same as text and as bytes; a frame's bytes aligned and the same by contracts alone; a STEP file over several chunks written the same into a stream and an array, line by line), `frame.test.ts`, `saveFile.test.ts`, `python/tests/test_wire.py`. Not verified: the fixed engine in the browser, since the wasm-tools workload is not installed on this machine (the fix was measured on the desktop engine).
Forum 149, 150, 152 (Rychlas, 0.1.1355; decision 672): (1) **Shift+Tab / Tab over overlapping elements work while a tool or command picks.** The cycle source in `PlanView` and `DrawingView` returned no candidates whenever a tool gesture or a command step was on; `cycleOpen` (viewport/pickCycle.ts) now keeps it open for pick and pickThenPoint gestures (candidates filtered by the tool's classes; the click takes the lit one) and for command steps asking for a pick or a selection, and shut for point and value steps and drags, where Tab keeps its meaning (the live box's Tab, the command bar). (2) **Split element snaps.** `Step.snap` makes a pick step snap the cursor like a point step (marker shown) and hands the snapped point to `pickElement`/`pick` while the element is still the one under the raw cursor (`feedPoint(raw, undefined, snapped)`); SPLITELEMENT sets it. (3) **The prompt banner no longer stands under the view toolbar.** `.hud-hint` sat at top 12 px, below the toolbar's z-index 2 row (underlay, scale, structure chips); it now starts at 46 px with a higher z-index, a max width and no pointer events, and the scope bar and breadcrumb offsets moved with it. Tests: `keys.test.ts`, `pickCycle.test.ts` (cycleOpen), `cad/session.test.ts` (snapped split), `shell/hudHintLayout.test.ts` (CSS contract).
**Text properties and a view's Mark (forum topic 148, in-app report #167, decision 673).** A drafted text's properties could not change its style, its alignment or its width. Causes: the panel sent `width` (and the leader note's "remove", `leader: null`) through `element.update`, which refused them because the annotation class had no such fields ("Annotation has no field „width”"); the style was a free text cell whose name never matched, since a style made in Drafting standards is kept by an id of its own (`text-…`) and `Standards.TextOf` looked up ids alone; and a text had no alignment at all (Texts.Marks drew every line from its start). Fixes: the annotation's fields `width` (length, ≥ 0), `align` (left, center, right) and `leader` (a new field kind `removeOnly`: null takes it off, a value is refused), shown only where an annotation has a text; `TextOf` and `DimOf` find a style by its name when no id matches (id first, as before); Texts.Marks aligns the lines: without a width the point is their start, middle or end, with a width they are aligned in the box, which stays where it was drawn; the pick box and the leader's attach follow; a text saved into a symbol keeps its alignment. The panel lists the text and dimension styles by name (kept by id) and has an Alignment list. Report #167: a section's marker wrote its name (Lapin's own head) or its number on its sheet (a head symbol, `{mark.number}`), and neither could be set: the view's `detailNumber` was read by `SymbolSlots.PlacedOn` but was no field. It is now the view's **Mark** (`SymbolSlots.MarkKey`), under Marker symbol in its properties, its placeholder what the marker writes without it (`symbols.slots` returns the view's `mark`: name, automatic number, sheet, own): Lapin's own section head, the section's trace in other views and the elevation mark write it over the name (`SymbolSlots.MarkerText`), symbols, view titles and callouts over the number on the sheet (a callout writes it even while its view is on no sheet); empty, everything is as before. Tests: `TextPropertiesTests` (6), `textProps.test.ts`, `viewMark.test.ts`; guide: Drafting (a text's properties) and Views (the marker's text), EN and RO, scenes `text-properties` and `section-mark`.
Place symbol could not be ended (report #164, 0.1.1175), fixed (decision 674). Placing repeats by design, but the step's `enter` turned the symbol, so Enter and the right-click (`finishStep`) turned it instead of ending, and the only way out was Esc, which the command line's prompt named in small text. Now `enter` ends the tool like every repeating command, and Space turns through a new optional `Step.space` (keys: `stepSpace` intent, `spaceStep` in `viewport/cad/session.ts`; without `space`, Space is Enter as before). A step can also carry a `hint`, which `ViewportHud` shows as the banner: "Click to place another · Space turns · Enter or Esc to finish" (all languages). No place-once setting was added. Tests: `symbolTools.test.ts`, `keys.test.ts`; guide: drafting page, Symbols (EN, RO).
A click outside an input box accepts it (forum 155, decision 675): Preferences → Keyboard, `clickOutAccepts`, off by default so nothing changes. Before: a typed live value (length/angle/width/height beside the cursor) stayed pending when the mouse went elsewhere, and a click in a plan or drawing already took it (the click giving the direction, `liveTaken`); text on the command line stayed pending while a click in the view fed its own point; Properties fields commit on blur and put their text back on Esc. On: `ui/src/app/clickOut.ts`, a capture-phase pointer listener installed with the shortcuts, outside the command line, live bar, fields, dialogs and menus: a left click submits the command line's text, or takes the live value (outside plans and drawings, which keep their own rule), and is used up, so it places no point of its own; a right click throws the typed value away (the command goes on) and is used up; Esc is as before. Tests: `clickOut.test.ts`; guide: Interface page (EN, RO).
**Wall axis and Tangent snaps (forum topic 151, Rychlas; decision 676, 18.11.2026).** Two object snaps join the snap menu and **Preferences → Grid and snaps**, both on by default. **Wall axis**: in a plan the cursor snaps along a shown wall's axis (its `start`, `end` and `arc`, the line it is placed by; curtain walls too), marked *wall axis*, and to the axis' ends and middle. With it on, the walls' own snap points at those ends (the engine's `[start, end]`, a curved wall's middle) are taken as wall-axis points; off, they stay endpoints as before. **Tangent**: drawing from a previous point, the cursor snaps where the line touches a circle, an arc or an ellipse (exact: the ellipse solved in closed form), drafted or an element's own curve (a curved wall's axis and its faces, found as the circles round the axis' centre that the chord-rule points of its cut lie on; an outline's curved edges from `contour` and `arcs`); a drafted circle or arc touched is recorded as the tangent inference records it. Order: endpoint > intersection > wall axis (ends, middle) > midpoint > tangent > centre > quadrant > perpendicular > on a line (nearest or along a wall axis, the nearer). Tangent goes before the centre because a curve offers its centre wherever the cursor is on it. Settings kept before carry `snapModesKnown` from now on: a snap added later comes on when it is on by default, one turned off stays off. UI only (`ui/src/viewport/cad/snaps.ts`, `elementSnaps.ts`, `aim.ts`, `arc.ts`'s `arcShape`, `app/prefs.ts`, PlanView); sections and 3D unchanged. Tests: `moreSnaps.test.ts` (19), `prefs.test.ts` updated for the new defaults; guide: Drafting page (EN, RO), scene `preferences`.

Object Snap Override (forum topic 154, Rychlas; decision 677). While a tool or command waits for a point in a 2D view, Ctrl (or Cmd) + right click opens a menu at the pointer with the SNAP menu's kinds (same labels, Wall axis and Tangent included), "No snap (for this point)" and "Snap settings…"; the one chosen is the only snap the next point takes (`snapOverride` in the CAD store: `modesFor` limits `findSnap`'s modes, `snapsOn` lets an armed kind work with SNAP off, none for "none"). It is spent by the point (`feed`, or a tool's left click through `spendOverride`), by Esc (which lets go of the override before the command; `keys.ts`) and by cancelling. `overridePress` runs first in PlanView's and DrawingView's pointer-down, so Ctrl + right click never reaches `finishStep` or the context menus; on macOS Ctrl + left click is accepted as the same gesture (it is the system's right click). Typed codes END MID INT CEN QUA PER TAN NEA AXI NON arm it at a point step, after the step's own keywords (so none is broken). The overlay shows "Snap: Midpoint · Esc cancels" under the cursor. Ortho, polar and the grid still apply to "No snap"; the 3D inference (snap3d.ts) is unchanged. UI only (`ui/src/viewport/cad/snapOverride.ts`, `snaps.ts`, `aim.ts`, `session.ts`, `canvas.tsx`, PlanView, DrawingView, `app/keys.ts`, i18n); tests `snapOverride.test.ts` (12); guide: Drafting page (EN, RO). Not run in the real app.

**Material graphics, stages 0 and 0b on the engine: reference details and the flexible configurator (docs/plan-material-graphics.md; decision 678, 17.11.2026).** Stage 0: `ReferenceDetails` (tests) builds seven small projects from commands — a composite wall (plaster, brick, a 1 mm vapour barrier, EPS, render) in an L, a T and a free end with a window and its sill; a wall on a layered floor (decision 497); a floor (tiles, screed, barrier, XPS, concrete); a strip footing with its waterproofing marked a membrane; a concrete column and beam in a brick wall; a tiled roof; a facade of facing brick, timber and metal cladding under tiles — each view made at 1:20, 1:50 and 1:100 and drawn by `Views.Render`. `MaterialGraphicsReferenceTests` checks no cut line between two elements' bands of one material, hatch strokes inside the cut bands, cut > seen > hatch, every band of a hatched material at least 3 mm on paper hatched, membranes on an interface or along their own band, and compares a snapshot of the model's pens (`MaterialGraphicsReference.snap.txt`: per pen its layer, colour, width, fill, dashes, strokes' count and length; `LAPIN_UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1` rewrites it). `LAPIN_REFERENCE_PDF=1` writes one PDF of all details' sheets and each detail as a `.lapin` to `build/material-graphics-reference/` (for the guide's scenes, stage 8). Today's drawings pass every rule. Stage 0b: each material's `section`, `plan` and `elevation` take `hatchColor`, `hatchWeight` (mm, over `hatchPen`), `hatchLineType`, `hatchOpacity` (0–100), `halftone`, `fillOpacity`, `backgroundHatch` {hatch, hatchScale, hatchAngle, hatchColor, hatchWeight} and `cutLine` / `separatorLine` {color, weight, lineType}; unset, a drawing is exactly as before (`ResolvedMaterial.Flex`). A plan takes the section's fields, its own over them. Opacity rides on the colour (`#rrggbbaa`, which PDF, SVG and now DXF's group 440 already read). `MaterialDrawing.Apply` adds the background hatch under the hatch and keeps each band's graphics (`MaterialDrawingResult.Graphics`); `Pens.MaterialLines` gives a cut line its material's line — a line between two layers of one element (or two joined elements of one class) the separator line, any other the cut line; where two materials meet and both set one, the heavier wins, then the name that sorts first; over the Object styles and the view's category weights, under line edits and filters' colours. Surface hatches (elevations, sections) the same; the material legend's swatch is drawn by `MaterialSample.Swatch` (so a solid with a fill now shows its fill there, as drawings do); `material.preview` draws a sample (a strip between two plain neighbours, or a band) by the same path. `material.update` checks the fields, takes `ids` (several materials, one undo step, no renaming), `null` takes a field back to its default (hatch and fill keep null = none) and `reset: [fields]` takes any back. IFC: a material with its own graphics writes an `IfcFillAreaStyle` per kind (`Section hatch`, `Plan hatch`, `Elevation hatch`) with its fill colour and an `IfcFillAreaStyleHatching` for the background hatch and the hatch, each `IfcCurveStyle` named after its pattern (`Background …`) with its colour and width; the import reads them onto a foreign material that sets none (Lapin's own come back by `Lapin_Intent`). Bonsai: the patterns take the hatch colour, weight, angle and the background hatch, the cut rule the material's cut line. DXF keeps hatches as lines (no HATCH entities, as before): the background's strokes on the same HATCH layer with their own colour and lineweight. Not done: 3D section caps drawn as a sheet (their own path) use only the fill and the hatch; renaming a project line type does not follow into the materials. Tests: `MaterialGraphicsReferenceTests` (138 cases), `MaterialGraphicsFlexTests` (13), `SolidHatchColourTests` (+1).

**Material graphics, stage 1 on the engine: real-size hatches (docs/plan-material-graphics.md; decision 679, 17.11.2026).** A hatch pattern has units (`HatchPattern.Units`): `paper`, millimetres as printed (every pattern before), or `model`, millimetres of the object, so it keeps its real size at any scale. `.pat` reads the format's public headers `;%TYPE=MODEL|DRAFTING` (after a pattern's header or before it) and `;%UNITS=MM|INCH` (inches × 25.4), and writes `;%TYPE=MODEL` after a model pattern's header only, so paper files are written as before. New built-in real-size patterns, drawn by Lapin, each joint one line: `BRICK_240X63` (the default brick, Î3), `BRICK_240X71`, `BRICK_215X65`, `BLOCK_400X200`, `BLOCK_600X250`, `TILE_300X300`, `TILE_600X600`, `BOARDS_150`, `STONE_600X300`, `ROOF_TILES` (a 330 mm gauge, 300 wide), `METAL_SEAM_500`. `HatchScale` (Lapin.Drawing) gives the drawing metres per pattern unit (`Factor`) and leaves out a model pattern whose closest lines lie under the view style's new `minHatchSpacing` (paper mm, default 0.7, 0 draws every one; a view sets its own through `view.setStyle`, Î4); paper patterns are always drawn. A material's kind of view takes `hatchUnits` (`paper` | `model`) over its pattern's. Cut bands (`Pens`), surface hatches, floor finishes, 3D section caps, drafted hatches, room fills and colour-scheme legends lay patterns by their units; material legends and `material.preview` draw at their view's scale; `hatch.preview` draws a model pattern at 1:20 (or `viewScale`) and says `units`; `hatch.list` gives each pattern's `units`; Bonsai's stylesheet draws one as at 1:100; IFC writes `ModelOrDraughting` true for one. A new project's facades and floor finishes take the real-size patterns from names (brick, BCA, blocks, stone, timber, cladding, roof tiles, tiles; floors: tiles 600×600, faianță 300×300, parquet boards); `MaterialHatchUpgrade` goes to `hatchRev` 3 and marks a project saved before `paperSurfaceHatches: true`, under which the suggestions stay the paper ones (a mark on the project, not a pattern pinned on each material as revisions 1 and 2 did: a floor finish takes a material's own elevation hatch first, so a pin would have changed old plans). An element's `hatchOrigin` `[u, v]` (metres in its frame: along its axis from its start, and up from its base in elevations and sections or to the axis's left in a plan along its axis; an element without an axis from its lowest left corner in the view) starts its surface hatches and its plan cut bands along its axis; `element.setHatchOrigin {id|ids, origin: [u, v] | null}`, one undo step. Without one a real-size pattern's courses start at the element's base (across, from the view's origin, so courses run on from wall to wall); paper patterns keep the view's origin, so nothing an older project drew moves. The facade reference detail's walls now face the elevation (drawn right to left), so the reference sheets show the brick, boards and seams at real size (snapshot rewritten for the facade only). Not done: symbols, the layer table (`TypeBuildUps`) and title blocks keep paper hatches; IFC import does not read `ModelOrDraughting`; 3D section caps take the units but not the element's origin. Tests: `ModelHatchTests` (21), `SurfaceHatchTests`, `SectionHatchDefaultTests`, `RoomFinishTests` and `RoofCoveringMaterialTests` turned to the new suggestions (the paper ones kept under the mark).

**Material graphics, stage 2 on the engine: representation by scale (docs/plan-material-graphics.md; decision 680, 18.11.2026).** Each kind of view of a material (`section`, `plan`, `elevation`) takes `byScale`, an ordered list of steps `{from?, to?, …fields}`: `from` and `to` are scale denominators, both held (`from: 100` is 1:100 and every smaller scale, `to: 50` is 1:50 and every larger one), at least one given, `from ≤ to`; the fields are any of stage 0b's and 1's (hatch, scale, angle, colour, weight, line type, opacity, halftone, fill and its opacity, background hatch, cut and separator lines, units). The first step whose range holds the view's scale lays its fields over the material's own, field by field (`MaterialGraphics.Resolve(…, scale)`, `ScaleStep`); a plan takes the section's step, then its own fields, then its own step; with no scale (IFC, Bonsai) no step is taken. `material.update` checks the steps as it checks the 0b fields (at most 20; in a step `hatch`/`fill` null is none, any other null is left out; `null`, `[]` or `reset: ["byScale"]` takes them off); `material.preview` shows the step for its `scale` (`resolved.step`: the kind and index); `material.list` gives each kind's `byScale`. A view style takes `smallScaleCut` `{mode: asMaterial | solid | outline | coreOnly, from (default 200), color?}`, written only when set, so a project's styles save as before and draw as before (Î5); a view overrides it field by field with `view.setStyle` (null: the style's again), and `viewStyle.update` sets it (null: none). From its threshold on it changes the cut bands of walls, slabs, roofs, columns, beams, members, plates, footings, piles and chimneys (a window's glass keeps its look): `solid` fills each element as one area in `color` (else ink; a Visibility / Graphics category fill colour still wins), its bands merged as a coarse view merges them (no lines between layers, membranes filled) and bands of one colour joined across elements; `outline` draws neither hatch nor fill, the element's outline alone (its bands merged; elements of one type joined, so a column keeps its outline in a wall); `coreOnly` fills the core (the layer marked core, else the thickest; an element with no layers is all core) and leaves the other layers empty, every line and membrane kept. One precedence, in one class (`ViewMaterialGraphics`, Lapin.Platform), the strongest first: an element's override (hook) > the view's filters (stage 5, hook) > the style's material graphics set (stage 4, hook) > `smallScaleCut` > the material's step > the material > the standard / name guess; the category graphics of Visibility / Graphics still lay themselves over all of it in the pens. Cuts (`Views.Render`, so sheets at each viewport's view scale, PDF and DXF), elevations' surface hatches, legends (at their placed view's scale, with steps, without the small-scale cut), 3D section caps drawn as a sheet (the step at their scale) and the preview go through it. The reference snapshot is unchanged. Not done: the preview does not show `smallScaleCut`; the layer table (`TypeBuildUps`) and civil corridors take materials without a step; the interface. Tests: `HatchByScaleTests` (9).

Material graphics stage 3 on the engine (decision 681, docs/plan-material-graphics.md): the standards as data. A material standard (`MaterialStandard`, Lapin.Drawing) is an id, name, author, country, norm, version, licence and languages, ordered rules (a material's name in several languages, words or small expressions matched on the folded name, or its category → its section, plan and elevation graphics with every field of stages 0b, 1 and 2, membrane, category), its own `.pat` patterns and default hatch and cut pens. Lapin's coded name tables became the built-in „Lapin” standard (`MaterialStandards/lapin.json`) with exactly the same results, checked on over 1500 names; the legacy tables for old projects stay in code. Four more ship as embedded JSON, drawn by Lapin from the norms' general principles and marked indicative: ISO 128-50, DIN 1356, România (SR ISO 128-50: ISO's graphics, Romanian names) and BS 8888 / BS 1192, with seven new patterns. The project names its standard (`materialStandard`, none: Lapin's, so nothing an existing project draws changes) and keeps its own (`materialStandards`); the standard is the lowest tier of `MaterialGraphics.Resolve`, below its scale step, the material and the material's step, and every drawing path passes the project's. Commands: `standards.materialList`, `materialGet`, `materialSet`, `materialDuplicate`, `materialRemove` (built-ins neither changed nor removed), `applyMaterials {id, overwrite?, preview?}` and the query `applyPreview` (before/after per material), `fromProject` (a rule per material that sets its own graphics, then the current standard's rules), `previewSheet` (the reference details, moved into the engine as `MaterialReferenceDetails`, drawn with a standard: each view's drawing, and one sheet as SVG and PDF). The `.lapinstandards` file carries `materialGraphics`, exported, reported by `readPack` and imported with the merge choices. The five packs are in `extensions-packs/standards/` as store examples (manifest, file, sample sheet). Also: grips for the hatch origin (`element.hatchOriginPoint`, `element.hatchOriginAt`) and line type renames and removals followed into materials and standards. The aesthetic revision of „Lapin” was proposed (rigid insulation reads darker than masonry, concrete lighter) but not applied. Tests: `MaterialStandardTests` (14) and every standard drawing the reference details by the rules.

Material graphics stage 4 on the engine (decision 682, docs/plan-material-graphics.md): graphics by discipline, style and subcategory. Material graphics sets (`materialGraphicsSets` on the project, `MaterialGraphicsSets`): named lists of entries `{match: {material?, materialName?, category?}, section?, plan?, elevation?}` with every field of stages 0b–2, checked as `material.update` checks a material; the most particular matching entry wins (a material's id over a name expression over a category over all), the first on a tie. A view style names a set (`materialGraphicsSet`), a view another or `none` (`view.setStyle`); commands `materialGraphicsSet.list/create/update/delete`, each one undo step (a deleted set leaves its styles none). The set is tier 3 of `ViewMaterialGraphics`, over the small-scale cut; Visibility / Graphics subcategories now give graphics (`cutHatch`, `fillColor`, `lineColor`, `lineWeight`, `halftone` beside `hidden`) as tier 2.5, over the set and over their category's cut hatch and fill; in views, graphics sets and custom links; the 3D takes halftone and colour per part. New projects carry the mark `materialLookRev: 1`: their Structural and MEP styles name the built-in „Structure” (concrete solid grey 40 %, masonry 45° lines, finishes plain, insulation halftone) and „MEP” (no hatch, grey lines) sets, they draw by „Lapin” 1.1 (rigid insulation 1.5, blocks 1.25, concrete 0.75 after measuring every pattern's ink density; floor coverings' surfaces), and their rooms' floor finishes take the project's standard instead of their own table; projects made before keep all of it exactly (1.0 is `lapin-1.0.json`). Sets travel in `.lapinstandards` (`materialGraphicsSets`, material ids written as names, the merge choices, reported by `readPack`). The reference snapshot (21 views' hatch pens) and the Lapin store pack (1.1.0, new screenshot) were rewritten. Tests: `MaterialGraphicsSetTests` (8), `SubcategoryGraphicsTests` (5), `MaterialLookRevisionTests` (3).

Material graphics stage 5 on the engine (decision 683, docs/plan-material-graphics.md): view filters change hatch and fill. A view's filter row (and a custom link's) takes `cutHatch` (a pattern or none), `cutFill` (a colour or none), `hatchColor`, `hatchHalftone` and `cutLine {color, weight}` for cut bands, and `surfaceHatch` / `surfaceFill` for the faces an elevation sees; field by field the first filter in the view's list that takes the element gives each (Revit's rule). They are tier 2 of `ViewMaterialGraphics`, over the subcategory, the style's set, the small-scale cut and the material, and Visibility / Graphics' category hatch and fill no longer lie over them; a filter's fill is not paled by the filter's line colour, a band with no material takes what the filter draws, and bands of one material join only when the filters draw them alike. Plans, sections, elevations, sheets, PDF and DXF all draw it through the pens. `view.graphics` checks a filter row's fields and `view.visibility` lists them (`filterFields`). Legends now draw with the placed view's material graphics set and list a filter that gives hatch or fill as a row of its own. A view whose filters give none of the new fields draws exactly as before. Tests: `FilterHatchTests` (6).

Material graphics stage 7 on the engine (decision 684, docs/plan-material-graphics.md): hatch patterns made by drawing. Line-family patterns from their numbers: `hatch.create`, `hatch.update` (a new name followed everywhere a pattern is named: materials and their steps and backgrounds, standards, material graphics sets, Visibility / Graphics rows and filters, drafted hatches, symbols), `hatch.delete` (refused while the pattern is used, unless forced; then what named it draws no hatch, a drafted hatch the 45° lines), `hatch.get`, `hatch.usage`, `hatch.exportPat`, each command one undo step; built-in patterns are fixed and their names taken. `hatch.preview` draws an inline, unsaved pattern for the editor. `drafting.toPattern` saves drafted linework in a rectangular cell as a pattern: straight pieces and dots that run along a small vector of the cell's grid become exact `.pat` line families (`TileFit`: period, the next line by p·b − q·a = 1, dashes; a piece and its copy one cell over one family); anything else (arcs, circles, odd angles) a new kind, the tile pattern (`HatchTile`: the cell's lines, dots and arcs in mm), kept in the project's pattern text as Lapin's own `;%LAPIN-…` comment headers, so `.lapinstandards` and standards carry it unchanged. `HatchFill` lays the cell on every grid place reaching the region and clips each piece even-odd, arcs by the one rule for curves; DXF gets the lines, IFC the nearest line hatching named after the pattern (not IfcFillAreaStyleTiles, which few readers draw), Bonsai an SVG tile; a `.pat` for another program leaves tiles out and names them. Tests: `PatternEditorTests` (7).
A finish never through the roof (forum topic 161, Rychlas; decision 686). A finish painted on a wall attached to a roof stood out through it: the wall's side faces already followed the roof (forum #123), but the painter's frame (`FinishZones.FrameOf`) replaced a wall side's outline by the box round it (bottom to the gable's peak, end to end along the outermost layer), so every region, body and measure was the rectangle. Now the frame keeps the face's own outline, its end edges carried out flat to the layer's footprint at an outer corner and clipped where a join stops it short (`AlongFootprint`): the one place a painted finish gets its extent, rebuilt on every ask, so a zone painted before the wall's top changed (attached, detached, a roof that cuts it) follows the new shape, a split drawn past the top paints only what is on the wall, and the stored zones and splits stay as drawn. The wall's faces also follow every body it is attached to (a Studio solid, an imported element), as its own body does: `IElementFaces.FacesOf` takes the session's services (a default overload, so other modules are unchanged) and `ArchitectureFaces` passes them to `WallTops.Profile` (it passed null, Lapin's roofs only). Finishes from a room finish painted on a face take the same path; the room finish's own wall ring (a flat top at the ceiling) is a separate path, unchanged. Tests: `FinishZoneTests` (6 new: gable attached, painted before attaching and detached again, a region over a roof's later cut, a rectangle past the gable, four mitred facades under a gable, a wall under a Studio loft); guide: Rooms (Finish painter), EN and RO. Not run in the real app.
Trace building (forum topic 158, Rychlas; decision 687). The Floor tool's bar has a new source, **Trace building** (`'building'` in `OutlineShape`/`PICKED`, offered only by `FloorShapeBar`): one click anywhere on the storey takes the building's outline from the engine (`architecture.buildingOutline {storeyId?, at?}` → `{points, arcs?, walls, area}`) and makes an ordinary floor of the tool's type through `addSlab`; `architecture.addFloorFromBuilding {storeyId?, at?, typeId?, offset?}` does it in one command for scripts and MCP (one undo step, a new GlobalId). `BuildingTrace` (architecture): the storey's walls make a plan graph of their axes, split where their joins put the joint (an end, or a point on a run), else where an end touches another wall within its half thickness or two straight walls cross; its bridges are taken out (Tarjan: stubs, fences, free walls, a wall between two blocks); each part's faces are walked turning as far right as possible, the one counter-clockwise face being its outside; the part round the click is taken, else the largest. Each outer wall is set off to its core's exterior face (`Walls.Section`, the core bands; the thickest when none is marked), an arc as a concentric arc, and consecutive faces meet where their lines or circles cross (a step when two in line differ). A free wall end within 1.5 m of another wall or end is a gap closed in thought: when the outside then runs through it, nothing is traced and the message names the gap and its two ends; gaps inside the building do not count. Courtyards are not made holes (they cannot be told from rooms by the walls alone). Tests `BuildingTraceTests` (8: rectangle with a stub and a free wall, 84.5625 m²; L of 300/200 cores, 95.615 m²; arc wall, R 7.35 core face, rise −2.0573, 77.0904 m²; a 1 m gap named; a passage inside ignored; two buildings by click; no loop), UI `outlineBuilding.test.ts` (2); guide: Floors page (EN, RO), scene `floor-trace-building`. Not run in the real app.

A wall's reference line kept with it (forum topic 159 by Petri, seconded by Rychlas; GitHub #63; decision 685). Walls were already drawn on a face (decision 237: the core's centre or faces, the finish faces), but the line only placed them: the axis went back to the core's centre. The axis still stays there (IFC's Axis, the layer set usage and every join unchanged), and a wall may now also keep a reference line, the attribute `referenceLine` ({kind, offset?, layer?}: coreCentre, coreExterior, coreInterior, exteriorFace, interiorFace, a layer face counted from the exterior, or a signed offset left of the core's centre); absent, it is the core's centre, so old walls and files are unchanged. The line is derived on every ask by one rule (`WallReference.LeftOf`, parallel, concentric on an arc by `WallReference.Offset`). `architecture.addWall` takes `reference` (the old names or the object) and `rememberReference`, and arcs are now drawn on the chosen line too; `architecture.setWallReference` changes the line, not the wall, in one undo step. A wall that keeps one grows round it when its thickness or type changes (`Walls.LayOut`, the no-layer branch of `WallThickness`) and turns round it when flipped, its joined neighbours' ends following (`Joins.MoveEnds`). The interface is told the line as `referenceAxis` (an `INodeProps`): the Wall tool offers an offset from the core centre and Remember reference line (off by default), Properties a Reference line field with the layer faces and an offset, the end grips sit on the line, the Wall axis snap takes it and its ends, and a selected wall draws it solid beside its axis. Guide: walls.md and drafting.md, both languages, scene `wall-reference`. Tests: `WallReferenceLineTests` (20, the 100 + 250 + 15 wall: faces at −0.225, −0.125, 0.125, 0.140; 365 → 465 keeps the exterior face, the axis to 0.275; a radius-5 arc on its exterior face has a 4.775 axis, 4.725 once thicker), `wallReference.test.ts` (6). Left: the wall's temporary dimensions on the line, a mode that moves the wall to keep the line, `updateWall` with a thickness on a wall without layers.

**A wall attached to a deleted target no longer rises to every roof (decision 689, docs/plan-attach-to.md stage A0).** A wall attached by name (`roofIds`) to an element that was then deleted kept the dead id, and `WallTops.Bodies` and `RoofTop.AttachedRoofs`, finding nothing named, fell back to every roof in the project: a wall attached to a deleted Studio solid rose to any roof over it on any storey, with no Problem. Now a new `IDeletionFollowers` (`AttachedLinks`, in `element.delete`'s undo step like `RoomFinishLinks`) takes the target out of every wall's `roofIds`; a wall left naming nothing goes to `roofTop: none`, standing at its own height, and keeps the lost targets' names in `roofTopGone`, which the Problems panel shows (`WallTops.Problem`, beside `WallTop.Problem`) until the wall is attached again, detached or its mode changed in Properties. On read, a non-empty `roofIds` takes only what still exists and never widens, and dead ids left by an older build or an unannounced removal are reported. An attachment with no names (every roof) and a split roof's pieces (`RoofPieces`) are unchanged. Tests: `AttachDeletedTargetTests` (3: the body deleted under a roof on the storey above, the wall at 3.00 and the Problem naming the body, undo and redo; a split roof's piece deleted, the wall keeping the other; dangling ids taking no roof).

**Attach top or bottom: the platform's contract and commands (decision 690, docs/plan-attach-to.md stage A1).** The sampler that reads a body's underside for a wall's top is now the platform's (`Lapin.Platform/Attach/BodySampler.cs`), moved out of `WallTops` with its numbers unchanged: a target's triangles flagged down or (new) up, cached per revision through `RevisionCache` as before; `Underside` along a line and its mirror `Overside` for bottoms; `UnderAt`, `OverAt` and `Area` for a section's points; `Merge`, `Simplify`, `Eval`. `BodySampler.Sample` adds the guard the engine lacked (§3.6): a per-thread list of the elements whose targets are being read; a target already on it is skipped, and an element its own targets come back to gets no triangles and stands at its own end, so a circle never overflows the stack. `IAttachable` is the contract a module registers per class (classes, ends, class rules, `Attach`, `Detach`, `StateOf`, and `Result`: the end as built or as it would be); `AttachRelations` stores an end as `IfcRelConnectsElements` ("Lapin attach top"), the storage A2 onwards use. The core commands `element.attachTo {ids, end, targets | level, offset, mode, add}` and `element.detach {ids, end?}` are one undo step for any number of elements; a class without the end refuses the whole command by name, and a target that is gone, the element itself, a body-less one, or one attached back to the element (the walk names the path) is refused, as are references and component members. The queries `element.attachments` (per end: targets, level, mode, result, problems; with no id, the attachable classes for the tool's filter) and `element.attachPreview` (the ghost: a wall's profile, its top line and the new top face's outline in the world; nothing written). Walls implement it over what they had: targets write exactly `attachToRoof`'s `roofTop`/`roofIds`, a level what `setWallTop` writes, detach is `detachFromRoof` (none) keeping the level; an offset, a mode other than both, and a bottom are refused (walls' bottoms are A8); a wall in a circle reports "Its top and „X” depend on each other". `AllowedRemote` is unchanged (an editor guest runs any command, `attachToRoof` included). No interface yet (A7) and no new strings; the catalogue is regenerated and docs/python.md has an example. Tests: `AttachToTests` (11: the loft body's 6.00 and 51/39 volume through `attachTo` equal to `attachToRoof` vertex for vertex, a gable roof the same, a level as `setWallTop`; a door refused by name and the whole command with it, wall to wall with today's message, no bottom, no gap, no mode; a circle refused naming both and through a third; a circle forced by hand, the wall at 3.00 and the probe at its own 4.5, each with the Problem; undo and redo of a changed attachment and `add`; attachments, detach and the preview; a relation dropped with its deleted target; the sampler's down and up faces, steps to a millimetre and areas), with a test probe class stored as relations standing in for A2's columns. Every existing `attachToRoof` test passes unchanged.

**Attach top or bottom: columns (decision 691, docs/plan-attach-to.md stage A2).** A column's top and bottom now attach to bodies through `element.attachTo` (relations, `AttachRelations`; `ColumnAttach` in `Lapin.Structure/ColumnEnds.cs`), with the modes (`both`, `extend`, `trim`, decided at the axis) and a gap. The end is built from the target's faces over the section: the axis hit gives the length; the section's corners, centre, a 9 × 9 grid and its sides sampled every centimetre decide the shape. When every sample lies within 0.5 mm of one plane, the prism is built to it directly (no boolean); several planes whose lowest (a top) or highest (a bottom) is the surface everywhere, a ridge, are cut by one half-space each; anything else (a step, a valley over a top, a section partly under a target) goes the boolean path (built 1 cm past its highest point, the region beyond the target's triangles taken off by manifold; where no target is, its own end). Problems: not over it / not under it, under its base / over its top (it stands at its own end), part of its section not under „X”, and the circle message. **Targets are read as their own producers build them, before the cut order** (`BodySampler.Sample(..., uncut: true)`, a cache of its own): a column attached under a slab its drawn envelope cuts reads the slab's soffit, not its own hole, and no booleans are spent on reading; walls still read the cut bodies (unchanged).
