The sampler's guard also refuses an element whose targets are asked for again while being read (a path no attachment names). `Columns.Built(m, column, services)` is the column as built, once per revision; `Columns.Spec` and `Columns.Envelope` stay the column as drawn. The callers, one by one: **switched to Built**: the column's own body (`Columns.Geometry`), its quantities (`Columns.Quantities`, from the last build, as quantities have no services: Length, GrossVolume), `StructureFaces` (through `FacesOf` with services: sides up to the ends, the ridge's and steps' points on them, a sloped top and bottom), `StructureAnalytical` (from `AnalyticalContribution.Services`), `ColumnBars.Of` and `TypeStirrups.Of` (an optional `services`, passed by `RebarBodies`; bars ending in the column, hooked, stop under the attached top; bars going on into a column standing on the drawn top keep the drawn height; the bottom always as built), and the IFC body (`FrameIfcBodies`, through a new default `IIfcSweptBodies.BodyOf(model, services, element)` the export now calls). **Kept on Spec**: `Beams.Solve` (a beam's span at column faces: what another element reads), `MemberAxes.Of` for columns (steel nodes and `fit` read other members' axes; steel columns are A3), and `Columns.Envelope` (what columns cut slabs and walls with). IFC: under planes, the sweep from the lowest corner to the highest clipped by an `IfcHalfSpaceSolid` per sloped plane (a level end needs none), only when nothing else cut the column (else, and on the boolean path, the B-rep). Not done here: a level as a column's end (refused: a column has its own height and base offset), steel columns with `steelEnds` (refused, A3; one with holes from the steel module is rebuilt by it as drawn), the interface (A7), the IFC round trip of the relation (A6). No new command, so the catalogue is unchanged; docs/python.md has a column example; the guide is unchanged (no interface yet). Measured (Debug, Apple-silicon Mac): 400 columns attached under one slab and built in 66 ms, built again after the slab moved in 54 ms, the slab's triangles worked out once a revision per building thread (7–8 times on this machine: the revision cache is not single-flight); 400 columns under 60 bodies of 14 400 triangles in 837 ms. Placing those 400 columns with their drawn tops in the slab took 8 minutes, all of it the slab cut by 400 envelopes after each command, not the attachment. Bloc-P-4.ifc was not reachable from the worktree, so the many-triangles case stands in for it. Tests: `AttachColumnTests` (13: the sloped body's 3.400 axis, 3.370 and 3.430 corners, 0.3060 m³, Length 3.400, one half-space of normal (−0.196, 0, 0.981), raised 0.50 to 3.930 live, detached 3.000 and 0.2700 m³ and undone; a Studio loft's slope, 4.5; trim 2.780 and 0.2502 m³ under the default floor, extend 4.000 kept and 2.50 to 2.780, trim 2.50 kept, the slab's volume unchanged; the ridge's two clips and 0.30465 m³ with the ridge on the side faces; the beam and slab step 0.2421 m³ on the boolean path with its Problem and a B-rep; a beam's clear span 4.70 unchanged over attached columns; a bottom on the ramp at 0.185 / 0.215, 2.800 and 0.2520 m³; bars to 3.40 less their cover and more stirrups; a target 5 m away; the preview; a circle refused and one forced; the two speeds). Every other test passes unchanged.

**Space flips the line a wall is drawn on (decision 692, 18.11.2026; Revit's Spacebar).** While the Wall tool is on (Line or Arc, before the first click and between clicks), <kbd>Space</kbd> or **Other side (Space)** in the command line's bar takes the same line on the other side of the wall: **Exterior face** ↔ **Interior face**, **Core, exterior face** ↔ **Core, interior face**, an offset changes sign; **Core centre** has no other side (a notice says to pick a face or an offset, or to flip the wall afterwards). The exterior stays the wall's right as drawn: what goes across the line is the body. The choice is the bar's list (stored as before), and the hint over the view says what the next Space gives (`ToolDef.spaceHint`; the HUD and the command line redraw on the tool tick). The plan now draws the wall's body faintly beside the line drawn, on the side it will be built (`twoPoints`/`threePoints` gestures' new `body`, set by `chainTool`; the type's layers laid out as the engine lays out a new wall's, `wallLine.ts`: `bandsOf`, `lineLeft` (WallReference.LeftOf for a wall not flipped), `mirrorLine`, `wallGhost`). Space keeps its other uses: tools without a `space` and steps with their own (symbols turn, a door's hand) are unchanged. No engine change. Tests: `wallLine.test.ts` (5); scene `wall-space-flip` (walls page, both languages).

**Temporary dimensions for walls (decision 693, 18.11.2026; Revit's, after doors' and windows' of decision 511 and grid axes' of 602).** One straight wall selected in a plan shows, through `TempDimMarks`: across, from its reference (the reference line it keeps, decision 685, else its axis) to the nearest parallel wall or grid axis on each side whose stretch along it overlaps its own, standing halfway along that stretch; along, from each end not joined to a wall to the first straight wall its line runs into beyond it (within 50 m; the crossing must hit that wall, not its line's extension). A new engine query, `architecture.wallLines {storeyId | id}` (`WallLines.cs`), gives the storey's straight walls: axis, faces and core faces left of the axis (the core only when it is not the whole wall), and the walls joined at its start, end and along it; nothing else in the engine changed (no new command: a value typed is `architecture.updateWall` with the grips' arguments, `detachJoinedEnds`, one undo step, so joins and hosted openings behave as when its grips are dragged). The pure logic is `wallDims.ts` (`wallDims`, `commitAcross`, `commitEnd`): the reference's end cycles near face → far face → core faces → axis, the wall's own end its reference → near face → far face; sums typed as elsewhere; the padlock makes an aligned dimension of the view. While a wall is drawn in Line mode, after its first click, the same dimensions show read-only from the line clicked (faces from the type and the line chosen, Space included) to the walls beside it and from the cursor to the wall ahead: the plan now publishes the straight piece being drawn (`viewport/drawnPath.ts`). Left out: curved walls (none shown, and curved walls are not targets), ends joined to a wall, parallel walls as targets of an end, sections and elevations. Overlay `WallTempDims.tsx` (the name avoids a case clash with `wallDims.ts`). Tests: `WallLinesTests` (4), `wallDims.test.ts` (6); guide: walls → Temporary dimensions, both languages; scene `wall-temp-dimensions`.

**Stair by sketch (decision 694, 19.11.2026), asked for by users as Revit has it.** Architecture → Circulation → Stair by sketch (STAIRSKETCH, STS) opens a board of its own (`studio.editStairSketch`, a plan of the storey) where the stair is drawn with Draft's tools as lines of three roles, two of them new drafting roles: `boundary` (the two sides, green; a line with no role counts as one), `riser` (each riser across; none: the rules' risers spread evenly along the line midway between the sides) and `path` (which way it climbs). n riser lines make n risers of H / n; each tread is the shape between two risers and the sides at its height; one deeper than max(0.5 m, 1.5 × the rules' deepest tread) is a landing; curved sides give winders. The side form picks the role, turns the climb round (`studio.flipStairSketch`) and shows live what the sketch makes (`studio.stairSketchCheck` → `architecture.stairSketchCheck`). Finish (`studio.finishStairSketch`, one undo step) calls `architecture.addSketchStair` or `architecture.updateStairSketch`, which refuse a sketch that makes no stair, saying why (two sides, a riser that does not reach both, risers crossing…), and keep the sketch on the stair (`sketch`, in the stair's own frame, so move and rotate carry it; `architecture.stairSketch` gives it back in plan). In the engine (`Stairs/StairSketch.cs`) a sketched stair is a `Resolved` like any other — its treads as winders, its landings as landings, plus a `SketchRun` — so its body, the plan symbol cut at the cut plane, step numbers, railings (along each side, on the pitch over steps, level over landings), the opening above, quantities and IFC are every stair's. Right-click → Edit sketch and the inspector's Edit sketch / Use the type's shape. Tests: `StairSketchTests` (9), `stairSketchBoard.test.ts` (2). Left: grips on the sides and risers in plan, ramps by sketch, a continuous soffit under sketched treads, risers square to a drawn path.

**Stairs: landing and the upper floor plan (decisions 695, 696; issues #35, #36, 19.11.2026, from users' pull requests).** A stair that starts on a lower storey and reaches the level of the one being drawn now shows in that storey's plan (`StairPlan`: the stair whose base is under the storey's elevation and whose top reaches it), with its steps, landing and walking line cut at the cut plane; its arrow ends in `DN` at the top step instead of `UP` at the foot, and the label (the sizes) is written only on the storey it starts from. The landing is set by two new fields of the stair spec and of a stair's own properties (`StairSpec.LandingWidth`, `LandingRiser`; `landingWidth`, `landingRiser` on the element): **Landing after step** puts the landing after that riser of the first flight (clamped to 1…n−1; empty, halfway) on every shape with a landing but spiral and curved, and **Landing width** is an L stair's landing across the turn, independent of its length (0: the stair's width; not with winders). Both are in Stair types → Shape and in the inspector. Tests: `StairTests`, `StairPlanCutTests`.

**Joinery: sliding window planes, open or closed (decisions 697, 698; issues #39, #40, 19.11.2026).** A sliding window's sashes now run on staggered tracks: every other sash of a row on the inner plane and the rest on the outer (`Joinery.Boxes`, as lift-and-slide doors already did), with glass and infill held within the track's thickness. A door or window has a `openMode` (`auto`, `open`, `closed`; command `architecture.setJoineryOpen`, the inspector's **State**): `auto` keeps what was drawn (open in plan, closed in 3D), `open` also turns a hinged leaf a quarter turn and slides a sliding one 85 % of its cell in 3D (`OpenPanelBoxes`; frames stay) and draws the plan open, `closed` draws the plan leaf shut with no swing. Tests: `JoineryDrawingTests`.

**IFC export: spaces as faceted B-rep (decision 699; issue #28, 19.11.2026).** `ExportOptions.SpaceBrep` (the export dialog's **Spaces** tick, argument `spaceBrep` of `project.exportIfc`) writes each IfcSpace's body as the faceted B-rep of its geometry parts instead of the `IfcExtrudedAreaSolid` over its outline, for readers that fail on the extruded profile (CYPECAD MEP). Off, the file is unchanged. Test: `IfcExportTests`.

**Pins: link to a grid axis or a reference plane (decision 700; issue #37, 19.11.2026).** An element carries `lockedTo` (`{kind: axis, gridId, tag}` or `{kind: plane, id}`), set by `element.lockTo` (also pins it; `null` clears the link, which is also cleared by unpinning), from the inspector's **Linked to** beside **Pinned**. When the axis (`Pins.FollowAxis`, from the grid axis's move in Drafting, not from its copy) or the plane (`Pins.FollowPlane`) moves, the linked elements follow by the same placement transform, a pinned element moving by that link only. Tests: `PinTests`.

**Roofs, forum topic 160 (decisions 701–706; issues #98–#102, #104–#107, 19.11.2026).** Eight changes to the parametric roof. Gutters of eaves meeting at one height are mitred at the corner and lose their end caps there (`Drainage.Gutter` `StartMiter`/`EndMiter`, `CapStart`/`CapEnd`; #98). Rafter tails come in six cuts, plumb, square, level, bevel, stepped, curved (`RoofParts.TailTypes`, `TailProfile`; #99). Selected in plan, each edge of a pitched roof shows a slope marker (`roofSlopeGrips`: `slope` grip kind, a click toggles eave and gable, the pitch or G drawn; #100). Reusable roof templates live in the browser's local storage, shared by all projects (`roofTemplateLibrary.ts`: save, rename, delete, JSON import and export; the configurator's **Save as template…** and the roof's style list; #101). **Explode** (`architecture.explodeRoof`, the inspector and the right-click menu, one undo step) turns a roof into Studio solids, one per part (#102). The wall plate, ridge, purlin and collar tie have their sections (`plateSection`, `ridgeSection`, `purlinSection`, `collarSection`), and the Wood tab has **Roof framing** and **Roof timber** (#104). A roof's outline takes arcs and rounded corners as a slab's (`arcs`, `architecture.setEdgeArc`; #105). A custom multi-layer buildup (`layers`: role, material, thickness; the configurator's **Layers** tab) with per-layer, per-edge `layerExtents` that stop short of the overhang or reach past it (#106). `architecture.unfoldRoofCovering` makes a drafting view of the covering laid flat at 1:50 with edge lengths, openings and areas (`RoofUnfold.cs`; #107). Tests: `RoofTests`, `RoofFramingTests`, `RoofUnfoldTests`, `roofEdges.test.ts`, `roofTemplateLibrary.test.ts`. Left: the templates stay on one computer (not in the project or the cloud).

**Zooming in stays fast (forum topic 162, 19.11.2026).** A tester on Windows found the desktop app nearly stuck when zoomed in to the maximum, the web version not. Two costs grew with the zoom. In 2D views (plans, sections, sheets) each pen was drawn whole in screen pixels while its dashes stay a few pixels long, so at 2000 px/m the browser dashed every line hundreds of thousands of pixels past the screen; `DrawingLayer` now cuts each segment to the screen (`viewport/screenClip.ts`: Liang–Barsky, the start moved by whole dash periods so the dashes stay where they were), leaves out loops wholly off it and texts beyond their reach. In 3D the occlusion (Medium and Realistic, kept per origin, so the desktop and the browser may differ) sampled 0.6 m round every pixel: close up that spanned the whole screen; its reach is now held to 96 device pixels at the distance looked at (`aoRadius`, `render3d.ts`). The 3D view asks for the high-performance graphics chip (`powerPreference`), as a webview on a two-GPU laptop may be given the integrated one. Tests: `screenClip.test.ts`, `render3dAo.test.ts`. Left: confirming on the tester's machine; WebView2's `--force_high_performance_gpu` would have to go on every window (secondary windows share the browser arguments).
**Align, forum topics 109, 170 and 171 (decision 708, 19.11.2026).** Revit's Align, asked for three times: Modify → Align (ALIGN or AL on the command line, the keys A L). The first click takes a reference line on anything the plan shows (a wall's face, axis or kept reference line, a grid axis, a reference plane, a floor's or a column's edge, a drafted line: the straight piece of its plan lines nearest the click, lit under the cursor), the second the line on the element to move, which must be parallel within 0.1°. The engine's `element.align` {ids, reference, line, referenceId, lock, frame} (`ElementAlign`) works out the move square to the reference and runs it through `ElementTransform.Run` and `JoinWhatMeets`, so a wall keeps its joins as Move does, a door or window slides along its wall, a grid axis or a drafted line moves as Move moves it, a pinned element refuses; one undo step. After each alignment a new reference is asked; Multiple alignment (the command bar, M, or Ctrl held on the click) keeps the reference until Enter or Esc. Lock (L) links an element aligned to a grid axis or a reference plane to it through `Pins.LockTo` (pinned, following it); other references align without it, as the existing locks link only to those two. In a section or an elevation the step takes elements by their drawn marks too (`Step.pickInViews`; the view's frame carries the move up or down), less tried than the plan. Left out: Revit's lock on any reference, and a wall's end extended to a perpendicular reference (Trim/Extend elements does that). Tests: `AlignTests` (a wall's face to another wall's, a wall onto a grid axis and locked, a column to a slanted drafted line, a drafted line to a wall's axis, non-parallel refused, the reference itself, a pinned element and a lock without axis refused), `align.test.ts`; the guide's Walls page (both languages) and the `walls-align` scene.
**Forum 90 and report #157: the Array dialog by method, a sheet's own title block fields, a drafted hatch's size (decisions 709–711).** Petri's polar array ("Round a centre") stopped with a row's message, "An array's step cannot be nothing"; today's engine and dialog make a polar array correctly (checked in the app with the guide runner), so the dialog must have sent a row, and the exact cause in his build could not be reproduced. The dialog no longer leaves room for it: `AskBox` takes fields shown only for some answers (`show`) and a `check` that keeps the box open with the field marked and the reason named by it, and the box moves by its title like every dialog (`dialogDrag.ts` takes `.ask-box`, never remembering its place); the rules live in `arrayDialog.ts` (per method: a row's copies and step, a grid's columns, rows, levels and steps, a polar array's copies, angle and centre, a path's as before), a polar array's centre is clicked in a plan (`ARRAYCENTRE`, the copies drawn round the pointer) or typed, and the engine's messages name the fields. Petri's Finnish title block printed `<sheet.…>` for fields of its own and the sheet had nowhere to fill them: any `{sheet.<key>}` is now a field of the sheet (its value, else the project information's field or parameter of the same key, else empty), `sheet.titleBlockFields` lists what a sheet types (the usual fields, then its template's own) with what prints when empty, and the sheet's properties show them all, the fallback greyed. Report #157 asked that a hatch keep its proportion to the drawing when the scale changes: an element's hatch does when its material's Units is Real size (a Drafting one keeps its look on paper by design), and a drafted hatch now has the same `units` (`drafting.hatch`, `drafting.updateDraft`; changed without a scale, the scale is turned by the view's so it looks the same; too dense real-size hatches are left out by `minHatchSpacing`), with **Units** in its properties. Tests: `TitleBlockTests`, `DraftTests`, `arrayDialog.test.ts`, `titleBlockModel.test.ts`; guide scene `array-polar`; the guide's Arrays, title block and real-size sections in English and Romanian.
**Walls meeting at one point clean up their layers; new plans keep their default (19.11.2026, decisions 712 and 713).** The forum's topic 164 ("with two walls it works, with a third wall the cleanup fails"): a node of three walls or more (decision 368) was cut as a fan on the bodies alone, each face to its neighbour's, which left a hole round the point (a triangle at a T drawn as three walls, a square at a cross of four ends) and no layer cleanup; two walls ending at one point on a run overlapped each other, and a wall drawn through an L corner was not joined at all. Now each layer edge at a node meets its like on the neighbour on its side, as at a mitred corner of two (`Walls.NodeCorner`, `NodeEdges`, the `CornerEdges` pairing; a neighbour in line leaves it square), and the gap the cores leave at the point is filled by one core (`NodeFill`: the highest core priority, then element priority, thicker core, first made), so the cores meet with no line and the finishes turn each inner corner. `Joins.NodeOf` also takes a T node: ends on one side of a wall running through the point, that wall in the list twice (its two halves); `Joins.Auto` joins an end at a point on a run to the ends already there (and the run to an L corner it passes through), the run's layers on their side interrupted between the halves' cuts (`NodeGaps`); `Pieces` orders a gap's lines along the band's middle, as a node's are slanted. Tests: `WallNodeTests` (T of three ends both drawing orders, L with a diagonal, cross of four ends, two types, a Y, two ends on a run, a run through an L: no hole nor overlap in the plan's cuts, plaster at the inner corners, brick through the node). In-app reports #165–166 (a 230 mm wall, plaster 15 + brick 200 + plaster 15, drawn in plan as its brick alone, the plasters only in the selection's outline): not a bug but decision 500's default, a new project's Architectural plans *Without finishes*. A change to draw every layer in a new project's plans was made and taken back: the user keeps decision 500's default (19.11.2026). *Entire model* in the chip beside the plan's scale, or Manage → View styles → Structure in plans, shows the finishes.

**One list of drafting layers (forum topic 168, decision 714).** A layer made with the `layer` command (`drafting.setLayer`) or Draft → Layers lived in the project's `draftLayers`, which drafted shapes draw by; Manage → Drafting standards → Layers edited a list of its own in the standards (`drafting.layers`, with ids of its own, pens as `lineWeight`, layer groups), which nothing drew by (`Standards.LayerShows` had no caller): a tester's LINE 0.6 showed in Properties but not in Drafting standards. Now there is one list, `draftLayers`, kept by `Lapin.Platform/DraftLayers.cs`: `DraftingResources.Book` shows it as the standards' layers (id = name, pen = `weight`, continuous as no line type), and `DraftingResources.Write` (every `standards.*` command, the standards library and its `.lapinstandards` import) writes their layers back into it, a layer the same as stored kept as stored (a DXF's line type the standards do not know survives other edits), the standards keeping no `layers` of their own. `standards.set` on a layer names it by its name (a new one taken: „Name (2)”), renames it with its shapes (`DraftLayers.Repoint`, shared with `drafting.setLayer`), refuses to rename layer 0; `standards.remove` moves its shapes to layer 0, as `drafting.removeLayer` does; `standards.replace` lays a file's layers over the project's by name and keeps the others. A `.lapinstandards` file carries the drafting layers by name; an older file's layers (the standards' own ids) come in by name, ignoring case, keeping the project's spelling. A drafting layer keeps the standards' `group`, and the group's switches now hide and lock its layers' shapes (`CadLayer.Shows`, `Held`). `DraftLayers.ListUpgrade` (core) merges an older project's `drafting.layers` into `draftLayers` on open: a layer both lists hold keeps the drafting layer's values and takes the standards' group, one only the standards held is added with its values, a default layer 0 adds nothing. Tests: `DraftLayerListTests` (the command's layer in the standards and an edit there changing the drawn pen, colour and dash, one undo step, off, rename, remove; a standards layer as a drafting layer, its group hiding it, other standards edits leaving the layers as stored; the upgrade; the `.lapinstandards` round trip, an older file, `standards.replace`).

**A drafted line's own line type and pen; dash dot dot among Lapin's line types (issue #89, report #159, decision 715).** Properties showed a drafted shape's line type and pen read-only, from its layer; only its layer and colour could change. Now a drafted line, polyline, rectangle, circle, arc or ellipse may hold its own `lineType` (one of Lapin's or the project's, by name) and `weight` (pen, 0.05–2 paper mm), which go before its layer's, set and cleared (null: by layer) by `drafting.updateDraft` in one undo step; `Drafts.WeightOf` and `Drafts.DashOf` draw it in views, so on sheets and in the PDF, and the DXF writes it on its layer with its own 370 line weight and 6 line type when they differ from the layer's (`DxfWriter.Own`). In Properties the Line type list starts with *by layer* (the layer's shown beside it) and the Pen field has a *by layer* link, as Colour has. A project line type renamed or removed is followed on drafted shapes (`StandardsLibrary.Repoint`). Lapin's line types gain `dashdotdot` (6, 1, 0.3, 1, 0.3, 1 paper mm: ISO 128's long dash double-dotted, the section and phantom line), offered wherever a layer's, an axis style's or a shape's line type is chosen; the Line types tab writes what each built-in is for („Dash dot dot: section lines, phantom lines”); a DXF line type with two dots to a dash, or named DIVIDE, PHANTOM or …DOTDOT, now reads as it rather than as dash-dot. Tests: `DraftPenTests` (own pen and dash drawn, the DXF's per-entity codes, undo, continuous over a dashed layer, back to by layer, refusals; a line type renamed and removed followed; dashdotdot listed with its description; DIVIDE read).
**DXF blocks kept as symbols in 3D, and placed symbols in 3D (GitHub issue #103, decision 716, 19.11.2026).** A DXF imported with **Blocks → keep as symbols** showed its blocks on the plan but nothing in 3D, though the copy's properties showed the import's **In 3D** ticked; exploded, the same blocks appeared. The cause: the 3D view's DXF batches (`Underlays3d.Batches`, decision 382) took only drafted shapes (`DRAFT`), and a placed symbol (`SYMBOL`) had no 3D geometry at all (`Drafts.Geometry3d` returned nothing for it), so an exploded block, made of drafted shapes, showed and the same block as a symbol did not; a symbol saved from drafting with **Replace the drafting** had no 3D either. Now `SymbolInstances.Linework` gives a placed symbol's strokes and fill loops in its view's metres, as the view draws it (pose, scale, flips, the layers its attributes hide; texts left out); a copy that came from a DXF (`dxf` or `dxfImport`) is part of the DXF's drawing (`Underlays3d.IsUnderlay`) and goes into its import's and layer's batch, under the import's and the link's 3D switches and View → DXF in 3D, where the exploded block would lie; any other placed symbol is an element with lines on its view's plane (`SymbolInstances.Geometry3d`, through `Drafts.Geometry3d`), as drafted shapes are, under View → Drafts in 3D; a symbol on a sheet or a 3D view has none. The interface asks for the batches again when a DXF copy or a symbol's definition changes (`underlays3d.ts`). Tests: `DxfSymbol3dTests` (a block with a line, a bulged polyline and a BYBLOCK circle inserted turned and red: in its batch at the plan's level, on its layer, in the insert's colour, where and as large as the same file exploded, the import's switch, moved; a symbol saved from drafting at model and annotation scale standing where its drafting stood, moved and turned; one on a sheet without 3D), `underlays3d.test.ts`.
**Grid axes snap; Grid only (decision 720, issue #109, forum 151).** A grid's axes were drawn in plans but never offered to the snap engine (the plan's `grid` items are skipped for snapping). They are now the **Grid axis** snap (`gridAxis` in `snaps.ts`; lines from `gridAxisShapes` in `elementSnaps.ts` over the shown IfcGrid nodes, passed by `PlanView` through `useCadCanvas`): axis ends, crossings of two axes (`gridCrossings`, each axis reaching 0.5 m past its ends), the perpendicular foot from the previous point, and a line snap along the axis; it is on by default and in the override menu. **Grid only** is a lasting mode (`gridOnly` in the CAD store, the GRID toggle beside SNAP, *Grid only* in the SNAP menu): `modesFor` leaves `gridAxis` alone, `snapsOn` snaps even with SNAP off, `aim` drops the guide lines, relations and snap-to-grid; Esc ends it when no command is running; typed `GRID` is the one-point override; a one-point override or *No snap* still wins. Tests in `gridSnap.test.ts`.
**An element's graphics in a view; finish layers on columns (19.11.2026, decisions 718 and 719).** Issue #88 (report #158, Enrique): a strip foundation's hatch scale and its hatched elevation had no place of their own; its hatch came only from its material or the view's category. A third level now lies over both, as Revit's Override Graphics in View → By Element: the view keeps, per element (`elementGraphics`, on the view itself, never in a graphics set or a style), its cut hatch (pattern or none, scale, angle), cut fill, the hatch and fill of its faces seen in elevations (none takes a hatched elevation off), the hatch colour and halftone, its cut and projection lines, or the whole element halftone. It is tier 1 of `ViewMaterialGraphics` (`ElementOver`), so plans, sections, elevations, sheets, the PDF and DXF draw it; `view.elementGraphics` writes it in one step, for the view in front or every plan, section and elevation; the properties panel's *Graphics in view* and a 2D view's right-click *Graphics in this view…* open its dialog. Forum topic 169 (Goe, issue #108): a column type takes finish layers round its core (`finishLayers`: plaster, fire boards, insulation; all round or on chosen faces), laid round the hull of its section (a steel section boxed), each a part `layer:n` in its material: cut in plans and sections with its hatch, ruled by Visibility / Graphics' subcategories and the view styles' layers modes as a wall's layers (through `LayerSets.DrawnBandsOf`), shown in 3D, measured per layer (`Lapin_ColumnLayerQuantities`, the core's base quantities its own), written to IFC as an `IfcMaterialConstituentSet` (IFC4 keeps layer sets for planar elements), a wall round the column stopping at its outer layer; a type without them builds as before. Tests `ElementGraphicsTests`, `ColumnLayerTests`; scenes `element-graphics`, `structure-column-layers`.
**R and U-values from the layers (issue #67, forum topic 129, decision 717).** Walls, floors and roofs work out their thermal resistance and U-value from their layers after EN ISO 6946, on every ask and never stored (`Thermal`, Lapin.Platform): each layer's R = d/λ, R = Rsi + Σ R + Rse, U = 1/R, from the element's own layer set when it has one, else its type's (a type from its own; a roof from its custom layers, a framed roof's rafter zone noted as not counted). λ is the material's own (`Pset_MaterialThermal.ThermalConductivity`, a parameter *Thermal conductivity* or λ), else, unless the project turns it off, Lapin's indicative λ by the material's name (`Thermal.IndicativeLambda`: concrete 2.00, reinforced 2.30, screed 1.40, solid brick 0.80, hollow brick 0.46, ceramic block 0.30, AAC 0.16, mineral wool 0.040, EPS 0.040 / graphite 0.032, XPS 0.035, PIR 0.024, softwood 0.13, gypsum board 0.25, gypsum plaster 0.40, lime-cement plaster and render 0.87, glass 1.00, steel 50…, typical tabulated values after EN ISO 10456 and C107/3, not normative); an unventilated air layer takes its R from Table 8. Lapin's materials are made by name and carry no data, so the indicative values are worked out from the name rather than written into materials (an import stays as its file gave it). A layer without λ is named and U is not given. The surface resistances come by case (external wall 0.13/0.04, wall to an unheated space, wall against the ground, roof 0.10/0.04, ceiling under an unheated space, floor over outside air 0.17/0.04, over an unheated space, on the ground), chosen by kind (a base slab on the ground, a roof slab a roof) or per type or element (`thermal.setCase`), the project's own values over Lapin's indicative ones (`thermal.setSettings`, Manage → Thermal settings). Shown in the properties panel of walls, floors, roofs and their types (the Thermal section: U, R, each layer's λ and R, indicative values starred, what is missing); read by tags, schedules and formulas as `UValue`, `RValue`, `UValueMissing` and `ThermalTransmittance` (the typed one first, so the *Thermal transmittance* tag type shows it); the type assemblies schedule gains λ, R and U columns. IFC export writes `Pset_WallCommon` / `Pset_SlabCommon` / `Pset_RoofCommon.ThermalTransmittance` from the worked-out U when every layer has its λ and the user typed none on the element or its type (the typed value wins; not for imported elements). Tests: `ThermalTests` (the hand-computed U of a four-layer wall, missing λ, cases and settings with undo, fields, field and type assemblies schedules, IFC, the indicative table); the guide (walls, floors and roofs, materials, EN and RO) and its scene `thermal`.
**How far a section sees: the far clip dragged in plan, Cut + near and Cut only (19.11.2026, decision 721, issue #93).** Enrique's report #163 asked for a limit on what a section shows, "most of the time just the cut (details), the rest in the background". A section had its depth already, set only as a number in its properties. Now it has a grip on its far line in plan, and a dashed far line when it is selected; the grip moves the depth in one undo step (`view.setDepth`). The depth can also be typed under **Depth** in the view's settings. A view style field, **Beyond the cut**, which a view can set for itself, decides what is drawn up to the depth. **Full depth** draws everything, as before. **Cut + near** draws the parts lying wholly farther than **Near depth** (1 m) with the existing `beyond` role: a thin grey pen on its own layer, with their surface hatches greyed (ArchiCAD's marked distant area). **Cut only** draws only the cut, with nothing seen, hidden, silhouetted, hatched on faces or shadowed beyond it. Elevations got the same, their depth counted from where they start seeing, and a far grip once they have a depth. One place applies the setting to every section spec (`Views.Seeing`), and `Generator` decides by `ViewSpec.BeyondFrom`/`CutOnly`, so sheets, the PDF and the DXF follow the view, and Show in 3D keeps the depth. No upgrade is needed: a style or view saved before reads as Full depth. docs/sections.md; tests `SectionDepthTests`, `viewDepth.test.ts`; the guide's scene `section-depth` (docs/user/views.md).
**Wall hatches turn with the wall; the batt runs along the wall's axis (decision 725).** Seen in the material-graphics tutorial: a plan's wall hatches kept their angle on the sheet whatever the wall's (a vertical wall's brick courses ran across it), and the insulation's batt drew wrong (loops on one face only in a corner band, nothing in a curved wall). The first was the default: a material's `hatchAlign` left unset was `view`, fixed to the sheet (Revit's drafting patterns), and only an element's own `hatchAlign: axis` (decision 369) turned it. Now a material's alignment has a third value, `element` (`HatchAlign.Element`, Revit's model patterns): in a plan every band of an element with an axis (`start`/`end`: walls, beams) takes the axis's angle and origin (decision 679's hatch origin), so a wall at 30° hatches MASONRY's 45° lines at 75° and the courses run on from layer to layer; an element without an axis (slabs, columns) and every element in a section draw it as `view`; a curved wall's hatch runs along its chord. A material left unset takes its project's default, `MaterialHatchUpgrade.AlignDefault`: `element` in a project made since, `view` in one saved before, which `MaterialHatchUpgrade` (`hatchRev` 4) marks `viewAlignedHatches` on opening, so old projects keep their drawing (a material set to `element` aligns there too). `MaterialGraphics.Resolve` takes the default (`alignDefault`), given by `ViewMaterialGraphics` and by `material.list` and `material.sample`, so the editor shows it; `Views.Render` gives `MaterialDrawing.Apply` each element's axis (`elementAxis`), the element's own choice still over its material's. The batt (`BattHatch`) is laid in a band frame (`BandAxis`: s along, y across), the element's axis when the drawing has one (`PathCurve.BandAxis`: straight, or the arc of a curved wall, so the loops follow the curve), else the band's longest edge as before (the same segments as before to a few micrometres); its faces are the band's sides that run along the frame (not every corner of the polygon, so a mitre, a corner piece or a notch no longer shifts the line off one face); the curve (s = 0.45h·t − 0.7h·sin 2t, y = h·cos t: a loop on each face, the strokes crossing between them, as AutoCAD's BATTING and Revit's batt insulation) is unchanged; its phase follows the axis, so the line does not move when a wall's ends do. DXF writes the pens, so it follows; IFC wrote no hatching for BATT (no line family) and now writes INSULATION's zig-zag with its curve style named BATT, which Lapin reads back. The Align field offers *With the element*, *Fixed to the view*, *Each layer* (in every language). Tests: `WallHatchDirectionTests` (walls at 0°, 30°, 90°, 147° hatched at 45°, 75°, 135°, 12°, and the material's own 15° on top; fixed to the view; an old project kept at 45° and moved by a material set to the element; a curved wall's brick along its chord and its batt between the wool's faces round the arc; the DXF's lines at 75°, the IFC's BATT), `BattHatchTests` (a band at 0°, 30°, 90°, 147° with a mitre and a notch: every point inside the faces, both faces touched at least every two pitches; a band laid along a curved axis), `WallHatchAlignTests` and `TitleBlockTests` (their old drawings asked for as before: a material fixed to the view, `Fixture.ViewAlignedHatches`), `hatchUnits.test.ts`; the material graphics reference snapshot's composite-walls plans redrawn. Guide: material-graphics.md (EN, RO).
**A view's annotations, north arrow and scale bar (issue #111, report #176; decision 722) and text tags (issue #92, report #162; decision 723), 19.11.2026.** What existed: Visibility / Graphics' Annotation categories (decision 394: dimensions, texts, tags, room tags, grids, levels, section and elevation marks, callouts, reference planes, view boundaries, legends, symbols), Hide in view for elements (forum topic 73), a scale bar annotation (`SCALEBAR`, decision 620) and a tag's own template (`{field}|{field}`). In Lapin, annotations drawn on a view stay on it; what a view shows that is not its own are the model's (grids, levels' marks, rooms' stamps, a site plan's ground-floor stamps among them) and other views' marks (section, elevation, callout marks). Now (1) Hide in view takes annotations (a text, a dimension, a tag, a level's mark, another view's marker, a north arrow) and one grid axis (`grid#A`), removed with their marks in `Views.Render` and printed so, drawn magenta with Reveal hidden; `VisibilityGraphics.OwnerOf` reads `grid#A` as its grid, so a grid's axes go with the grids category too (they did not before); (2) `ownAnnotationsOnly` in a view's graphics (the tab's *Only this view's own annotations*, `view.graphics`): an annotation mark whose owner is not the view's leaves (`VisibilityGraphics.Foreign`), an inset's content stays; (3) a north arrow annotation (`NORTHARROW`, `sheet.addNorthArrow`, Annotate → North arrow) on a plan or a sheet's paper (there pointing as its viewport lays the plan out, a turned plan included): size, its own symbol (the `northArrow` slot on it, else its view's, else the project's), its category *North arrows and scale bars* (`keys`); a north arrow or a scale bar on a view without `at` stands where a site plan drew its own (`DrawingKeys.AutoAt`) until moved by its grip (`sheet.updateKey` `at`; null puts it back); a scale bar's `look: site` draws as a site plan's did. A site view's north arrow and scale bar are such annotations (made with the view, copied with it, added or removed by the site settings' checkboxes, Move takes them), and a project saved before gets them on open (`SitePlans.KeysUpgrade`, the `siteKeys` flag; `northArrow`/`scaleBar` false leaves one out), so the drawing is as before. Per sheet viewport: a sheet's own north arrow or scale bar names its viewport. (4) A text tag: `drafting.addTag`/`updateTag` `text` (lines on new lines, `{…}` read from the element, a `|` kept as typed), drawn by the tag's box or elbow and its type's style, never a build-up's or a symbol's; the Tag tool's *Free text* source asks for the words in a box of several lines (`askText`, Ctrl/⌘+Enter takes it), Properties edit them, a double-click asks them again; a field chosen takes the text off. Tests: `ViewAnnotationTests`, `TagTests` (two), `SitePlanTests`, `CdeTests`, `viewKeys.test.ts`; guide: `interface.md`, `views.md`, `site.md`, `drafting.md`, `sheets.md` in both languages, scenes `view-north-arrow`, `tag-free-text`, `views-vg-filters`.

**A merged terrain keeps its boundary; a terrain's cut and fill shown beside the net (19.11.2026, decision 726).** Two problems found filming the terrain tutorial. (1) *Merge terrains* removed the boundary outright (`attrs.Remove(boundary)`), so a terrain with a drawn boundary, split and merged again, came back as its points' convex hull. Split was right (each half carries the boundary cut to its side). A merge now writes the union of the terrains' boundaries (`OutlineUnion`, Lapin.Platform, new; no dependency): each outline counter-clockwise, every straight edge broken where another's edge crosses or ends on it, an edge kept when outside every other outline, an edge two share running opposite ways (the cut) dropped, one running the same way kept once; walked into loops (the rightmost turn at a shared corner), and corners left in the middle of a straight run dropped only where an edge was dropped or none of the outlines had a corner (a corner the user drew in line stays). An arc touched by no other outline stays an arc, so a split arc comes back as its two arcs; a touched one is taken as its chords (`Chords`). A terrain with no boundary counts as its TIN's outline; when none of them had one, nothing changes (as before). When the union is not one outline with no gap (terrains apart, or a ring round a gap), the merge goes on with no boundary and its result carries `problems` (the message `SiteCommands.MergedBoundaryProblem`, shown by the Merge tool, in all ten languages). GlobalIds, one undo step and saved projects are unchanged (no upgrade: a merge done before stays as it was). Found on the way, not changed: `civil.setBoundary` with `points` picks the terrain under the first point or by `terrainId`, not by `id`. (2) Not an engine defect: `Lapin_TerrainQuantities` already had *CutVolume* and *FillVolume* beside *NetVolume*, and each platform its own `Lapin_EarthworkQuantities` (part `pad:<id>`, named after it), all written to IFC; the properties panel's Quantities list (`QuantitiesSection.SHOWN`) let only *NetVolume* through. It now shows *CutVolume* and *FillVolume* (their labels in the `earthwork` context, so Romanian reads Săpătură/Umplutură, not a section's Tăietură), *Net cut − fill* is translated, and a set with nothing to show (a subregion's plan area alone, which showed an empty heading) is left out. Tests: `TerrainMergeBoundaryTests` (six: a seven-corner boundary split along x = 20 and merged back to the same seven corners and area, undo; two overlapping rectangles → their eight-corner union, area 1100; terrains apart → the problem; none bounded → unchanged; `OutlineUnion` on a cut arc, pieces apart and a ring with a gap; a platform levelled at 1.2 m on the 5 % slope → cut 9, fill 49, net −40 m³, a second platform's fill 125 m³, three *CutVolume* and three *FillVolume* in the IFC file), `CivilTests.Irregular_concave_boundary…` (the L comes back, 1200 m²), `QuantitiesSection.test.ts`; guide `site.md` in both languages.
**Marks of types and elements; dimension values in their text style's colour, Edit style… (forum topics 177 and 173, decisions 731 and 732, 19.11.2026).** Topic 177 (LVK): a door's or window's type had its Mark at the top of its editor, a wall nowhere. A wall type already held `mark` (created with one, read by `{mark}` after an element's own and before the type's name, `{type.mark}`, schedules, type exchange), but no editor showed it and nothing set it after creation. Now `element.setMark {ids, mark}` (core, one undo step; empty clears; refused on the spatial structure and annotations; a door's or window's type writes its spec's mark too) sets a type's or an element's mark; the UI shows the type's **Mark** under its name in the Wall, Floor, Ceiling, Curtain wall, Stair and ramp and Structure (column, beam, footing) types editors (`TypeMarkField`), at the top of a selected element's **Type parameters**, and an element's own **Mark** under its name in Properties (`ElementMarkField`, its type's shown greyed). Tags read a new `{TypeMark}` (the type's alone, also in formulas); IFC writes the type's mark as Pset_…Common **Reference** (its name, as before, when it has none) and an element's own mark as its **Tag** (its id, as before, when it has none). Topic 173 (ingciccio): a dimension style naming a text style of its own colour drew its values in the dimension style's colour, black: `Dimensions.Draw`, `DimensionKinds` and `PartDimensions` wrote the values with the lines' colour and never read the text style's. Now `Standards.DimTextColor`: a dimension's own colour (`o.color`) first, else the dimension style's new **Text colour** (`textColor`, a clearable column of the Dimensions tab), else its text style's colour, else the style's colour; the lines keep theirs. The engine draws it, so views, sheets, the PDF and the DXF follow. **Edit style…** beside a dimension's Dimension style, a text's Text style and a tag's Tag type opens Manage → Drafting standards on that tab with the style lit (`openStandards(tab, styleId)`, `standardsFocus`). Tests: `ElementMarkTests`, `DimensionTextColourTests`, `markField.test.ts`; guide: `walls.md`, `drafting.md` in both languages.
**Forum topics 176, 178 and 180 (tester LVK, 19.11.2026; decisions 734–736).** (734, topic 180) A lift-and-slide portal drew a mullion right across the leaf's inner track, so the leaf could not slide past the fixed pane. `Joinery.SlidesPast` finds, for a lift-and-slide or tilt-and-slide leaf (or a sliding window's sash, whose tracks alternate), the column boundary on its sliding side where a mullion parts it from a neighbour on the other track (`TrackOf`: −1 inner, +1 outer, 0 none; `HasTracks`); `Bars` builds that mullion only off the slider's track (the fixed pane's frame, on the outer track), and `PanelRect` lets the slider reach over the mullion so the meeting stiles overlap. Plans, sections, 3D, the configurator's preview, IFC panel widths and the elevation follow, as they all read `Boxes`/`PanelRect`; two sliding sashes passing each other lose the mullion between them altogether; swing doors and sashes on the same track keep it whole. No upgrade: the geometry is derived. Tests `LiftSlideTrackTests` (6). (735, topic 176) Edit type… opened Doors and windows (and most types dialogs) on the first type of the list; only the wall types dialog read the current type. `shell/typeToEdit.ts` (`openTypeDialog(dialog, id)`, `typeToEdit(dialog)`, forgotten when that dialog closes) now opens the joinery, floor, stair/ramp, ceiling, skirting, curtain wall, wall and structure types dialogs on the element's type, from the right-click menu and from the gear beside Type in the properties; modules give their own editor by type class (`LapinModule.editType`: structure, furniture, sunshades, gates, fans, site works), so Edit type… appears for columns, beams and footings too. Test `typeToEdit.test.ts`. (736, topic 178) A window's sills (`WindowSills`: the detailed SillInside/SillOutside, else the type's plain boards; not doors, bay windows or frameless glass) are parts of the window with their own fields: an `IPartSource` `sills` (side, material, length, depth across the wall, projection, thickness, overhang, slope, area, count, `partLabel`), with their corners (`ElementPart.Points`, new); the shipped field schedule *Window sills* (Architecture → Window sill schedule: a row per kind of sill, its count, the windows' marks, total length and area); the window's own derived fields `sillInterior…`/`sillExterior…`; a tag of a part (`drafting.addTag` `part`, or `pick`: the click's point, the smallest part whose outline holds it), its text reading the part's fields first (default `partLabel`), its leader to the part, red with a question mark when the part is gone; the Tag tool's bar chooses the element or a sill, and a click on a sill tags it anyway. IFC unchanged: IFC4 has no element for a sill (IfcCoveringTypeEnum has none) and separate IfcMember/IfcPlate/IfcBuildingElementPart elements would be read back twice, so the sills stay in the window's body with their lengths in its quantities. Tests `WindowSillTests` (5), `tagPart.test.ts`; guide `doors-windows.md`, `schedules.md`, `drafting.md`, `interface.md` in both languages, scene `window-sills`.
**Temporary dimensions core face to core face, for walls, columns and beams (19.11.2026, decision 733).** A user asked for "core face to core face temporary dimension constant whenever I draw and compare wall distance"; the project owner added columns and beams and a universal behaviour. What existed: a selected wall's temporary dimensions (decision 693), across to the parallel walls and grid axes and along from its free ends, read-only while drawing, from its reference line to the other's near face, the dots cycling one dimension's ends without remembering; doors, windows and grid axes had their own; columns and beams none. Built: (1) a **dimension reference** kept per user (`Prefs.tempDimRef`, File → Preferences → Grid and snaps → Temporary dimensions; the Wall, Column and Beam tools' bars, `DimRefChoice`; a right click on a dimension's dot: *Make this the default* and the four choices): *Core faces* (the element's core face nearest the other to the other's core face nearest it; a wall with no distinct core uses its faces), *Faces (finish)*, *Axis / centre*, *Reference line* (the default, decision 693's output unchanged: own reference line to the other's near face); the old Lapin's "Wall dimension snap". Picks made on the dots still win for that dimension and are forgotten when the reference changes. (2) One engine query, `view.dimLines` (core, `DimLines`, Lapin.Platform), gathering `IDimLineProvider` services: each element of the storey as straight lines with axis, faces, core faces, a wall's kept reference line and its joins; walls from architecture (`WallLines.LinesOf`, which `architecture.wallLines` now reads too, its answer unchanged), columns and beams from structure (`MemberDimLines`): a column is two lines through its insertion point along its own x and y, its core the section's outline, its faces the finish layers' outer outline (decision 719); a straight beam its axis with its width; slabs, stairs or footings can plug in later. (3) One UI measuring module (`viewport/lineDims.ts`, `wallDims.ts` now a thin wrapper) and one component (`viewport/LineTempDims.tsx`: the query, the axes, the picks, the drawing with `TempDimMarks`). Walls measure to walls and axes as before; columns to walls, columns and axes (along both their axes, no along dimensions), a value typed moving them with `element.transform`; beams to beams, walls and axes, across and from free ends, moved with `structure.updatePath`; each one undo step. While drawing a wall the line clicked is measured by its core faces worked out from the chosen type's layers and the draw line; while placing a column, from the cursor's column (its type's section and finish layers, `columnLines`; the plan now reports a point tool's cursor through `useDrawnPath`); while drawing a beam, from the line drawn with its type's width. No new command; `AllowedRemote` unchanged. Tests: `DimLinesTests` (5, including core-face distances with layered walls and a plastered column), `lineDims.test.ts` (6), `memberDims.test.ts` (5), `wallDims.test.ts` unchanged; guide: `walls.md` (Dimension reference), `structure.md` (Temporary dimensions), `interface.md` (Preferences) in both languages; scenes `wall-temp-dims-core`, `column-temp-dimensions`, and a check in `preferences`. Not done: curved walls and beams, columns placed by an IFC (no point of their own), slabs' edges and stairs as providers, sections and elevations.

**Rebar in plans, mark prefixes and rebar sets (forum topics 174 and 175; decisions 728–730, 19.11.2026).** (728) A structural plan drew every bar in space, whatever its storey: the bars drawn in a foundation's section showed in every floor's plan. `Rebar3D.Marks` now draws in a plan only its storey's bars, as the types' stirrups already were: a bar in a host when the host is on the plan's storey, a bar with none when some of it lies in the plan's view range (`Views.Range`, depth to top). The `rebar` category of Visibility / Graphics gets subcategories `longitudinal`, `stirrups`, `sets` (`VisibilityGraphics.RebarSubs`), read by the module (`RebarVisibility`): the category hidden now takes the types' stirrups and column bars too (their marks are their beams' and columns', so the platform's `HidesMark` did not), a subcategory its bars, in 2D and in `rebar.bodies`; a host hidden (category, filter, Hide in view) takes the bars in it. (729) Marks keep their number (`mark`, never renumbered by itself) and are written by `RebarMarks`: a prefix by shape group from the settings (`rebarSettings.marks.prefixes`: straight, L, U, stirrup, tie, polyline, other; column bars take the straight prefix, a type's stirrups the stirrup prefix), a bar's own `markPrefix` over it, and a pattern (`marks.pattern`, coded default `{prefix}{n}`; tokens `{prefix} {n} {partition} {d} {shape}` and `{= …}` formulas, e.g. `{prefix}{= n | 00}`). Numbering is per partition and prefix (`Rebars.NextMark(m, partition, prefix)`); with no prefix set every bar shares one numbering, as before. Balloons (smaller text for a long mark), the label token `mark`, the bar bending schedule (grouped by the written mark, sorted by its letters then number), the field schedule (`rebarMark` now the text, `rebarMarkNumber`, `rebarMarkLetters` for sorting) and BVBS (`p` the written mark) write it. `rebar.renumber {partition?}` renumbers from 1 in each partition and prefix, skipping the numbers the types hold. `LabelFacts.Mark` is a string now. (730) `LapinRebarSet` (`RebarSets`): one element laying straight bars (U or L with `legs`) across a slab's, a footing's or a straight wall's extent, at a spacing (`Runs`-like: as many as keep it at most the step) or a count, in a layer (B1/B2/T1/T2; E1/E2/I1/I2, exterior the side of the wall's first layer), at an angle in plan (slab) or horizontal/vertical (wall), the cover in from every face; the outline's holes, slab openings and a wall's door and window holes (`Openings.Place`; Lapin.Rebar now references Lapin.Architecture) stop the bars the cover off their edges; an optional plan `region` bounds a slab set (its edges cut the bars without cover); a bar over the stock length is cut in pieces lapped by the settings' lap (`splice`, default on). The bars are derived on every ask: schedule rows per shape and length (`RebarSets.Owned`; the schedule now takes the geometry), bending schedule and BVBS with sub-marks 12a, 12b… for lengths that differ, `rebar.describeSet`, `rebar.bodies`. Drawn where the host is seen (its storey's structural plan, sections): face on, the bars `show` asks (one by default, the ends, all) and one tag for the set, the multi-rebar annotation — a distribution line across the bars with a tick at each end, the label „28Ø12/150 B1” (label kind `sets`, token `layer`) and the mark's balloon, its place per view (`rebar.placeLabel`, key `set`; the string's offset slides it along the bars); across, the bars as dots tagged along the row. A deleted host takes its sets. Not exported to IFC (bars as model elements stay premium, decision 402). Commands `rebar.addSet` (a host, or a point or a region in a plan), `rebar.updateSet`, `rebar.renumber`; UI: the *Sets* group (Rebar set, Rebar set in region), the set's property section, the Marks block of Rebar settings, the Mark prefix field, the rebar subcategories' names. Tests: `RebarForumTests` (13); guide `rebar.md` in both languages, scene `rebar-sets`. Left: sets in curved walls and sloped slabs, a wall set's region, sets in other directions than along the host's plane, bars staggered at laps and the lap by the Eurocode, a set's own IFC (premium), bars bent round a slab edge (only straight legs).

**Marks built by the user, and tags the same (decision 737, 19.11.2026).** The project owner's decision: „marks should be built by the user: prefix and suffix, like a formula — and the tag the same”. One mechanism for every category, `MarkRules` in the core: a rule per category (walls, wall types, doors, windows, door and window types, floors, columns, beams, footings and their types, stairs and ramps) kept on the project (`markRules`), a **prefix**, a **suffix** and a **pattern** of live text, `{prefix}{mark}{suffix}` by default, where `{mark}` is the mark Lapin gives by itself (the type's mark, else the type's name, else the name: what `TagText.Mark` gave before, now `TagText.Coded`), `{n}` the element's number in its category, `{= n | 00}` a formula, and any field a tag reads (`{storey.code}`, `{type.mark}`, `{width}`, parameters; the rule is filled by `TagText.Fill` with prefix, suffix, n and mark given). The built-in rule gives exactly the marks of before, and nothing is stored while the user has no rule (no `markRules`, no numbers, the IFC unchanged), so a project changes only where a rule is edited. `TagText.Mark` is now: the shop drawings' register (decision 583), the element's own mark (`element.setMark`, which still wins and, cleared, goes back to the rule), the rule's mark, the coded one; `{TypeMark}`, formulas' `typemark`, the field schedules' `type.mark`, the joinery schedule's type mark and IFC (an element's **Tag**, a type's Pset_…Common **Reference**) read the same. Numbers (`markNo`) are kept on the elements and never move by themselves: given when a rule first reads `{n}` (seeded from the digits of the names Lapin numbered, D3 → 3, or of an element's own mark), then to each new element by an `ICommandFollowUp` after any command (the next in its category, or on its storey with **Numbered: On each storey**; a copy whose number another holds gets a new one, an element rebuilt keeps its own); `markRules.renumber` numbers a category again from 1, storey by storey, by rows (top to bottom, left to right), columns or the present order, one undo step (host only in a shared session). Elements an IFC import brought keep the file's marks. Tag types take a **Prefix** and a **Suffix** round their template (`TagType.Written`; build-up tags as they are), with **+ Field…** (the field picker) adding any field to the template; empty by default. `MarkRules.NumbersUpgrade` (an `IProjectUpgrade`) gives the numbers a project's `{n}` rules lack when it opens; projects without rules (all those before) are given back as they came. UI: **Manage → Mark rules…** (`MarkRulesDialog`: prefix, pattern with the field picker and quick tokens, suffix, numbering, the marks previewed by `markRules.preview` as you type, back to the built-in rule, renumber with its order). Commands `markRules.set`, `markRules.renumber`; queries `markRules.list`, `markRules.preview`. Tests: `MarkRulesTests` (11: defaults pinned, marks, tag text and IFC Tag/Reference identical after a rule set and reset; `{storey.code}-W{= n | 00}` → P-W01; seeding; override; renumber and its undo; per-storey numbering; type rules in IFC; a tag type's prefix and suffix; refusals; the upgrade), `markRules.test.ts`; guide `walls.md`, `drafting.md` in both languages, scene `mark-rules`. Rebar keeps its own scheme (decision 729), written the same way.
**Temporary Dimension Properties and angular temporary dimensions (decision 738; a user's feedback with Revit's dialog: "in a composite wall we always consider dimensions from core face to core face", and users asking for angular temporary dimensions they can fix).** Revit's *Temporary Dimension Properties*: Annotate → Dimension → **Temporary dimensions…** (also Manage → Settings, and a dimension dot's right click → **Temporary dimension properties…**) opens a dialog of two groups, kept for the user on this computer (`app/prefs.ts`, OK keeps, Cancel leaves): **Walls** (columns and beams too) **Centrelines** (the old *axis*), **Faces**, **Centre of core** (new), **Faces of core** (the default), and Lapin's **Reference line** below them; **Doors and windows** **Centrelines** (new, `tempDimOpeningRef: 'centre'`) or **Openings** (the default, decision 511's way). Preferences → Grid and snaps shows the same two groups (`viewport/TempDimSettings.tsx`), the tools' lists and the dot's menu stay in step. The centre of core: `DimLine.CoreCentre` (the middle of `CoreMin…CoreMax`) given by `view.dimLines` as `coreCentre` for every provider (walls, columns, beams); a place of its own in `lineDims.ts` only when it is off the axis (a native wall's axis stands on its core: an imported wall's layer usage offset, a column's section off its point). Doors' and windows' temporary dimensions now start by the user's references (`openingDims.tempDims(…, modes)`: the opening's centre with Centrelines, another opening's centre too, a wall's face, core face, centre of core or axis by the walls' choice), the dots still cycling one dimension. Angular temporary dimensions (`viewport/angleDims.ts`, `AngleTempDims.tsx`, a core plan overlay): while a wall, a beam, a line or a polyline is drawn, the angle at the segment's start to the previous segment (inside the corner), else to the wall or beam it starts on, else from X, drawn as an arc; the live box has it as a third field **∡** (`liveInput`: `ref`, `rel`; Tab cycles `liveFields`), typed it sets the direction on the cursor's side (the CAD LINE and PLINE steps give their previous point, `Step.angleFrom`; a chain tool its last piece's start). A selected straight wall or beam shows, at each end where it meets a non-parallel wall or beam (joined there or its end on it), the smaller angle at the point their axes cross; typed, `element.transform` rotate turns it about that point (one undo step, joins worked out again). The padlock makes a permanent angular dimension held on both elements (`drafting.addDimension` `dimKind: angular` with `holds: [{element, way}…]`, `DimensionKinds.Held`: vertex where the axes cross, rays along them, so it follows them; orphaned when one goes or they turn parallel); typed (double-click, in degrees), it turns the second element about the vertex (`DriveAngle`). It does not lock the angle: a constraint that re-turns an element when its reference turns would be new machinery (pins/`lockTo` only translate, decision 700), left for later. Tests: `TempDimPropertiesTests` (3: an off-centre core, a window's centreline numbers, the held angle followed, driven, undone, orphaned), vitest `angleDims.test.ts`, `tempDimProps.test.tsx`, `openingDims.test.ts` (3 more); guide `walls.md` (Dimension reference, Angular temporary dimensions), `doors-windows.md`, `structure.md`, `interface.md`, `drafting.md` in both languages, scenes `temp-dim-properties` and `wall-temp-dim-angle`.
**Locked angles (decision 739; the user's answers to decision 738: doors' and windows' temporary dimensions keep following the walls' choice, and the angle lock is built).** As Revit's padlock on an angular dimension: an angle held on two straight elements (`holds`, decision 738) takes **Locked** (`locked`, the Annotation's field, shown for angular dimensions), and keeps the smaller angle it read when locked (`lockAngle`, degrees; recorded again when its value is typed). `AngleLocks` (drafting) is an `ICommandFollowUp`, so it runs after every command in its undo step, not only after `element.transform` (pins and `lockTo`, decision 700, are refused inside each command and only translate; the follow-up is the one place after any command): an element whose direction the command changed (a turn, an end dragged by `updateWall`, Align, a transform, a batch) has its partner turned about the point where their axes cross by `element.transform`'s rotate, its joins worked out again (`JoinWhatMeets`). Both changed and still at the angle (turned or mirrored together): nothing more. Both changed and off it: the one the command did not name follows (a wall whose end was dragged by its turned neighbour), else the one that turned less. Chains (A–B, B–C) propagate, each element turned by a lock at most once, so there is no loop; a loop that disagrees, a pinned element (or one locked to an axis or a plane) that would have to turn, or two elements turned parallel fails the command with the reason (`CommandException`, the transaction rolled back). A value typed on a locked angle (`DriveAngle`) records the new value and turns the second element. A deleted element orphans the dimension as before; dimensions without `locked` (every project before) are untouched, so no `IProjectUpgrade`. The temporary angle's padlock now makes the dimension locked (`addDimension` `locked: true`); `drafting.dimension` gives `locked`, `vertex` and `at`. UI: `modules/drafting/AngleLock.tsx`, a plan overlay that draws the padlock (shut or open) beside the arc of a selected held angle, a click or its right-click toggling it (`element.update` `locked`), and **Lock the angle / Unlock the angle** in the plan's right-click menu (`dimensionMenu.angleLockItems`); a refused command shows its reason in the error toast. Tests: `AngleLockTests` (6: one turned keeps the angle and undoes in one step, unlock and relock, an end dragged, both turned, a chain, pinned and loop refusals with nothing changed, the typed value, the orphan, the project saved and opened again, unlocked dimensions untouched), vitest `angleLock.test.tsx` (2); guide `walls.md` (Angular temporary dimensions) and `drafting.md` (Locked angles) in both languages, scene `wall-temp-dim-angle` (checks the lock; its picture unchanged).

Temele Clasică și Contrast ridicat (decizia 740): două aspecte luminoase noi lângă paper și graphite. Clasică (`classic`) este un aspect neutru de program desktop — chrome gri, panouri și pânză albe, accent albastru, fontul sistemului mai mic, colțuri drepte, etichete simple; Contrast ridicat (`contrast`) este alb/negru cu text și chenare la ≥ 7:1, accent albastru închis, focus de 3 px, selecție mai groasă în plan și 3D, grilă discretă. `ThemeChoice`/`Theme` le includ, `resolveTheme` le întoarce neschimbate (`system` rămâne paper/graphite), Preferințele le listează, iar butonul soare/lună comută între întunecat și ultima temă luminoasă aleasă (`Prefs.lastLight`). Tokenii sunt în `theme.css` (după `:root { --font }`), accentul mărcii din `boot.tsx` se aplică doar la paper/graphite; 3D are fundal alb și selecție în albastrul temei. Teste: vitest `themeContrast.test.ts` (rapoartele WCAG ale perechilor de tokeni; ≥ 7 pentru text în contrast, ≥ 4,5 în clasică) și `prefs.test.ts` (alegerea, valorile vechi, comutarea rapidă); ghid `interface.md` în ambele limbi, FAQ-ul `classic-contrast-theme`, scena `themes`.

Acoperișuri cu laturi curbe (decizia 741): pe o latură făcută arc sau un colț racordat, solverul dă fiecărei coarde un plan propriu (fațetele unui con, după regula `Chords`), iar piesele acoperișului se făceau tot pe coardă — filmul de azi a ascuns burlanele pentru că streașina curbă dădea 65 („39 m · 70 burlane”). Acum ce poartă acoperișul urmează curba: `Drainage.Runs` leagă coardele unei laturi curbe (și streșinile drepte în care un racord continuă tangent) într-un jgheab continuu, cu un burlan la 10 m la capete, un singur burlan unde două jgheaburi se întâlnesc într-un colț, panta pe tot jgheabul și o singură piesă de jgheab (65 → 3 burlane, 65 → 3 piese de jgheab); `RoofCurves.WithoutCreases` scoate pliurile dintre fațete din liniile acoperișului (104 → 5 căpriori de coamă, 107 → 45 linii în plan, coamele curbe dintr-o piesă); căpriorii stau la distanță de-a lungul curbei și în jurul racordului (155 → 46, fără perechi lipite la racord); cosoroaba și fiecare rând de pane de pe o curbă sunt o piesă (`Member.Path`, corp mitruit; 65 + 65 → 5 + 5), panele la o înălțime; prelungirile straturilor numesc latura, nu coarda. În interfață indicatorul pantei stă pe arc, clic dreapta pe rombul sau grip-ul rotund al laturii dă Fă arc / Fă dreaptă (`edgeOfGrip`), antetul tabelului laturilor nu se mai suprapune, iar șabloanele de acoperiș (versiunea 2) păstrează straturile, prelungirile, secțiunile și laturile, cele vechi aplicându-se ca înainte; ghidul (ambele limbi) nu mai spune că acoperișurile au doar laturi drepte. Teste: xUnit `RoofCurvedEdgeTests` (13, pe acoperișul filmului și pe forme/șarpante curbe), vitest `roofEdges`, `gripKit`, `roofTemplateLibrary`.

**Modelele de mobilier, fiecare în catalogul de pus (decizia 743, 20.11.2026).** Un utilizator a spus că biblioteca de mobilier pare mult mai mică decât înainte. Nu se pierduse nimic (16 feluri, 39 de modele CC0, neschimbate din 11.11); se schimbase afișarea: ribbonul simplificat (15.11) a strâns butoanele pe feluri într-unul, Plasează mobilier, iar Mobilierul meu… deschide catalogul de pus (16.11), unde cele 39 de modele erau o singură intrare, „From a model”. Acum fiecare model e o carte a lui în catalog, cu imaginea, în grupa lui (Sofas, Armchairs, Chairs, Tables, Lamps, Plants, Rugs) și mărimea în mm; și modelele utilizatorului apar. Un clic ia tipul proiectului din acel model (cel ales, dacă e al lui) sau face unul (`furniture.createType`, numit de motor: modelul și mărimea) și pornește Plasează mobilier pe el. Doar interfața (`ui/src/modules/furniture/index.tsx`); ghidul (Mobilier, EN și RO) și scena `place-catalogue`, care verifică cel puțin 39 de cărți de model și că un clic pornește unealta.
**S3: analiza modală și seismică, zidăria ca plăci (decizia 744, 20.11.2026, ramura `feat/fem-s3`).** După hotărârile utilizatorului din 20.11 (P100-1/2013 implicit, EN 1998-1 opțional; zidăria calculată ca plăci; fără diafragmă rigidă impusă peste planșee-placă), specificate în `docs/plan-fem.md` §11. Pereții portanți de zidărie sunt acum plăci MITC4/MITC3 și în calculul static (E = KE·fk, fk = K fb^0,7 fm^0,3, G = 0,4 E după EN 1996-1-1, cum le ia CR 6-2013; valori orientative pe grupe de unități, schimbabile pe material prin `Lapin_Masonry`), liniar elastici, fără limitarea la întindere. Asamblarea și factorizarea solverului sunt un sistem păstrat (`FrameSystem`), pe care analiza modală (`Seismic/Modal.cs`, `Solver/Subspace.cs`: iterația în subspațiu a lui Bathe pe Cholesky-ul rar, masa concentrată din G + ψ2 Q, modurile crescute până la 90 % din masă pe X și Y) și cea spectrală (`Seismic/SeismicRun.cs`, `Spectrum.cs`: spectrul P100 sau EN, CQC/SRSS, torsiunea accidentală, regula de 30 %, forțele tăietoare și deplasările relative de nivel cu verificările SLS/ULS, rigiditatea fisurată, diafragma rigidă opțională) le folosesc. Comanda `analysis.setSeismic` (un pas de undo) și interogările `analysis.seismicOptions`, `modal`, `mode`, `spectrum`, `seismic`; înfășurătoarea `seismic` (G + ψ2 Q ± AEd) în rezultate, diagrame și izolinii, și în cea SLU când parametrii sunt dați. În interfață, secțiunea Seismic a dialogului de rezultate (parametrii, modurile, graficul spectrului, nivelurile, factorii q ai zidăriei) și animația modului în 3D; textele și notele în toate limbile; ghidul (ambele limbi) și scena lui. Teste: xUnit `ModalTests`, `SpectrumTests`, `SeismicAnalysisTests`, `ShellAnalysisTests` (zidăria), vitest `seismic.test.ts`, `notes.test.ts`. Valorile din P100-1/2013 și CR 6-2013 pe care nu le-am putut citi din textul normelor sunt listate la §11.9 pentru confirmare.

**S4: verificările după normă (20.11.2026, ramura `feat/fem-s4`, decizia 745).** Spec la `docs/plan-fem.md` §12, ce s-a hotărât la §12.10. Din rezultatele calculului, derivate la fiecare cerere: **oțel** după SR EN 1993-1-1 (clasa secțiunii, rezistența la N, V, M pe ambele axe cu interacțiunea plastică 6.2.9.1 sau liniară, flambajul 6.3.1 cu lungimi din eliberări, flambajul lateral 6.3.2.2/6.3.2.3 cu Mcr după SN003 și C1 din forma momentului, interacțiunea 6.3.3 cu Anexa B); **beton** după SR EN 1992-1-1 (grinzi: armătura longitudinală simplă sau dublă, etrierii cu cot θ variabil, minimul și maximul, raportul deschidere/înălțime; stâlpi: N cu încovoiere oblică prin compatibilitatea deformațiilor, excentricitatea minimă, curbura nominală; plăci: Wood–Armer pe noduri, armătura pe patru straturi, izolinii; pereți: simplificat), comparată cu armătura pusă pe tip din setările modulului `rebar`; **lemn** după SR EN 1995-1-1 (kmod după clasa de serviciu și durata acțiunii celei mai scurte a combinației, încovoiere, forfecare, flambaj, flambaj lateral, săgeata finală cu kdef); **zidărie** după CR 6-2013 (forțele fiecărui perete la talpă, la mijloc și la cap din forțele nodale ale elementelor, exacte; NRd = Φ A fd la capete și la mijloc, forfecarea prin lunecare, încovoierea în plan cu forța axială; tabelul pereților pe niveluri). Interogările `analysis.checks`, `check`, `walls`, `reinforcement`, `designOptions`, armătura plăcilor în `analysis.contours`; comanda `analysis.setDesign` (un pas de undo). Interfața: secțiunea Checks a dialogului de rezultate, culorile după utilizare în 3D și plan, „Design checks” în Proprietăți, textele în toate limbile, ghidul (ambele limbi) și scenele `analysis` și `analysis-masonry`; scena seismică reparată (poza tăiată sus, forma modului). E-ul BCA trece la 800 fk (CR 6). Teste: xUnit `DesignCheckTests` (cifrele de mână din formulele normelor), `DesignRunTests`; vitest `checks.test.ts`, `notes.test.ts`. Orientativ; valorile de confirmat de utilizator la §12.9.
**Corecturi la afișarea calculului structural după filmare (20.11.2026, ramura `fix/fem-display`, decizia 746).** Filmarea tutorialului seismic a arătat patru probleme (docs/plan-fem.md §11.10). Forma unui mod e acum desenată umbrit (`Mode3D.tsx`: plăcile ca suprafețe opace cu rețeaua schițată, barele ca tuburi), cu modelul în repaus transparent prin `setLooks` (sau ascuns, **Arată nedeformat**), iar amplitudinea e dimensionată în interfață (`modeAmplitude` în `seismic.ts`: deplasarea cea mai mare = 7 % din diagonala modelului, ori **Scalează** de pe card). Dialogul de rezultate are acțiunile într-un subsol fix (`dialog-foot`, cu modul de animat lângă **Animă**) și notele strânse în **Note (n)** (`NotesList.tsx`, `orderNotes`: cele de verificat primele). Oprirea animației readuce cardul diagramelor (`useMode.ts`). În motor, modelul analitic ia acum ca portant un perete care nu spune nimic despre încărcare dacă e din zidărie sau beton cu miezul de cel puțin 10 cm (`AnalyticalModel.UnsaidLeftOut`, `AnalyticalShell.Unsaid`), cu note care îi enumeră; LoadBearing false sau PARTITIONING îl scot, true îl ține. Testele: xUnit `ShellAnalysisTests` (regula nouă), două teste schimbate deliberat să marcheze despărțitorul; vitest `mode.test.ts`; notele noi în toate limbile; ghidul (ambele limbi) și scena `analysis` (pozele `analysis-seismic`, `analysis-mode`).

**Combinațiile configurabile (20.11.2026, ramura `feat/fem-combinations`, decizia 747).** Aprobat de utilizator în aceeași zi (docs/plan-fem.md §13). Coeficienții combinațiilor sunt ai proiectului (`analysisCombinations` pe IfcProject, `CombinationSettings`): un set numit, **CR 0-2012** (γ după Tabelul 7.2, ψ după Tabelul 7.1: zăpada 0,7/0,5/0,4, vântul 0,7/0,2/0, acoperișurile ψ0 0,7, restul ca EN) sau **EN 1990 A1 recomandat**, și peste el γG,sup, γG,inf, γQ și ψ pe categorie ale proiectului (`analysis.setCombinationOptions`); ψ-ul unui caz: al lui > al proiectului > al setului. Un proiect fără setare (toate cele de dinainte) ia EN, adică exact combinațiile de până acum (teste de aur pe planșeul din ghid și pe hala de oțel, cu zăpadă și vânt, scrise de codul vechi); un proiect nou primește CR 0 explicit, prin serviciul nou al platformei `INewProject` (`Session.New`); fără `IProjectUpgrade`. Opțiunea 6.10a/6.10b cu ξ (0,85). Generatele se dezactivează după cheie (`analysis.setCombinationEnabled`). Combinațiile utilizatorului sunt entități `IfcStructuralLoadGroup` (`addCombination`, `updateCombination`, `removeCombination`), cheia `user/<id>`, felurile uls, sls-*, seismic (factorii ± AEd, în înfășurătorile seismică și SLU), accidental (ca SLU); intră în rezultate, înfășurători, verificări (durata la lemn din cazurile lor) și SAV (nu cele seismice); una care numește un caz șters rămâne, spusă, în afara rezultatelor. Exportul JG nu scrie combinații. Interfața: tabul Combinations cu secțiunea Factors, comutatorul fiecărei combinații, formularul „Add a combination”, traduse în toate limbile; ghidul (EN, RO) și scena `loads` (poza nouă `loads-combination-factors`). Teste: xUnit `CombinationGoldenTests`, `CombinationSettingsTests` (CR 0, γ proprii, ψ, 6.10a/b de mână, dezactivare, combinațiile utilizatorului în rezultate/înfășurători/verificări, seismică, SAV, salvare și redeschidere, undo); vitest `loads.test.ts`, `notes.test.ts`. Sursa CR 0: textul trimis la notificare (aprilie 2012); vântul și acoperișurile de confirmat pe textul publicat.

**Desenul de execuție în axele elementului (20.11.2026, issue #114, decizia 748).** Un utilizator a trimis planșa unei bucătării (8064 × 875 × 2275) cu vederea din dreapta aplecată și izometria rotită față de vederea din față și de sus. Cauza: mobilierul nu avea un `IElementFrames`, deci `ElementFrames.FromBody` lua axele principale ale corpului; la o bucătărie corpurile de sus, mai sus și mai în spate decât cele de jos, înclină axa „sus” cu ~3,6°, peste toleranța de 1°, și tot desenul se lua într-un sistem înclinat (adâncimea măsurată 875 în loc de ~600). Acum `FurnitureFrames` (modulul `furniture`) dă axele din așezare (originea în `at` la cota nivelului plus `offset`, X după `rotation`, Z în sus; o piesă oglindită își păstrează axele), iar `FromBody` alege sistemul drept (Z în sus, X după latura lungă a cutiei de arie minimă în plan) când cutia lui nu e mai mare decât cea în axele principale, așa că un corp importat stă drept, iar un căprior înclinat își păstrează axele. Opțiunea **Axe** din fereastra **Fă desenul…** (`axes: element | ucs`, ținută în opțiunile desenului și pe fiecare vedere ca `of.axes`, citită de `ElementViews.FrameOf` și de `PartDimensions`) desenează elementul în UCS-ul proiectului (`ElementFrames.InUcs`). Testele: xUnit `ShopDrawingFrameTests` (bucătăria la 0°, 17° și −123,5°: cadrul, mărimea în axele ei, vederile din față/sus/dreapta și izometria în același sistem ortonormat, cotele; id-urile păstrate la refacere după o rotire; corpul în trepte drept; căpriorul înclinat; opțiunea UCS ținută la refacere); șirurile noi în toate limbile; ghidul în ambele limbi.

**Parapetul de la pardoseala finită (decizia 749, 20.11.2026, ramura `fix/sill-finished-floor`; subiectul 192 de pe forum).** Un parapet se măsura doar de la baza peretelui (nivelul structural), arhitecții îl dau de la pardoseala finită. Acum fiecare deschidere are `sillFrom` (`structural`, implicit și pentru orice import IFC, sau `finished`), proiectul dă valoarea deschiderilor noi (`architecture.setOpeningDefaults`), iar pardoseala finită peste baza peretelui e derivată: `finishedFloor` al peretelui, altfel al tipului de perete, altfel 0 (finisajul încăperii nu se ia, la hotărârea utilizatorului: straturile lui coboară de la nivelul încăperii). Conversia e într-un singur loc (`Openings.Place`, `OpeningSills`), așa că golul, tâmplăria, glafurile, finisajele, parasolarele, verificarea încadrării, desenele, cantitățile și IFC (înălțimea structurală; referința în `Lapin_Intent`) o urmează; mutarea pardoselii finite mută deschiderile legate de ea și e refuzată când una nu mai încape. Fără nimic setat, comportamentul e identic bit cu bit. Teste: xUnit `SillFinishedFloorTests`.
